# The checks of B1 (2026-10-19 to 2026-10-21)

The reports behind [ADR-0109](../../0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md) ("the check of B1" and its finding ids) and [ADR-0097](../../0097-a-step-takes-in-only-what-its-prompt-shows.md)'s Later note, and the finding ids the backlog and the playbook cite (REC2-6, HIST2-2): the adversarial check of the batch on fix/fx-batch (the records pass, the evidence brought in from Temp and B1, the money path before the first live run), made from 2026-10-19 to 2026-10-21; the re-check of its fix round; and the small re-check of the second fix round, both on 2026-10-21. Each had checking lenses and two refuters for each serious finding, who tried to show it was not real; the check also had a completeness critic. They were kept only in the session's scratchpad under Temp (`C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/`): the report given to the owner, `byway-batch-check-2026-10-21.md`, and the results it was written from, `check2-result.json`, `recheck-result.json` and `recheck2-result.json`. Claude brought them in on 2026-10-21. The first part below is the owner's report up to its issues; the rest is each result in full, every finding, refuter's vote, note and lens's coverage in the file's own order (the owner's report left out the refuters' evidence and fixes, and what the critic checked). Claude added the headings and the layout. The text is unchanged but for these:

- **Redactions, by Claude.**
  - The check's evidence and records lenses quoted the D4 brief as it stood before the owner's answer of 2026-10-21 13:41 (finding EVID-PRIV-1 and its refuters' votes), and one vote named a fourth repository the brief did not. Those places are "[redacted]", and such a word is "[a word that holds one of the names]".

## The check of B1 (2026-10-19 to 2026-10-21)

### The report as given to the owner, up to its issues

*byway, branch `fix/fx-batch` at `481b2e5` against main at `8401820`. Checked 2026-10-19 to 2026-10-21 by seven lenses (money path, run start, evidence and tests, records, backlog and cross-document, security, merge integrity), two refuters per serious finding (one asks "does it reproduce?", the other "is it actually wrong, given the records and your decisions?"), and a completeness critic. 58 agents. Nothing has been pushed.*

#### The gate

- Type-check: clean.
- Unit tests: 5,049 of 5,050 passed. The one failure is `packages/runner/test/renderer-check.test.ts`, D2's start-up check: its probe without the sandbox has a real 15 s limit and ran past it while vitest ran many files at once, so the check said "sandbox unknown" instead of "fails". The file is unchanged from main, passed 21 of 21 three times alone, and failed the same way under load in B1's own run. It is a timing dependency in the test, already on main, not a product fault.
- Browser tests: 55 of 55 passed (4.5 min).

## The check of B1: its findings in full

*From `check2-result.json`. Seven lenses (money path, run start, evidence and tests, records, backlog and cross-document, security, merge integrity), two refuters for each serious finding, and a completeness critic; the branch at 481b2e5 against main at 8401820.*

### Findings that survived the refuters (25)

#### MONEY-1: OpenAI cache writes are billed at 1.25x input, but byway records them at the plain input rate, and ADR-0109 says unrecorded rates lean high

*Finding. Severity blocking; outcome confirmed; lens money; where `packages/gateway/src/providers/openai.ts:91`.*

**What is wrong:**

OpenAI's pricing page lists a cache-write rate for every model byway's OpenAI rows name: gpt-6-astra $12.50, gpt-6.1-sol $2.50 and gpt-6-luna $0.125 per 1M tokens, against $10, $2 and $0.10 input. The prompt-caching guide says caching is on by default and that 'For GPT-5.6 and later, cache writes cost 1.25x the standard, uncached input-token rate'. parseChatCompletion reads only prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens and ignores prompt_tokens_details.cache_write_tokens. The rows at prices.ts:64-70 have no cacheWritePerMTok. So tokenCost prices every written token as plain input, which is lower than what OpenAI bills. ADR-0109 Context says 'Every price row matched its page but two', and Decision 1 says unrecorded rates mean 'a cached token is charged at the input rate, so the cost leans high'. For cache writes the cost leans low, and neither the ADR nor the prices.ts comments name this rate.

**Evidence:**

developers.openai.com/api/docs/pricing, read 2026-10-19. Column headers: 'Short context input | Short context cached input | Short context cache writes | Short context output'. Rows: gpt-6-astra $10.00 | $1.00 | $12.50 | $50.00; gpt-6.1-sol $2.00 | $0.10 | $2.50 | $10.00; gpt-6-luna $0.10 | $0.01 | $0.125 | $0.50. developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/prompt-caching says 'Prompt caching is enabled by default for supported OpenAI models', that cache writes cost 1.25x on GPT-5.6 and later, and that 'Cache-write pricing is not an additive fee'. The installed SDK, node_modules/.pnpm/openai@7.28.0.../resources/completions.d.ts:154, has PromptTokensDetails.cache_write_tokens: 'The unadjusted number of prompt tokens written to cache.' A community.openai.com thread dated July 10, 2026 shows prompt_tokens_details.cache_write_tokens on GPT-5.6, so this predates B1's 2026-10-18 reading. openai.ts:91-92 sets usage to { inputTokens: prompt_tokens, outputTokens, cacheReadTokens: cached } with no cacheWriteTokens. grep finds no cache_write, prompt_cache or cacheWrite handling for OpenAI anywhere in packages/gateway/src, and the backlog has no row for it. Balanced uses gpt-6-astra as its otherFamily critic (profiles.ts), and C3, C6 and C7 have family:'other' GREAT critics.

**When it goes wrong:**

A Balanced C3 run with an OpenAI key. The GREAT critic (refs 9, family other, so gpt-6-astra) sends about 16,500 prompt tokens under the advisor's own assumption of PROMPT_TOKENS plus 10 prose views. shuffle:true changes the prefix each round, so each call writes a new cache entry. OpenAI bills 16,500 x $12.50/M = $0.206, and byway ledgers 16,500 x $10/M = $0.165: $0.041 low per call, repeated per loop round. The spend book, the run budget and the first live run's comparison with OpenAI's dashboard all count low, while ADR-0109 tells the owner that unrecorded rates make byway count high. The OpenAI profile is affected the same way on every gpt-6-astra and gpt-6.1-sol call over 1,024 tokens.

**Proposed fix:**

Add cacheWritePerMTok to the three OpenAI rows (12.5, 2.5, 0.125), with the page's wording in the comment. In parseChatCompletion, read prompt_tokens_details.cache_write_tokens into cacheWriteTokens; tokenCost already subtracts it from plain input. OpenRouter's reportedCostUsd still takes precedence. Add a providers or pricing test with a usage that carries cache_write_tokens, and extend pricing.test.ts's row table to cover cacheWritePerMTok for OpenAI. Correct ADR-0109's Context ('matched its page but two') and Decision 1 before it lands on main; it is not yet on main's first-parent history, so it can still be edited.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, blocking.**

**Reasoning:**

I tried to refute the finding on three points and could not.

1. Do cache writes happen without byway asking? Yes. The prompt-caching guide alone does not give the default mode; it says only that "When no explicit breakpoints are placed" in explicit mode, no writes happen. The installed SDK does give it, on the Chat Completions create params at chat/completions/completions.d.ts:1827-1833: "Supported for `gpt-5.6` and later models. By default, OpenAI automatically chooses one implicit cache breakpoint ... Set `mode` to `explicit` to disable the implicit breakpoint. The `ttl` defaults to `30m`." openaiChatParams (openai.ts:80-87) sends no prompt_cache_options. So every gpt-6-astra or gpt-6.1-sol call with a new prefix of 1,024 tokens or more writes to the 30-minute cache. The scenario does not depend on shuffle: any new prompt content up to the last user message is written.

2. Do the rates exist and apply to byway's models? Yes. Fetched today, the pricing page's text-token table has a "Short context 30m write" column: gpt-6-astra $12.50, gpt-6.1-sol $2.50, gpt-6-luna $0.125, against $10, $2 and $0.10 input. The guide says writes cost 1.25x input for GPT-5.6 and later, and that cache-write pricing "is not an additive fee": it replaces the input rate for those tokens, which matches tokenCost's subtraction model.

3. Does byway price them low? Yes. I ran the real parseChatCompletion and tokenCost against PRICES. A usage with cache_write_tokens is parsed with no cacheWriteTokens, the rows have no cacheWritePerMTok, and the gateway settles a direct OpenAI call through tokenCost (gateway.ts:871), since there is no usage.cost. The ledger comes out exactly 25% of the written tokens' input cost low.

Exposure: gpt-6-astra is otherFamily in both Balanced (profiles.ts:42) and Best (profiles.ts:53), and the OpenAI profile uses gpt-6-astra and gpt-6.1-sol as its big and mid tiers (profiles.ts:58).

The fault was already there before B1's reading. SDK openai@7.28.0, which carries cache_write_tokens and the implicit-breakpoint default, entered pnpm-lock.yaml in 658e774 on 2026-10-07. No ADR, backlog row or comment outside the evidence names OpenAI cache writes.

The record is wrong in a money direction:
- ADR-0109 Decision 1 lists the rates byway does not record without these three, and says such rates make "the cost leans high".
- Consequences line 70 says "Rates byway does not record charge a cached token at the input rate, so those calls are recorded above their cost". For writes this is false: they are recorded below cost.
- The spend book and run budgets count low on the default OpenAI path, which ADR-0109 exists to prevent before the first live run.

ADR-0109 is not on main's first-parent history (git log --first-parent main returns nothing for the file), so it can still be corrected in place.

Minor inaccuracies in the finding, none of which change the verdict:
- It quotes the column header as "Short context cache writes"; the page today says "Short context 30m write". The values are identical.
- shuffle:true is not needed for the scenario.
- If part of the prefix is already cached from an earlier call, only the rest is written. The shortfall is then 0.25 x written tokens x input rate, not the whole prompt.
- The "matched its page but two" sentence is defensible for the figures byway records. The false parts are Decision 1's list and the "leans high" claims in Decision 1 and Consequences.

**Evidence:**

- packages/gateway/src/providers/openai.ts:32: ChatCompletionLike has `prompt_tokens_details?: { cached_tokens?: number | null }` and no cache_write_tokens.
- openai.ts:91-92: `const cached = res.usage?.prompt_tokens_details?.cached_tokens ?? 0; const usage = { inputTokens: prompt_tokens, outputTokens, cacheReadTokens: cached }`.
- openai.ts:80-87: openaiChatParams sets no prompt_cache_options.
- packages/gateway/src/prices.ts:65,67,70: the OpenAI rows have no cacheWritePerMTok.
- prices.ts:204: `cacheWrite * (price.cacheWritePerMTok ?? price.inputPerMTok)`.
- gateway.ts:860,871: reportedCostUsd if present, else `tokenCost(priceOf(out.servedModel), out.usage)`.
- SDK node_modules/.pnpm/openai@7.28.0_ws@8.22.0_zod@4.6.5/node_modules/openai/resources/completions.d.ts:151-154: "The unadjusted number of prompt tokens written to cache. cache_write_tokens?: number".
- Same SDK, resources/chat/completions/completions.d.ts:1827-1833: "By default, OpenAI automatically chooses one implicit cache breakpoint ... Set `mode` to `explicit` to disable the implicit breakpoint. The `ttl` defaults to `30m`".
- `git log -S'openai@7.28.0' -- pnpm-lock.yaml` gives `658e774 2026-10-07`.
- WebFetch of developers.openai.com/api/docs/pricing (2026-10-21): headers "Short context input | Short context output | Short context cached input | Short context 30m write | Short context 12h write ...". Rows: gpt-6-astra $10.00 | $50.00 | $1.00 | $12.50; gpt-6.1-sol $2.00 | $10.00 | $0.10 | $2.50; gpt-6-luna $0.10 | $0.50 | $0.01 | $0.125. The image table has no write column.
- WebFetch of developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/prompt-caching:
  - "cache writes cost 1.25x the standard, uncached input-token rate for the default 30-minute lifetime" (GPT-5.6 and later).
  - "Cache-write pricing is not an additive fee: input tokens use the uncached-input, cached-input, or cache-write rate".
  - cached_tokens "excludes tokens charged as writes".
  - Minimum cacheable prompt is 1,024 tokens.
- Reproduction script C:\Users\arvik\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-arvik-code-byway\67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422\scratchpad\money1\repro.mts (imports the worktree's openai.ts and prices.ts; worktree unchanged), with a usage of prompt_tokens 16,500 and cache_write_tokens 16,500:
  - `gpt-6-astra usage parsed: {"inputTokens":16500,"outputTokens":0,"cacheReadTokens":0} row.cacheWritePerMTok: undefined`
  - `gpt-6-astra byway ledgers $0.165 OpenAI bills $0.20625 difference $0.041250`
  - `gpt-6.1-sol byway ledgers $0.033 OpenAI bills $0.04125 difference $0.008250`
- docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:
  - line 33: "Rates byway does not record (the cached input of `gpt-6-luna` and the gpt-image models, OpenRouter's cache rates) are named in the comments: a cached token is charged at the input rate, so the cost leans high."
  - line 70: "Rates byway does not record charge a cached token at the input rate, so those calls are recorded above their cost."
- Grep of docs/ (excluding evidence) for cache-write, 30m write, implicit breakpoint or prompt_cache finds only Anthropic study-cost notes, nothing for OpenAI.
- profiles.ts:42 and 53: otherFamily `ref('openai', 'gpt-6-astra')`; profiles.ts:58: OpenAI tiers gpt-6-astra and gpt-6.1-sol.

Checked: the parse path, the price rows, the settle path, the profile exposure, the SDK default, the page rates, the ADR wording, that ADR-0109 is not on main, and the backlog.

Not checked:
- The live API response shape on a real call (no keys used).
- The pricing page as it stood on exactly 2026-10-18; the SDK in the lockfile since 10-07 shows the mechanism predates that date.
- The advisor's 16,500-token figure and the shuffle claim.

**Fix:**

1. prices.ts: add `cacheWritePerMTok: 12.5` to gpt-6-astra, `2.5` to gpt-6.1-sol and `0.125` to gpt-6-luna. The comments should quote the page's "Short context 30m write" column and the guide's 1.25x wording. Chat Completions supports only the 30m ttl, so the 12h rate (2.5x, Responses API) does not apply. Optionally add cacheReadPerMTok 0.01 to luna as well.

2. openai.ts:
   - Widen ChatCompletionLike.usage.prompt_tokens_details to `{ cached_tokens?: number | null; cache_write_tokens?: number | null }`.
   - In parseChatCompletion, set `cacheWriteTokens: res.usage?.prompt_tokens_details?.cache_write_tokens ?? 0`. tokenCost already subtracts it from plain input, and the guide defines uncached = input - cached - write.
   - The OpenRouter path is unaffected while it reports usage.cost; without it, its rows have no write rate and fall back to input, as today.

3. Tests:
   - providers.test.ts: a chat usage that carries cache_write_tokens, asserting cacheWriteTokens.
   - pricing.test.ts: extend the row table so the three OpenAI rows assert cacheWritePerMTok.
   - A tokenCost case where the write tokens cost 1.25x input.

4. ADR-0109 (not yet on main, so it can be edited in place):
   - Decision 1: name OpenAI's 30m cache-write rate among the rates now recorded, or among the unrecorded ones.
   - Decision 1 and Consequences line 70: drop or qualify the claim that unrecorded rates always lean high.
   - The prices.ts header comment (lines 37-39) should match.

**Refuter 2: confirmed, blocking.**

**Reasoning:**

I tried to refute MONEY-1 and could not. The defect is real, the owner never chose it, and no record or backlog row names it.

1. OpenAI bills cache writes on these models by default.
   - developers.openai.com/api/docs/pricing, read 2026-10-21, lists a "Short context 30m write" column: gpt-6-astra $12.50, gpt-6.1-sol $2.50, gpt-6-luna $0.125. Input is $10, $2 and $0.10.
   - The gpt-6-astra model page lists "Cache writes: $12.5", supports prompt_caching, and marks v1/chat/completions "Supported".
   - The prompt-caching guide says caching is "enabled by default for supported OpenAI models". It also says writes on GPT-5.6 and later cost 1.25x the uncached input rate for the default 30-minute lifetime.
   - In implicit mode, the default, OpenAI places a breakpoint at the end of the latest eligible message. So each of byway's single-turn prompts over 1,024 tokens is written at 1.25x unless it hits the cache.
   - OpenRouter's GPT-5.6 migration page says the 1.25x applies "even with automatic caching, no opt-in required", on both Chat Completions and Responses. Chat Completions reports it in prompt_tokens_details.cache_write_tokens.
2. The rate was published before B1's reading. A yottalabs.ai post dated 2026-10-07 already gives gpt-6-astra's $12.50 cache-write rate. A community.openai.com thread from 2026-07-10 shows prompt_tokens_details.cache_write_tokens on GPT-5.6. On 2026-10-11 OpenAI_Support wrote there that write pricing "replaces the ordinary input charge".
3. byway ignores the field, and the row has no rate to charge.
   - packages/gateway/src/providers/openai.ts:32 types prompt_tokens_details as only { cached_tokens }.
   - Lines 91-92 build usage as { inputTokens: prompt_tokens, outputTokens, cacheReadTokens: cached }, with no cacheWriteTokens.
   - packages/gateway/src/prices.ts:64-70: the three OpenAI rows have no cacheWritePerMTok.
   - So tokenCost charges every written token at the plain input rate. That is 0.8x of what OpenAI bills for those tokens, and it leans low.
   - The installed SDK has the field: openai@7.28.0, resources/completions.d.ts:154, `cache_write_tokens?: number`.
4. The batch writes this into a record and a test.
   - ADR-0109 Context, line 25: "Every price row matched its page but two."
   - ADR-0109 Decision 1, line 33: "every other figure is unchanged", and unrecorded rates mean "a cached token is charged at the input rate, so the cost leans high". The list of unrecorded rates leaves out OpenAI's cache writes, the one rate that leans low.
   - ADR-0109's Negative consequence "Rates byway does not record ... are recorded above their cost" is false for this rate.
   - The batch's new pricing.test.ts row table (commit 6e4eb65) pins the OpenAI rows without cacheWritePerMTok as "every row's figures as read on 2026-10-18" (lines 202-204).
   - Under ADR-0103, ADR-0109's Context and Decision become final once on main's first parent, so the record is cheapest to correct now.
5. Neither the owner nor any record chose this.
   - No ADR mentions OpenAI cache writes: grep across docs/adr found only ADR-0051 and ADR-0059 on cross-arm cache hits.
   - The backlog has no row for it.
   - Neither conversation.md nor conversation-continued.md mentions prompt caching or cache writes.
   - The prices.ts comments name gpt-6-luna's and the gpt-image models' cached input, but not cache writes.
6. It is on the first live run's path. Balanced, the profile ADR-0109 says the first live run uses, has otherFamily: ref('openai', 'gpt-6-astra') at profiles.ts:42. The OpenAI profile's big and mid tiers are gpt-6-astra and gpt-6.1-sol. With an OpenAI key, the ledger, the spend book and the run budgets all count OpenAI input low, by 25% of the write tokens' cost. The advisor estimate uses the same rows, so it is low too. The finding's figure checks: 16,500 tokens x $12.50/M = $0.206 billed against $0.165 ledgered.

The parts I could not refute:
- The code defect is older than this batch; main has the same openai.ts and rows. But B1's stated purpose is an honest money path before the first live run.
- The batch adds a false record about money and a test that pins the incomplete row. The rubric names both wrong money and a false record as blocking.

Corrections to the finding:
- Its column header "Short context cache writes" differs from what I read today, "Short context 30m write" and "Short context 12h write". The page may have changed, or the reading summarized it differently. The rates are the same, so this does not matter.
- Its suggested fix needs one guard. The SDK calls cache_write_tokens "the unadjusted number of prompt tokens written to cache". The July thread showed cached 3945 + write 4580 > prompt 4583, which OpenAI called an accounting bug since fixed. tokenCost floors plain input at 0 but does not clamp writes. If the counts overlap, the fixed code would charge reads plus writes on more tokens than were sent. That leans high, which matches the ADR's stance, but clamping is safer.

**Evidence:**

- packages/gateway/src/providers/openai.ts
  - Line 32: `prompt_tokens_details?: { cached_tokens?: number | null } | null;`
  - Lines 91-92: `const cached = res.usage?.prompt_tokens_details?.cached_tokens ?? 0;` then `const usage: TokenBreakdown = { inputTokens: res.usage?.prompt_tokens ?? 0, outputTokens: res.usage?.completion_tokens ?? 0, cacheReadTokens: cached };`
- packages/gateway/src/prices.ts
  - Line 65: `{ provider: 'openai', model: 'gpt-6-astra', inputPerMTok: 10, outputPerMTok: 50, cacheReadPerMTok: 1, source: OPENAI }`
  - Line 67: gpt-6.1-sol, the same shape.
  - Line 70: gpt-6-luna, the same shape. None has cacheWritePerMTok.
  - tokenCost: `cacheWrite * (price.cacheWritePerMTok ?? price.inputPerMTok)`, with cacheWrite always 0 for OpenAI.
- packages/gateway/test/pricing.test.ts:202-204 (added in 6e4eb65) pins `tokens(10, 50, { cacheReadPerMTok: 1 })` and the like, with no write rate.
- docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md
  - Line 25: "Every price row matched its page but two."
  - Line 33: "every other figure is unchanged. Rates byway does not record (the cached input of `gpt-6-luna` and the gpt-image models, OpenRouter's cache rates) ... so the cost leans high."
- packages/gateway/src/profiles.ts:42: `otherFamily: ref('openai', 'gpt-6-astra')`, Balanced. Lines 58-59: the OpenAI profile's tiers are gpt-6-astra and gpt-6.1-sol.
- node_modules/.pnpm/openai@7.28.0_ws@8.22.0_zod@4.6.5/node_modules/openai/resources/completions.d.ts:152-154: "The unadjusted number of prompt tokens written to cache." `cache_write_tokens?: number;`
- Web sources, read 2026-10-21:
  - developers.openai.com/api/docs/pricing, Standard text table. Columns include "Short context 30m write" and "12h write". gpt-6-astra $10 | $50 | $1.00 | $12.50 | $25.00; gpt-6.1-sol $2 | $10 | $0.10 | $2.50 | $5.00; gpt-6-luna $0.10 | $0.50 | $0.01 | $0.125 | $0.25.
  - developers.openai.com/docs/models/gpt-6-astra: "Cache writes: $12.5"; prompt_caching supported; Chat Completions supported.
  - developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/prompt-caching: "Prompt caching is enabled by default for supported OpenAI models"; 1.25x for 30m writes on GPT-5.6 and later; "Cache-write pricing is not an additive fee"; implicit mode places a breakpoint at the end of the latest eligible message.
  - openrouter.ai/docs/cookbook/evaluate-and-optimize/model-migrations/gpt-5-6: 1.25x "even with automatic caching, no opt-in required"; cache usage in usage.prompt_tokens_details for Chat Completions.
  - yottalabs.ai/post/gpt-6-astra-pricing-api-cost-2026, dated 2026-10-07: "Cache writes cost $12.50 per million".
  - community.openai.com/t/.../1386256, 2026-07-10 to 2026-10-11: a Chat-style usage with prompt_tokens 4583, cached_tokens 3945, cache_write_tokens 4580, called an accounting bug and fixed.
- Repo searches:
  - grep for cache-write terms across docs/adr/*.md finds only ADR-0051 and ADR-0059, on cross-arm cache hits.
  - docs/backlog.md has no OpenAI caching row.
  - Neither owner conversation file mentions prompt caching or cache writes.

**Fix:**

1. In prices.ts, add `cacheWritePerMTok: 12.5` to gpt-6-astra, `2.5` to gpt-6.1-sol and `0.125` to gpt-6-luna. In the comments, say this is the 30-minute write rate, 1.25x input, which OpenAI applies by default from GPT-5.6. byway sets no prompt_cache_retention, so the 12h rate (2.5x) does not apply.
2. In openai.ts, add `cache_write_tokens?: number | null` to ChatCompletionLike's prompt_tokens_details. In parseChatCompletion, set `cacheWriteTokens` to `cache_write_tokens` clamped to `max(0, prompt_tokens - cached_tokens)`, so overlapping "unadjusted" counts cannot charge more tokens than were sent.
3. Tests:
   - In pricing.test.ts, extend the row table with the three write rates.
   - In providers.test.ts, add a case whose usage carries cache_write_tokens and check that it reaches usage.cacheWriteTokens and the ledgered cost.
4. Before the batch lands, correct ADR-0109 while it is not yet on main:
   - Context: the OpenAI rows lacked their cache-write rate.
   - Decision 1: the OpenAI cache-write rates are added.
   - Negative consequences: drop or qualify "recorded above their cost".
   - Keep the rows' "read 2026-10-18" provenance honest by naming when the write rates were read.
5. Note in ADR-0109 or the backlog that the advisor estimate still plans input at the plain rate. OpenAI estimates therefore remain about 25% low on input unless the estimate also plans for writes.

#### MONEY-2: A Gemini image's text and 'internal' answer tokens go uncharged when candidatesTokensDetails lists only IMAGE

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed; lens money; where `packages/gateway/src/providers/google.ts:102`.*

**What is wrong:**

textAndThinkingTokens takes the answer's billable tokens as the sum of the non-IMAGE entries in candidatesTokensDetails, and falls back to candidatesTokenCount only when the array is absent. Google's API reference defines candidatesTokensDetails only as 'List of modalities that were returned in the response'; nothing promises that it sums to candidatesTokenCount. A published measurement of Nano Banana responses (third party) says the details list only the IMAGE payload, and that candidatesTokenCount was 88 to 630 tokens above it in all 49 successful generations. With that shape, byway charges none of those tokens, and none of the answer's text if TEXT is not listed. An empty details array charges zero answer tokens. This leans low, against the policy B1 states elsewhere ('the cost leans high'). The tests never cover it: every fixture's details sum exactly to candidatesTokenCount (fakes.ts: 1290/IMAGE 1290; providers.test.ts and pricing.test.ts: 1130 = IMAGE 1120 + TEXT 10).

**Evidence:**

ai.google.dev/api/generate-content, UsageMetadata: candidatesTokensDetails 'Output only. List of modalities that were returned in the response'; totalTokenCount 'prompt + tool-use prompt + thoughts + response candidates'. docs.apiyi.com/en/api-capabilities/nano-banana-usage-metadata (third party, Nano Banana Pro sample): candidatesTokenCount 2478, thoughtsTokenCount 208, candidatesTokensDetails [{IMAGE, 2240}]; 'Covers only the image portion - not a complete breakdown'. I ran parseGoogleImage in my clone. Input {promptTokenCount:615, candidatesTokenCount:2478, thoughtsTokenCount:208, candidatesTokensDetails:[{IMAGE,2240}]} gave usage {inputTokens:615, outputTokens:208, units:1}, so 238 candidate tokens were not charged. Input {candidatesTokenCount:1300, thoughtsTokenCount:300, candidatesTokensDetails:[]} gave outputTokens 300, so 0 answer tokens were charged.

**When it goes wrong:**

gemini-nano-banana-2.1 answers an image request with a caption or other non-image answer tokens, and Google's details list only IMAGE (as the third-party measurement reports for this model family). byway ledgers only thoughtsTokenCount at $7.50/M. Google bills the extra candidate tokens too: at $7.50/M that is $0.0007 to $0.0047 an image, more if Google bills them at the $30 image rate. Over 12 C2 boards or a C1 run's 12 images, the ledger and budgets count low, the opposite of what ADR-0109 and the backlog row say about this model.

**Proposed fix:**

Compute the answer's text-rate tokens as max(sum of non-IMAGE details, candidatesTokenCount - sum of IMAGE details), and when candidatesTokenCount is absent fall back to the non-IMAGE sum. This keeps the image's own tokens out and charges unlisted tokens at the text rate, which leans high as B1 intends. Add a providers.test.ts case with IMAGE-only details and candidatesTokenCount above them, and one with an empty array. Name the reliance on the details shape in the backlog row 'Gemini image thinking tokens', so the first live run checks it against Google's usage records.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding reproduces in both the code and the evidence, and nothing I found refutes it.

1) The code at packages/gateway/src/providers/google.ts:98-103 (textAndThinkingTokens) does what the finding says. When candidatesTokensDetails is an array, the answer's text-rate tokens are only the sum of the non-IMAGE entries, and candidatesTokenCount is never consulted. It is read only when the array is absent. Any candidate token the details do not list is therefore charged nothing, and an empty array charges no answer tokens.

2) Google's API reference does not promise that the details list sums to candidatesTokenCount or always includes a TEXT entry. I re-read ai.google.dev/api/generate-content. candidatesTokensDetails is "Output only. List of modalities that were returned in the response." totalTokenCount is described as "(prompt + tool-use prompt + thoughts + response candidates)."

3) The only published measurement I could find says the details list only IMAGE, and that candidatesTokenCount was 88 to 630 tokens higher in 49 of 49 successful generations. That page is docs.apiyi.com/en/api-capabilities/nano-banana-usage-metadata, a third party that tested gemini-3-pro-image and gemini-3.1-flash-image, the same Gemini 3 image family ADR-0109 describes. It says the details cover "only the image portion" and that on success total = prompt + candidates + thoughts. So candidatesTokenCount does not contain the thoughts there, and the gap is real answer output. The page also shows responses that carry a TEXT part. It only implies that text is left out of the details; it does not say so.

4) Neither the builders nor the repo had evidence that TEXT is listed. No ADR evidence file contains candidatesTokensDetails. Every test fixture has details that sum exactly to candidatesTokenCount.

Caveats that limit, but do not refute, the finding:
- The empty-array case is constructed. I found no evidence that Google sends [] with a non-zero candidatesTokenCount.
- Which rate Google bills the gap at is unknown (text $7.50/M or image $30/M). Either way byway charges $0 for it, which breaks the "cost leans high" policy that ADR-0109 and the code comments state.
- The amount is small. It is about $0.0007 to $0.0047 an image at the text rate, up to about $0.019 at the image rate. The pre-call reservation plans 2,000 tokens an image, which covers it per call, so a single call cannot overshoot a budget check.
- The ledger, the spend book and cumulative budgets still count low. ADR-0109's Consequence "Balanced's images are charged for their text and thinking, so its budgets do not count low there" would be untrue for this response shape. That sentence is Claude's claim, not the owner's words.
- The backlog row "Gemini image thinking tokens" sends the first live run to check the 2,000 planning number and the thought images. It does not name the reliance on the details shape, so a coarse dashboard comparison might not attribute the gap.

It is a real money defect in the change B1 itself made, with a one-line fix and no other changes needed, before the first live run. It is not blocking given its size and the high per-call reservation. Should-fix is the right severity.

**Evidence:**

Code (worktree at 481b2e5), packages/gateway/src/providers/google.ts:98-103:
  const answer = Array.isArray(details) ? details.reduce((sum, d) => sum + (d?.modality === 'IMAGE' ? 0 : Number(d?.tokenCount ?? 0)), 0) : (u.candidatesTokenCount ?? 0);
Ledger cost, packages/gateway/src/gateway.ts:374: costUsd = tokenCost(row, out.usage) + perImageUsd * units. So outputTokens is the only path by which non-image answer tokens are charged.

Reproduction without mutation: tsx run of the real parseGoogleImage from the worktree, script at C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/money2/repro.ts:
  apiyiSample_IMAGEonly {cand 2478, thoughts 208, details [IMAGE 2240]} -> {"inputTokens":615,"outputTokens":208,"units":1}  (238 candidate tokens uncharged)
  oneImage_gap630 {cand 1750, thoughts 200, [IMAGE 1120]} -> outputTokens 200  (630 uncharged)
  emptyDetails {cand 1300, thoughts 300, []} -> outputTokens 300  (0 answer tokens)
  noDetails {cand 1300, thoughts 300} -> outputTokens 1600  (leans high, as documented)

Test coverage: grep for "modality" in packages/ and apps/ finds only google.ts, fakes.ts:106 (1290 = IMAGE 1290), pricing.test.ts:166 (same) and :239 and providers.test.ts:410-417 (1130 = IMAGE 1120 + TEXT 10). No fixture has unlisted candidate tokens. providers.test.ts and pricing.test.ts pass at head (73/73).

Google docs (fetched today): candidatesTokensDetails is "Output only. List of modalities that were returned in the response."; totalTokenCount is "(prompt + tool-use prompt + thoughts + response candidates)". Third party (docs.apiyi.com/en/api-capabilities/nano-banana-usage-metadata): sample candidatesTokenCount 2478, thoughtsTokenCount 208, candidatesTokensDetails [{IMAGE, 2240}]. The details cover "only the image portion". candidatesTokenCount exceeded the details sum in 49 of 49 successful samples, by 88 to 630 tokens. On success, total = prompt + candidates + thoughts. In 6 of 6 refusals, candidatesTokenCount equals thoughtsTokenCount. With details absent, the current code charges 2x thoughts on those refusals, which leans high and is harmless.

Not checked: a live Google call (no keys, not allowed), so this exact model's (gemini-nano-banana-2.1) details shape and the billing rate Google applies to the gap are unverified beyond the third-party measurement. Separate observations, outside this finding and not verified: units is fixed at 1 even if the details show 2240 IMAGE tokens (two images), and parts.find picks the first inlineData without excluding thought parts.

**Fix:**

In textAndThinkingTokens (packages/gateway/src/providers/google.ts), charge the candidate tokens the details do not list at the text rate:
  const d = u.candidatesTokensDetails;
  if (!Array.isArray(d)) return thoughts + (u.candidatesTokenCount ?? 0);
  const listed = sum of non-IMAGE tokenCount; const image = sum of IMAGE tokenCount;
  const answer = u.candidatesTokenCount === undefined ? listed : Math.max(listed, u.candidatesTokenCount - image);
  return thoughts + answer;
I checked this against every existing expectation in a scratch script (C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/money2/fix.ts). It gives 0 for fakes 1290/IMAGE 1290, 410 and 10 for the 1130 = 1120 + 10 fixtures, 1530 with no details, and NaN for the 'ten' case. It also gives 446 for the IMAGE-only sample and 1600 for an empty array.
Add providers.test.ts cases for IMAGE-only details with candidatesTokenCount above them, and for an empty array. Update the google.ts comment and ADR-0109's wording (in the new record, which is not yet on main) to say that unlisted answer tokens are charged at the text rate. Name the reliance on the candidatesTokensDetails shape in the backlog row "Gemini image thinking tokens", so the first live run checks it against Google's usage records.

**Refuter 2: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I could not refute the finding. The code does what the finding says. Nothing in the records makes it an owner-chosen behaviour or a known limit with a trigger.

1. The code. packages/gateway/src/providers/google.ts:102 computes the answer's text-rate tokens like this:
`Array.isArray(details) ? details.reduce(... d?.modality === 'IMAGE' ? 0 : Number(d?.tokenCount ?? 0) ...) : (u.candidatesTokenCount ?? 0)`
It falls back to candidatesTokenCount only when the array is absent. An empty array, or one that lists only IMAGE, charges 0 answer tokens at the text rate.

2. It is Claude's design, not the owner's. ADR-0109 Decision 2 describes the mechanism ("the answer's tokens of every modality but IMAGE (candidatesTokensDetails)"), but nothing records it as an owner choice. B1's policy wherever it is unsure is to lean high:
- google.ts:91-96: "Without that breakdown every answer token is counted at the text rate ... so the cost leans high."
- The same docstring on thought images: "if they are, this leans high too."
- prices.ts: cached tokens are charged at the input rate "so the cost leans high".
The details array is treated differently. It is assumed to be a complete breakdown and the code leans low, although Google's reference promises no such thing.

3. The records do not cover it as a limit. Backlog row "Gemini image thinking tokens" (docs/backlog.md:65) names two open questions, the 2,000-token planning number and the thought images. It does not name reliance on the details shape. ADR-0109 Consequences says "Balanced's images are charged for their text and thinking, so its budgets do not count low there." That sentence is only true if the details are complete.

4. The external evidence holds up:
- Google's reference defines candidatesTokensDetails only as "Output only. List of modalities that were returned in the response."
- The third-party page (docs.apiyi.com) gives the sample with candidatesTokenCount 2478 and candidatesTokensDetails [{IMAGE, 2240}]. It says the details cover "only the image portion" and that candidatesTokenCount exceeded the sum of the details "by 88–630 tokens" in all 49 successful samples.
- Those measurements are of gemini-3-pro-image and gemini-3.1-flash-image, not gemini-nano-banana-2.1. Nothing shows 2.1 differs.
- Google's image guide says nothing about usageMetadata.

5. One uncertainty is open. Whether Google bills the gap at the text rate, the image rate, or not at all (for example if the gap holds the uncharged "thought images") is not documented. Under B1's own stated policy, that uncertainty calls for leaning high, which the suggested fix does.

6. There is an internal inconsistency, independent of the third-party data. With no details array, all 1,300 candidate tokens are charged; with an empty array, none are. A response that drops a TEXT entry while text parts are present also charges the caption nothing.

7. Severity. Should-fix, not blocking: at $7.50 per 1M tokens the gap is about $0.0007 to $0.0047 an image, roughly 2-10% of the about $0.049 byway now charges for each Balanced image. The first live run is owner-supervised with a small budget, and backlog row 155 already compares byway's ledger with each provider's dashboard. But it is a real undercount that goes against the policy B1 states, the ADR's "do not count low" claim depends on it, no test covers it, and the fix is one line.

8. Since ADR-0109 is new in this batch and not yet on main's first-parent history, its Decision 2 and Consequences can still be edited directly; no Later note is needed.

**Evidence:**

Code at packages/gateway/src/providers/google.ts:99-103 (head 481b2e5): `const answer = Array.isArray(details) ? details.reduce((sum, d) => sum + (d?.modality === 'IMAGE' ? 0 : Number(d?.tokenCount ?? 0)), 0) : (u.candidatesTokenCount ?? 0);`

Reproduced read-only. I ran a Node 24 script, C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/money2/probe.mts, which imports the worktree's google.ts and calls parseGoogleImage with an image response:

| Case | Input | Result | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Third-party sample | {615, cand 2478, thoughts 208, [IMAGE 2240]} | {"inputTokens":615,"outputTokens":208,"units":1} | 238 candidate tokens uncharged |
| Empty details | {cand 1300, thoughts 300, []} | outputTokens 300 | 0 answer tokens |
| No details | {cand 1300, thoughts 300} | outputTokens 1600 | |
| Full breakdown | [IMAGE 1120, TEXT 10] | outputTokens 410 | |

Test fixtures: every one that has details sums exactly to candidatesTokenCount:
- fakes.ts:106: 1290 / IMAGE 1290
- pricing.test.ts:166: 1290 / IMAGE 1290
- pricing.test.ts:239: 1130 = IMAGE 1120 + TEXT 10
- providers.test.ts:410-413: 1130 = 1120 + 10
- The NaN case (providers.test.ts:417) has no candidatesTokenCount.
No case covers IMAGE-only details, an array summing below candidatesTokenCount, or an empty array.

Google sources (fetched 2026-10-21):
- ai.google.dev/api/generate-content UsageMetadata: candidatesTokensDetails "Output only. List of modalities that were returned in the response."; totalTokenCount "...(prompt + tool-use prompt + thoughts + response candidates)."; nothing says the details sum to candidatesTokenCount.
- ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/image-generation: no usageMetadata guidance; only "thought images" are "not charged" and "thinking tokens are billed by default for thinking models".

Third party (docs.apiyi.com/en/api-capabilities/nano-banana-usage-metadata):
- The sample usageMetadata as quoted in the finding.
- Says the details cover "only the image portion — not a complete breakdown".
- Says candidatesTokenCount exceeded the details "by 88–630 tokens" across 49 successful generations.
- Models measured: gemini-3-pro-image and gemini-3.1-flash-image.

Records:
- ADR-0109:34 (Decision 2 describes the details-based split).
- ADR-0109:57 ("so its budgets do not count low there").
- docs/backlog.md:65 (open items are only the 2,000 planning number and thought images, not the details shape).
- `git log --first-parent 8401820 -- docs/adr/0109-*` is empty, so ADR-0109 is not yet on main.

**Fix:**

1. In textAndThinkingTokens (google.ts:99-103), charge any candidate tokens the details leave unlisted at the text rate, which leans high as B1 intends:

```ts
const image = details.reduce(IMAGE tokenCount)
const other = details.reduce(non-IMAGE tokenCount)
const answer = !Array.isArray(details)
  ? (u.candidatesTokenCount ?? 0)
  : u.candidatesTokenCount === undefined
    ? other
    : Math.max(other, u.candidatesTokenCount - image)
return thoughts + answer
```

Math.max keeps NaN, so finiteUsage still flags a bad count.

2. Add providers.test.ts cases:
- [IMAGE 1120] with candidatesTokenCount 1200 and thoughts 400 gives outputTokens 480.
- An empty array with candidatesTokenCount 1300 and thoughts 300 gives 1600, the same as when the array is absent.

3. ADR-0109 is still editable in this batch:
- Amend Decision 2 to say unlisted answer tokens are charged at the text rate.
- Add to backlog row "Gemini image thinking tokens" that the split relies on candidatesTokensDetails, so the first live run should check it against Google's usage records.

The third-party page says a safety-blocked answer reports candidatesTokenCount equal to thoughtsTokenCount. If that is right, the fix (like today's no-details branch) counts that thinking twice. That leans high and needs no change.

#### MONEY-3: ADR-0109's stated budget effects are wrong: C3's Balanced default budget is $2.50 before and after B1, and VOICE removal changes every configuration, not just C3 and C4

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed-partly; lens money; where `docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:65`.*

**What is wrong:**

Consequences says 'On Balanced, C3's default run budget goes from $2 to $2.50 by this alone' and 'Estimates with a VOICE lose one mid-tier call per VOICE (C3 one, C4 two)'. I recomputed from the code with estimateRecipe and apps/web's suggestBudget (a quarter above the high estimate, rounded up to 50 cents). On main at 8401820, Balanced C3's high estimate is $1.64, giving a $2.50 budget. At head it is $1.62, still $2.50. Without an OpenAI key it is $1.08 to $1.06, giving $1.50 both times. $2 does not appear at either commit, nor at 6e4eb65, where only the image change had landed. No Balanced default budget changes between main and head. The only default budget that changes on Balanced or Google is Google C1, which goes from $3.50 to $4.00. VOICE removal lowers the high call count in every configuration: C1 61 to 60, C2 24 to 23, C3 24 to 23, C4 12 to 10, C5 83 to 81, C6 32 to 31, C6a 44 to 43, C7 18 to 17. The per-image figures are right: +$0.015 an image, and 12 boards at $0.049 = $0.59 against $0.41 before.

**Evidence:**

In my clone I ran estimateRecipe over PROTOCOL_CONFIGS with images, video and otherFamily true at 2026-10-19, at head and with main's packages/{advisor,gateway,catalog}/src. Head: 'balanced c3 high=1.62 budget=2.5 calls=23'. Main: 'balanced c3 high=1.64 budget=2.5 calls=24'. Head without the image change: 1.60, budget 2. Google c1: main 2.75, budget 3.5; head 2.88, budget 4. With otherFamily false: head c3 high=1.06, budget 1.5; main 1.08, budget 1.5. The image each: 0.0336 + 300 x 1.5e-6 + 2000 x 7.5e-6 = $0.04905.

**When it goes wrong:**

The owner reads ADR-0109 to know what to expect before the first live run. They are told Balanced C3's default budget rose from $2 to $2.50 because of Gemini thinking, but it was already $2.50 and did not change. They are not told that the Google profile's C1 default rose from $3.50 to $4.00. The record misstates what B1 changed in money the person sees.

**Proposed fix:**

Before ADR-0109 lands on main, replace the C3 sentence with what the code gives: no Balanced default budget changes, because the VOICE saving and the image charge roughly cancel; Google C1 goes from $3.50 to $4.00; images cost about $0.049 each. Replace '(C3 one, C4 two)' with the actual call reductions per configuration, or with 'every configuration's VOICE'.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I reproduced the finding in my own clone and it holds. I ran estimateRecipe for every PROTOCOL_CONFIG, every non-demo profile and four key combinations, using apps/web's suggestBudget formula, ceil(high × 1.25 × 2) / 2. I did this at main (8401820), 6e4eb65 (image charge only), afcc3fd (image charge plus VOICE) and head, plus head with only the Gemini image row reverted.

One nuance, which the finding itself measured: the ADR's "$2 to $2.50 by this alone" can be reproduced, but only against a state that never existed. That state is head with the image charge taken out: C3 high is 1.60, budget $2.00. Putting the $0.015 for C3's single planned image back gives 1.62, which crosses the 50-cent rounding step to $2.50.

No commit ever showed the person a $2 default for C3:
- main: 1.64, budget $2.50
- 6e4eb65: 1.65, budget $2.50
- afcc3fd and head: 1.62, budget $2.50

Without an OpenAI key, C3 is $1.50 at every one of these commits. "By this alone" measured from main, the natural before, changes C3 not at all. From main, the image charge alone moves C1 instead: Balanced $7.50 to $8.00 (the VOICE change later brings it back to $7.50) and Google $3.50 to $4.00.

Whichever baseline is used, the ADR leaves out Google C1 going from $3.50 to $4.00, which is a real change from main to head. Between main and head no Balanced default budget changes, in any key combination.

The Positive bullet "(C3 one, C4 two)" is incomplete. Every configuration has a VOICE that produces 'artifact' (c1 via voiceByHuman; c2, c3, c6, c6a and c7 one each; c4 and c5 two each), and every configuration loses high-end calls. This matches the finding's counts exactly.

The record misstates which budgets the owner will see change before the first live run. It is not the owner's words and the code is right, so should-fix rather than blocking.

A side observation, not part of this finding and not new in B1: the same bullet's "C2's twelve mood boards" fits the run but not the estimate. 1. The C3 sentence is true as written, but a reader can easily take it the wrong way. Line 65 says C3's budget "goes from $2 to $2.50 by this alone". At head with only the Gemini thinking charge taken out of the price table, Balanced C3's high estimate is $1.60, giving a $2.00 budget. At head it is $1.62, giving $2.50. Commit 56afdde, which added the sentence, came after the VOICE change (afcc3fd), and it says the builder measured the image change on its own. The after-value is right: the owner will see $2.50.

   What the record leaves out is the net effect. Main was already $2.50 ($1.64). The VOICE change (item 5) cancels the image charge, so no Balanced default budget changes between main and head, for any set of keys. The finding is right about that, and a reader of the Negative bullet would think the budget rose.

It is still worth the one-line fix before the push, because the record exists to tell the owner what B1 changed in money.
