#### START-1: New real-Temporal test asserts 'waiting' at once, racing two concurrent activities

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed; lens runstart; where `packages/runner/test/engine-state.test.ts:122`.*

**What is wrong:**

The test 'Temporal accepted it: the run goes on under its workflow, not marked failed' waits only for the open checkpoint, then reads the run's status once and expects 'waiting'. The workflow writes the checkpoint (openCheckpoint) and the 'waiting' status (control.ts status loop, setRunStatus) as two activities running at the same time, in no fixed order. So the assertion holds only when setRunStatus happens to finish within one 20 ms waitFor poll of the checkpoint appearing. On a stalled 2-CPU Windows CI machine it can fail, which is the kind of timing flake the owner removed elsewhere. The file's own helper (waitingThenLost, line 38) waits with statusIs(run.id, 'waiting') for this reason.

**Evidence:**

Line 121-122: `await openCheckpoint(run.id); expect(await h.store.getRun(run.id)).toMatchObject({ status: 'waiting' });`. interpreter.ts ask() puts the entry in control.pending before it schedules quick.openCheckpoint, and control.ts startLoops() schedules quick.setRunStatus as soon as desiredStatus() turns 'waiting'. The harness's waitFor polls every 20 ms (harness.ts:286). In my clone I added a 250 ms delay to setRunStatus only for 'waiting' (lifecycle.ts). Result: 'Temporal accepted it' failed with `- "status": "waiting" + "status": "running"`. The three OPS-1 tests that wait with statusIs still passed. (The older reconcile test at line 84 has the same pattern with more slack, and failed too.)

**When it goes wrong:**

On CI, Temporal's store stalls (up to 12 s seen in run 39152075629) between handing out the openCheckpoint task and the setRunStatus task. The checkpoint row appears, the probe returns, getRun reads 'running', and the test goes red even though the code is correct.

**Proposed fix:**

Replace the one-shot read with `await statusIs(run.id, 'waiting')`, as waitingThenLost does. Or assert only what the test is about: the status is not 'failed' and error is undefined. Line 84 can be fixed the same way.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I tried to refute the finding and could not. I reproduced it on unmodified product code, using the test's exact steps.

Why the race exists (from the code):
- Interpreter.ask() (interpreter.ts:276-278) puts the checkpoint in control.pending, then schedules quick.openCheckpoint.
- The status loop (control.ts:87-92) sees desiredStatus() turn 'waiting' and schedules quick.setRunStatus in the same activation.
- Nothing orders the two activities. The checkpoint row is written by openCheckpoint. 'waiting' is written by setRunStatus (lifecycle.ts:34-38).
- The new test (engine-state.test.ts:121-122) waits only for the row, using h.waitFor with a 20 ms poll, and then reads the status once.

How I reproduced it:
- I made my own clone of fix/fx-batch at 481b2e5 (check-clones/start1-refute-c) and changed no product code.
- I added one test file. It repeats the test's exact steps: startWith(replyLost(true)), openCheckpoint via h.waitFor, then a single h.store.getRun.
- On the idle 16-thread machine, 800 iterations gave 800 'waiting'. That is very rare, not impossible.
- I then pinned the vitest node processes and the Temporal dev server to 2 CPUs (affinity 3), like the 2-CPU Windows CI runner. 1000 iterations gave {"waiting":997,"running":3}.
- In each 'running' case, 'waiting' appeared 25-27 ms later (one more poll). So the code is correct and only the one-shot assertion fails.
- Each such iteration would have failed the test with status "running".

What the finding gets right:
- The test is racy and will occasionally go red on correct code.
- The file's own helper (waitingThenLost, line 38) uses statusIs(run.id, 'waiting') after openCheckpoint, and so does packages/runner/test/e2e/controls.ts:38 and :231. That is the established convention, and this new test breaks it.

What the finding overstates:
- The framing of a 12 s Temporal store stall between the two dispatches. In the observed failures the gap was one poll (25-27 ms), not seconds. The real rate is about 0.3% per run under 2 CPUs.
- So it is an occasional red CI run, not a frequent one. That still fits should-fix, not blocking: a newly added test that can fail on correct code, with a one-line fix that keeps its guard.

Lines 84-87 (the reconcile test) have the same one-shot pattern. But they are unchanged from main at 8401820 and have much more slack, so they are outside this batch.

**Evidence:**

The code:
- engine-state.test.ts:120-123:
  `const run = await startWith(replyLost(true)); await openCheckpoint(run.id); expect(await h.store.getRun(run.id)).toMatchObject({ status: 'waiting' });`
- interpreter.ts:276-278:
  `this.control.pending.set(id, entry); ... await this.act(call, () => quick.openCheckpoint(...))`
- control.ts:89-91:
  `await condition(() => this.finished || this.desiredStatus() !== written); ... await quick.setRunStatus({ runId: this.runId, status });`
- harness.ts:280-286: waitFor sleeps 20 ms between probes.
- The worker and the test share one in-process SqliteStore (harness.ts, book 'memory'), so both writes are visible at once.

Reproduction, in a fresh clone C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/check-clones/start1-refute-c (481b2e5, product code untouched). The only addition is packages/runner/test/zz-start1-repro.test.ts, which repeats the test's steps N times.
- Idle machine, N=800: `N=800 COUNTS {"waiting":800} elapsed=227946ms` (file check-clones/start1c-repro1.txt).
- Pinned to 2 CPUs (Start-Process plus ProcessorAffinity=3; node.exe and temporal-sdk-typescript-1.24.0.exe both confirmed at affinity=3), N=1000 (file check-clones/start1c-aff.txt):
  `iter 20 ... one-shot status=running; 'waiting' seen 25 ms later`
  `iter 279 ... status=running; 'waiting' seen 27 ms later`
  `iter 284 ... status=running; 'waiting' seen 27 ms later`
  `N=1000 COUNTS {"waiting":997,"running":3}`
- Dev server: Temporal CLI 1.9.1 (Server 1.32.0), in-memory persistence.

The fix keeps the guard: setRunStatus returns early for an ended run (`if (!run || ENDED.has(run.status) ...) return;`). So a run wrongly marked failed by the start path never reaches 'waiting', and statusIs would time out and fail.

What I checked:
- The new test's code, ask(), the status loop, both activities and the harness poll.
- That lines 84-87 are byte-identical on main 8401820.

What I did not check:
- The real CI runner's rate. The 2-CPU affinity run on this 16-thread machine is a stand-in.
- Whether eager activity dispatch was on in my runs. An earlier attempt's scratch data showed an activity_eager_execution metric above 0, but I did not re-measure it.

**Fix:**

In packages/runner/test/engine-state.test.ts, line 122, replace

  `expect(await h.store.getRun(run.id)).toMatchObject({ status: 'waiting' });`

with

  `await statusIs(run.id, 'waiting');`

and keep line 123's `error` toBeUndefined check. A run wrongly marked failed never turns 'waiting' (setRunStatus skips ended runs), so the test still catches the regression it guards. The same one-line change can be made at line 87 if wanted, but that line comes from main and is outside this batch.

**Refuter 2: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I tried to refute the finding three ways: by finding an ordering guarantee, by finding an owner decision that allows it, and by finding errors in its evidence. None of them refutes it. Only small details of its framing are off.

1. Nothing fixes the order of the two writes. In interpreter.ts, ask() (lines 272-283) adds the entry to control.pending and then schedules quick.openCheckpoint. In control.ts, the status loop (lines 86-95) wakes on desiredStatus() !== written and then schedules quick.setRunStatus. Both are ordinary activities from the same `quick` proxy (acts.ts:15), not local activities, so Temporal hands them out as two separate tasks that run at the same time. The openCheckpoint activity (human.ts:46-91) writes no run status. setRunStatus (lifecycle.ts:34-38) is the only thing that writes 'waiting'. The test's openCheckpoint probe (line 30) returns as soon as the checkpoint row exists, and waitFor polls every 20 ms (harness.ts:280-288). The one-shot getRun on line 122 therefore passes only if setRunStatus has already committed. The finder's injected 250 ms delay in setRunStatus('waiting') makes exactly this test fail, as expected. Usually setRunStatus does less work and finishes first. That makes the race rare, not impossible.

2. No owner decision or recorded limit covers this. The owner's "Not now" (ADR-0107 Decision item 4, line 23: "Keep CI as it is; timing flakes get named and set aside.") says how flakes already seen on CI are handled. It does not allow a new test with a race to be added. The backlog rows that say a fix "needs the owner's go-ahead (ADR-0107)" (backlog.md:170-172) name specific existing tests, and this one is not among them. The Windows CI write-up (docs/reviews/2026-10-18-windows-ci.md:87) gives the fix pattern: a test checks the order of events instead of assuming it. The codebase's own convention is to wait on the status after a checkpoint opens. This file does that in waitingThenLost (line 38) and in the C7 test (line 150), and packages/runner/test/e2e/controls.ts does it at lines 38 and 231. The new test at line 122 is the only place that breaks the convention with almost no slack.

3. The parts of the finding that are slightly off do not change the verdict:
   - "The owner removed" timing flakes elsewhere: builders made those tests time-free (fx-ci-timing, write-up line 87). The owner chose to name and set flakes aside.
   - "Line 84" for the older reconcile assertion: in this head it is line 87; line 84 is main's numbering. That assertion is already on main, not new in this batch. It also has far more slack before the read (reconcileLostRuns plus two expectClosed calls), so it is a weaker case. Fixing it the same way is optional cleanup.

Severity: should-fix, not blocking. ADR-0107 item 5 says a checker blocks only on a regression, a failure on realistic input, or a false statement. Here product behaviour and money are unaffected, and CI only reports (ADR-0103). The defect is real, though. The test is new in this batch, a false red on Windows CI would notify the owner (who asked not to be drawn into administration), and the fix is one line that keeps the guard. With statusIs(run.id, 'waiting'), a regression that wrongly marks the run 'failed' still fails the test. setRunStatus never overwrites an ended status (lifecycle.ts:36), so statusIs would time out after 30 s, inside the 60 s test limit, and line 123 still checks error. On process: under ADR-0107 items 2 and 5, whether this fix goes into this batch (and gets checked again) or rides with the next checked batch is a fix-round decision to report to the owner, not to make alone.

**Evidence:**

- packages/runner/test/engine-state.test.ts:121-122: `await openCheckpoint(run.id); expect(await h.store.getRun(run.id)).toMatchObject({ status: 'waiting' });`. In the same file, waitingThenLost (lines 35-41) and the C7 test (line 150) both call `await statusIs(run.id, 'waiting')` after the checkpoint opens.
- packages/runner/src/workflow/interpreter.ts:276-278: `this.control.pending.set(id, entry);` comes before `await this.act(call, () => quick.openCheckpoint(...))`.
- packages/runner/src/workflow/control.ts:89-92: `await condition(() => this.finished || this.desiredStatus() !== written); ... await quick.setRunStatus({ runId: this.runId, status });`.
- packages/runner/src/workflow/acts.ts:15: `quick = proxyActivities<RunnerActivities>({ startToCloseTimeout: '1 minute', ... })`. These are regular activities, run concurrently, with no ordering between them.
- packages/runner/src/activities/human.ts:46-91: openCheckpoint calls stepStarted, pageQuestion, store.openCheckpoint and emit, and writes no run status.
- packages/runner/src/activities/lifecycle.ts:34-38: setRunStatus is the only writer of 'waiting', and returns early if the run has ENDED.
- packages/runner/test/helpers/harness.ts:280-288: waitFor polls every 20 ms (`await sleep(20)`).
- packages/runner/test/e2e/controls.ts:36-38 and 229-231: after openCheckpoint, both wait with `await statusIs(run.id, 'waiting')`.
- `git diff 8401820..481b2e5 -- packages/runner/test/engine-state.test.ts` shows lines 94-135 (the B1 describe block) are new in this batch. Line 87 (`expect((await h.store.getRun(live.id))?.status).toBe('waiting')`) is already on main (line 84 there).
- docs/adr/0107-code-is-checked-before-it-is-pushed.md:23 records the owner's "Not now" ("Keep CI as it is; timing flakes get named and set aside."). It covers how flakes are handled on CI, not adding new tests with races. Line 33 (item 5) says checkers block only on a regression, a failure on realistic input, or a false statement.
- docs/reviews/2026-10-18-windows-ci.md:75 and 87: Temporal gRPC calls took up to 12 s in run 39152075629; the fix pattern is that a test "checks the order of events" instead of assuming it.

**Fix:**

In packages/runner/test/engine-state.test.ts, replace line 122 with `await statusIs(run.id, 'waiting');` and keep line 123's `error` check. A regression that wrongly fails the run is still caught: setRunStatus never overwrites an ended status, so the wait times out after 30 s, inside the 60 s test limit. Optionally, change line 87 (already on main, with more slack) to `await statusIs(live.id, 'waiting')`. Whether this goes into this batch or rides with the next checked batch is the owner's fix-round call (ADR-0107 items 2 and 5).

#### START-2: ADR-0109 and ADR-0099's Later note say the second double fault is 'closed'; a variant remains

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed-partly; lens runstart; where `docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:77`.*

**What is wrong:**

Describe-first closes the double fault only when the other delivery's start reaches Temporal before the writer's describe. The SDK retries UNAVAILABLE about 10 times over roughly 20 s per call. So with an engine that stops and comes back, delivery B's retried start can land after delivery A's describe got NotFound. A then marks the run failed with ENGINE_DOWN_AT_START ('so this run did not start. Nothing was spent.'). B returns the run as started, and B's workflow runs under the failed run: the same state ADR-0099 described. The 'A late start' residual (line 67) speaks only of 'a start Temporal was still handling', which B's start was not at describe time. The Positive consequence 'A run whose error says "Nothing was spent" did not start' (line 58) is also untrue here and in the late-start case. Nothing is spent, because the guarded gateway refuses the calls.

**Evidence:**

A probe in my clone (stand-in client, real store): A's start throws 14, and B's start is held until A's describe has been called. Output: `PROBE A: 14 UNAVAILABLE ... | B returned run: 3c2b1a09-... queued | workflow accepted: true | store: failed byway's run engine was not running, so this run did not start. Nothing was spent. Restart byway and start it again.` The SDK retry is in @temporalio/client lib/grpc-retry.js: retryableCodes includes UNAVAILABLE, maxAttempts 10, delays capped at 5 s. ADR-0099 line 90 (Later note): 'The second double fault ... is closed'.

**When it goes wrong:**

A Start is delivered twice (no answer came back, pressed again) while byway is restarting. A's start exhausts its retries, then A's describe reaches the restarted engine and gets NotFound. B's start, still in its retry backoff, lands a moment later. The run's History says it did not start because the engine was not running, while its workflow was accepted, and B's HTTP answer told the screen the run started.

**Proposed fix:**

Word it as narrowed, not closed, in ADR-0109's 'What this changes' and in the ADR-0099 Later note: closed when the other delivery's start reached Temporal before the describe. Extend 'A late start' to cover another delivery's start that lands after the describe. Soften line 58. The batch lands as one merge, so this can still be edited in place.

**Refuter 1: partly, note.**

**Reasoning:**

The wording part of the finding is right. The failure scenario it gives cannot happen in byway as it runs.

What is right:
- The fix in packages/runner/src/client.ts (around lines 107-113 and 162-179) checks first and acts after. It describes the workflow, gets NotFound, then calls store.updateRun(failed). Nothing stops a start from another delivery that lands after the describe. That leaves the same end state ADR-0099 described: a workflow running under a failed run.
- So "closes ADR-0099's double fault" (ADR-0109's Alternatives) and the ADR-0099 Later note's "is closed" are absolute claims. They only hold when the other start reached Temporal before the describe.
- 'A late start' (ADR-0109 line 67) speaks of "a start Temporal was still handling". It does not cover another delivery's start that had not reached Temporal yet.
- Line 58 ("A run whose error says 'Nothing was spent' did not start") already conflicts with the ADR's own late-start residual. There, ENGINE_SLOW_AT_START says "did not start. Nothing was spent." and a start lands afterwards. beginRun (lifecycle.ts:29-30) then emits a 'run-started' event on that run, though it leaves a failed status unchanged.

What does not reproduce is the stated mechanism: an engine that stops and comes back while byway is restarting, with B's start landing after A's describe through the SDK's retries.
1. To fail on the transport path, A's start must run through the SDK's 10 attempts. The delays are 100 ms × 1.7^n, capped at 5 s, with jitter between 0.8 and 1.2. They sum to about 19.7 s (15.8 s to 23.7 s), all with the engine unreachable.
2. OPS-2's watch stops byway long before the engine can come back:
   - It makes a TCP check every 5 s (3 s timeout) and stops byway after 3 failures in a row (engine-watch.ts; serve.ts 123-133, 258-264).
   - Any outage that long covers 3 checks on Linux. On Windows it fires within about 24 s.
   - stop() runs stopWeb first (serve.ts 96-105, 236-238). The web process's stop closes its gateways and then its store (shutdown.ts closeServices; services/create.ts:95-96), then exits.
   - So A's later store.updateRun cannot write the failed mark.
3. Nothing brings the engine back under a running web process:
   - The Temporal server is created by serve.ts through TestWorkflowEnvironment.createLocal (dev-engine.ts:129). Nothing restarts it.
   - serve.ts is the only engine entry point; the CLI does not use the Runner.
   - "byway is restarting" means a new serve.ts and a new web process. The handlers that held A and B are gone by then.
4. A and B run in one web process and share one cached gRPC channel (engine-runner.ts `connected`). B can be waiting out a retry while A's describe gets through only if that channel has just recovered from a transport outage. That is the case points 2-3 rule out.
5. A second delivery of one Start reaches the server only after the browser's first fetch failed with no answer (startIdentity.ts; api.ts sets no timeout). Against a live local web process that is itself rare.

Paths left in principle:
- A's start fails while the engine still answers, with an error the SDK does not retry (a server-side DEADLINE_EXCEEDED, or INTERNAL other than RST_STREAM 0/2). B's start must then be sent within moments after A's describe.
- A gRPC-level Unavailable lasting 16 s or more while TCP is still accepted, so the watch stays quiet. I could not show either one happening.

Even then, nothing is spent: the run stays failed (beginRun and setRunStatus do not move an ended run), and calls are refused. That is the same benign state ADR-0099 accepted.

Net: the record overstates "closed" for a race whose remaining window is narrower than the double fault ADR-0099 named, and the finding's scenario does not happen. It is a wording note, not should-fix.

**Evidence:**

- Code: packages/runner/src/client.ts 175-178:
  `const workflow = await workflowAfterFailedStart(id); if (workflow === 'exists') return record; await store.updateRun(id, { status: 'failed', ... })`
  This is check-then-act, so a start that lands after the describe is not covered.
- SDK retries: node_modules/.pnpm/@temporalio+client@1.24.0*/.../lib/grpc-retry.js 34-56: maxAttempts 10, factor 1.7, initialIntervalMs 100, maxIntervalMs 5000, jitter 0.8-1.2. The nine delays sum to 19,722 ms nominal. Retryable codes are UNKNOWN, RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED, UNAVAILABLE, ABORTED, DATA_LOSS and OUT_OF_RANGE; INTERNAL only for RST_STREAM 0/2. connection.js:63 installs this interceptor by default.
- No call deadline: client-entry.ts:28-29 is `Connection.connect({ address })`, and there is no withDeadline in packages/runner/src or apps/web/src.
- OPS-2 watch:
  - apps/web/src/server/engine-watch.ts: interval 5_000, failures 3, onDown.
  - scripts/serve.ts 123-133: reachable() is a TCP connect with a 3 s timeout.
  - serve.ts 258-264: onDown leads to stop(1).
  - serve.ts 236-238: stop runs `await stopWeb(web)` before engine.stop.
  - apps/web/src/services/shutdown.ts closeServices leads to host.close(); services/create.ts:95-96 closes the gateways, then `store.close()`.
- Engine never restarted under a live web:
  - packages/runner/src/dev-engine.ts:129 `TestWorkflowEnvironment.createLocal`.
  - The engine refuses a port already in use (dev-engine.ts header, OPS-1).
  - startDevEngine is used only by scripts/serve.ts (and tests).
  - packages/cli/src has no Runner usage.
- One shared channel: apps/web/src/services/engine-runner.ts `connected ??= connect()`, used by every start in the process.
- Re-delivery only after a lost answer: apps/web/src/components/startIdentity.ts and e2e/start-identity.spec.ts header; apps/web/src/components/api.ts:33 has a plain fetch with no timeout or abort.
- Failed run stays failed: packages/runner/src/activities/lifecycle.ts:29 (`if (run && run.status === 'queued') ... 'running'`), with the 'run-started' event emitted regardless at line 30. setRunStatus returns early for ENDED.
- Record text:
  - ADR-0109 line 58: "A run whose error says "Nothing was spent" did not start."
  - Line 67, 'A late start': "A start Temporal was still handling that lands afterwards finds the run failed".
  - Alternatives: "closes ADR-0099's double fault with the same code".
  - ADR-0099 line 90 (Later note): "The second double fault ... is closed".
- Test run: `pnpm exec vitest run packages/runner/test/start-describe.test.ts` gave 6/6 passed. Its double-fault case (line 116) has B's start accepted before A's start fails and A describes. It does not exercise a start landing after the describe.

**Fix:**

These are wording edits in place; the batch lands as one merge, so the Accepted text can still change.
- ADR-0109, the Alternatives bullet and the ADR-0099 line under 'What this changes in earlier records': say the second double fault is closed when the other delivery's start reached Temporal before the describe, and narrowed otherwise.
- ADR-0099 Later note (line 90): use the same wording.
- ADR-0109 'A late start': widen it to "a start, this delivery's or another delivery's, that lands after the describe finds the run failed...".
- ADR-0109 line 58: qualify it, for example "A run whose error says 'Nothing was spent' did not start, unless a start lands after the describe (A late start); nothing is spent either way."
No code change is needed.

**Refuter 2: partly, note.**

**Reasoning:**

The code-level mechanism is real, and "closed" claims too much. The failure scenario as written cannot happen under byway's process model, and the outcome is one ADR-0109 already records for a late start. That leaves a wording overclaim in Claude's own delegated record (ADR-0109's "Decided by" says no item needed an owner decision). No owner decision protects the word "closed", and no recorded limit covers another delivery's late start. It is still a corner case where nothing is spent.

What holds:
- In packages/runner/src/client.ts, delivery B (created=false) reads the record while it is still 'queued' (startedBefore), passes the ENDED check, and calls workflow.start.
- Delivery A, the writer, gets 14. Its describe throws WorkflowNotFoundError, which means 'absent', and it writes failed with ENGINE_DOWN_AT_START.
- B's start then lands and B returns its stale 'queued' record.
- The SDK default retry is real. @temporalio/client 1.24.0 grpc-retry.js retries UNAVAILABLE with maxAttempts 10, factor 1.7, a 100 ms first interval, a 5 s cap and ±20% jitter: about 19.7 s of backoff per call.
- ADR-0109's "A late start" residual (line 67) speaks only of "a start Temporal was still handling". B's start, waiting in client backoff, was not one.
- Nothing is spent. lifecycle.ts and events.ts skip ended runs, and liveness.ts refuses calls with ENDED_CALL. This is the same harm ADR-0099 line 65 gave for the original fault ("that workflow cannot spend ... and ends").

What does not hold:
1. "While byway is restarting" / "an engine that stops and comes back" cannot happen with the web process still alive.
   - scripts/serve.ts stop() runs stopWeb(web) before engine.stop(), so a restart ends both A's and B's handlers.
   - An engine that stops under a running byway is caught by watchEngine. It needs 3 failed TCP pings, 5 s apart, by default (engine-watch.ts), so it fires within about 10 to 15 s and stops the web. That is before A's roughly 19.7 s of start retries run out.
   - A refused port also means the Temporal child is gone, and it does not come back on its own.
   - So the variant needs an engine that keeps its port open while it answers gRPC 14 for about 20 s, then recovers before B's retries end. One possible source is Temporal's SQLite persistence failing; the backlog row on the C5 flake saw it time out on CI's Windows machine. I could not confirm that Temporal maps those errors to 14.
2. Both this variant and ADR-0099's original need two same-id deliveries running at once.
   - The shipped screen's useAction guard sends no second Start while one is in flight.
   - startIdentity sends the id again only after A's fetch was rejected with no answer.
   - The api.ts send() helper sets no client timeout, so A's answer has to be lost to a network failure while A's handler keeps running.
3. Line 58's "A run whose error says 'Nothing was spent' did not start" is already qualified two lines below by the ADR's own late-start residual, which admits a late workflow and even a call that begins in the gap. Here "Nothing was spent" stays true, because B lands after the store write. Only "did not start" is loose.

The finding's headline is still right, and there is a second gap it does not name. In the literal ADR-0099 case (the other delivery's start went through first), the fault is closed only if Temporal answers the describe. If the describe fails, the result is 'unknown', and startFailureMessage still returns ENGINE_DOWN_AT_START for code 14 ("did not start. Nothing was spent."). So the finder's proposed wording would also overclaim unless it adds that condition.

Severity is a note, not should-fix: no money is involved, the case is a narrow one that byway's own process model mostly blocks, and the mechanism is recorded in a residual. The word is cheap to correct in this unmerged batch, and harder to correct once it is on main.

**Evidence:**

- C:/Users/arvik/code/byway/.worktrees/fx-batch/packages/runner/src/client.ts, the start() catch block: `const workflow = await workflowAfterFailedStart(id); if (workflow === 'exists') return record; await store.updateRun(id, { status: 'failed', error: startFailureMessage(e, workflow) });`. B's path is `record = await startedBefore(...)`, then `if (!created && ENDED.has(record.status)) return record;`, then start, then `return record`.
- start-failure.ts: `if (isTransportFailure(err)) return ENGINE_DOWN_AT_START;` runs before the workflow is looked at, so with 14 and 'unknown' the message is still "did not start. Nothing was spent."
- node_modules/.pnpm/@temporalio+client@1.24.0/.../lib/grpc-retry.js: `maxAttempts ?? 10`, `factor ?? 1.7`, an initial interval of 100 ms, `maxIntervalMs() { return 5_000; }`, and retryableCodes includes UNAVAILABLE. connection.js line 63 installs this interceptor by default, and client-entry.ts passes no interceptors.
- scripts/serve.ts: stop() calls `await stopWeb(web)` before `engine.stop()`, and the watch is `watchEngine({ ping: () => reachable(engine.address), ...})`, where reachable is a TCP connect. engine-watch.ts sets `interval = opts.intervalMs ?? 5_000; limit = opts.failures ?? 3`.
- apps/web/src/components/useAction.ts says "a second press meanwhile must do nothing". startIdentity.ts says the id "is sent again only while that Start got no answer". api.ts send() has no timeout.
- packages/runner/src/activities/lifecycle.ts lines 29, 36 and 139 skip ended runs; liveness.ts line 83 throws ENDED_CALL.
- Wording to change:
  - docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md line 48 ("closes ADR-0099's double fault with the same code"), line 77 ("is closed"), line 67 ("A start Temporal was still handling").
  - docs/adr/0099-paid-operations-keyed-to-their-caller.md line 90 ("is closed").
  - docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md line 15 ("ADR-0109 fixed the other").
- Not checked:
  - How Temporal 1.24's dev server maps SQLite persistence errors to gRPC codes. This decides whether "port open but 14 for about 20 s" can occur.
  - grpc-js reconnect backoff after a short outage.
  - The finder's own probe. I did not rerun it, because the ordering is plain from client.ts.

**Fix:**

Change the wording only, in this batch.
- ADR-0109 line 77 and the ADR-0099 Later note (line 90): say the second double fault is "narrowed, not closed". It is closed when the other delivery's start reached Temporal before the describe and Temporal answered the describe.
- Name what is left:
  - Another delivery's start that the client is still retrying can land after a describe that found nothing. The run then reads failed with the engine-down message, and its workflow does nothing.
  - When the describe goes unanswered, the run is still marked failed, with code 14's "Nothing was spent" message.
- ADR-0109 line 48: change "closes ADR-0099's double fault" to "narrows".
- Line 67 ("A late start"): extend it to "a start Temporal was still handling, or another delivery's start still being retried, that lands afterwards ...".
- Line 58: optionally add "except a late start (below)".
- Review-fix-status line 15: change "fixed the other" to "narrowed the other".
- No code change is needed: nothing is spent, and the case cannot arise from a byway restart.

#### START-3: Backlog trigger points at the describe-first row, which this batch removed

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed-partly; lens runstart; where `docs/backlog.md:76`.*

**What is wrong:**

The row 'Run start: reopen a run whose workflow never started (P3 double fault)' got the trigger 'Pick up with the describe-first row, or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down' from the records branch. The integration commit removed the describe-first row because B1 built it. The trigger now names a row that no longer exists, and its condition ('with the describe-first row') has already come and gone without this row being picked up or re-deferred.

**Evidence:**

`git show 481b2e5 -- docs/backlog.md` removes '| Run start: describe the workflow before marking a run failed (P3 double fault) | ...' and keeps line 76 unchanged, ending '...Pick up with the describe-first row, or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)'.

**When it goes wrong:**

Someone reading the backlog for 'when does this come back' finds a trigger that refers to a closed row and cannot fire. The reopen work is silently left behind.

**Proposed fix:**

Re-trigger the row. For example, keep only the second condition, or name B1/ADR-0109 as the point where it was considered and why it was not built (the 'absent' describe is not a reliable never-started marker because of the late-start window).

**Refuter 1: partly, note.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding is factually right that the row now points at a row that is gone. Its failure scenario goes too far.

Right: the records branch (af3bf5d) gave the reopen row the trigger "Pick up with the describe-first row, or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down". B1 built describe-first in parallel. The integration commit 481b2e5 then deleted the describe-first row and the timed-out-start row as closed, and left line 76 as it was. So the first condition names a row that is no longer in docs/backlog.md (grep for "describe" finds no describe-first row anywhere in it). That moment has also passed: B1 did pick up the describe-first work. ADR-0109 chose not to build the reopen fix ("The first ... is unchanged", line 77), and the backlog row was never re-deferred with a reason. The batch caused this. 481b2e5's own message says it fixed "three test comments that named closed rows" and dropped the records builder's "conditional edits ... with the rows they were for", but it missed this reference.

Overstated: "cannot fire" and "the reopen work is silently left behind" are not accurate. The trigger has a second condition that still works ("if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down"). The row therefore still says when it comes back, so the 489483e rule that every open row does so still holds. The decision is also not silent. ADR-0109 line 77 and ADR-0099's new Later note (line 90) both say the first double fault is unchanged. What the row loses is its only forward-looking pick-up point, and it records no reason for leaving the work at B1. That is a stale cross-reference costing one line of docs, not a lost item. I rate it a note, not should-fix. It is still worth fixing in this batch, because the batch introduced it.

The suggested fix's reason holds up. ADR-0109 line 67 ("A late start. When Temporal says it has no workflow, the run is marked failed. A start Temporal was still handling that lands afterwards finds the run failed ...") supports the point that a describe answering "absent" is not a reliable "never started" marker.

**Evidence:**

- `git show <rev>:docs/backlog.md` for each commit shows the history. On main (8401820) the reopen row (line 70) has no "Pick up" trigger and the describe-first row is line 71. On af3bf5d (records) the reopen row, line 76, gains "Pick up with the describe-first row, or if a Start is seen ...". On c6bdc8b both rows are present (76, 77). On 481b2e5 only line 76 remains.
- `git show 481b2e5 -- docs/backlog.md` removes "| Run start: describe the workflow before marking a run failed (P3 double fault) | ..." and the "a start that timed out is reported as 'engine was not running'" row. That second row's own text said "as the P3 describe-first row proposes".
- `grep -n -i describe docs/backlog.md` at head returns only lines 56, 76 and 97. No describe-first row exists.
- docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:77: "The first (a refused Start pressed again gets that failed run back) is unchanged."
- docs/adr/0099-paid-operations-keyed-to-their-caller.md:90 (Later note): "The first double fault is unchanged."
- Commit message of 481b2e5: "The records builder's conditional edits fall away with the rows they were for" and "three test comments that named closed rows".
- packages/runner/test/start-describe.test.ts:3 already calls the describe-first row closed.

**Fix:**

In docs/backlog.md line 76, replace "Pick up with the describe-first row, or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down" with, for example: "Considered with ADR-0109's describe-first start (2026-10-18) and not built: Temporal answering that it has no workflow is not a reliable 'never started' marker, because a late start can still land (ADR-0109, Negative). Pick up if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down". Keep the "(trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)" label.

**Refuter 2: partly, note.**

**Reasoning:**

The core fact is right. The records branch (489483e) gave docs/backlog.md:76 the trigger "Pick up with the describe-first row, or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)". The records branch and B1 were built in parallel from main. B1 then built the describe-first check (22f521b, ADR-0109 item 3), and the integration commit 481b2e5 deleted that row. Its message says "The records builder's conditional edits fall away with the rows they were for", but nobody updated this row's reference to the closed row. So the first half of the trigger names a row that no longer exists, and that condition has in effect already fired (describe-first was picked up) without this row being picked up or re-triggered. The owner cares about this: they asked "is everything filed in backlog with triggers?" and wanted to see whether items were "filed with trigger in backlog or not".

Two parts of the finding are wrong, though, and they bring the severity down:
(1) "cannot fire" is false. The trigger has two conditions joined by "or", and the second one, "if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down", is still live. It is also the only condition that ever fit this row. B1's describe does nothing for the engine-down case: when gRPC 14 or ECONNREFUSED fails the start, the describe cannot reach Temporal either. It returns 'unknown', startFailureMessage still returns ENGINE_DOWN_AT_START, and nothing new is written to the run record. So B1 provides no "never started" marker for this case.
(2) "silently left behind" is false. Four places record that the row is deliberately still open: ADR-0109 lines 77 and 92 ("The first (a refused Start pressed again gets that failed run back) is unchanged."), ADR-0099's Later note at line 90 ("The first double fault is unchanged."), the status page's F03 row ("the run-start double fault that reopens a run whose workflow never started (ADR-0109 fixed the other...)"), and the backlog row itself.

What is left is a dangling reference in a trigger that still has a working condition. That is a minor wording fix inside the batch, not a lost item. No owner decision or recorded limit covers the stale words, so the finding is not refuted. But the failure it describes, a row nobody will come back to, does not happen.

**Evidence:**

- `git diff 8401820..af3bf5d -- docs/backlog.md`: the records branch added the trigger to the reopen row while the row "| Run start: describe the workflow before marking a run failed (P3 double fault) | ..." was still there, unchanged.
- `git show 481b2e5 -- docs/backlog.md`: removes the describe-first row and the gRPC-4 row ("...as the P3 describe-first row proposes..."). Line 76 is kept unchanged. At head, `grep -n describe-first docs/backlog.md` finds only line 76.
- The trigger's second condition is still live. docs/backlog.md:76 ends "...or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)".
- The row is recorded as open:
  - docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:77: "The first (a refused Start pressed again gets that failed run back) is unchanged."
  - docs/adr/0099-paid-operations-keyed-to-their-caller.md:90: "The first double fault is unchanged."
  - docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:15, F03 Rows: "the run-start double fault that reopens a run whose workflow never started (ADR-0109 fixed the other: a failed start now asks Temporal first)".
- Code:
  - packages/runner/src/client.ts (481b2e5): workflowAfterFailedStart returns 'unknown' on any describe error other than WorkflowNotFoundError, and the run is updated only with `error: startFailureMessage(e, workflow)`. No marker field is added.
  - packages/runner/src/start-failure.ts: `if (isTransportFailure(err)) return ENGINE_DOWN_AT_START;` comes before any check of `workflow`. The engine-down case this row is about is unchanged by B1.
- ADR-0109's residual "A late start" (the window between the describe and the store write) confirms that 'absent' is not a fully reliable never-started marker either.
- Owner's words on triggers: docs/adr/evidence/conversation-continued.md:226 "is everything filed in backlog with triggers?"; :401 "...or at least filed them with trigger in backlog or not".
- No test enforces backlog triggers. scripts/test/ci.test.ts is the only scripts/test file that names backlog.md, and nothing checks triggers.

**Fix:**

In docs/backlog.md:76, replace "Pick up with the describe-first row, or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)" with words like these: "ADR-0109 added the describe-first check and left this unchanged: when the engine is down the describe cannot reach it either, so no 'never started' marker is written, and an 'absent' describe is not one (ADR-0109's late start). Pick up if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)". Optionally add ADR-0109 next to ADR-0099 in the row's references.

#### CLAIMS-1: The records describe the old answer generator's defect wrongly: the measurement's runs mostly did not draw from the 10,466-draw cycle

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed; lens evidence-tests; where `docs/adr/0097-a-step-takes-in-only-what-its-prompt-shows.md:106`.*

**What is wrong:**

The ADR-0097 Later note says the measurement's 248,340 runs "drew the person's answers from that cycle". The oracle.ts comment (scripts/test/claims-oracle/oracle.ts:65-67) and commit 24e69f5 say the generator "cycled after 10,466 draws, so runs late in a corpus repeated each other's answers". The ADR-0098 note (line 238 bullet) repeats the cycle claim. Every one of those oracles reseeds per variant (seed = 1000 + idx*7919), and a variant uses only a few hundred draws. So the place a variant sits in the corpus makes no difference, and almost no draws reached the 10,466-state cycle. The real defect is that the float product loses its low bits, and a few seeds reach short cycles quickly. The Math.imul fix itself is correct.

**Evidence:**

Reproduced with node using the old map `(s*1103515245+12345)&0x7fffffff` over the measurement's seeds 1000+idx*7919, idx<20,695. 20,398 seeds end in the 10,466-draw cycle, and 227/46/23/1 end in cycles of 220/247/378/10 draws. The median transient before the cycle is 3,929 draws (p10 421). I instrumented the ported oracle in a scratch clone: one variant's 12 runs use a median of 231 draws and at most 540 (first 3,000-try corpus, 118 variants). At 231 draws per variant only 3.0% of the measurement's draws are cycle states, and 20,443 of the 20,695 trajectories are still distinct after 50 draws. Only 128 seeds repeat within 540 draws. Every earlier oracle under Temp reseeds per variant: semantics-2-r6/r7/r8/r10, verify-semantics-2-r9 and verify-semantics-2-r11/claims12h all have `seed = 1000 + idx * 7919` (diff8 uses 7000+). verify-semantics-2-r9's round-9 final verification used the same float generator, but neither note names it.

**When it goes wrong:**

A reader of ADR-0097 concludes that all 248,340 measured runs shared one 10,466-draw answer stream, and discounts or re-runs the wrong things. In fact about 97% of draws came from per-variant streams, and only the few variants whose seeds hit a short cycle repeated answers. Once on main, the wrong account can only be corrected by another note.

**Proposed fix:**

Reword the ADR-0097 and ADR-0098 notes, the oracle.ts comment and the claims-oracle header. Say that the product lost its low bits, so the stream was not the intended LCG, and that a variant's runs repeated answers only where its seed reached a short cycle (about 0.6% of the measurement's variants within 540 draws). Drop 'drew the person's answers from that cycle' and 'late in a corpus'. Name round 9's verification among the oracles that had it.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I set out to refute the finding and could not. Every part of it reproduces, and the full-size run makes the case stronger.

1. The cycles are as the finding says. I ran the old map (s*1103515245+12345)&0x7fffffff over the seeds 1000+idx*7919 for idx < 20,695 (Brent's cycle detection). 20,398 seeds end in the 10,466-draw cycle, and 227, 46, 23 and 1 seeds end in cycles of 220, 247, 378 and 10 draws. The transient before the cycle has a median of 3,929 draws and a p10 of 421. Seed 1 enters the 10,466 cycle after 689 draws, which is likely where the "10,466" came from.

2. Every oracle reseeds per variant.
- The ported oracle sets `seed = 1000 + idx * 7919` before each variant's runs (scripts/test/claims-oracle/oracle.ts:325).
- So did the measurement's own oracle (Temp scratch/ports-rendered/claims-pr.oracle.test.mts:289; run-claims.sh runs it over gp-admitted.json with OFFSET/LIMIT, so idx 0..20,694).
- Every earlier claims oracle does the same with the float product: semantics-2-r6/r7/r8/r10, and verify-semantics-2-r6/r7/r8/r9/r10 and r11 (claims12 and claims12h). Round 8's differential oracle (semantics-2-r8/diff8) uses 7000+ and 9000+.

3. I instrumented the ported oracle in my own clone to count draws per variant, switched it to the old float generator, and ran one judging pass at the measurement's size (26 seeds × 40,000 tries, 20,695 variants, 12 runs each, about 35 minutes):
- A variant draws a median of 239 times (p10 97, p99 549, max 1,000), 4,951,521 draws in all.
- Only 3.91% of draws are cycle states.
- Only 1,253 variants reach any cycle.
- Only 23 variants (0.11%) have a stream that repeats a state within their own runs: 12 on the 220-draw cycle, 9 on 247, 1 on 378 and 1 on 10.
- Those 23 are spread evenly through the corpus (6/3/8/6 by quarter), and the median transient is about 3,900 draws in every quarter.

So "the measurement's 248,340 runs drew the person's answers from that cycle" (ADR-0097:106) is false for about 96% of draws. "Runs late in a corpus repeated each other's answers" (oracle.ts:64-66 and commit 24e69f5) is false: where a variant sits makes no difference.

4. Beyond the finding: that same full-size old-generator run reports 3 non-info E-REACT-CLAIM-BUT-NEVER-SHOWN problems, all in C2 variants (2502, 2677 and 14772). These are the kind of reports the commit blames on the repeated answers. None of the three streams reaches any cycle within its draws (transient 2,486 / 4,077 / 7,628 draws against 527 / 336 / 434 draws used).
- I re-judged variants 2502 and 2677 over 2,700 seeds each (12 runs).
- Variant 2502 fires at 14 seeds with the old generator and 6 with the fixed one.
- Variant 2677 fires at 1 seed with each.
- With the old generator, both fire at their measurement seed.

These reports are rare sampling outcomes that the fixed generator also produces. They are not caused by repeated answers. So the note's "found that its random answers repeated", used as the explanation, is unsupported too.

5. The finding slightly overstates one number. "128 seeds repeat within 540 draws" (I get 130 by that bound) counts every seed as if it drew 540 times. At the actual draw counts the figure is 23 variants (0.11%), not "about 0.6%".

6. The ADR-0098 note (line 238) names the oracles of rounds 6, 7, 8 and 10 and round 8's differential oracle. ADR-0097's note names only the round-11 verification. The verifiers' oracles of rounds 6 to 10 (including round 9's final verification, which ADR-0098 cites) used the same generator and are named nowhere.

Both notes are new in this batch (git diff 8401820..481b2e5; main has no "10,466"). Under ADR-0103, once they are on main they can only be corrected by another note. It is a wrong technical account in the decision log, not a false record of the owner's words or decision, and the Math.imul fix is right. So should-fix, not blocking.

Checked: the old map's cycle structure; per-variant reseeding in the ported oracle, the measurement's oracle and every Temp round and verifier oracle; draws per variant at full size with the old generator; the share of draws on a cycle, which variants repeat, and whether position in the corpus matters; the 3 non-info reports and their streams; the rates over 2,700 seeds, old against new generator. My cycle count runs from the seed itself, so it may be off by one draw; that does not change any figure that matters.

Not checked: the builder's own full-size old-generator run that found "two" C2 variants (I found three with the current port; the difference may come from changes to the port). The draw counts of the Temp oracles before the port (I measured the ported oracle with the old generator, not each original).

I modified nothing in the worktree. All my mutations are in my clone check-clones/claims1-refute-c. My scratch outputs are in scratchpad/claims1, a folder that already held other sessions' files; I deleted nothing there.

**Evidence:**

The claim: docs/adr/0097-a-step-takes-in-only-what-its-prompt-shows.md:106 says the generator "cycled after 10,466 draws, and the measurement's 248,340 runs drew the person's answers from that cycle". scripts/test/claims-oracle/oracle.ts:64-66 says "cycled after 10,466 draws, so runs late in a corpus repeated each other's answers". docs/adr/0098-recipe-limits-disclosure-and-held-voice.md:238 says rounds 6, 7, 8 and 10 and diff8 drew from "a generator that cycled after 10,466 draws".

Reseeding per variant: oracle.ts:325 `seed = 1000 + idx * 7919;`, and C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/byway-fixes/scratch/ports-rendered/claims-pr.oracle.test.mts:289 has the same line (float product at :40).

**Fix:**

Before the merge, reword three places:
- the ADR-0097 Later note bullet (line 106),
- the ADR-0098 note sentence (line 238),
- the oracle.ts comment (lines 64-66).

The new wording should say: the old generator's product `seed * 1103515245` lost its low bits past a double's 53, so it was not the intended LCG, and its states fall into short cycles (most seeds into one of 10,466 draws). Each oracle reseeded per variant (1000 + idx × 7919), and a variant used about 240 draws (at most 1,000). So about 4% of the measurement's draws came from a cycle, and only 23 of its 20,695 variants (0.11%) had a stream that repeated within their 12 runs, wherever they sat in the corpus.

Drop "drew the person's answers from that cycle" and "late in a corpus". Do not blame the old run's C2 E-REACT-CLAIM-BUT-NEVER-SHOWN reports on repeated answers: their streams reach no cycle, and the fixed generator produces the same report at some seeds (variant 2502: 6 of 2,700). Use the actual 23 / 0.11% rather than the finding's 128 / 0.6% bound.

In the list of oracles that had the generator, name the verifiers' claims oracles of rounds 6 to 11 (round 9's final verification included), not only round 11's.

**Refuter 2: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I could not refute the finding. Its account of the old generator holds, and the records' account does not.

1. Per-variant reseed. Every oracle that had the float LCG reseeds for each variant with `seed = 1000 + idx * 7919`:
   - the ported oracle, scripts/test/claims-oracle/oracle.ts:325;
   - the measurement's own claims-pr.oracle.test.mts:289, where idx = OFFSET-1 so it is the global index;
   - r6, r7, r8 and r10's claims oracles, verify-r8, verify-r9 (claims.oracle.test.mts:263), verify-r10 (claims11) and verify-r11 (claims12h:275);
   - diff8 uses 7000+idx*7919.

   So where a variant sits in the corpus does not decide whether its runs repeat. The oracle.ts:65-67 comment and commit 24e69f5 say "runs late in a corpus repeated each other's answers". That is wrong, and it contradicts line 325 of the same file. The variants that repeat are spread evenly over the corpus deciles: [2,3,1,3,0,7,1,1,3,2].

2. Most draws never reached "that cycle". ADR-0097:106 says the measurement's 248,340 runs "drew the person's answers from that cycle". I re-derived the cycle structure with node over seeds 1000+idx*7919, idx<20,695:
   - 20,398 seeds end in the 10,466-draw cycle;
   - 227, 46, 23 and 1 seeds end in cycles of 220, 247, 378 and 10 draws;
   - the median transient before the cycle is 3,929 draws (p10 421);
   - from seed 1, the transient is 689 draws.

   I instrumented a fresh scratch clone of the ported oracle to count draws and repeated states per variant. I switched it to the old float product (OLD_LCG) and ran it at the measurement's size: 26 seeds × 40,000 tries, 20,695 variants, 12 runs each, 2,082 s. Results:
   - draws per variant: median 239, p90 469, max 1,000;
   - 4,951,521 draws in total, of which 3.60% are states of the 10,466 cycle;
   - only 23 of 20,695 variants (0.11%) repeat any state within their runs, all on the short cycles (10:1, 220:12, 247:9, 378:1).

   No variant's runs can repeat each other from the 10,466 cycle itself, because no variant uses more than 1,000 draws.

3. The finding's own numbers hold where I checked them. On the first 3,000-try corpus (118 variants), the median is 231 draws and the max 540. 20,443 trajectories are distinct after 50 draws. I count 130 seeds that repeat within 540 draws; the finding's 128 differs by an off-by-one convention.

4. Something new: the symptom the builders blamed on the cycle is not caused by repeated draws. With the old generator at full size, the port reports E-REACT-CLAIM-BUT-NEVER-SHOWN on idx 2502, 2677 and 14772. Each of those variants has 0 repeated states and never reaches a cycle within its draws (mu 2486, 4077 and 7628 against 527, 336 and 434 draws). The 2026-10-09 measurement, with the same old generator, reported none of this kind (sum-cl-adm.log). Round 9's verifier had already called this check's hits sampling ("rerun with 64 runs and other seeds, all are shown", semantics-final-verifications report line 232). So the commit's story, that fresh answers cured two false C2 reports, is sampling luck, not the generator fix. The Math.imul fix itself is right.

5. The ADR-0098 bullet (line 238) only says the oracles drew "from a generator that cycled after 10,466 draws". That is loosely true of the generator's main cycle, but it omits the short cycles that actually caused the repeats. Round 9's verification (verify-semantics-2-r9/claims.oracle.test.mts, float LCG, the V2 run of 11,379 variants × 12 runs) is named by neither note nor by the commit.

Why it is not refuted: no owner decision or recorded limit covers this. It is Claude's own factual account in Later notes that this batch adds. It overstates how far the defect reached into the measurement's evidence. Once on main (ADR-0103) it could be corrected only by another note.

Why should-fix and not blocking: it is not the owner's words or decision, no test is weakened, and the conclusion the note reports holds either way (the exact-product port is clean at full size).

**Evidence:**

Record text:
- docs/adr/0097-a-step-takes-in-only-what-its-prompt-shows.md:106: "so it cycled after 10,466 draws, and the measurement's 248,340 runs drew the person's answers from that cycle, as did the round-11 verification's".
- scripts/test/claims-oracle/oracle.ts:65-67: "...cycled after 10,466 draws, so runs late in a corpus repeated each other's answers". The same file at :325 has `seed = 1000 + idx * 7919;` inside the per-variant loop.
- docs/adr/0098-recipe-limits-disclosure-and-held-voice.md:238: "a generator that cycled after 10,466 draws".

Original oracle under Temp: byway-fixes/scratch/ports-rendered/claims-pr.oracle.test.mts:38-41 is the float LCG, `let idx = OFFSET - 1`, and :289 reseeds per variant.

Cycle analysis (scratchpad/claims1/cyc.mjs and where.mjs):

- tail p10 421, median 3929, p90 7233;

Full-size probe with the old generator:

**Fix:**

Reword before the push. These lines are not on main yet, so they can be edited rather than corrected by a later note.

(1) ADR-0097:106: replace "so it cycled after 10,466 draws, and the measurement's 248,340 runs drew the person's answers from that cycle, as did the round-11 verification's" with this account:
- the product lost its low bits, so the stream was not the intended LCG;
- the oracle reseeds each variant (1000 + idx × 7919), and most seeds fall into one 10,466-draw cycle only after thousands of draws;
- a variant's 12 runs used at most 1,000 draws (median 239), so 3.6% of the measurement's draws were states of that cycle;
- 23 of the 20,695 variants, whose seeds reach cycles of 10 to 378 draws, repeated answers across their runs;

(3) oracle.ts:65-67: drop "late in a corpus". Say the product lost its low bits, so a few variants' seeds fell into short cycles and their runs repeated each other's answers.

Do not attribute the E-REACT reports to the cycle anywhere: the reported variants had no repeated draws. Use 23 variants, not the finding's 0.6%, which is only an upper bound at 540 draws. Commit 24e69f5's message cannot change without rewriting history, so the records must carry the correct account.

#### CLAIMS-2: The claims oracle's checks for refused person critiques can never fire on real variants, and disabling ADR-0098's ST12 refusal still passes it

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed-partly; lens evidence-tests; where `scripts/test/claims-oracle/oracle.ts:571`.*

**What is wrong:**

J-REFUSED-BUT-APPLIED, J2-REFUSED-BUT-REACHES-WORK-VIA-AI-DIRECTION and J3-PER-CANDIDATE-REFUSED-BUT-APPLIED only fire when a variant carries `unappliedSteps` or `perCandidateSteps`. variants.ts never sets either field, and neither did the original gen-pr.mts. So these checks run only under the FORCE_J control. A person critique answered but never applied on any run is reported as J-ANSWERED-NEVER-APPLIED(info), which the test ignores. Yet the ADR-0098 note says "round 6's check of whether an AI writer took a person's answer in, with its FORCE_J control ... is ported ... and runs with the unit tests".

**Evidence:**

`grep unappliedSteps|perCandidateSteps` finds them only as reads in oracle.ts (lines 566, 569, 571) and in the Temp original claims-pr.oracle.test.mts, never in variants.ts or gen-pr.mts. Mutation in a scratch clone: packages/validator/src/checks/declarations.ts `if (contained) continue;` changed to `if (contained || true) continue;` (no ST12 critique-unapplied or critique-per-candidate refusal). The default corpus then grows from 118 to 162 admitted variants and J-ANSWERED-NEVER-APPLIED(info) from 2 to 44, but `pnpm exec vitest run scripts/test/claims-oracle.test.ts` gives 6 passed (6). The rule is still guarded by validator and advisor tests: semantics-rules.test.ts, flow-memo.test.ts and prompt-shows-admission.test.ts name 'critique-unapplied'.

**When it goes wrong:**

Someone relies on the ported claims oracle to catch a regression where a person's list or critique is admitted but never reaches the work, which is ADR-0098's core promise. It stays green; only the validator's own unit tests would catch it.

**Proposed fix:**

Do one of two things. Either make the oracle blocking on 'answered on every run, applied on none, and the static semantics say no path reaches the work': compute that from the recipe as the J check's control does, or keep ST12-refused variants as a refused corpus with unappliedSteps as round 6 did. Or reword the ADR-0098 note and the test header: the refused-critique checks run only under FORCE_J, and what never reaches the work is guarded by the validator (and was measured by the unported execution oracle).

**Refuter 1: partly, note.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding's facts reproduce exactly. Its account of why the mutation passes, and one of its two fixes, are wrong. Its severity is too high.

What holds:
- J-REFUSED-BUT-APPLIED, J2-REFUSED-BUT-REACHES-WORK-VIA-AI-DIRECTION and J3-PER-CANDIDATE-REFUSED-BUT-APPLIED can only fire when a variant carries unappliedSteps or perCandidateSteps (oracle.ts:566, 569 and 571).
- No caller supplies either field: Variant in variants.ts:893-900 has only nearest, muts and recipe. The source the port names, ports-rendered/gen-pr.mts, does not set them either.
- Admitted answers that are never applied come out as J-ANSWERED-NEVER-APPLIED(info). The test's `blocking()` filter drops every (info) kind.
- With ST12's critique loop disabled, the default corpus grows from 118 to 162 variants and J-ANSWERED-NEVER-APPLIED(info) grows from 2 to 44, yet claims-oracle.test.ts still passes 6 of 6.

What is wrong or overstated:
1. **The dead checks guard the other direction.** On a refused corpus, J-REFUSED-BUT-APPLIED fires when a step the validator refused as unapplied is in fact applied. That catches a false refusal (over-refusal), not a missing refusal. With ST12 disabled nothing is refused, so unappliedSteps would be empty and the check could not fire. The finding's option 2 (bring back a refused corpus with unappliedSteps, as round 6 did) therefore would not catch the stated mutation. The mutation passes because "admitted but never applied" is an (info) kind, and it was (info) in every round:
   - round 6's claims.oracle.test.mts:486 has it as (info);
   - ADR-0098 line 133 reports that half as a measurement a person read, not as an assertion.
2. **The port is faithful, not a regression.** Rounds 6 to 8 did carry the fields on refused corpora (readmit6.mts:32, readmit7.mts:32, gen-variants7.mts:244-245). The last form the ADR-0098 note says was ported (ports-rendered claims-pr + gen-pr.mts) already had these branches dead. The oracle.ts header says the REFUSED switch "for other corpora" is gone.
3. **Inexact detail:** J2-REFUSED and J3 do not run even under FORCE_J. Only the J-REFUSED branch is shared with the control. In my probe, the FORCE_J run reports J2-REACHES-WORK-VIA-AI-DIRECTION(info): 1, not the refused kind.
4. **No document says the oracle guards ST12.**
   - The test header says it checks the explainers' and VOICE wording's claims, and ST12 is neither.
   - The backlog rows that cite the oracle concern explainer claims (canPrecede, directionsReactedTo, promptShows).
   - The ADR-0098 note's words "ported in its last form ... and runs with the unit tests" are literally true.
   - The rule itself is guarded where the finding says it is: under the same mutation, packages/validator/test/semantics-rules.test.ts fails 10 of 38.

So the true defect is narrower:
- Three problem kinds, and the claimed counters refusedPersonCritique and refusedAnswered, are unreachable in the port, and nothing says so.
- The ADR-0098 note's mention of "round 6's check of whether an AI writer took a person's answer in" can suggest a guard. In the port the check only passes or fails through its FORCE_J control (J-FORCED > 0); its findings on real variants are all (info).

That is a wording and clarity problem in a test tool and a Later note. It is not wrong behaviour, not a false record of the owner's words, and not the only guard on the rule. Severity: note.

**Evidence:**

Run in my own clone, C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/check-clones/claims2-refute-r2, at 481b2e5 (pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --offline). The probe is scripts/test/zz-probe.test.ts in that clone, on the same corpus as the real test: generateVariants(3000, seed 9100003+130363), 6 runs each.

**Baseline** (c2r2-base.json in check-clones):
- 118 admitted (2,519 unique tries, 2,401 refused).
- Problems: J-ANSWERED-NEVER-APPLIED(info) 2, J-SOMETIMES-NOT-APPLIED(info) 7, J2-REACHES-WORK-VIA-AI-DIRECTION(info) 1.
- Claimed: personCritique 89, personApplied 87.
- FORCE_J: J-FORCED(control) 87, plus J2-REACHES-WORK-VIA-AI-DIRECTION(info) 1. So J2 does not take the refused branch under FORCE_J.
- The two never-applied cases are both nearest c6a, step 'react', answered 6.

**Mutation:** in packages/validator/src/checks/declarations.ts, `if (contained) continue;` became `if (contained || true) continue;` (one line, at line 58).
- Probe (c2r2-mut.json): 162 admitted; J-ANSWERED-NEVER-APPLIED(info) 44; personCritique 158, personAnswered 157, personApplied 113; no refused kinds.
- `pnpm exec vitest run scripts/test/claims-oracle.test.ts` gave "Tests 6 passed (6)".
- `pnpm exec vitest run packages/validator/test/semantics-rules.test.ts` gave "Tests 10 failed | 28 passed (38)".

**Grep results:**
- unappliedSteps and perCandidateSteps appear only as reads, in oracle.ts:566, 569 and 571.
- In ports-rendered, the only file with them is claims-pr.oracle.test.mts (lines 530, 533 and 535); gen-pr.mts has none.
- Rounds 6 to 8 did set them: semantics-2-r6/readmit6.mts:32 (`refusedOut.push({ ...v, refusals: keys, unappliedSteps: ... })`), r7/readmit7.mts:32, and r8/gen-variants7.mts:244-245.
- In round 6, J-ANSWERED-NEVER-APPLIED is already (info) (semantics-2-r6/claims.oracle.test.mts:486), and its refused run was the separate control6-refused*.json.

**Direction of the check:** oracle.ts:571-574 adds J-REFUSED-BUT-APPLIED only when the step is in unappliedSteps and `applied > 0`. That is an over-refusal check.

**ADR-0098 Later note** (docs/adr/0098...md:238): "round 6's check of whether an AI writer took a person's answer in, with its FORCE_J control; ... is ported in its last form ... and runs with the unit tests".

**Fix:**

Wording only, in this batch; the ADR-0098 note is new here, so it can still be edited before it lands.
1. In the scripts/test/claims-oracle/oracle.ts header, next to "the switches ... (LOOSE_D, LOOSE_G, REFUSED) are gone", add a sentence saying:
   - J-REFUSED-BUT-APPLIED, J2-REFUSED-BUT-REACHES-WORK-VIA-AI-DIRECTION and J3-PER-CANDIDATE-REFUSED-BUT-APPLIED need a refused corpus whose variants carry unappliedSteps or perCandidateSteps;
   - the ported generator, like gen-pr.mts, admits only, so these checks do not run;
   - an admitted answer never applied is reported (info).
   Deleting those three branches and the refusedPersonCritique and refusedAnswered counters is an equally small alternative.
2. In the ADR-0098 Later note, add: "On the admitted corpus its result is information only. The refusal it measured (ST12 critique-unapplied and critique-per-candidate) is guarded by the validator and advisor tests."

Do not use the finding's option 2 (restore a refused corpus with unappliedSteps). It would only catch over-refusal, never a refusal that was disabled.

**Refuter 2: partly, note.**

**Reasoning:**

The facts in the finding are right. The defect it builds on them is not.

What holds:
1. J-REFUSED-BUT-APPLIED, J2-REFUSED-BUT-REACHES-WORK-VIA-AI-DIRECTION and J3-PER-CANDIDATE-REFUSED-BUT-APPLIED read v.unappliedSteps and v.perCandidateSteps (oracle.ts:566, 569, 571).
2. The Variant interface (variants.ts:893-900) has neither field, and generateVariants never sets them.
3. The Temp original gen-pr.mts never set them either. Under Temp/byway-fixes/scratch, only round 6's readmit6.mts, round 7's readmit7.mts and round 8's gen-variants7.mts and admit-list.mts set them.
4. One correction to the finding: J2-REFUSED and J3 do not fire even under FORCE_J. The forceJ option only gates lines 571-574.
5. I reproduced the mutation: with `if (contained || true) continue;` the corpus grows from 118 to 162 admitted variants, J-ANSWERED-NEVER-APPLIED(info) goes from 2 to 44, and claims-oracle.test.ts still gives 6 passed (6).

What is wrong:
(a) No record is false or overclaims.
- ADR-0098's Later note says round 6's check is ported "in its last form, after ADR-0097" and runs with the unit tests. That is literally true.
- Its last form is ports-rendered's claims-pr, ADR-0097's measurement. It also ran only on admitted variants. ADR-0097 line 51 records its J output as an info count ("an answer given and never applied 143 against 1,997") and FORCE_J as a control (1,754).
- The same ADR-0098 note says rounds 6 to 11's "re-admission and verdict scripts, differential oracles and per-round reports are not kept". So the refused corpus that readmit6 made is disclosed as gone.
- ADR-0097's note says the execution oracle (vpr.oracle.test.mts) is "not a test that runs today".
- The final-verifications report keeps two jobs apart: "Soundness / no false refusal" belongs to the execution oracle, and "No false claim" to the claims oracle. In rounds 9 to 11, ST12's reach was the execution oracle's job, not the claims oracle's.

(b) The oracle passing under the ST12 mutation fits its stated scope: "every claim the advisor's explainers make is checked".
- present.ts:318 says "and the AI removes it" only when an applier is shown with the step.
- My probe of the mutated corpus read the explainer of each of the 44 lost answers. None claims the answer is applied: 'Your turn: mark what the work could lose.' (20), 'Your turn: say what you want, what pulls you and what to avoid.' (21), 'A look for defects only. It makes no taste calls.' (2), 'Your turn.' (1).
- So no false claim gets admitted, and passing is the right result.

(c) ST12 is guarded elsewhere. The same mutation fails 12 tests in packages/validator/test/semantics-rules.test.ts and packages/advisor/test/prompt-shows-admission.test.ts.

(d) The finding's second fix ("keep ST12-refused variants as a refused corpus with unappliedSteps") would fail on correct code.
- At HEAD, without the mutation, I ran the oracle on the default seed's ST12-only refused variants: 44 variants, step ids resolved from the violation paths, none unresolved.
- It reports J3-PER-CANDIDATE-REFUSED-BUT-APPLIED 3 and J-REFUSED-BUT-APPLIED 1. The single J-REFUSED-BUT-APPLIED hit is a C5 cut whose fix sits only inside an unpicked replicate (rep-g7396). The round-8/9 verifications showed that case is lost: "Wc 64/64 and F 0/64".
- The J check counts an answer as applied when an AI writer outside a lens takes it in. That is coarser than ST12's "reaches the work", which is why the verifiers moved ST12 onto a lineage-following execution oracle.
- The finding's first fix (computing reach from the recipe) would be a new check, not a port fix.

What is left is a disclosure gap, so the severity is note:
- The ported oracle carries three problem kinds that cannot fire on any corpus the port makes.
- oracle.ts says "The checks are unchanged" and does not say this.
- A reader of ADR-0098's parenthetical about round 6's check could think the ST12 measurement still runs.
- No behaviour is wrong, no money is affected, and no owner decision is misquoted.
