#### EVID-1: The evidence secret scan does not match fal's real key shape

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed; lens evidence-tests; where `scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts:52`.*

**What is wrong:**

The test says the evidence files hold "no key-shaped string". Its pattern for fal is `fal-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,}`, which follows the test sentinel 'fal-SENTINEL', not a real FAL_KEY. The repository's own tests use the real form `<uuid>:<32 hex>`. A real fal key pasted into a log or transcript under docs/adr/evidence would pass. fal is one of byway's five paid providers (env.ts lists FAL_KEY).

**Evidence:**

packages/gateway/test/billed.test.ts:19 and fal-submit.test.ts:21 use FAL_KEY '[a key-shaped string]'. env.test.ts:99 uses 'fal-SENTINEL'. The regex at adr-evidence.test.ts:52 has no uuid:hex branch. A manual scan of docs/adr/evidence, packages/runner/test/f07-corpus and scripts/test for uuid:hex, sk-, AIza, gh*_ and xox* finds none, so nothing leaks today.

**When it goes wrong:**

A later evidence import, for example a server log or a conversation extract that echoes FAL_KEY, commits a live fal key while this check stays green.

**Proposed fix:**

Add `[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}:[0-9a-f]{32}` to the key pattern, and a self-test line with the fixture key to prove it matches.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding is right. I reproduced it in my own clone, and it does not rise to blocking.

1. The scan cannot see a real fal key. Line 52 of scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts has one fal alternative, `fal-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,}`. That is the shape of the repository's stand-in keys, such as 'fal-test-not-a-real-key-0000'. It is not the shape of a real FAL_KEY. The repository says what the real shape is in packages/gateway/src/redact.ts lines 8 and 15: "fal.ai `<key id>:<secret>`", matched as uuid:hex32 with the /gi flags. The gateway tests use that form too (billed.test.ts:19, fal-submit.test.ts:21, apps/web/test/http.test.ts:14). So the test's own name, "the evidence files hold no ... key-shaped string", promises more than the scan checks for one of the five providers in KEY_VARS.

2. Nothing else covers this. No other check scans docs/adr/evidence for keys: the only workflow, verify.yml, has no secret scanner, and no other script under scripts/test looks for this shape. The failure scenario can happen: a fal key pasted into an evidence log or transcript would be committed while the gate stays green.

3. Why it is not blocking. Nothing leaks today. Scanning all 161 evidence files with the uuid:hex32 pattern finds 0 matches, and not even a uuid followed by ':'. Server logs written by byway already pass through makeRedactor, which removes this shape. And the scan still catches the Anthropic, OpenAI and OpenRouter shapes (all via sk-) and the Google one (AIza). It is a gap in a guard for the future, not a secret that has been committed or money handled wrongly.

4. One small error in the finding. It says the pattern "follows the test sentinel 'fal-SENTINEL'". 'fal-SENTINEL' does not match the pattern at all, because only 8 characters follow 'fal-' and the pattern needs 16. The substance is unaffected: the pattern follows the 'fal-' prefix that the stand-in keys use, and no real fal key has that prefix.

**Evidence:**

The pattern at line 52 of C:/Users/arvik/code/byway/.worktrees/fx-batch/scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts:
`const key = /\b(?:sk-(?:ant-)?[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,}|AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{20,}|gh[pousr]_[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}|xox[abp]-[A-Za-z0-9-]{10,}|fal-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,})/g;`
The test file was added in this batch (eedc340, fix/fx-evidence) and changed again in 481b2e5.

Checked with node, script at C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/evid1-regex.mjs:
- The fixture key '0d1e2f3a-...-8192a3b4c5d6:0011...eeff' gives null. So do `FAL_KEY=<fixture>` and `Authorization: Key <fixture>`, and 'fal-SENTINEL'. Only 'fal-test-not-a-real-key-0000' matches.
- The suggested pattern matches the fixture and finds 0 hits in all 161 files under docs/adr/evidence, so it would add no false positives.

Mutation in my own clone (C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/check-clones/evid1-refute, at 481b2e5):
1. I wrote docs/adr/evidence/logs/zz-mutation-fal-key.log with two lines holding the uuid:hex32 fixture key. Running `pnpm exec vitest run scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts` gave "Tests 3 passed (3)". The scan stays green.
2. Control: I added the line '[a key-shaped string]' to the same file. The test then failed and reported only that line, which proves the file is scanned and the two fal lines are missed.
3. With the suggested alternative added (`/gi` flags), the test failed on both fal lines. After I removed the planted file it passed 3 of 3 on the real evidence.
I then restored the clone to clean.

What I checked about other guards: the only workflow is .github/workflows/verify.yml, and it has no secret scanner. KEY_SHAPES in packages/gateway/src/redact.ts lines 10 to 17 is module-private and protects the gateway's own diagnostic copies, not files in the repository.

**Fix:**

In scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts line 52, add fal's real shape, the one redact.ts already uses, and make that part case-insensitive. For example, append `|[0-9a-fA-F]{8}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{12}:[0-9a-fA-F]{32,}` inside the group. The 'fal-' alternative can stay, since it catches the stand-in keys. Also add a self-check that the pattern matches the fixture key '[a key-shaped string]' (and 'sk-ant-...'), so the scan cannot quietly drift again. The fix matches nothing in today's evidence.

**Refuter 2: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I could not refute it. No record limits this scan's scope, and no owner decision or backlog trigger accepts a fal-shaped gap. The repository's own code says a real FAL_KEY is `<key id>:<secret>`, and the scan's only fal pattern (`fal-…`) does not match that.

- **The test's own claim.** The header comment (adr-evidence.test.ts:9) and the test name (line 50) both say the evidence holds "no key-shaped string".
- **The code already knows fal's real shape.** packages/gateway/src/redact.ts:8 lists "fal.ai `<key id>:<secret>`" among provider key shapes, and line 15 matches it with `/[0-9a-f]{8}-…-[0-9a-f]{12}:[0-9a-f]{32,}/gi`. held-key.ts:23 says the same: "a FAL_KEY (`<key id>:<secret>`)".
- **The evidence scan did not reuse that.** At line 52 it has a `fal-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,}` branch. That follows a test sentinel ('fal-SENTINEL' in env.test.ts:99, 'fal-test-not-a-real-key-0000' in estimate.test.ts:38), not a real key.
- **Only fal is missed.** The other four paid providers in KEY_VARS (env.ts:5-11) are caught: Anthropic sk-ant-, OpenAI sk-/sk-proj-, OpenRouter sk-or-v1- (through the sk- branch) and Gemini AIza.
- **Nothing else would catch it.** The test file is new in this batch (61 lines added). CI (.github/workflows/verify.yml) has no other secret scanner, and the repository has no pre-commit hook.
- **The builders meant to keep key-shaped strings out.** docs/adr/evidence/decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md:15 says the skeptic's estimate output "is not copied, because it holds key-shaped test strings". So a gap in the guard for one provider is a real defect.
- **Why should-fix and not blocking.** Nothing leaks today: no uuid:hex string is under docs/adr/evidence. The guard catches four of five providers. The risk is a future import, so this does not endanger the push on its own.

**Evidence:**

- **The pattern.** scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts:52 is `const key = /\b(?:sk-(?:ant-)?[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,}|AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{20,}|gh[pousr]_[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}|xox[abp]-[A-Za-z0-9-]{10,}|fal-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,})/g;`. It has no uuid:hex branch.
- **Run against sample strings in node:**
  - 'FAL_KEY=[a key-shaped string]' gives null, so it is not caught. This is the fixture from billed.test.ts:19, fal-submit.test.ts:21 and operation-keys.test.ts:27.
  - 'sk-or-v1-0123…' is caught.
  - 'sk-proj-…' is caught.
  - 'AIzaSy…' is caught.
- **The repo's own fal shape.** packages/gateway/src/redact.ts:8 reads "fal.ai `<key id>:<secret>`", and line 15 reads `/[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}:[0-9a-f]{32,}/gi`. packages/gateway/src/held-key.ts:23 reads "a FAL_KEY (`<key id>:<secret>`)".
- **No other scanner.** grep for gitleaks, trufflehog or secret scan over .github, *.yml and *.json finds nothing. The only workflow is verify.yml, and the repository has no husky, lefthook or pre-commit hook.
- **No leak today.** `grep -rnoE "<uuid>:[0-9a-fA-F]{16,}" docs/adr/evidence` returns nothing.
- **New in this batch.** `git diff 8401820..481b2e5 --stat -- scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts` shows 61 insertions, from commit eedc340.

**Fix:**

In scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts:52, add fal's real shape to the key pattern, mirroring redact.ts:15: `[0-9a-fA-F]{8}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{12}:[0-9a-fA-F]{32,}`. The pattern has no `i` flag, so it needs explicit upper-case ranges, or the `i` flag added. The invented `fal-…` branch can stay, since it is harmless, or be dropped.

Add a one-line self-check in the test that the pattern matches the repo's fixture key. For example: `expect('[a key-shaped string]'.match(key)).not.toBeNull()`, with `key.lastIndex` reset or a fresh non-global copy. That way a later edit cannot silently drop the branch.

A sturdier alternative is to export KEY_SHAPES from packages/gateway/src/redact.ts and reuse it, so the two lists cannot drift apart.

#### F07-1: compare-hand.mjs reports a pass for an empty or partial hand-case run

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed; lens evidence-tests; where `packages/runner/test/f07-corpus/compare-hand.mjs:49`.*

**What is wrong:**

`missing` counts only cases of files that appear in the run (`ran.has(fileOf(k))`). A file whose harness crashed, wrote [] or was never run is not missing, and an empty or mistyped folder exits 0. The README's 'What a pass means' and the script header say otherwise: "compare-hand.mjs finds every case's as in round 13" and "Exits 1 when ... a case is missing". compare.mjs handles this correctly.

**Evidence:**

`node compare-hand.mjs <empty dir>` prints '0 cases: 0 as in round 13, 0 different ..., 0 missing (round 13 has 746)', exit=0. A folder holding only cases/cases-h1.json = [] also exits 0. `node compare.mjs <empty dir>` exits 1 with '10055 of round 14's missing'.

**When it goes wrong:**

Before a Playwright upgrade someone runs the hand cases, a harness dies or the wrong &lt;out> path is passed, and compare-hand prints 0 differences and exits 0. The upgrade is then judged safe on no evidence.

**Proposed fix:**

Count every expected key that is not in `now` as missing, as compare.mjs does. If partial runs are meant to be allowed, require the files to compare to be named explicitly, and exit 1 when `now` is empty.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding reproduces exactly. packages/runner/test/f07-corpus/compare-hand.mjs:47-49 counts a case as missing only if its file is in `ran`, the set of files that have at least one case in `now`. A file that was never written, or was written as [], adds nothing to `ran`. So none of its cases count as missing, and the script exits 0.

This contradicts the script's own header (line 7): "Exits 1 when an outcome differs, or a case is missing or not in round 13". It also contradicts the README's "What a pass means" (README.md:68): "for the hand cases, `compare-hand.mjs` finds every case's as in round 13". compare.mjs has no such filter (line 44) and exits 1 with "10055 of round 14's missing".

The failure scenario can really happen, and in more ways than the finding names:
(a) hand12.mts:52, hand13.mts:50 and cases13.mts:106 each write their output once, at the very end. A harness that crashes partway leaves no file, not a partial one, so its whole file drops out silently.
(b) hand12.mts:12 and cases13.mts:13 create a `.lock` directory, and if it already exists they print "is run elsewhere" and `process.exit(0)`. A rerun into the same &lt;out> after a crash therefore skips that file with exit 0, writes nothing, and compare-hand still passes.
(c) cases13.mts:11 writes by default to `<F07_OUT>/cases13.json`, but compare-hand.mjs:18 reads `<out>/cases/cases13.json`. Anyone who runs cases13 without the README's explicit CASES_OUT, then points compare-hand at F07_OUT, loses all 319 cases13 cases and gets exit 0.
(d) A mistyped or nonexistent &lt;out> exits 0.

The comment at line 46 shows the per-file filter was put there on purpose, to allow partial runs. But neither the README nor the header says a pass covers only the files present, and nothing requires the run to be non-empty.

Severity is should-fix, not blocking. The tool is a manual check run before a Playwright upgrade or a page-VOICE change. It is not part of pnpm test, pnpm verify or CI, so it does not make this push wrong. It also prints "N cases ... (round 13 has 746)", so a careful reader can spot the shortfall. Still, its exit code, which is what the README defines as a pass, is false for empty and partial runs. That is a real defect in a guard this batch adds (d754402, merged through fix/fx-evidence).

**Evidence:**

I ran everything in packages/runner of the fx-batch worktree, with read-only scratch folders under C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/f07-1-refute-q7. The existing scratch folder f07-1 belonged to an earlier check and already held files, so I used a fresh one.

- `node test/f07-corpus/compare-hand.mjs <empty dir>` printed "0 cases: 0 as in round 13, 0 different (...), 0 not in round 13, 0 missing (round 13 has 746)" with exit=0. A nonexistent dir gave the same output and exit=0. A dir holding only cases/cases-h1.json = [] also gave exit=0.
- `node test/f07-corpus/compare.mjs <empty dir>` printed "0 page runs: ... 10055 of round 14's missing" with exit=1.
- I rebuilt a harness-format run from expected/round13-hand.json using my own build.cjs in that scratch folder. The full run gave "746 cases: 746 as in round 13 ... 0 missing", exit=0, which confirms the rebuilt format is faithful.
- With hand12's cases-r10h1.json deleted (a crash before writeFileSync): "689 cases ... 0 missing (round 13 has 746)", exit=0.
- With cases/cases13.json deleted: "427 cases ... 0 missing", exit=0.
- With the whole cases-v folder deleted (no hand13 file at all): "453 cases ... 0 missing", exit=0.
- Control: with cases-r10h1.json cut to its first 10 entries: "699 cases ... 47 missing", exit=1. So only a file that is absent or empty escapes.
- Round 13's 746 cases split across 26 files: cases13 319, hand12 8 files, hand13 17 files.
- hand12.mts:12 and cases13.mts:13 hold the lock-and-skip code (`catch { console.log(... 'is run elsewhere'); process.exit(0); }`).
- cases13.mts:11 has the default `.../byway-f07/cases13.json`, against compare-hand.mjs:18's `path.join(dir, 'cases', 'cases13.json')`.
- The header, line 7: "Exits 1 when an outcome differs, or a case is missing or not in round 13."
- README.md:68: "for the hand cases, `compare-hand.mjs` finds every case's as in round 13".

**Fix:**

In compare-hand.mjs:49, count every expected key that is not in `now`, as compare.mjs:44 does: `const missing = Object.keys(expected).filter((k) => !(k in now));`. Then delete the `fileOf`/`ran` lines (46-48), or keep them only to print which files are absent. If partial runs should stay allowed, take the files to compare as explicit arguments, and exit 1 whenever `now` is empty or a named file has no output. Update README.md:64 to say that missing cases also exit 1. Optionally, make cases13.mts's default CASES_OUT `<F07_OUT>/cases/cases13.json` so it matches where compare-hand reads.

**Refuter 2: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding holds. compare-hand.mjs is new in this batch (d754402, fix/fx-evidence). At line 49 it counts a case as missing only when its file appears in the run: `ran.has(fileOf(k))`. A file that is absent, or that holds [], or a whole run that is absent is never counted, and the script exits 0.

The comment at line 46 shows that limiting the check to files that ran was deliberate, probably so one file can be rerun on its own (README line 77 mentions two more runs of cases-b). But no record accepts that limit. The script's header (line 7) says it "Exits 1 when an outcome differs, or a case is missing or not in round 13". README "What a pass means" (line 68) says compare-hand.mjs "finds every case's as in round 13". The backlog's "Playwright bump policy" row (docs/backlog.md:56) calls this script the pre-bump check of the hand cases against round 13. I searched the backlog, ADR-0081's Later note (line 135), the evidence README and the 2026-10-16 decision brief. None of them records partial runs as an accepted limit or gives them a trigger.

The owner's decision (decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md:1754) was to copy the corpus "so they survive and can be rerun before any Playwright upgrade". The comparison scripts are the builders' addition. A comparison that passes when there is no run works against the reason the corpus was kept.

The failure modes are realistic:
- The hand cases are 26 separate commands: cases13, 8 hand12 files and 17 hand13 files. README lines 54-55 abbreviate them as "# and cases-h2 to h5, ..." and "# and every other file in vcases/".
- hand12.mts (line 52) and hand13.mts (line 50) write their JSON only after the last case. A harness that crashes partway therefore leaves no file at all, and the README notes that crashed browsers do occur.
- hand12.mts line 12 leaves a .lock behind. A plain rerun on the same folder then prints "is run elsewhere", exits 0 and writes nothing.
- An out path with a typo is caught only by `existsSync`, which skips quietly.

Why should-fix and not blocking:
- This is a manual tool that the gate does not run. It does not affect money, run behaviour or a record of the owner's words.
- The summary line still prints the run's case count next to "(round 13 has 746)", so a careful reader can see the shortfall.
- The builders' own recorded run covered every file and reported 745 of 746 (README line 77), so the evidence recorded in this batch does not depend on this defect.

Why not a note: the exit code and the "0 missing" count contradict the documented meaning of a pass. The code is new in this batch, and the fix is a line or two.

**Evidence:**

Code: packages/runner/test/f07-corpus/compare-hand.mjs:46-49
  // A case of a file this run ran that the run does not have (cases13 counts as one file).
  const ran = new Set(Object.keys(now).map(fileOf));
  const missing = Object.keys(expected).filter((k) => !(k in now) && ran.has(fileOf(k)));
Header, line 7: "Exits 1 when an outcome differs, or a case is missing or not in round 13."
README:68: "for the hand cases, `compare-hand.mjs` finds every case's as in round 13".
compare.mjs:44 counts every expected key: `Object.keys(expected).filter((k) => !seen.has(k))`.

Reproduced read-only. Test folders are under C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/f07-1, built by C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/f07-1/build.cjs, which rebuilds a run from expected/round13-hand.json.
- Empty folder: "0 cases: 0 as in round 13, ... 0 missing (round 13 has 746)", exit=0.
- Folder holding only cases/cases-h1.json = []: the same output, exit=0.
- Folder that does not exist: the same output, exit=0.
- compare.mjs on the empty folder: "... 10055 of round 14's missing", exit=1.
- Synthetic full run: "746 cases: 746 as in round 13 ... 0 missing", exit=0. This shows the rebuilt run is faithful.
- The same run without hand13 cases-a and hand12 cases-r10h1 (91 cases): "655 cases: 655 as in round 13, ... 0 missing (round 13 has 746)", exit=0.
- Also without cases/cases13.json (319 more cases): "336 cases: ... 0 missing (round 13 has 746)", exit=0.

How runs end up with whole files absent:
- hand12.mts:52 and hand13.mts:50 write the output only after the last case, so a crash writes nothing.
- hand12.mts:12 exits 0 with "is run elsewhere" when the lock directory left by a crashed run exists.

No accepted limit is recorded: docs/backlog.md:56 calls compare-hand.mjs the pre-bump check, and no backlog row, Later note or brief accepts partial-run passes.

Not checked: no real harness was run, because they need hours and Chromium.

**Fix:**

At compare-hand.mjs:49, count every expected key that is absent from `now`, as compare.mjs does:
`const missing = Object.keys(expected).filter((k) => !(k in now));`
Print the first 20 missing keys, plus a count for each file that has nothing.

Before reading the folder, exit 2 when it does not exist:
`if (!existsSync(dir)) { console.error('no such folder', dir); process.exit(2); }`

If single-file reruns, such as the cases-b reruns, should still be able to pass, make that explicit: accept an optional list of files after &lt;out> (for example `hand13|cases-b`), limit `expected` to those files, and still exit 1 when `now` is empty.

Then update the header (line 7) and README line 64 so they state the missing-case rule, and mention the optional file list if it is added.

#### REC-1: Playbook says 37 Delegated records; the index has 38

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed; lens records; where `docs/playbook.md:49`.*

**What is wrong:**

The playbook row says "Review the 37 Delegated records", but the merged index has 38 Delegated rows and its count line says "38 delegated". The records branch moved ADR-0100 out of Delegated (38 to 37). B1 then added ADR-0109 as Delegated (back to 38). The merge kept the records branch's 37 in the playbook.

**Evidence:**

At 481b2e5, counting the index rows' Decided-by values gives `38 Delegated` (with 15 By continuation, 7 Pending, 20 User, 29 User-approved; 109 rows). The count line reads "**109 records:** 102 accepted (15 of them by continuation, awaiting ratification; 38 delegated)". Per branch: `git show fix/fx-records:docs/playbook.md` gives "Review the 37 Delegated" (index there: 37 delegated). `fix/fx-b1` gives "Review the 38 Delegated" (its index: 39 delegated). At head the playbook still says "Review the 37 Delegated records". Only dca74a7 touched the playbook in this batch.

**When it goes wrong:**

The owner opens the playbook to review Claude's delegated decisions and is told there are 37. The index lists 38, and ADR-0109, this batch's money-path record, is the one the stale number leaves out.

**Proposed fix:**

Change the playbook to "Review the 38 Delegated records". Optionally extend scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts's count check to the playbook's number.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding reproduces exactly. At head 481b2e5, docs/playbook.md:49 reads "Review the 37 Delegated records". The merged index docs/adr/README.md has 38 rows whose Decided by is Delegated (109 rows in all), and its count line at line 13 says "38 delegated". adr-records.test.ts checks that count line against the rows, so the index itself is right. Only the playbook number is stale.

How it happened:
- On main at 8401820, the playbook and the index both said 38.
- fix/fx-records moved ADR-0100's row from Delegated to User-approved. It set the index to 37 and the playbook to 37 in dca74a7, the batch's only commit that touches the playbook.
- fix/fx-b1 added ADR-0109 as Delegated, which took its index to 39, but never touched the playbook. B1's branch was already stale on its own: playbook 38, index 39.
- The merge took the records branch's 37 because B1 had no playbook change to conflict with it. The integration commit 481b2e5 did not touch the playbook either.

The net change from main is one record out (ADR-0100) and one record in (ADR-0109), so the true count is 38.

Nothing guards this number. adr-records.test.ts lines 153-162 check only the index's own count line. The only playbook check, in scripts/test/ci.test.ts lines 330-342, looks at a different row.

One nuance in the failure scenario: a bare count cannot name which record it leaves out. But the stale 37 was computed before ADR-0109 existed, so ADR-0109 is in effect the record the number misses. The playbook row also never mentions ADR-0109 (grep for 0109 in docs/playbook.md finds nothing).

On severity: this is the owner's working list of Claude's delegated decisions to review, it miscounts by one, and the one it misses is this batch's money-path record. It is not a misquote of the owner or a broken log rule, so should-fix (not blocking) is the right severity.

**Evidence:**

- `grep -n Delegated docs/playbook.md` at head gives line 49: "| **Review the 37 Delegated records** (decided by Claude under authority the owner delegated; ...".
- docs/adr/README.md:13 reads "**109 records:** 102 accepted (15 of them by continuation, awaiting ratification; 38 delegated) and 7 proposed."
- Parsing the index rows at head gives 109 rows: {User:20, By continuation:15, User-approved:29, Delegated:38, Pending:7}. The Delegated list includes "ADR-0109 Accepted".
- Per ref, playbook number / index count line / rows actually counted as Delegated:

| Ref | Playbook | Count line | Rows |
|---|---|---|---|
| 8401820 (main) | 38 | 38 delegated | 38 |
| fix/fx-records | 37 | 37 delegated | 37 |
| fix/fx-b1 | 38 | 39 delegated | 39 |
| fix/fx-evidence | 38 | 38 delegated | 38 |
| 481b2e5 (head) | 37 | 38 delegated | 38 |

- `git diff 8401820..fix/fx-records -- docs/adr/README.md` shows ADR-0100's row changing from "| Delegated |" to "| User-approved |".
- `git log --oneline 8401820..481b2e5 -- docs/playbook.md` returns only dca74a7 (records). `git log 8401820..fix/fx-b1 -- docs/playbook.md` is empty.
- docs/adr/0109-*.md line 6: "| **Decided by** | Delegated: Claude built backlog batch B1 ...".
- scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts lines 153-162 compare only the README count line with the index rows. Nothing compares the playbook's number with anything.

**Fix:**

In docs/playbook.md line 49, change "Review the 37 Delegated records" to "Review the 38 Delegated records". Optionally, in scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts, read the number from the playbook's "Review the N Delegated records" row and check it equals the count of index rows marked Delegated, so a later merge cannot leave it stale again.

**Refuter 2: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I tried to refute this by checking whether 37 could still be right: perhaps ADR-0109 is not Delegated, the playbook means a different set of records, or the owner chose the number somewhere. None of these holds.

1. ADR-0109 is Delegated, both in its own header and in its index row. A Delegated record is "flagged for review", which is exactly the set the playbook row tells the owner to review.
2. The playbook's own Source column points to the "ADR index". At head that index has 38 Delegated rows, and its count line, which scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts checks, says 38.
3. No other playbook row or record redefines the set or holds the number at 37.

How the number went stale: main had the playbook and the index in sync at 38. fix/fx-records moved ADR-0100 to User-approved and correctly changed both numbers to 37. fix/fx-b1 added its money-path record (ADR-0107, renumbered to ADR-0109 in 88853f5) as Delegated, which made 39 in its own index. B1 never edited the playbook, so on that branch the playbook already lagged, still at main's 38. Because only the records branch touched the playbook, the merge took its 37. Neither the merge commits nor 481b2e5 (the integration commit) changed it. The result at head: the index says 38 and the playbook says 37.

No test catches this. The count check in adr-records.test.ts (lines 153-162) reads only the index's count line, never the playbook. So this is a real inconsistency this batch introduced in an owner-facing to-do list.

Severity stays should-fix, at the low end. One part of the failure scenario is overstated: the playbook does not list the records itself, so it does not "leave out" ADR-0109 as such. It sends the owner to the index, where all 38 rows, ADR-0109 included, are listed. Still, the owner is given a wrong count, and the batch's own new Delegated record is the one the number misses. This is not blocking: no money, behaviour, quote of the owner's words or ADR-0103 rule is affected.

**Evidence:**

At head 481b2e5:
- docs/playbook.md:49 reads "| **Review the 37 Delegated records** (decided by Claude under authority the owner delegated; reversible and flagged for review)..." and its Source column is "ADR index, ADR-0105 ("Who decided what"), ADR-0107".
- docs/adr/README.md:13 reads "**109 records:** 102 accepted (15 of them by continuation, awaiting ratification; 38 delegated) and 7 proposed."
- Counting the Decided-by column of the index rows gives 15 By continuation, 38 Delegated, 7 Pending, 20 User, 29 User-approved (109 rows). The Delegated rows include ADR-0109.
- docs/adr/0109-*.md line 6: "| **Decided by** | Delegated: Claude built backlog batch B1 ... not explicitly approved; reversible. |"

Per branch (playbook count / index count line / index Delegated rows):
- 8401820 (main): "Review the 38 Delegated" / 38 delegated / 38 rows
- fix/fx-records: "Review the 37 Delegated" / 37 delegated / 37 rows (its diff changes ADR-0100 from "| Delegated |" to "| User-approved |")
- fix/fx-b1: "Review the 38 Delegated" (left at main's number) / 39 delegated / 39 rows
- fix/fx-evidence: 38 / 38 / 38

`git log --oneline 8401820..481b2e5 -- docs/playbook.md` shows only dca74a7 (the records commit).

scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts:153-162 parses only the INDEX count line and compares it with the index rows. Nothing reads the playbook's number.

**Fix:**

In docs/playbook.md:49, change "Review the 37 Delegated records" to "Review the 38 Delegated records". Optional: extend the count check in scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts to match the playbook's "Review the N Delegated records" against the number of Delegated rows in the index.

#### REC-2: ADR-0109's estimate consequences are wrong: every configuration lost VOICE calls, and C3's Balanced budget did not move from $2

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed; lens records; where `docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:60`.*

**What is wrong:**

Line 60 says "Estimates with a VOICE lose one mid-tier call per VOICE (C3 one, C4 two)". In fact every catalogue configuration has a VOICE that writes the work, and all of their estimates lost those calls. Line 65 says "On Balanced, C3's default run budget goes from $2 to $2.50 by this alone". C3's Balanced default budget is $2.50 on main and $2.50 at head. $2 belongs to a state no commit ever had: VOICE's call removed but Gemini's text and thinking still unpriced.

**Evidence:**

I walked the catalog at head for VOICE steps: c1 voice, c2 voice, c3 voice, c4 voice + claim-sign-off, c5 vo-script + voice, c6 voice, c6a voice, c7 voice. Each produces voice+artifact, which the old estimate charged as a voice.apply call. I ran advisor.estimate through the advisor test helpers with fake keys, on main (a clone at 8401820) and at head; model calls low-high on Balanced: C1 25-61 to 24-60, C2 21-24 to 20-23, C3 16-24 to 15-23, C4 5-12 to 4-10, C5 33-83 to 31-81, C6 4-32 to 3-31, C6a 15-44 to 14-43, C7 12-18 to 11-17. C3 on Balanced: main highUsd 1.64, suggestBudget 2.5 (apps/web/src/view/format.ts:66, ceil(high*1.25*2)/2). Head highUsd 1.62, suggestBudget 2.5. Head with the gemini-nano-banana-2.1 row's outputPerMTok set back to 0: high 1.60, budget 2. On fix/fx-b1 the pricing commit 6e4eb65 came before the VOICE commit afcc3fd, so the budget was $2.50 at every step. Some defaults did move, for example C6 Best $6 to $5.50 and C1 on the Google profile $3.50 to $4.

**When it goes wrong:**

The record says the first live run compares estimates with the ledger. A reader of ADR-0109 expects C3 on Balanced to start with a $2.50 budget where it used to be $2, and VOICE changes only in C3 and C4. They will misread both the C3 comparison and the drop in every other configuration (C1, C2, C5, C6, C6a, C7).

**Proposed fix:**

In Consequences, say every configuration's estimate loses one mid-tier call per VOICE that writes the work (C4 and C5 two, the others one). State C3's net effect against main: on Balanced its high estimate goes from $1.64 to $1.62 and its default budget stays $2.50. Or give the Gemini figure as an increment (about $0.02 on C3's high estimate) rather than a budget step that never happened.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I reproduced both halves of REC-2 independently. I used my own clone (scratchpad/check-clones/rec2-refute-x) and a probe test that calls createAdvisor with demoGateway({demo:false}) and fake keys for every provider. It applies apps/web's suggestBudget formula, Math.max(0.5, ceil(high*1.25*2)/2), copied from apps/web/src/view/format.ts:66-68. I measured four states: main (8401820's advisor, gateway and catalog source), main plus the head prices only, head, and head with gemini-nano-banana-2.1's outputPerMTok set to 0.

Line 60, "(C3 one, C4 two)": every one of the eight protocol configurations lost VOICE calls, not only C3 and C4. Every VOICE step in the catalog writes the work (produces ['voice','artifact']; parts.ts:205-206, steps.ts:156-163, c4.ts:62), so the old voice.apply term applied to each. C5 also loses two (vo-script and voice) and is not named. The qualifier "Estimates with a VOICE" is accurate in a narrow sense, because C1-H (it lists VOICE in sitsOut) and the baselines have no VOICE. But all eight configurations the product offers have one, so the parenthetical reads as if only C3 and C4 changed. One small refinement: C4 loses two calls only at the high end (5-12 to 4-10), because claim-sign-off is optional; C5 loses two at both ends.

Line 65, "On Balanced, C3's default run budget goes from $2 to $2.50 by this alone": C3 Balanced is $2.50 at every state anyone had.
- main: high 1.64, budget 2.5
- main plus the Gemini charge alone, which is 6e4eb65's estimate state (git diff 8401820 6e4eb65 shows no advisor or catalog change, and 6e4eb65..head changes no price row): high 1.65, budget 2.5
- head, which is also afcc3fd's state: high 1.62, budget 2.5

$2 appears only in the counterfactual "head without the Gemini charge" (high 1.60; 1.60*1.25*2 = 4.0 exactly, so ceil gives 4 and the budget $2). No commit had that state, because 6e4eb65 (pricing) precedes afcc3fd (VOICE) on fix/fx-b1. The most natural reading of "by this alone" is to apply only this change to what was there before, and that gives $2.50 to $2.50, so the sentence misleads. It is literally true only as the Gemini charge's marginal effect on top of the VOICE removal, and on a rounding knife-edge.

Severity: should-fix rather than blocking. It touches no money and no behaviour, and it misquotes no owner words. ADR-0103 decision 1 (docs/adr/0103-adr-log-rule-and-ci.md:40) freezes only Context and Decision and says "the other sections may change", so Consequences could still be corrected after the push. It is still a factual misstatement in a new money record. That record ties the VOICE fix to "the first live run's comparison of estimates with the ledger" (line 18), so it should be corrected while the record is being written.

Also observed: in the same line 65, "C2's twelve mood boards cost about $0.60 rather than $0.40" is a statement about a run's cost, not about the estimate. C2's estimate plans images 0-3 (DEFAULT_SLOTS = 3; estimate.ts:236-237), while a run makes one board per option (slot-plan.ts:36-39). This gap is already filed as backlog row 86, so it is not new.

**Evidence:**

ADR text (docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md):
- :60 "Estimates with a VOICE lose one mid-tier call per VOICE (C3 one, C4 two); budgets derived from the high estimate drop a little."
- :65 "On Balanced, C3's default run budget goes from $2 to $2.50 by this alone". This was added in 56afdde, whose message says "C3's default budget on Balanced goes from $2 to $2.50 by this alone".

Old estimate code (git diff 8401820..481b2e5 -- packages/advisor/src/estimate.ts):
- removed: "const apply = s.produces.includes('artifact') ? completion(w, 'voice.apply', 'mid', count(1, 1)) : NONE;"

VOICE steps in the catalog (all produce artifact):
- c1 voiceByHuman('voice')
- c2 voice('voice')
- c3 voice('voice')
- c4 voice('voice','key-lines') plus optional claim-sign-off
- c5 voice('vo-script') and voice('voice')
- c6, c6a and c7 voice('voice')

Probe output (my clone, fake keys for all five providers), Balanced, model calls and high USD / default budget, main to head:
| Config | Model calls | High USD / budget |
|---|---|---|
| c1 | 25-61 to 24-60 | 5.87/7.5 to 5.92/7.5 |
| c2 | 21-24 to 20-23 | 1.03/1.5 to 1.03/1.5 |
| c3 | 16-24 to 15-23 | 1.64/2.5 to 1.62/2.5 |
| c4 | 5-12 to 4-10 | 0.53/1 to 0.46/1 |
| c5 | 33-83 to 31-81 | 26.92/34 to 27.03/34 |
| c6 | 4-32 to 3-31 | 2.97/4 to 2.94/4 |
| c6a | 15-44 to 14-43 | 3.35/4.5 to 3.31/4.5 |
| c7 | 12-18 to 11-17 | 1.56/2 to 1.50/2 |

Counterfactual states for C3 on Balanced:
- main estimate.ts with head prices.ts (= 6e4eb65's state; git diff --stat 8401820 6e4eb65 -- packages/advisor/src packages/catalog/src is empty): high 1.65, budget 2.5
- head with nano-banana outputPerMTok set to 0: high 1.60, budget 2

Other figures the finding cites, all reproduced:
- C6 Best: 6 to 5.5
- C1 Google: 3.5 to 4

Commit order on fix/fx-b1 (first-parent, oldest first): 6e4eb65 (prices), d4bd96b, 22f521b, afcc3fd (VOICE), 2b1021a, e885788, 56afdde, 88853f5. No commit had VOICE removed while Gemini was still unpriced.

ADR-0103 decision 1 (line 40): "The header table (its Status row included) and the other sections may change". So Consequences stay editable after main.

The fx-batch worktree was untouched (git status clean). My clone was restored to head afterwards.

**Fix:**

Replace the two sentences in ADR-0109's Consequences:
- Line 60: "Every configuration's estimate loses one mid-tier call for each VOICE that writes the work: C1, C2, C3, C6, C6a and C7 one, C4 and C5 two (C4's second only when its claim log runs). Budgets derived from the high estimate drop a little where no Gemini image offsets it (on Best, C6 $6 to $5.50, C6a $7 to $6.50, C7 $3.50 to $3)."
- Line 65: state the Gemini effect as an increment and the net against main, for example: "...by about $0.015 for each image planned (C3's high estimate on Balanced by about $0.01 to $0.02; with VOICE's call gone it goes from $1.64 to $1.62, and its default budget stays $2.50). On the Google profile, C1's default budget goes from $3.50 to $4."

Optionally, also make the C2 clause say it is the run's cost of twelve boards, not the estimate's, since the estimate plans at most three (backlog "C2 mood boards").

**Refuter 2: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I tried to refute both halves and could not. The one defensible reading of line 65 still leaves the record misleading.

1) Line 60, "Estimates with a VOICE lose one mid-tier call per VOICE (C3 one, C4 two)". Every catalogue configuration has a human VOICE step that produces 'artifact', and main's estimate.ts charged a mid-tier voice.apply completion for each one. All of them lost calls. The general clause is true, but the parenthetical reads as the full list, and the commit message afcc3fd does the same. It names exactly the two recipes the new test checks (estimate.test.ts uses c3 and c4). It leaves out C1, C2, C6, C6a and C7 (one each) and C5 (two: vo-script and voice). The finding's suggested fix needs one correction: C4's second VOICE (claim-sign-off) sits inside optional('claim-log'), so C4 loses two calls only at the high end. Its range goes from 5-12 to 4-10. C5 loses two at both ends (33-83 to 31-81).

2) Line 65, "On Balanced, C3's default run budget goes from $2 to $2.50 by this alone". C3's Balanced default budget (suggestBudget, apps/web/src/view/format.ts:64-66) is $2.50 on main and $2.50 at head. Applying only the Gemini change to main leaves it at $2.50 (high $1.64 to $1.65). That matches fix/fx-b1's own order: 6e4eb65 (pricing) came before afcc3fd (VOICE), and the budget was $2.50 at every step. The $2 figure comes from one place only: head with item 2 taken out. There the high estimate is $1.60, and $1.60 x 1.25 x 2 = 4.0 lands exactly on the rounding boundary, giving $2. Commit 56afdde added the sentence after afcc3fd, so the builder measured "by this alone" as that ablation. The number is reproducible, not made up. But it is reported as a before/after change that no user or commit ever saw. The real effect on C3 is $0.02 on the high estimate, and the net change against main is none. Line 60 says budgets "drop a little", while C3's Balanced budget does not move at all.

No owner decision or recorded limit covers this. The record is "Delegated: Claude built backlog batch B1", and these are Claude's own computed consequences, not the owner's words. The backlog rows (65, 156) make no per-configuration claim. So this is a real defect in the record. It is not blocking. No money or behaviour is wrong, and the backlog's first live run is "one Balanced run estimated under $1", which fits C4 ($0.07-$0.46), and C4 is named correctly at the high end. Also, ADR-0103 rule 1 freezes only Context and Decision, so Consequences could still be corrected after the push. It should still be fixed in this batch, because the record exists to support the first live run's comparison of estimates with the ledger.

**Evidence:**

Catalog VOICE steps at head (all role 'voice', produces ['voice','artifact']): c1.ts:61 voiceByHuman('voice'); c2.ts:61; c3.ts:57; c4.ts:58 voice('voice','key-lines') and c4.ts:59-63 optional('claim-log', step 'claim-sign-off' role 'voice'); c5.ts:69 vo-script and c5.ts:136 voice; c6.ts:63; c6a.ts:44; c7.ts:83. The helpers are parts.ts:205-206 and steps.ts:157-166. Main's estimate.ts stepSpan 'voice' case: `const apply = s.produces.includes('artifact') ? completion(w, 'voice.apply', 'mid', count(1, 1)) : NONE;`

My own runs: a script at C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/rec2z/est.mts calling estimateRecipe with images/video/otherFamily on and suggestBudget. Head was run from the fx-batch worktree, read-only. Main ran in my clone .../scratchpad/check-clones/rec2-z-main at 8401820. Outputs are in .../scratchpad/rec2z/*.txt. Balanced results:
- main: c1 calls 25-61, usd 1.14-5.87, budget 7.5; c3 16-24, 0.29-1.64, budget 2.5; c4 5-12; c5 33-83; c6 4-32; c6a 15-44; c7 12-18; c2 21-24.
- main with only the new nano row (o 7.5, 2000 tokens): c3 0.29-1.65, budget 2.5.
- head with the nano row zeroed: c3 15-23, 0.27-1.6, budget 2.
- head: c1 24-60, 1.08-5.92; c2 20-23; c3 15-23, 0.27-1.62, budget 2.5; c4 4-10; c5 31-81; c6 3-31; c6a 14-43; c7 11-17.
- Other profiles: Best c6 budget 6 to 5.5; Google c1 budget 3.5 to 4.

git log 8401820..fix/fx-b1 shows the order 6e4eb65 (prices) then afcc3fd (VOICE) then 56afdde. 56afdde's message reads "C3's default budget on Balanced goes from $2 to $2.50 by this alone". The recommendation estimates the canonical recipe with its routes open (advisor.ts:161 estimate(chosen, ...)), which is the estimate measured here. RecommendationView.tsx:377 seeds the budget field with suggestBudget(estimate). docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md, Consequences.

Line 65: replace the budget step with the actual increment and the net effect, for example: "On Balanced, C3's high estimate rises by about $0.02 from this, and with the VOICE change its net goes from $1.64 to $1.62; its default run budget stays $2.50." Keep the C2 mood-board sentence.

No code change is needed.

#### REC-3: Extending D2's merge condition to later risky changes relaxes an owner-chosen gate on a reading of "Keep CI as it is"; README states it unlabelled and drops "report first"

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed-partly; lens records; where `docs/adr/0107-code-is-checked-before-it-is-pushed.md:27`.*

**What is wrong:**

ADR-0107 item 4 records that "That it holds for later risky changes too is Claude's reading of the \"Not now\" answer, delegated and reversible". The "Not now" option text was "Keep CI as it is; timing flakes get named and set aside." Read literally, that keeps ADR-0103 §3, whose risky-change rule ("merges only when both jobs pass ... (the owner chose this on 2026-10-16)") is a User decision. Its "named and set aside" repeats §3's existing rule for a red main. The owner also chose "both jobs pass" again for the CI-timing branch. So delegated authority relaxes a gate the owner chose more than once. The label is there in ADR-0107 and ADR-0103's note, but README.md:208 states the looser rule as the rule, cites only ADR-0107 (indexed as "User") and says "any other failure is fixed first". That drops the "report first" the owner chose and contradicts ADR-0107's own "Any other failure stops the merge and is reported."

**Evidence:**

conversation-continued.md, 2026-10-18 14:02: "Adopt the CI proposal?" answered "Not now", whose description was "Keep CI as it is; timing flakes get named and set aside." ADR-0103 §3: "A red main is fixed, or a flaky test is named and set aside, before more merges. A risky change ... is run on its branch first and merges only when both jobs pass ... (the owner chose this on 2026-10-16)." Owner choices for the CI-timing branch: 2026-10-17 20:17, "Merge to main and push only when both jobs pass; if either fails, I report and we decide."; 2026-10-17 20:44, "merge only when both pass". The D2-only options: 14:02, "Report before anything further."; 15:23, "If either finds something, I report first."; 15:49, "merge D2 if ... on Windows passes or fails only on the named timing flake". README.md:208: "It merges when the Linux job passes and the Windows job passes or fails only on a timing flake already named in the backlog; any other failure is fixed first (ADR-0107)". The index row for ADR-0107 has Decided by "User".

**When it goes wrong:**

The next risky change (for example the macOS bring-up) is red on Windows on a flake named in the backlog. Following the README, an agent merges it to main without asking, although the owner's last general answer was "Keep CI as it is" and the owner chose "both jobs pass" for every risky branch except D2. If the run instead shows another failure, the README tells the agent to fix it rather than report first.

**Proposed fix:**

Preferred: limit item 4 to D2 and keep ADR-0103 §3 for later risky changes until the owner decides, filed as an owner question with the D2 wording as the recommended option. At minimum, label README:208 as Claude's reading pending the owner's review, and change "any other failure is fixed first" to "any other failure stops the merge and is reported to the owner", matching ADR-0107 item 4 and the owner's options.

**Refuter 1: partly, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

Every quote in the finding is accurate, and the README part of the failure scenario can happen. The claim is three things, and only one of them holds up as a defect.

1) "Delegated authority relaxes an owner-chosen gate." The facts are right, but this is not a broken rule or a false record. ADR-0103 §3's "both jobs pass" is the owner's, as the finding says. The transcript has it at 2026-10-17T03:12:51Z: Claude proposed "I push the branch first, wait for both machines to pass, then merge ... for risky changes", and the owner answered "Yes. Go with that and create the rulesets." at 03:17:31Z. The owner also picked "both pass" options on 10-17 20:17 and 20:47, and on 10-18 09:22 (D2 round 2: "merge only if both pass"). That last one is not in the finding but supports it. On the other side:
- Every one of those "both pass" options was Claude's recommended option.
- The owner's last three choices (10-18 14:02, 15:23, 15:49) all went looser.
- ADR-0107 labels the extension four times: the Decided-by row ("Delegated to Claude, reversible: ... applying item 4 to risky changes after D2"), item 4 line 27, the Alternatives entry that names the D2-only option and why Claude did not choose it, and ADR-0103's Later note at line 167.
- The repo's own rules ask that Claude's additions be marked and that no gates be added. This removes a gate rather than adding one, and it is marked. Whether to keep the reading is the owner's call. It is not a defect in the record.
- One piece of supporting context: docs/reviews/2026-10-18-windows-ci.md:22-23 was written for the owner after the "Not now" answer. It still lists the middle option as "merges only when both jobs pass", next to "Not now ... CI stays as it is". So the wider reading was made later and was never put to the owner. That is a good reason to name it in the push report, not to block the push.

2) "README states it unlabelled; the index says User." Refuted. README.md never gives provenance for any rule: a grep for owner, delegated, "Claude's reading" or "owner chose" in README.md finds nothing. It cites ADRs, and the cited ADR-0107 carries the label. The index marks mixed records "User" as a rule; ADR-0105 has delegated parts and is indexed "User" too. ADR-0107's index title limits the owner's choice to D2: "for D2 the owner chose to merge a risky change when Linux passes and Windows passes or fails only on a named timing flake". So no false statement there.

3) "README drops 'report first'." Confirmed. README.md:208 ends "any other failure is fixed first". ADR-0107:27, the record it cites, says "Any other failure stops the merge and is reported". The owner chose a report step in every D2 option and in the CI-timing branch option:
- "Report before anything further."
- "If either finds something, I report first."
- "if either fails, I report and we decide."

ADR-0107 item 2 also has every further fix round decided with the owner. ADR-0103 §3 says the README's CI section is read by "whoever merges next", so an agent reading only that section would fix a real branch failure and go on toward a merge without reporting. This batch introduced the sentence, and it contradicts the batch's own ADR. That is a real, cheap, should-fix wording defect. It does not endanger the push on its own.

The other half of the failure scenario does not hold as a README defect. An agent merging on a backlog-named Windows flake without asking is exactly what the labelled ADR-0107 item 4 allows, so the README did not create it. A named flake does exist (docs/backlog.md:171, C5's 120 s wait), so that path is open.

One small inaccuracy in the finding: the owner's "merge only when both pass" choice was answered at 20:47 local; the question was asked at 20:44.

**Evidence:**

- README.md:208: "It merges when the Linux job passes and the Windows job passes or fails only on a timing flake already named in the backlog; any other failure is fixed first ([ADR-0107](...))". Main's earlier text was "...merges only when both jobs pass" (git diff 8401820..481b2e5 -- README.md).
- docs/adr/0107-code-is-checked-before-it-is-pushed.md:27: "Any other failure stops the merge and is reported. The owner chose this for D2. That it holds for later risky changes too is Claude's reading of the \"Not now\" answer, delegated and reversible". Line 6 (Decided by): "Delegated to Claude, reversible: ... applying item 4 to risky changes after D2". Line 40, under Alternatives, names the D2-only option.
- docs/adr/0103-adr-log-rule-and-ci.md:72 (§3: "merges only when both jobs pass ... (the owner chose this on 2026-10-16)"). Line 167 (Later note: "with Claude's reading that it holds for later risky changes too").
- conversation-continued.md:
  - line 338 ("Not now" = "Keep CI as it is; timing flakes get named and set aside.")
  - line 294 (10-17 20:16 option: "Merge to main and push only when both jobs pass; if either fails, I report and we decide.")
  - line 302 ("merge only when both pass", answered 20:47)
  - line 330 (10-18 09:20 D2 round 2: "merge only if both pass. Report before any further round.")
  - line 346 ("If either finds something, I report first.")
  - line 379 (15:49 suggestion)
- Session transcript, read-only scan:
  - 2026-10-17T03:12:51Z, Claude: "I push the branch first, wait for both machines to pass, then merge ... risky changes". The owner's answer at 03:17:31Z is "Yes. Go with that".
  - No owner message after 2026-10-19T03:53Z addresses the merge condition (the only ones are about the evidence workflow, then "Try again").
- docs/reviews/2026-10-18-windows-ci.md:22-23: after the "Not now" answer, it still lists "merges only when both jobs pass" as the owner's middle option.
- A grep of README.md for owner/delegated/"Claude's reading"/"owner chose" finds nothing. docs/adr/README.md:146 indexes ADR-0105, a mixed record, as "User"; line 43 is ADR-0107's D2-scoped title.
- docs/backlog.md:171 names the C5 120 s Windows flake.

**Fix:**

In README.md:208, replace "; any other failure is fixed first" with "; any other failure stops the merge and is reported to the owner, who decides what happens next". This matches ADR-0107:27 and item 2. No ADR change is needed for this: the README is not under the log rule. Optionally, in the push report to the owner, name the wider reading in ADR-0107 item 4 (later risky changes may merge red on a named Windows flake) as Claude's delegated reading for the owner to confirm or reverse. Restricting item 4 to D2 is the owner's choice, not a required fix.

**Refuter 2: partly, note.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding has three parts. Two of them do not hold up. The third is a real wording slip, but it is note-level.

1) "Delegated authority relaxes a gate the owner chose more than once": not a defect.
- The records say clearly that the extension is Claude's reading, in four places: the ADR-0107 header ("Delegated to Claude, reversible: ... applying item 4 to risky changes after D2"), item 4 ("Claude's reading of the 'Not now' answer, delegated and reversible, until the owner says otherwise"), Alternatives (the D2-only option was considered and rejected, with the reason given), and ADR-0103's new Later note ("with Claude's reading that it holds for later risky changes too").
- It is also flagged for the owner's review. This batch adds it to the playbook's review row for delegated choices (docs/playbook.md:49: "and its reading that item 4's merge rule holds after D2"). That is the repository's mechanism for "Delegated (reversible and flagged for review)", so the finding's "preferred fix" (file it as an owner question) is already done in substance.
- The authority is real. The 2026-10-16 "middle option", which the owner approved with "Yes. Go with that", was Claude's proposal. It made the branch-CI wait discretionary: "only when I choose to wait, so I could use it for risky changes" (transcript, 2026-10-17T03:12:51Z).
- The "chose more than once" framing is selective:
  - The two 2026-10-17 "both pass" answers were for the CI-timing test-fix branch, whose own purpose was to show Windows passing. They were not a general rule for risky changes. The second answer is 20:47; 20:44 is when the question was asked.
  - Every later owner choice, three times on 2026-10-18 (14:02, 15:23, 15:49), was the looser condition.
  - The same 14:02 answer chose "Not now", which reads "timing flakes get named and set aside". The owner also wrote on 2026-10-17: "over 30 minutes on CI every time isn't practical. We will never get anything done."
- So reading "Not now" as also covering risky-branch merges is a reading, but a defensible one. It is labelled, reversible, and points the same way as the owner's latest choices.
- The memory rule "never add gates the owner did not choose" is about adding gates, not loosening them.
- ADR-0107's Consequences already state the residual risk: a real Windows failure that looks like a known flake could get through.

2) "README states it unlabelled; the index says User": not a defect.
- The README never labels provenance anywhere. Searching it for delegat, Claude's, "the owner chose" and owner's finds nothing. It cites ADRs bare, including the Delegated ADR-0109 in the same diff.
- The index uses one label per record, and mixed records like ADR-0105 are marked "User". ADR-0107's index row title is itself limited to D2: "for D2 the owner chose to merge a risky change when ...".

3) "Any other failure is fixed first" drops "report first": partly right, as wording only.
- README:208 says less than ADR-0107 item 4 ("Any other failure stops the merge and is reported"), but the two do not conflict. Both stop the merge until the failure is dealt with.
- The sentence matches the README's existing main-branch rule two paragraphs up: "is a real failure: fix it before merging anything else".
- Reporting to the owner is a rule for Claude, held in ADR-0107 items 2 and 4, which the same sentence cites. The README also serves contributors other than Claude.
- The failure scenario (an agent fixes without reporting) would need the agent to ignore ADR-0107 item 2, which the sentence cites. So the sentence alone does not cause wrong behaviour.
- Aligning the words is cheap.

**Evidence:**

Labelling and review flag:
- docs/adr/0107-code-is-checked-before-it-is-pushed.md line 6 (Decided by: "Delegated to Claude, reversible: ... applying item 4 to risky changes after D2"), line 27 ("Claude's reading of the 'Not now' answer, delegated and reversible, until the owner says otherwise"), line 40 (the D2-only alternative and why it was rejected).
- docs/adr/0103-adr-log-rule-and-ci.md:167, Later note: "with Claude's reading that it holds for later risky changes too, and the README's CI section says so".
- docs/playbook.md:49 (added in this batch): "Review too the choices Claude made inside records the owner decided: ... ADR-0107's item 5 ... and its reading that item 4's merge rule holds after D2."
- docs/adr/README.md:43, index row title: "for D2 the owner chose to merge a risky change when Linux passes and Windows passes or fails only on a named timing flake".

Origin of §3's gate (transcript C:\Users\arvik\.claude\projects\C--Users-arvik-code-byway\67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422.jsonl):
- Claude, 2026-10-17T03:12:51Z: "A middle option. I push the branch first, wait for both machines to pass, then merge ... but only when I choose to wait, so I could use it for risky changes".
- The owner, at 20:17 local: "Yes. Go with that".

Owner choices in docs/adr/evidence/conversation-continued.md:
- 2026-10-17 20:17 and 20:47: answers for the CI-timing branch.
- 2026-10-18 14:02: round 3 "merge to main when Linux passes and Windows passes or fails only on a named timing flake", plus "Not now", whose option read "Keep CI as it is; timing flakes get named and set aside."
- 15:23: "(Windows passes or fails only on a named timing flake). If either finds something, I report first."
- 15:49: "go ahead".
- Line 318: "even if everything passes, over 30 minutes on CI every time isn't practical. We will never get anything done."
- The CI proposal itself (Claude, 2026-10-18T05:21Z) included "Timing-only failures get named and set aside, not chased the same day."

README:
- No provenance labels anywhere.
- README.md:206: "is a real failure: fix it before merging anything else" (on main before this batch).
- README.md:208: "any other failure is fixed first (ADR-0107)", against ADR-0107 line 27: "Any other failure stops the merge and is reported."

**Fix:**

Optional, note-level. In README.md:208, change "any other failure is fixed first" to "any other failure stops the merge and is reported", matching ADR-0107 item 4. No change is needed to ADR-0107's scope: the extension is already labelled as Claude's reading (delegated and reversible) and is flagged for the owner's review in docs/playbook.md:49.

#### REC-4: Decided-by is applied inconsistently: ADR-0100 becomes User-approved on a chosen option, while ADR-0109 omits the owner's explicit choice of B1; ADR-0100's own corrections leave the review queue

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed-partly; lens records; where `docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:6`.*

**What is wrong:**

The same audit relabels ADR-0100 from Delegated to User-approved because the owner chose an option that named it. ADR-0109 stays "Delegated: Claude built backlog batch B1 ... from the coordinator's brief; ... not explicitly approved", though on 2026-10-18 19:17 the owner explicitly chose the option that named B1. The header does not mention that answer. Meanwhile ADR-0100's relabel takes it out of the playbook's delegated-review queue, but most of its 18 corrections were not in the option the owner chose. The playbook's new "Review too the choices Claude made inside records the owner decided" lists ADR-0105 and ADR-0107 but not ADR-0100.

**Evidence:**

conversation-continued.md, 2026-10-18 19:12/19:17: the owner chose "Records + evidence + B1 together (Recommended)", whose description ends "plus B1, the money-path fixes before your first live run. Then check, push, CI." ADR-0100 line 6 now reads "User-approved: ... the owner chose the recommended option \"One cleanup pass, then done\", which named \"a correcting ADR-0100 for the three false sentences in Accepted ADRs (0083, 0088, 0084)\". The corrections it did not name are Claude's". ADR-0100's Decision has 18 items. Items 7-9 and 12-18 correct ADR-0085, 0087, 0089, 0091, 0092, 0094 and 0096, none of which the option named. docs/playbook.md:49's "Review too" sentence names only ADR-0105 and ADR-0107. ADR-0103 §1.1 allows the header change itself: "The header table (its Status row included) and the other sections may change".

**When it goes wrong:**

The owner reads the index and playbook. ADR-0100 now reads as owner-approved and drops out of the Delegated review, so its ten or more Claude-made corrections are never put to the owner. ADR-0109 reads as never put to the owner, although the owner chose to do B1 in this batch. Under the batch's own rule, one of the two labels is wrong.

**Proposed fix:**

In ADR-0109's Decided by, cite the owner's 2026-10-18 19:17 choice of the batch (scope chosen by the owner; describe-first, the 2,000-token planning number and COMPOSED-MEDIUM delegated) and drop "not explicitly approved". Either keep that as Delegated with the citation, or use the label the batch used for ADR-0100, but use one rule for both. Add ADR-0100's corrections that the option did not name (items 7-9 and 12-18 at least) to the playbook's "Review too" list.

**Refuter 1: partly, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding's facts hold, but its main inference does not.

What holds:
(1) ADR-0100's Decided-by row was changed from Delegated to User-approved. The change is in 8401820..481b2e5, from commit dca74a7, and ADR-0100 has a Later note about it.
(2) ADR-0109:6 says "Delegated: Claude built backlog batch B1 ... from the coordinator's brief; none of its items needed an owner decision; not explicitly approved". ADR-0109 never cites the owner's 2026-10-18 19:17 choice of "Records + evidence + B1 together (Recommended)". I grepped ADR-0109 for owner, 19:17, Records + evidence and brief: the only hits are line 6 and an unrelated Context line.
(3) Moving ADR-0100 to User-approved takes it out of the playbook's Delegated queue. The count fell from 38 to 37 on fix/fx-records. The new "Review too the choices Claude made inside records the owner decided" sentence (docs/playbook.md:49) names only ADR-0105 and ADR-0107. ADR-0100 is now exactly that kind of record: its own header says "The corrections it did not name are Claude's". Items 7-9 and 12-18 correct ADR-0085, 0087, 0089, 0091, 0092, 0094 and 0096, and the option did not name any of them. So nothing now puts those corrections to the owner.

What does not hold: the claim that "under the batch's own rule, one of the two labels is wrong". The two owner choices differ in kind.
- On 2026-10-16 one alternative was not to write ADR-0100 at all: "Backlog it all, finish now ... The three false ADR sentences stay in the record until someone writes a correction". So the owner's choice decided that ADR-0100 would exist and named its content (three sentences in 0083, 0088 and 0084). That fits the legend's "User-approved (Claude recommended, the user explicitly chose it)".
- On 2026-10-18 at 19:17, every option contained B1: "Records + evidence + B1 together", "Records + evidence first, B1 after" and "B1 first, records after". The owner chose only how the work was bundled and in what order. B1's actual decisions were not put to the owner: describe-first, the 2,000-token planning number, COMPOSED-MEDIUM's refusal and VOICE in the estimate. So Delegated is a defensible label for ADR-0109.

The real defect on the ADR-0109 side is smaller. The row credits "the coordinator's brief" and says "not explicitly approved" without quoting the owner's explicit choice of B1's timing. That falls short of the owner's quote-verbatim rule. It is not a wrong label.

Related precedent, on main and not changed by this batch: ADR-0106:6 is "User-approved (the owner approved this fix on 2026-10-18 by starting the D2 wave after the investigation's report)". That label rests on the same kind of basis as ADR-0109's (the owner starting a batch or wave), so the log does not apply one rule. This supports the finding's spirit, but it sits outside this batch.

New, concrete, and created by this merge: docs/playbook.md:49 says "Review the 37 Delegated records", but the index at head has 38.
- The count line at docs/adr/README.md:13 says 38 delegated. Counting the rows by their Decided-by column also gives 38 Delegated.
- fix/fx-records lowered the playbook count to 37 when ADR-0100 left. fix/fx-b1's playbook said 38 while its own index said 39. The merge kept records' "37" after B1 added ADR-0109 as Delegated.
- scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts:153-162 checks only the index's count line, so nothing catches the playbook number.

Severity: should-fix, at the low end. All three problems were created in this batch: the playbook queue misses ADR-0100's Claude-made corrections, the playbook count is wrong, and ADR-0109's row does not cite the owner's choice. All are cheap to fix. None is wrong money, and no record misstates the owner's words: "not explicitly approved" is true of B1's contents, which is what the record decides. So it is not blocking.

Checked:
- the git diff of the ADR-0100 header and its Later note;
- ADR-0109's header, Context and Evidence;
- the owner's two choices in conversation-continued.md (lines 119/123 and 409/413) and all their alternatives;
- the index legend and count line;
- the label counts at head and on each source branch;
- playbook line 49 at head and on both branches;
- ADR-0103 rule 1 (it allows header changes);
- ADR-0105, 0106, 0107 and 0108's Decided-by rows;
- what the records test checks.

Not checked:
- the coordinator's brief for B1, which is not in the repository. B1 appears only in ADR-0109:6 and backlog row 65, not in main's backlog, so I cannot verify the row's "from the coordinator's brief" or "none of its items needed an owner decision".
- whether other Delegated records were also put to the owner as bundled batches.

**Evidence:**

- ADR-0100 header change, from `git diff 8401820..481b2e5 -- docs/adr/0100-...md`:
  - before: "-| **Decided by** | Delegated: the owner approved this cleanup pass on 2026-10-16."
  - after: "+| **Decided by** | User-approved: on 2026-10-16 the owner chose the recommended option "One cleanup pass, then done", which named "a correcting ADR-0100 for the three false sentences ..." The corrections it did not name are Claude's".
- docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:6: "Delegated: Claude built backlog batch B1 (...) from the coordinator's brief; none of its items needed an owner decision; not explicitly approved; reversible." No other line in ADR-0109 cites the owner's choice.
- conversation-continued.md:409, the options at 19:12, all of which contain B1: "Records + evidence + B1 together (Recommended)", "Records + evidence first, B1 after", "B1 first, records after". Line 413 is the answer at 19:17, choosing "Records + evidence + B1 together (Recommended)".
- conversation-continued.md:119, the 2026-10-16 alternative: "Backlog it all, finish now ... The three false ADR sentences stay in the record until someone writes a correction." So the 10-16 choice did decide whether ADR-0100 would exist.
- ADR-0100's Decision: items 7-8 correct ADR-0085, 9 corrects 0087, 12 corrects 0089, 13 corrects 0091, 14 corrects 0092, 15 corrects 0094 and 16-18 correct 0096. The option named none of them.
- docs/playbook.md:49: "Review the 37 Delegated records ... Review too the choices Claude made inside records the owner decided: ADR-0105's ... and ADR-0107's item 5". ADR-0100 is not named.
- Counts at head, by Decided-by column of the index rows: 38 Delegated, 29 User-approved, 20 User, 15 By continuation, 7 Pending. docs/adr/README.md:13 says "109 records: 102 accepted (15 ...; 38 delegated)".
- Counts on the branches:
  - fix/fx-records: playbook "Review the 37 Delegated", index "37 delegated".
  - fix/fx-b1: playbook "Review the 38", index "39 delegated".
  - Head: playbook 37, index 38, so the playbook is off by one.
- scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts:153-162 regex-checks only the index count line; nothing checks the playbook.
- ADR-0106:6, on main: "User-approved (the owner approved this fix on 2026-10-18 by starting the D2 wave after the investigation's report)". This is the same kind of basis that ADR-0109 calls "not explicitly approved".

**Fix:**

1. ADR-0109's Decided by row: keep Delegated, because the owner's 19:17 choice set when B1 lands, not its contents. Replace "from the coordinator's brief; ... not explicitly approved" with the owner's choice quoted word for word, and say what was delegated. For example: "Delegated: on 2026-10-18 the owner chose "Records + evidence + B1 together (Recommended)" ("plus B1, the money-path fixes before your first live run"); every option included B1, so the choice set only its timing. What B1 decided (describe-first, the 2,000-token planning number, COMPOSED-MEDIUM's refusal, VOICE in the estimate) is Claude's, not put to the owner; reversible." ADR-0109 is new in this batch, so the row can be edited freely.
2. docs/playbook.md:49: add ADR-0100's corrections that the 2026-10-16 option did not name (items 7-9 and 12-18 at least) to the "Review too" sentence, and add ADR-0100 to the Source column.
3. docs/playbook.md:49: change "Review the 37 Delegated records" to 38, to match docs/adr/README.md:13. Optionally, have scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts check that the playbook's number matches the index's count of Delegated records.

**Refuter 2: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I tried to refute REC-4 by finding a recorded rule or owner choice that would explain treating ADR-0100 and ADR-0109 differently. I found none, and the log's own precedents count against ADR-0109.

1. The legend and the precedents back the finding. docs/adr/README.md:8 defines **User-approved** as "Claude recommended, the user explicitly chose it". Two records already on main apply that label to a choice of a whole batch:
   - ADR-0104 reads "User-approved (the owner chose \"fix all\" on 2026-10-17 from the combined check's findings, this change among them)".
   - ADR-0106 reads "User-approved (the owner approved this fix on 2026-10-18 by starting the D2 wave after the investigation's report)". An earlier check, now in the batch's evidence, found that honest because the option offered "starting D2 (which now includes this fix)".
   - The batch relabels ADR-0100 on the same reasoning. Its Later note says "by the index's legend an option the owner explicitly chose is User-approved".

   B1 matches those cases. On 2026-10-18 at 19:12 Claude recommended "Records + evidence + B1 together (Recommended)", whose description ends "plus B1, the money-path fixes before your first live run". The owner chose it at 19:17. ADR-0109 still says "Delegated: … from the coordinator's brief; none of its items needed an owner decision; not explicitly approved". It does not cite that answer anywhere, neither in the header, the Context nor the Evidence. ADR-0107 says nothing that would support a different rule for builder batches.

   The narrow reading is defensible: the specific designs (asking Temporal before marking a failed start, the 2,000-token planning number, COMPOSED-MEDIUM's refusal, the wording kept for code 14) were never put to the owner. That justifies a split header like ADR-0105's or ADR-0107's ("User: … Delegated to Claude, reversible: …"). It does not justify leaving out the owner's explicit choice of B1 or saying "not explicitly approved" without qualification. The provenance is understated rather than overstated: no owner choice or condition is invented, and the record stays in the review queue. So this is should-fix, not blocking.

2. The relabel of ADR-0100 is consistent with the legend, and its header is honest: "The corrections it did not name are Claude's". The finding's item numbers are accurate:
   - Items 7-8 correct ADR-0085, 9 corrects ADR-0087, 12 ADR-0089, 13 ADR-0091, 14 ADR-0092, 15 ADR-0094, and 16-18 ADR-0096. The option named none of those records.
   - Even for the three records it did name (0083, 0084, 0088) there are 8 items, against the option's "three false sentences".

   The relabel takes ADR-0100 out of the Delegated count. The playbook's new "Review too the choices Claude made inside records the owner decided" (docs/playbook.md:49) lists only ADR-0105 and ADR-0107, so ADR-0100's Claude-made corrections are not in the owner's review list anywhere. On its own this sub-point is the weakest part of the finding. Those corrections are factual statements checked against the code, not design choices, and ADR-0100's header already marks them as Claude's.

3. New evidence: the review-queue row itself is now stale. dca74a7 (records branch) changed the playbook to "Review the 37 Delegated records" when the index had 37 Delegated rows. c6bdc8b then merged B1, which added ADR-0109 as Delegated. The index count line at head reads "38 delegated" and has 38 "| Delegated |" rows. docs/playbook.md:49 still says 37. Neither 481b2e5 nor any later commit touched the playbook, and no test checks that number (the only playbook test in scripts/test/ci.test.ts checks another row). So at head the owner's review list disagrees with the index whichever label is right.

**Evidence:**

- **ADR-0109 header (docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:6):** "Delegated: Claude built backlog batch B1 (\"Before the first live run: honest money path\") from the coordinator's brief; none of its items needed an owner decision; not explicitly approved; reversible." Searching the 95-line file for owner/chose/19:17 finds no citation of the owner's choice.
- **The owner's choice of B1 (docs/adr/evidence/conversation-continued.md):**
  - Line 409 (CLAUDE-ASKS, 2026-10-18 19:12): option "Records + evidence + B1 together (Recommended)", whose description ends "plus B1, the money-path fixes before your first live run. Then check, push, CI."
  - Line 413 (USER, 19:17:38): "=\"Records + evidence + B1 together (Recommended)\"".
- **The ADR-0100 choice (same file):**
  - Line 119: the option "One cleanup pass, then done (Recommended)" names "a correcting ADR-0100 for the three false sentences in Accepted ADRs (0083, 0088, 0084)".
  - Line 123: the owner chose it.
- **Legend (docs/adr/README.md:8):** "User-approved (Claude recommended, the user explicitly chose it)".
- **Precedents on main:**
  - ADR-0104:6 "User-approved (the owner chose \"fix all\" on 2026-10-17 from the combined check's findings, this change among them)", matching conversation-continued.md:266 "Fix all, bounded recheck, push (Recommended)".
  - ADR-0106:6 "User-approved (the owner approved this fix on 2026-10-18 by starting the D2 wave after the investigation's report)". The D2 check report in evidence says: "ADR-0106's \"User-approved\" is honest: the 07:42Z report offered \"starting D2 (which now includes this fix)\"".
- **ADR-0100's Later note added in the batch:** "by the index's legend an option the owner explicitly chose is User-approved. The row now says so, and marks the corrections that option did not name as Claude's."
- **docs/playbook.md:49 at head:** "Review the 37 Delegated records … Review too the choices Claude made inside records the owner decided: ADR-0105's … and ADR-0107's item 5 …". ADR-0100 is not named.
- **Delegated counts across the batch** (counting "| Delegated |" rows in the index, the index count line, and the playbook's number):

  | Commit | Index rows | Count line | Playbook |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | main 8401820 | 38 | 38 | 38 |
  | dca74a7, af3bf5d, 8b16f18 | 37 | 37 | 37 |
  | c6bdc8b, 481b2e5 | 38 | "38 delegated" | still 37 |

  `git log 8401820..481b2e5 -- docs/playbook.md` lists only dca74a7. `git grep` finds no test of that count (scripts/test/ci.test.ts reads only the "Public record for the charter" row).

**Checked:** both option texts and answers in the evidence transcript; the Decided-by rows of ADR-0100 to 0109; the index legend, rows and count line; how the Delegated count and playbook row changed across every batch commit; the Decision items and target ADRs of ADR-0100; ADR-0109's whole Evidence section; ADR-0001, 0103 and 0107 for any other labelling rule (none).

**Not checked:** whether the owner saw B1's item list before 19:17. The B1 batch definition and backlog visualisation were in a Temp artifact that is not in the repository; the option's naming of "B2" and "B1" suggests the owner was shown them.

**Fix:**

1. ADR-0109's Decided by (new in this batch, so freely editable): cite the owner's 2026-10-18 19:17 choice of "Records + evidence + B1 together (Recommended)" ("plus B1, the money-path fixes before your first live run") and drop "not explicitly approved". Use the split form of ADR-0105 and ADR-0107: "User-approved: the owner chose …; Delegated to Claude, reversible: …". The delegated part names asking Temporal before marking a failed start failed, the 2,000-token planning number per image, COMPOSED-MEDIUM's refusal, and keeping "Nothing was spent" for code 14 and reset connections. Update ADR-0109's index row label and the count line (38 to 37 delegated), which adr-records.test checks. Alternatively keep Delegated but cite the choice; either way, apply one rule to ADR-0100 and ADR-0109.
2. docs/playbook.md:49: make the Delegated count match the index (37 after relabelling ADR-0109, otherwise 38). Add to "Review too" ADR-0109's delegated choices (if relabelled) and ADR-0100's corrections that the chosen option did not name (at least items 7-9 and 12-18).

#### XDOC-1: First-live-run row: its steps lead to a run that cannot produce the data it promises, and it leaves out a row that is due first

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed-partly; lens backlog; where `docs/backlog.md:155`.*

**What is wrong:**

The row says "After D1, D2 and D4 nothing in the code blocks it" and lists only the C5 and C2 holds. But the asset.integrate row (line 85), whose trigger was added in this same batch, is due "before the first live run with an image key". The row's step, "start one Balanced run estimated under $1", in practice allows only C4, and C4 makes no image.
