Yet the row says this run settles or feeds "Gemini image thinking tokens" and "how often C3's header is cut or gets advice". The Gemini row (line 65) also expects images from "that run". The steps also leave out the playbook's key rotation, which is due before the first live run (playbook.md:50).

**Evidence:**

backlog.md:85: "Fix it, or record that it stays, before the first live run with an image key (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)". The Balanced estimates at 481b2e5 (estimateRecipe with all keys, my tsx script) are: c1 $1.08–5.92, c2 0.37–1.03, c3 0.27–1.62, c4 0.07–0.46, c6 0.10–2.94, c6a 0.25–3.31, c7 0.30–1.50. RecommendationView.tsx:443 shows the estimate as a range (formatCostRange). C4 has 0 asset steps (walk over catalog c4). C1, C2 and C3 each have 1.

**When it goes wrong:**

The owner follows the row and starts the only Balanced run whose range is under $1, which is C4. No Gemini image is bought and no C3 header is made, so the rows the run is meant to settle stay open. If the owner instead picks C3 or C1 to get images, the run goes ahead while the asset.integrate row, due before any live run with an image key, is still open. The fix-or-record step for losing paid images is then skipped.

**Proposed fix:**

Name the configuration or configurations for the first run, for example C4 first and then a Balanced C3 for images. Add the asset.integrate row to the rows that must be settled before an image run, or change that row's trigger. Point to the playbook's "Protect the product's provider keys" row, which includes the rotation.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

Every concrete claim reproduces at 481b2e5.

(1) Estimates. I re-ran estimateRecipe on the Balanced profile, dated 2026-10-21, for all eight configurations. The numbers match the finding exactly. With every key present, C4 is the only configuration whose whole range is under $1 (0.07–0.46). C2 is 0.37–1.03, C3 0.27–1.62 and C7 0.30–1.50. Leaving out keys does not change this: without an OpenAI key, C2 is 0.37–1.03, C3 0.24–1.06 and C7 0.26–1.01. Without an image key, C2 drops to 0.37–0.88, but then it makes no images. The web UI shows the estimate as a range (RecommendationView.tsx:443, formatCostRange). The Library lets a person start any configuration (apps/web/app/library/page.tsx:64, `/?recipe=${p.recipeId}`), so C4 is reachable. C4 has no ASSET step (c4.ts:69 excludes ASSET), so a C4 run buys no Gemini image and makes no C3 header.

(2) The asset.integrate trigger was added in this batch (commit 489483e): "Fix it, or record that it stays, before the first live run with an image key (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)". 481b2e5 then rewrote the First-live-run row, adding the C5 holds by name. It kept "After D1, D2 and D4 nothing in the code blocks it" and did not list the asset.integrate row. The trigger says "with an image key", not "making images". The row tells the owner to set limits at Google and put the keys in .env.agents, and Balanced's image model is Google's gemini-nano-banana-2.1. So the owner's first live run is "a live run with an image key" whatever configuration is chosen, and that row is due before it. A C1 or C3 run (web-page or prose medium) goes through the integrate path in activities/asset.ts lines 168–196, where the "Place every image" miss is.

(3) Other rows assume the first run makes Gemini images. The Gemini row (backlog.md:65, rewritten in this batch) says "Check both against the first live run's ledger ... (that run's Balanced images come from this model)". ADR-0109's Consequences (line 66) say "the first live run's ledger shows it". A C4 first run shows neither.

(4) Key rotation. playbook.md:50, "Protect the product's provider keys before the first live run", includes rotating keys the dev server held on 2026-10-07. The backlog row says only "set a spending limit at each provider first" and does not point to that playbook row. README step 2 (edited in this batch) does point to it.

What weakens parts of the finding:
- The "settles or feeds" list and "estimated under $1" were already on main at 8401820. The list also names fal's live media host set, which only a C5 run can settle while the same row leaves C5 out, so the list was never a promise about that one run alone. That makes "the data it promises" weaker for the row itself. It still holds for row 65 and ADR-0109, which were written in this batch.
- The rotation omission is also on main and is partly covered by the README's pointer, so on its own it is a note.

The asset.integrate contradiction is new in this batch: both rows changed here, and the row the owner follows next contradicts a due row. It is a one-sentence documentation fix. It is not wrong money (one lost Balanced image costs cents) and not a false record of the owner's words. So should-fix, not blocking.

**Evidence:**

Estimates: I ran `node_modules/.bin/tsx C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/xdoc1/est.mts` from C:/Users/arvik/code/byway/.worktrees/fx-batch. It calls estimateRecipe with Balanced from packages/gateway/src/profiles.ts, at 2026-10-21. With images, video and otherFamily all true: c1 1.08–5.92 (images 0–12), c2 0.37–1.03 (0–3), c3 0.27–1.62 (0–1), c4 0.07–0.46 (0–0), c5 3.07–27.03, c6 0.10–2.94, c6a 0.25–3.31, c7 0.30–1.50. With images true and the other two false: c2 0.37–1.03, c3 0.24–1.06, c7 0.26–1.01. With no keys: c2 0.37–0.88 and c3 0.24–1.01, both with 0 images.

Catalog: c4.ts:28 `medium: 'prose'`; c4.ts:69 `{ block: 'ASSET', reason: 'Pure text...' }`; c1.ts:44, c2.ts:62 and c3.ts:54 each have one optional asset(). asset.ts:168 `if (medium === 'web-page' || medium === 'prose')` leads to the helper.asset.integrate call, whose parse adds "Place every image by its address; missing: ...". C2 ('options') takes the attach path.

Batch history: `git show 489483e -- docs/backlog.md` adds the asset.integrate trigger "before the first live run with an image key (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)". `git show 481b2e5 -- docs/backlog.md` rewrites the First-live-run row: it lists the C5 holds and keeps "After D1, D2 and D4 nothing in the code blocks it", with no asset.integrate. Main's row (`git show 8401820:docs/backlog.md`, line 147) already had "start one Balanced run estimated under $1" and the same "settles or feeds" list, including fal's host set and C3's header.

Row 65 at head: "Check both against the first live run's ledger and Google's usage records (that run's Balanced images come from this model)". ADR-0109 line 66: "the first live run's ledger shows it (backlog \"Gemini image thinking tokens\")".

playbook.md:50 (unchanged from main): "...and rotate any real key byway's dev server had in its environment on 2026-10-07... | User | Before the first live run with real keys". Backlog row 155: "set a spending limit at each provider first", with no rotation and no pointer to the playbook. README.md:23 does point to the playbook row.

UI: RecommendationView.tsx:443 `{formatCostRange(estimate.lowUsd, estimate.highUsd)}`; Library page.tsx:64 links `/?recipe=${p.recipeId}`.

**Fix:**

Edit docs/backlog.md row 155 ("First live run with real keys and real money") only. No change to the Context or Decision of any Accepted record is needed.
(a) Say which runs feed which rows, labelled as Claude's proposal. For example: a Balanced C4 run (estimate $0.07–$0.46) checks the money path. Gemini image thinking tokens and C3's trims and advice need a Balanced C3 run with the header image (estimate $0.27–$1.62). Alternatively, drop "estimated under $1" in favour of "with a small run budget". Then make row 65's "(that run's Balanced images ...)" and ADR-0109's Consequences line 66 refer to "the first live run that makes a Balanced image".
(b) Add the asset.integrate row to the rows that must be settled first. Note that it applies to any live run with an image key in .env.agents. Alternatively, narrow that row's trigger to "before the first live run that places images on a web page or prose (C1, C3)", still labelled as Claude's trigger.
(c) Replace "set a spending limit at each provider first" with a pointer to the playbook's "Protect the product's provider keys before the first live run" row, which includes the key rotation.

**Refuter 2: partly, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

Most of the finding holds up. One part is overstated, and one part was already on main before this batch.

1. CONFIRMED: row 155 leaves out row 85, and the two rows now contradict each other. Commit 489483e (the records branch) gave row 85 the trigger "before the first live run with an image key". Integration commit 481b2e5 then rewrote row 155 to list what holds back C5 and C2. It still says "After D1, D2 and D4 nothing in the code blocks it" and does not mention row 85. The README added in this batch tells the owner to put GEMINI_API_KEY in .env.agents. Balanced takes its images from google gemini-nano-banana-2.1. So the owner's first live run will almost certainly be a "live run with an image key", and row 155 says nothing blocks it. Neither the owner's words nor any ADR settles this. The only owner input is the question "anything that will block us on that after D1 and D4 are done?" (conversation-continued.md:228), and row 85's trigger is labelled as Claude's. So this is an internal contradiction in Claude's records, not a choice the owner made.
   Two refinements. First, the defect behind row 85 can fire only in C1 (web-page) and C3 (prose). asset.integrate runs only for those media; C2's options are attached without it. So row 85's trigger is wider than the defect. Second, it fires only after the model fails to place every image twice (askModel retries once), and the images are a few cents each. That is why this is should-fix and not blocking.

2. CONFIRMED (I recomputed the numbers): the "estimated under $1" step gives no image run. I ran estimateRecipe with the Balanced profile on 2026-10-21. With all keys, only C4's whole range is under $1 ($0.07–0.46), and C4 has no ASSET step. Without an image key, C2 also comes in under $1 ($0.37–0.88), but then no image can be made at all. With the image key but no OpenAI key, C2 is $0.37–1.03, C3 $0.24–1.06 and C7 $0.26–1.01. So read strictly, as the whole range shown by formatCostRange, no Balanced run under $1 makes a Gemini image in any key set. Yet row 65 (rewritten in 481b2e5) says to check the thinking-token planning number "against the first live run's ledger ... (that run's Balanced images come from this model)". ADR-0109 lines 50 and 66 assume the same. Those checks cannot be made from the run row 155 prescribes.

3. OVERSTATED: "promises data it cannot produce". The list after "It settles or feeds" was already on main. It is plainly not a promise for one run: it names fal's live media host set, but the same row holds C5 back. Row 138 (C3 header) already limits itself to "the first live C3 runs". For C3 this is loose wording, not a defect. The Gemini row and ADR-0109 are where the assumption is actually wrong.

4. OLDER THAN THIS BATCH, note level: key rotation. Main's row 155 already said only "set a spending limit at each provider first". The playbook row "Protect the product's provider keys before the first live run" (playbook.md:50) holds the rotation and is the owner's own action item. The README added in this batch (step 2) points to that playbook row, but for the spending limit only. The gap is real, but it was not introduced here. A pointer to the playbook row fixes it.

Overall: part 1 and the Gemini side of part 2 are real record defects introduced by this batch, and cheap to fix inside it. They do not endanger the push on their own: no code is wrong and only cents are at risk.

**Evidence:**

- docs/backlog.md:155 (481b2e5): "After D1, D2 and D4 nothing in the code blocks it ... start one Balanced run estimated under $1 ... Leave C5, and C2 on Best or OpenAI, until their rows are settled ... It settles or feeds: fal's live media host set and Gemini image thinking tokens (their rows); how often C3's header is cut or gets advice". Row 85 is not named.
- docs/backlog.md:85, trigger added in 489483e: "Fix it, or record that it stays, before the first live run with an image key (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)".
- docs/backlog.md:65 (481b2e5): "Check both against the first live run's ledger and Google's usage records (that run's Balanced images come from this model)". ADR-0109:66 says "the first live run's ledger shows it".
- docs/backlog.md:138 says "Measure it from the first live C3 runs", so it is already scoped to C3 runs.
- `git log -S`: "before the first live run with an image key" came in 489483e; "C5 waits on the rows" and "that run's Balanced images come from this model" came in 481b2e5. Main (8401820) already had "estimated under $1", the "settles or feeds" list, and no mention of rotation.
- My tsx run of estimateRecipe on the Balanced profile (at 2026-10-21, video on), images/otherFamily on/off:
  - c1: $1.08–5.92 (images 0–12) / $1.08–5.33 with no image key
  - c2: $0.37–1.03 (0–3 images) / $0.37–0.88 with no image key
  - c3: $0.27–1.62 / $0.24–1.06 with the image key but no OpenAI key / $0.24–1.01 with neither
  - c4: $0.07–0.46, 0 images in every key set
  - c6: $0.10–2.94
  - c6a: $0.25–3.31
  - c7: $0.30–1.50 / $0.26–1.01 with no OpenAI key
- apps/web/src/view/format.ts:15-22: formatCostRange shows "low–high".
- ASSET steps found by walking the catalog: c1 web-page 1, c2 options 1 (mood-boards), c3 prose 1, c4 0, c5 2, c6/c6a/c7 0.
- packages/runner/src/activities/asset.ts:168-192: the integrate path, with the "Place every image" check, runs only when medium is web-page or prose; other media take the attach branch.
- packages/runner/src/activities/call.ts: askModel tries twice ("attempt < 2") before ContractMissed.
- README.md:22-23 tells users to make keys for byway alone and to set a spending limit "(the playbook's "Protect the product's provider keys")". README.md:36 lists GEMINI_API_KEY in the example .env.agents.
- docs/playbook.md:50 has the rotation of keys in the dev server's environment on 2026-10-07, due "Before the first live run with real keys". It already existed on main.
- conversation-continued.md:228, the owner: "So anything that will block us on that after D1 and D4 are done?" The owner chose no configuration or dollar figure for the first run; "$1" and the procedure are Claude's.

**Fix:**

These are edits to records only; no code changes.
1. Narrow row 85's trigger to where the defect can fire: "before the first live C1 or C3 run with an image key (asset.integrate runs only for web-page and prose; C2's options are attached without it)". Keep the "(trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)" label.
2. In row 155, add this to the list of holds: "C1 and C3 with an image key wait on the asset.integrate row". Then soften "nothing in the code blocks it" so it does not contradict that hold.
3. Make the run step concrete. For example: "with all keys only C4's Balanced estimate is wholly under $1 ($0.07–0.46), and it makes no image; the Gemini image row and the C3 header row need a later Balanced C3 run with Yes to the header image".
4. Change row 65 to "the first live run that makes a Balanced image" instead of "the first live run". ADR-0109's wording sits in Alternatives and Consequences, not in Context or Decision, so a Later note or nothing is enough there.
5. Replace "set a spending limit at each provider first" in row 155 with a pointer to the playbook row "Protect the product's provider keys before the first live run". That row covers both the limit and the rotation.

#### XDOC-2: Status page F18 still lists the closed secrets-file-names row as remaining

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed; lens backlog; where `docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:30`.*

**What is wrong:**

F18's "What remains" still lists "secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6)". B1 closed that row (ADR-0109 item 4), and the backlog no longer has it. F18's Records column does not cite 0109. The integration commit updated only F01 and F03.

**Evidence:**

The backlog row "Secrets-file names independent of module identity" was removed. ADR-0109 item 4 says "ADR-0084's sentences on the renderer's environment hold on every start again". README.md:142 and spec §2 dropped the exception. The message of 481b2e5 reads: "The status page's F01 and F03 rows".

**When it goes wrong:**

A reader of the status page concludes that Chromium still receives names from .env.agents on a lowercase-drive start, and looks for a backlog row that does not exist.

**Proposed fix:**

Remove that item from F18's What remains, or mark it fixed by ADR-0109, and add 0109 to F18's Records.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding holds as stated. At head 481b2e5, line 30 of the status page (F18) still lists "secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6)" among its "Rows", and its Records column does not cite 0109. Line 5 of the same page says "The residuals it names are [backlog](../backlog.md) rows, each with what brings it back", so this entry points to a backlog row that no longer exists.

How it happened: fix/fx-records (31bdb62) rewrote the F18 row and added this residual while the backlog row was still open. fix/fx-b1 fixed the defect but left its backlog edit as a hand-off: `git show fix/fx-b1:docs/backlog.md` still counts 1 match for "module identity". The integration commit 481b2e5 then removed the backlog row ("Secrets-file names independent of module identity", shown at -line 184 of `git diff fix/fx-b1..481b2e5 -- docs/backlog.md`). It updated F01 and F03 on the status page but not F18. Its message says only "The status page's F01 and F03 rows". F03 was handled the right way: it cites [0109] and says "ADR-0109 fixed the other". F18 was missed.

The fix is real on the branch. packages/gateway/src/env.ts:101-102 keeps the map under Symbol.for('byway.gateway.env-file-names') on globalThis, and scripts/test/renderer-env.test.ts and its fixture renderer-env-twice.ts exist. Every other document now calls the defect fixed:
- ADR-0109 item 4 (line 41)
- ADR-0100's Later note of 2026-10-18 (line 96): "Item 6's defect is fixed"
- README.md:142
- the spec's line 42

So only the status page contradicts the records. No test reads the status page (grep for "review-fix-status" in scripts, packages and apps finds nothing), so nothing would catch this.

On severity: this is a real, batch-introduced inconsistency in a page that claims to be the traced status, and it sends readers to a row that does not exist. It is not wrong money or behaviour, it does not misquote the owner, and it breaks no ADR log rule. So should-fix, not blocking.

**Evidence:**

- docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:30 (F18), at head 481b2e5: "...Rows: what D2 left; what the late-close fix left; secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6); the network service at Medium integrity;..." Its Records column is "[0084], 0105, [0106], 0100, 0090, [0103]", with no 0109.
- Same file, line 5: "The residuals it names are [backlog](../backlog.md) rows, each with what brings it back."
- Same file, F03 row (line 15): cites [0109] and says "(ADR-0109 fixed the other: a failed start now asks Temporal first)". So the integration did update F03.
- `grep -n -i "lowercase|module identity|env-file" docs/backlog.md` at head finds nothing.
- `git diff fix/fx-b1..481b2e5 -- docs/backlog.md` shows the line "-| Secrets-file names independent of module identity | loadEnvFile keeps the .env.agents names in a module-level map. ...". `git show fix/fx-b1:docs/backlog.md | grep -c "module identity"` returns 1. So 481b2e5 removed the row.
- `git log -1 --format=%B 481b2e5` says: "- The status page's F01 and F03 rows, the ADR index's evidence pointer, and three test comments that named closed rows."
- `git log --oneline 8401820..481b2e5 -- docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md` lists 481b2e5, e6fc542 and 31bdb62. 31bdb62 (records) introduced the F18 residual text.
- docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:41: "ADR-0084's sentences on the renderer's environment hold on every start again, whatever the drive letter's case."
- docs/adr/0100-corrections-found-by-the-final-re-check.md:96: "Item 6's defect is fixed".
- packages/gateway/src/env.ts:101: `const LISTED_BY_FILE = Symbol.for('byway.gateway.env-file-names');`
- No test in scripts, packages or apps references review-fix-status.

**Fix:**

In docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md line 30 (F18):
1. Delete "secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6); " from "What remains". Optionally add a clause in the F03 style, such as "(ADR-0109 fixed the secrets-file names on a lowercase-drive start)".
2. Add "[0109](../adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md)" to F18's Records column.

**Refuter 2: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I tried to refute it and could not. Every part of the finding is accurate.

1. The F18 text at line 30 of docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md comes from the records branch (commit 31bdb62, the trace of 2026-10-18 against main at 8401820). That branch was cut before B1. The integration commit 481b2e5 changed only the title line and the F01 and F03 rows. Its message says so: "The status page's F01 and F03 rows". F03 got [0109] and a sentence about what ADR-0109 fixed. F18 got neither, so it still lists "secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6)" as a remaining row.

2. B1 fixed that defect, and the records say so in four places:
   - ADR-0109 item 4 keeps the names on globalThis under Symbol.for('byway.gateway.env-file-names'), and says ADR-0084's sentences "hold on every start again, whatever the drive letter's case".
   - ADR-0100 has a later note dated 2026-10-18: "Item 6's defect is fixed".
   - README.md:142 and spec line 42 now say the environment is clean on a lowercase-drive start too.
   - The ADR index row for ADR-0084 (README.md:131) now reads "changed by ADR-0100, ADR-0105, ADR-0109".

   F18's Records column cites 0084, 0105, 0106, 0100, 0090 and 0103. It does not cite 0109, even though 0109 changes 0084, the record F18 rests on.

3. The backlog row "Secrets-file names independent of module identity" is on main at 8401820 (line 126) and in the records branch, and it is gone at head. I checked F18's other named rows and all still exist in docs/backlog.md:
   - "What D2 left (ADR-0105)" (line 174)
   - "What the late-close fix left (ADR-0106)" (line 175)
   - "Windows: Chromium's network service runs at Medium integrity" (line 57)
   - "Renderer egress: narrow the CDN allowlist" (line 55)
   - "Renderer environment as an allowlist" (line 58)
   - "Playwright bump policy" (line 56)
   - "Protocol runbook's RENDER snippet" (line 59)

   So F18 now names exactly one row that does not exist. That breaks the page's own claim: "The residuals it names are [backlog] rows, each with what brings it back."

4. Nothing excuses the stale entry. It is not a limit the owner accepted, and no record keeps it open with a trigger. ADR-0109 closes it outright, and the backlog row's own exit condition ("add a lowercase-drive test, then drop the README and spec exception") was met by scripts/test/renderer-env.test.ts and the README and spec edits. No test reads the status page: grep finds no reference to review-fix-status under scripts, packages or apps. So nothing would have caught the omission.

Severity: should-fix, not blocking. The error is conservative: it says a fixed defect remains, not that an open one is fixed. It touches no money, behaviour, secret or owner quote, and the page is not an ADR, so the ADR-0103 log rule does not apply. But this page was rewritten on 2026-10-18 because it "said more than main holds". The same commit updated F03 for ADR-0109 and left F18 inconsistent. A reader would hunt for a backlog row that is gone and think Chromium still gets the file's names. It is a one-line fix and belongs in this batch.

**Evidence:**

- docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:30 (head 481b2e5), F18 "What remains": "Rows: what D2 left; what the late-close fix left; secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6); the network service at Medium integrity; ..." Its Records column is "[0084], 0105, [0106], 0100, 0090, [0103]", with no 0109.
- `git show 481b2e5 -- docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md` changes only the title line and the F01 and F03 rows. F03 gains "[0109](../adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md)" and "(ADR-0109 fixed the other: a failed start now asks Temporal first)".
- `git log 8401820..481b2e5 -- docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md` lists 481b2e5, e6fc542 and 31bdb62. The F18 text was written in 31bdb62, before B1.
- The backlog row is on main (`git show 8401820:docs/backlog.md`, line 126: "| Secrets-file names independent of module identity | ... then drop the README and spec exception |") and in af3bf5d (line 136). At head, `grep -i 'lowercase|module identity' docs/backlog.md` finds nothing.
- docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:41: "ADR-0084's sentences on the renderer's environment hold on every start again, whatever the drive letter's case."
- docs/adr/0100-corrections-found-by-the-final-re-check.md:96: "2026-10-18, ADR-0109. Item 6's defect is fixed".
- docs/adr/README.md:131: ADR-0084 is "Accepted; changed by ADR-0100, ADR-0105, ADR-0109".
- README.md:142 now says the environment is clean "on Windows also when byway is started from a current directory whose drive letter is lowercase".
- No test in scripts, packages or apps reads review-fix-status (grep found none).

**Fix:**

In docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md, F18 row (line 30):
- Records column: add "[0109](../adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md)".
- "What remains": delete "secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6); ". Optionally add a clause after the Rows list, in F03's style: "ADR-0109 closed the secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6)."

#### XDOC-3: Playbook says 37 Delegated records; the ADR index counts 38

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed; lens backlog; where `docs/playbook.md:49`.*

**What is wrong:**

The records branch changed the count from 38 to 37 when ADR-0100 became User-approved. B1 then added ADR-0109, decided by "Delegated". The integration commit did not reconcile the two, so the playbook's review item undercounts the records the owner is asked to review.

**Evidence:**

playbook.md:49: "**Review the 37 Delegated records**". docs/adr/README.md:13: "**109 records:** 102 accepted (15 of them by continuation, awaiting ratification; 38 delegated)". README.md:147 lists ADR-0109 as Delegated. `grep -c "| Delegated" docs/adr/README.md` returns 38. No test ties the playbook to the index.

**When it goes wrong:**

The owner reviews 37 Delegated records and misses one, likely ADR-0109, the newest.

**Proposed fix:**

Change the count to 38. Optionally name ADR-0109 among the records to review, since it holds Claude's judgement calls, such as the code-14/reset wording.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding reproduces at head 481b2e5. The playbook says 37 Delegated records. The ADR index has 38 rows whose Decided-by cell is exactly "Delegated", all of them Accepted, and its own count line says 38. Each step of the drift is visible in the history:
- main 8401820: the playbook and the index both said 38, which was consistent then.
- fix/fx-records af3bf5d: ADR-0100 moved from Delegated to User-approved, and both files went to 37, which was correct on that branch.
- fix/fx-b1 88853f5: ADR-0109 was added as Delegated and the index went to 39, but B1 left the playbook at 38. B1 was already inconsistent on its own branch.
- The merge c6bdc8b set the index to 38 and kept the records branch's playbook at 37. The integration commit 481b2e5 does not touch that row.

No test ties the playbook to the index. adr-records.test.ts ("the count line above the tables counts the index rows") checks only the index's count line against its rows. The only playbook check, in ci.test.ts, covers the "Public record for the charter" row.

ADR-0109 really is Delegated. Its own table says: "Delegated: Claude built backlog batch B1 ... not explicitly approved; reversible". So it belongs in the owner's review list.

The finding overstates one thing: the "owner misses ADR-0109" scenario. The playbook row lists no records beyond its six starting ones. It names the "ADR index" as its source, and the index marks all 38 rows. So the more likely effect is a mismatch that confuses the owner, not a silently skipped record.

It is still a false statement in the owner's to-do list, introduced by this batch's merge, and fixing it is a one-character change. That fits should-fix, at its low end. It does not block the push.

**Evidence:**

All checks were read-only, in C:/Users/arvik/code/byway/.worktrees/fx-batch at HEAD 481b2e5db383816976c6da7d1a0b8897fa2ab0eb.

- docs/playbook.md:49: "| **Review the 37 Delegated records** (decided by Claude under authority the owner delegated; ...) | ... | ADR index, ADR-0105 ..., ADR-0107 |"
- docs/adr/README.md:13: "**109 records:** 102 accepted (15 of them by continuation, awaiting ratification; 38 delegated) and 7 proposed."
- An awk parse of the index's Decided-by column found 24 "Delegated / Accepted; changed by" rows and 14 "Delegated / Accepted" rows: 38 in total, none Proposed. They are ADR-0063 to ADR-0099 (37 records) plus ADR-0109.
- docs/adr/README.md:147: "| [ADR-0109](...) | The money path before the first live run ... | Accepted | Delegated | 2026-10-18 21:30 |"
- docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:6: "| **Decided by** | Delegated: Claude built backlog batch B1 ... not explicitly approved; reversible. |"
- The count by commit, as playbook / index count line / rows with "| Delegated":
  - 8401820: 38 / 38 / 38
  - af3bf5d: 37 / 37 / 37
  - f77cc6f: 38 / 38 / 38
  - 88853f5: 38 / 39 / 39 (B1 did not update the playbook)
  - 8b16f18: 37 / 37 / 37
  - 0b9dbcc: 37 / 37 / 37
  - c6bdc8b: 37 / 38 / 38
  - 481b2e5: 37 / 38 / 38
- `git diff 8401820..af3bf5d -- docs/adr/README.md` shows ADR-0100's row going from "| Delegated |" to "| User-approved |".
- The only test on this count is scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts:152-165, which compares the index count line with the index rows. The only playbook test, scripts/test/ci.test.ts:330-342, reads the "Public record for the charter" row. Nothing in scripts/test compares the playbook's Delegated count with the index.

**Fix:**

In docs/playbook.md:49, change "**Review the 37 Delegated records**" to "**Review the 38 Delegated records**". Optionally name ADR-0109, the newest Delegated record (B1's money path, 2026-10-18), alongside the starting set, so the owner sees it was added after the 2026-10-08 hand-back.

**Refuter 2: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

XDOC-3 is right, and every claim in it holds. On main (8401820), ADR-0100 was Delegated, the index counted 38 delegated and the playbook said "Review the 38 Delegated records". fix/fx-records made ADR-0100 User-approved and correctly changed both numbers to 37. fix/fx-b1 added ADR-0109 as Delegated. B1 raised the index count to 39 but never touched docs/playbook.md, so B1 on its own was already stale (38 against 39). The playbook change from records merged in without a conflict, and the integration commit 481b2e5 did not edit the playbook. The head therefore pairs the index's 38 (which the test checks) with the playbook's 37.

Nothing in the records makes 37 right. The playbook row defines the set as records "decided by Claude under authority the owner delegated" and names the ADR index as its source. ADR-0109's own record says it is Delegated: "Claude built backlog batch B1 ... not explicitly approved; reversible". No owner decision takes ADR-0109, or any other Delegated record, out of the review. adr-records.test.ts only checks the index's count line against the index rows, and the one playbook test in ci.test.ts reads only the "Public record for the charter" row, so no test catches this.

There is one small mitigation. The row's Source column points to the ADR index, which is correct and test-checked, so an owner working from the index would still see all 38. That is why this is not blocking: it is a wrong count in an owner-facing to-do item, not a false record of a decision. It is still a real error that this batch introduced, in the list the owner uses to review Claude's delegated decisions, and the newest record is the one left out. The fix is one character, so it belongs in this batch. should-fix is the right severity.

**Evidence:**

Head 481b2e5. docs/playbook.md:49: "**Review the 37 Delegated records** (decided by Claude under authority the owner delegated; ...)". docs/adr/README.md:13: "**109 records:** 102 accepted (15 of them by continuation, awaiting ratification; 38 delegated) and 7 proposed." docs/adr/README.md:147 has the ADR-0109 row, Accepted, decided by Delegated. Counting the Decided-by column (awk over the index rows) gives 38 Delegated, 29 User-approved, 20 User, 15 By continuation and 7 Pending.

The count on each branch (playbook / index line / rows):
- main 8401820: 38 / 38 / 38
- fix/fx-records: 37 / 37 / 37 (ADR-0100 was Delegated on main and is now User-approved)
- fix/fx-b1: 38 / 39 / 39
- fix/fx-evidence: 38 / 38 / 38
- 481b2e5: 37 / 38 / 38

`git diff 8401820..fix/fx-b1 --stat -- docs/playbook.md` is empty, so B1 never updated the playbook. `git diff c6bdc8b^1..481b2e5 -- docs/playbook.md` is empty, so the integration did not reconcile it either.

ADR-0109 line 6: "Delegated: Claude built backlog batch B1 ... none of its items needed an owner decision; not explicitly approved; reversible."

scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts:152-165 checks only the index's own count line against its rows. scripts/test/ci.test.ts:330 reads only the "Public record for the charter" playbook row. No test ties playbook.md:49 to the index.

**Fix:**

In docs/playbook.md:49, change "**Review the 37 Delegated records**" to "**Review the 38 Delegated records**". Optionally, add ADR-0109 (B1's money path, the newest Delegated record) to the records named for review, but present that as Claude's suggestion and not as a priority the owner set. The "starting with ADR-0071 ... 0078" order comes from the 2026-10-08 hand-back and should stay as it is.

#### XDOC-4: Residuals named in ADR-0109 have no backlog row

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed-partly; lens backlog; where `docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:67`.*

**What is wrong:**

ADR-0109's Negative consequences name two new paid-call residuals that no backlog row holds. The first is the window between the describe and the store write, in which a call could begin. The second is a connection reset after Temporal accepted the start: the run is marked failed with "Nothing was spent" while its workflow may run. In this batch, the owner chose to file every residual named only in ADRs, and the batch created a row, "Paid-call residuals named only in ADRs", for exactly that. The row lists only residuals from ADR-0099, ADR-0095 and ADR-0091.

**Evidence:**

ADR-0109:67: "A late start ... between the describe and that store write, which follows it at once, a call could begin." ADR-0109:68: "A connection reset after Temporal accepted the start is the exception; ... judged too rare to word for (Claude's judgement)." `grep -i "reset\|late start\|cannot tell whether" docs/backlog.md` finds nothing. The owner's batch choice (conversation-continued.md, 2026-10-18T19:17): "Records + evidence + B1 together (Recommended)", whose option text was "file the 10 unfiled items and give the ~35 trigger-less residuals triggers".

**When it goes wrong:**

After the first live run, a run shows "Nothing was spent" although its workflow ran and was charged, or a call starts after a late start. No backlog row says when to look again, which repeats the ADR-only gap this batch was meant to close.

**Proposed fix:**

Add both residuals to "Paid-call residuals named only in ADRs", with a labelled trigger, for example: when a live run's ledger shows spend on a run marked "Nothing was spent".

**Refuter 1: partly, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

The defect itself is real, and I reproduced it. One premise in the finding overstates what the owner chose.

What holds:
1. No backlog row holds either residual. Of the Negative consequences in ADR-0109 (lines 63-70), "2,000 thinking tokens" points to the backlog row "Gemini image thinking tokens" (backlog.md:65), and "Four providers' billing" is in the billing row (backlog.md:142). The other two have no row:
   - "A late start" (line 67).
   - "Code 14 and reset connections keep 'Nothing was spent'" (line 68).
   The row "Paid-call residuals named only in ADRs" (backlog.md:130) describes itself as "Accepted limits that had no row", but it lists only ADR-0099, ADR-0095 and ADR-0091. Greps of backlog.md and playbook.md for reset / ECONNRESET / code 14 / late start / marked failed / "Nothing was spent" / store write find nothing on these two. The nearest rows do not cover them:
   - "Cancel stops calls already in flight" (backlog.md:47) triggers on a call billed after a cancel.
   - "Run start: reopen a run whose workflow never started" (backlog.md:76) covers ADR-0099's first double fault.
2. The scenario can happen. I checked client.ts:170-180 and start-failure.ts. When a start fails, the client calls describe, then `store.updateRun(... failed ...)`. `startFailureMessage` returns ENGINE_DOWN_AT_START ("Nothing was spent") for any transport error (ECONNRESET, code 14), whatever the describe found.
3. The status page's F03 row (docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:15) lists ADR-0109 under Records. It sends readers to "the residuals only ADRs named (row \"Paid-call residuals named only in ADRs\")". Line 5 says "The residuals it names are backlog rows, each with what brings it back." So a reader takes ADR-0109's paid-call residuals to be in that row, and they are not.
4. This is the gap the owner asked to close, on 2026-10-18 at 18:37: "have we solved the root cause of the verified findings or at least filed them with trigger in backlog or not ... so we are sure that nothing is unaddressed."

What is overstated: the finding says the owner "chose to file every residual named only in ADRs". The option the owner chose (19:17) reads "file the 10 unfiled items and give the ~35 trigger-less residuals triggers". That was a fixed list from an audit made before B1 wrote ADR-0109. The row title "Paid-call residuals named only in ADRs" and its trigger are Claude's ("trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18"). The obligation comes from the owner's 18:37 request, the row's own definition and the status page's claim, not from the chosen option's words. Any fix or report should not cite the option as saying more than it does.

Severity: should-fix, not blocking. ADR-0109 records both residuals honestly and labels the reset one as Claude's judgement, so no record is false about the code. Both cases are rare: a run marked failed with "Nothing was spent" only costs money if a call begins in the window before the store write. The status page's completeness claim is a little wrong, and the fix is a one-row edit that can ride in this batch.

Side observation, also from this batch: backlog.md:76, added in 489483e on the records branch, says "Pick up with the describe-first row". B1's edits applied in 481b2e5 closed that row ("Run start: describe the workflow before marking a run failed"), and no row named describe-first is left, so the reference points nowhere. Severity: note.

**Evidence:**

- Branch head checked: 481b2e5 in C:/Users/arvik/code/byway/.worktrees/fx-batch.
- docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:67 says "between the describe and that store write, which follows it at once, a call could begin". Line 68: "A connection reset after Temporal accepted the start is the exception; ... judged too rare to word for (Claude's judgement)".
- docs/backlog.md:130 (row "Paid-call residuals named only in ADRs") names only ADR-0099, ADR-0095 and ADR-0091. `git log -S` shows the row came from 489483e (records branch, written before ADR-0109). 481b2e5 did not extend it.
- packages/runner/src/client.ts:176-178 does the describe first, then `store.updateRun(id, { status: 'failed', error: startFailureMessage(e, workflow) })`. packages/runner/src/start-failure.ts:65 has `if (isTransportFailure(err)) return ENGINE_DOWN_AT_START;`, which ignores the describe result. TRANSPORT_TEXT includes ECONNRESET.
- Repro: I cloned fix/fx-batch to C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/check-clones/xdoc4, ran pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --offline, and added packages/runner/test/xdoc4-repro.test.ts, built on the stand-in Temporal from start-describe.test.ts. `pnpm exec vitest run test/xdoc4-repro.test.ts` passed 2/2:
  - (a) start accepted, then ECONNRESET; describe UNREACHABLE. The run ends {status:'failed', error: ENGINE_DOWN_AT_START ("...Nothing was spent...")}, and the workflow id is in the accepted set.
  - (b) start times out; describe finds none; Temporal accepts after the describe. The run ends failed with ENGINE_SLOW_AT_START ("...Nothing was spent..."), and the workflow exists.
- docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:15 (F03) lists [0109] under Records and points to the "Paid-call residuals named only in ADRs" row. Line 5: "The residuals it names are backlog rows, each with what brings it back."
- Owner's words, in docs/adr/evidence/conversation-continued.md:
  - 18:37 (2026-10-18): "filed them with trigger in backlog or not ... so we are sure that nothing is unaddressed".
  - The chosen option (lines 409/413, answered 19:17): "file the 10 unfiled items and give the ~35 trigger-less residuals triggers". This is a fixed audit list, not "every residual named only in ADRs".
- Side note: backlog.md:76 says "Pick up with the describe-first row". `git show 8401820:docs/backlog.md` had that row at line 71, and the batch diff removes it (B1 closed it).

**Fix:**

In docs/backlog.md:130 ("Paid-call residuals named only in ADRs"), add the two ADR-0109 residuals before the trigger sentence, in the row's style. For example:

"A run start that failed is marked failed after a describe of its workflow. A start Temporal was still handling lands afterwards and finds the run failed, but a call could begin between the describe and that store write. A connection reset or code 14 after Temporal accepted the start, when byway cannot ask afterwards, keeps 'Nothing was spent' while the workflow may run (ADR-0109)."

The row's existing trigger ("Revisit any of them when a live run or a person's report shows it", Claude's) already covers them. If a sharper trigger is wanted, label it as Claude's. For example: "when a run marked failed at start has spend in the ledger (trigger proposed by Claude)".

Do not describe this as the owner's chosen option. The owner chose a fixed list; cite the 18:37 request instead.

Separately (note): in backlog.md:76, replace "Pick up with the describe-first row, or" with wording that does not point to the closed row. For example: "B1 (ADR-0109) closed the describe-first row; pick up if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down".

**Refuter 2: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I tried to refute the finding three ways: that another row already holds these residuals, that a recorded owner choice excuses them, or that ADR-0109 gives them a trigger. None of the three holds, so the finding stands as should-fix.

1. No backlog row holds either residual. B1 closed and removed two rows. One was "Run start: a start that timed out is reported as 'engine was not running'", whose text included "a call already started would still be charged". The other was "Run start: describe the workflow before marking a run failed". No row replaced either one.
   - The rows that remain about run start do not cover these cases:
     - "Run start: reopen a run whose workflow never started" (backlog.md:76) is about a lost refusal followed by a second press.
     - "Reconcile lost runs while the engine runs" (backlog.md:53) is about runs stuck waiting.
     - The first-live-run row's "settles or feeds" list does not name them.
   - The batch's catch-all row "Paid-call residuals named only in ADRs" (backlog.md:130) cites only ADR-0099, ADR-0095 and ADR-0091.

2. ADR-0109 gives no trigger. It does point to the backlog for its other residuals: line 44 says "(backlog)" for the billing assumptions and line 66 names the backlog row "Gemini image thinking tokens". Line 67 (the late start) has no pointer at all. Line 68 (a reset after Temporal accepted the start) says only "judged too rare to word for (Claude's judgement)". That is Claude's choice not to change the message, not the owner's, and it sets no time to look again. Neither is "a limit already recorded with a trigger".

3. The code matches what ADR-0109 describes.
   - In client.ts, the describe (handle(runId).describe(), where WorkflowNotFoundError means 'absent' and any other error means 'unknown') runs before store.updateRun(status 'failed'). A start that lands between the two can begin an activity.
   - In start-failure.ts, isTransportFailure matches ECONNRESET, and it is checked before the describe result is used. So a reset combined with a describe that fails gives ENGINE_DOWN_AT_START ("Nothing was spent") even when Temporal had accepted the start.

4. The batch's own status page claims coverage it does not have. docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:15 (F03, which this batch edited) lists ADR-0109 among F03's records. It then says the residuals only ADRs named are in the row "Paid-call residuals named only in ADRs". For ADR-0109 that is not true.

5. The owner's stated aim covers new residuals too. On 2026-10-18 at 18:37 the owner asked for a mapping showing "have we solved the root cause of the verified findings or at least filed them with trigger in backlog or not) so we are sure that nothing is unaddressed". The records commit 489483e says "every open row says when it comes back".

Two corrections to the finding:
- (a) It overreads the owner's batch choice. The option chosen at 19:17 was "file the 10 unfiled items and give the ~35 trigger-less residuals triggers", which counts items from an audit made before B1 existed. It did not literally cover residuals B1 would create. The defect rests instead on the owner's 18:37 request and on the status page's claim (item 4).
- (b) Its failure scenario overstates the money at risk.
  - In the reset case, the run is already marked failed, so liveness refuses the workflow's calls. Only a call that began before the store write could be charged.
  - If the engine really went down, the worker cannot have started a call.
  - If only the client's connection reset, the describe most likely reconnects and returns 'exists'.
  - So the realistic symptom is a workflow that exists under a run that says "Nothing was spent", or a tiny paid-call window. That is why this is should-fix, not blocking.

Related things found while checking:
- backlog.md:76's trigger, "Pick up with the describe-first row", now names a row this batch removed (B1 closed it), so the trigger's first clause points at nothing.
- ADR-0109:70's residual (cached tokens of unrecorded rates charged at the input rate) also has no row. It errs high, so it is conservative.

**Evidence:**

- docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:67 says: "A late start ... between the describe and that store write, which follows it at once, a call could begin." It has no backlog pointer.
- ADR-0109:68 says: "A connection reset after Temporal accepted the start is the exception; the backlog asked only about code 4, and this was judged too rare to word for (Claude's judgement)." It has no backlog pointer either. Compare line 44, "(backlog)", and line 66, "(backlog 'Gemini image thinking tokens')".
- `grep -c -i "reset\|late start\|cannot tell whether\|nothing was spent\|describe" docs/backlog.md` returns 3. The three hits are unrelated: "describes" at :56 and :97, and the dangling "describe-first row" at :76.
- `grep -n 0109 docs/backlog.md` finds only rows 65, 86, 142, 155 and 156. These are the Gemini tokens, C2 boards, billing assumptions, first live run and planning numbers rows; none covers run start.
- backlog.md:130, the row "Paid-call residuals named only in ADRs", ends its list with "(ADR-0099) ... (ADR-0095) ... (ADR-0091)" and has nothing from ADR-0109.
- The diff 8401820..481b2e5 of docs/backlog.md removes the row "Run start: describe the workflow before marking a run failed (P3 double fault)" and the row "Run start: a start that timed out is reported as 'engine was not running' | ... a call already started would still be charged".
- docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:15 (F03) lists [0109] among its records, then says: "the residuals only ADRs named (row "Paid-call residuals named only in ADRs")".
- packages/runner/src/client.ts (diff): `const workflow = await workflowAfterFailedStart(id); if (workflow === 'exists') return record; await store.updateRun(id, { status: 'failed', error: startFailureMessage(e, workflow) });`
- packages/runner/src/start-failure.ts:
  - `if (isTransportFailure(err)) return ENGINE_DOWN_AT_START;` is checked before the workflow argument is used.
  - TRANSPORT_TEXT includes `\bECONNRESET\b`.
  - ENGINE_DOWN_AT_START reads "...did not start. Nothing was spent."
- docs/adr/evidence/conversation-continued.md:
  - 2026-10-18T18:37 (owner): "have we solved the root cause of the verified findings or at least filed them with trigger in backlog or not) so we are sure that nothing is unaddressed."
  - 19:12 (option text): "file the 10 unfiled items and give the ~35 trigger-less residuals triggers".
- Commit 489483e: "Backlog: every open row says when it comes back".

**Fix:**

Append to the row "Paid-call residuals named only in ADRs" (docs/backlog.md:130). Docs-only, in this batch.

Text to append:
"A start Temporal is still handling can land between the describe and the run's failed mark, so one call could begin before the gateway refuses the rest. A connection reset after Temporal accepted a start, with the describe also failing, marks the run failed with 'Nothing was spent' though its workflow exists (ADR-0109)."

Extend the row's trigger with Claude's label, for example: "or if a live run's ledger shows spend on, or a workflow under, a run whose error says it did not start (trigger proposed by Claude)".

In the same edit, fix backlog.md:76's trigger "Pick up with the describe-first row", which names a row B1 closed. For example: "Pick up at the next change to Runner.start's failure path, or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down".

#### XDOC-5: The reopen row's trigger points to a row this batch closed, and that trigger has fired

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed; lens backlog; where `docs/backlog.md:76`.*

**What is wrong:**

The row "Run start: reopen a run whose workflow never started" says "Pick up with the describe-first row". B1 built and closed the describe-first row in this batch but did not do the reopen, as ADR-0109 records. So the trigger fired with no recorded decision not to act, and it now points to a row that no longer exists.

**Evidence:**

backlog.md:76: "Pick up with the describe-first row, or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)". ADR-0109:77: "The first (a refused Start pressed again gets that failed run back) is unchanged." The row "Run start: describe the workflow before marking a run failed (P3 double fault)" was removed.

**When it goes wrong:**

A reader waits for a describe-first row that will never come back, or assumes the reopen was considered and declined, when nothing records that.

**Proposed fix:**

Say that B1 (ADR-0109) built describe-first without the reopen, and why. Keep only the observable trigger, or give a new one labelled as Claude's.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding is correct, and it starts in the integration commit, not in B1.

1. On main (8401820), the reopen row had no trigger. The records branch added "Pick up with the describe-first row, or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)" in 489483e. That commit is only on fix/fx-records, where the describe-first row was still open.
2. B1 built describe-first and its branch did not touch docs/backlog.md (`git diff --stat 8401820..fix/fx-b1 -- docs/backlog.md` is empty). B1 was briefed from main's backlog, so it never saw this trigger.
3. The integration commit 481b2e5 removed the describe-first row and the code-4 timeout row. Its message says "The records builder's conditional edits fall away with the rows they were for". This conditional edit was kept anyway. `git log -S` confirms both facts: 489483e added the "Pick up with the describe-first row" text, and 481b2e5 removed the "Run start: describe the workflow before marking a run failed" row.
4. At head, no backlog row is the describe-first row. Its only remaining mentions are ADR-0109's test list and ADR-0099's Later note. No scripts/test check looks for row references in backlog.md.
5. The trigger fired and nothing says why it was not acted on:
   - ADR-0109:77 and ADR-0099's Later note (line 90) only say "The first double fault is unchanged".
   - ADR-0109's Alternatives considered does not mention reopen.
   - The status page's F03 cell lists the reopen row as remaining, with no reason.
   - The owner's words never mention reopen or the double fault (searched conversation.md and conversation-continued.md).
   So "assumes it was considered and declined, when nothing records that" holds.

Why should-fix and not blocking:
- The row still has a valid trigger in its second clause. I traced it after B1: a Start refused by a down engine still gets ENGINE_DOWN_AT_START, because isTransportFailure comes before the describe result in startFailureMessage. Pressing it again returns the existing failed record (`if (!created) throw e;` / the repeated-start path in client.ts). So the observable condition can still happen.
- Nothing wrong reaches money, behaviour or the owner's words.
- It is a dead cross-reference plus a fired dependency with no recorded outcome, in a batch whose stated purpose (489483e) was that "every open row says when it comes back".

**Evidence:**

- docs/backlog.md:76 (head 481b2e5) ends "...given a reliable \"never started\" marker on the run record (ADR-0099). Pick up with the describe-first row, or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)".
- `git log -S"Pick up with the describe-first row" 8401820..481b2e5 -- docs/backlog.md` gives 489483e (records branch). `git branch --contains 489483e` gives fix/fx-batch and fix/fx-records only.
- `git log -S"Run start: describe the workflow before marking a run failed" 8401820..481b2e5 -- docs/backlog.md` gives 481b2e5. Its message says "The records builder's conditional edits fall away with the rows they were for".
- `git diff --stat 8401820..fix/fx-b1 -- docs/backlog.md` is empty: B1 made no backlog edits.
- A grep for "describe-first|reopen a run|first double fault" over the repo finds only backlog.md:76, ADR-0099:90 and ADR-0109:92 (the test list). No backlog row exists for describe-first.
- ADR-0109:77: "The first (a refused Start pressed again gets that failed run back) is unchanged." No reason is given, and Alternatives considered names only gRPC-4-as-unknown, passing names explicitly, and leaving the Gemini price.
- docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:15 (F03): "the run-start double fault that reopens a run whose workflow never started (ADR-0109 fixed the other: a failed start now asks Temporal first)".
- packages/runner/src/start-failure.ts at head: `if (isTransportFailure(err)) return ENGINE_DOWN_AT_START;` comes before the workflow check. In client.ts, `if (!created) throw e;` leaves a repeated start with the failed record, so the second trigger clause can still be observed.
- ADR-0109 Consequences, "A late start": "When Temporal says it has no workflow, the run is marked failed. A start Temporal was still handling that lands afterwards finds the run failed". So B1's 'absent' answer is not by itself the "reliable never started marker" the reopen row requires. This is a reason a fix could cite, labelled as Claude's.
- Not checked: the coordinator's B1 brief, which ADR-0109 calls "the coordinator's brief" and which is not in the repo, so I could not see whether reopen was explicitly scoped out there.

**Fix:**

Edit docs/backlog.md:76 only; ADR-0109 needs no change. Replace "Pick up with the describe-first row, or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)" with wording that:

- says B1 (ADR-0109) built describe-first without the reopen;
- says why, labelled as Claude's: Temporal's "no workflow" answer is not a reliable never-started marker, because a late start can land after it (ADR-0109's "A late start");
- keeps only the observable trigger: "Pick up if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)". A new trigger can be added instead if it is labelled as Claude's with its date.

The edit lands in the batch before the --no-ff merge.

**Refuter 2: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding is right. The batch itself created the stale trigger: two of its builders worked in parallel, and the integration commit closed one row without updating the other row that refers to it.

1. The trigger was added in this batch by the records builder. fix/fx-records commit 489483e ("Backlog: every open row says when it comes back") gave the reopen row "Pick up with the describe-first row, or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)". On fix/fx-b1 and fix/fx-evidence the reopen row has no trigger at all, so B1 never saw this one.

2. The describe-first row was closed in this batch, and it is gone, not marked done. The integration commit 481b2e5 ("B1's and the evidence builder's edits (8 rows closed...)") removed "Run start: describe the workflow before marking a run failed (P3 double fault)". It also removed the code-4 row "a start that timed out is reported as 'engine was not running'", which pointed to "the P3 describe-first row". docs/backlog.md has no closed-rows section, so a closed row disappears entirely, and the reopen row at backlog.md:76 now names a row that does not exist. The integration commit says the records builder's conditional edits "fall away with the rows they were for", and that it fixed "three test comments that named closed rows". This reference was missed.

3. The trigger has fired with no reason recorded. Describe-first was built (ADR-0109 item 3), but the reopen was not. ADR-0109:77 says only "The first (a refused Start pressed again gets that failed run back) is unchanged.", and ADR-0099's Later note (line 90) says only "The first double fault is unchanged." Neither says why. No B1 report in docs/adr/evidence/reports, and no B1 commit message, mentions the reopen. The status page (docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:15) lists it as still open.

4. Checked for an owner decision that makes this a non-defect. On the "Next batch" question (conversation-continued.md:409-413) the owner chose "Records + evidence + B1 together (Recommended)". B1 there is "the money-path fixes before your first live run". The reopen is not a money item: the failed run's message says nothing was spent. So leaving it out of B1 fits the scope the owner chose, and the decision not to act is defensible. But it is not written down anywhere, and the backlog row still reads as if describe-first were pending.

Severity: should-fix, not blocking. Nothing about money or behaviour depends on it. The second trigger ("if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down") is still observable and usable, and ADR-0109 and the status page make clear that describe-first was done. So the scenario of "a reader waits for a row that never comes back" is slightly overstated. Even so:
- The batch created the inconsistency.
- It breaks the property the owner asked this batch to give every row, a working "when it comes back" (the owner chose to "give the ~35 trigger-less residuals triggers").
- Under ADR-0107 item 5, a checker blocks on a false statement in the records. A trigger condition that names a closed row is a small false statement, which supports should-fix over note.
- The fix is one sentence in a docs-only file, inside this batch.

**Evidence:**

- docs/backlog.md:76 at 481b2e5 (head) ends: "Pick up with the describe-first row, or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)".
- `git show fix/fx-records:docs/backlog.md` has this trigger (line 76) and the describe-first row (line 77). fix/fx-b1:docs/backlog.md:70 and fix/fx-evidence:docs/backlog.md:70 have the reopen row without any trigger.
- c6bdc8b (the batch before 481b2e5) still has the describe-first row at line 77. In `git diff 8401820..481b2e5 -- docs/backlog.md`, "-| Run start: describe the workflow before marking a run failed (P3 double fault) |..." and the code-4 row ("...as the P3 describe-first row proposes...") are both removed, and the reopen row is re-added with the trigger.
- 481b2e5's message says "The records builder's conditional edits fall away with the rows they were for" and "three test comments that named closed rows". This reference was not updated.
- `git grep -i "describe-first"` on head finds only backlog.md:76 and ADR-0109:92 (a test description). No row by that name exists.
- ADR-0109:77: "The first (a refused Start pressed again gets that failed run back) is unchanged." ADR-0099:90 (Later note): "The first double fault is unchanged." Neither gives a reason, and nothing in ADR-0109 under "Alternatives considered" covers the reopen.
- docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:15 lists the reopen ("the run-start double fault that reopens a run whose workflow never started (ADR-0109 fixed the other...)") as an open row.
- conversation-continued.md:409/413: the owner chose "Records + evidence + B1 together (Recommended)". B1 = "the money-path fixes before your first live run". The reopen was not in it by name.
- `git log 8401820..fix/fx-b1` mentions no reopen, "pressed again" or "first double fault".
- No test under scripts/test checks the backlog's references to other rows.

Not checked: whether describe-first's "Temporal has none" path writes a structured marker that the reopen could use. The reopen row needs "a reliable 'never started' marker on the run record", and it looks like only the error text differs. This affects only how hard the reopen is, not the finding.

**Fix:**

Rewrite the trigger at docs/backlog.md:76, for example: "Describe-first was built without this (ADR-0109 item 3): B1 was the money-path batch and a re-press returns a run that spent nothing. Pick up if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down, or at the next change to Runner.start's failure path (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)". The minimum fix is to drop "Pick up with the describe-first row, or" and keep only the observable trigger. Do not attribute a reason to the owner. The owner chose B1's scope ("the money-path fixes before your first live run"), not a decision on the reopen, so the reason must be labelled as Claude's reading.

#### XDOC-6: Triggers Claude added or re-armed are not labelled as Claude's

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed-partly; lens backlog; where `docs/backlog.md:147`.*

**What is wrong:**

The Structured "Changes" and "Later records" row records that its trigger fired, then re-arms it without a label as "Revisit when another missing link is found", and does not say who chose not to act. The "missing link" trigger is Claude's. The D4 brief the owner decided on named only the reader trigger. The Gemini row's new trigger (line 65), "Check both against the first live run's ledger...", is also Claude's (B1) and has no label. The owner's rule is that Claude's proposals are labelled as Claude's.

**Evidence:**

backlog.md:147: "Its trigger fired on 2026-10-18 ... Revisit when another missing link is found, or when a reader such as a paper reviewer needs each record to carry its own pointers". decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md (D4 recommendation): "Structured Changes and Later-records rows, if a reader such as a paper reviewer needs the pointers inside each record." On main the Gemini row read "Verify how they are billed before changing the image price (ADR-0091)".

**When it goes wrong:**

The owner reads a deferral after a fired trigger as an agreed decision, when it was Claude's alone.

**Proposed fix:**

Add "(trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)" to both new triggers. In the Structured row, state that Claude deferred it after the trigger fired, or put it to the owner.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I could not refute it. Every factual claim holds against the repository. The Structured row carries most of the weight; the Gemini half is real but smaller.

1. **The "missing link" trigger is Claude's.**
   - The D4 brief the owner approved ("D4: ... proceed", conversation-continued.md, 2026-10-16) names only the reader trigger: "Structured Changes and Later-records rows, if a reader such as a paper reviewer needs the pointers inside each record." (decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md:1173).
   - ADR-0103 says only "Kept in the backlog with triggers" (line 118) and names no missing-link trigger.
   - The trigger first appears in D4's merge commit 643616e (2026-10-17). It is in neither parent (`git show 643616e^1/^2:docs/backlog.md` has no Structured row), so Claude wrote it while merging, with no label. It has been on main unlabelled since then.

2. **The batch records that the trigger fired, then re-arms it unlabelled and does not say who kept the item parked.**
   - Commit dca74a7 (fix/fx-records) changed "Revisit when a missing link is found" to "Its trigger fired on 2026-10-18: ... Revisit when another missing link is found, or when a reader such as a paper reviewer needs ...".
   - The fired trigger was not put to the owner. The status page's list of items open for the owner (docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:45) does not include it. Its R3 row (line 35) says only that the missing link was added.
   - So the decision to keep the item parked after its trigger fired was Claude's alone, and nothing says so.

3. **The Gemini row's new "Check both against the first live run's ledger and Google's usage records" is B1's text, unlabelled.** It came in with integration commit 481b2e5. Main's row read "Verify how they are billed before changing the image price (ADR-0091)".

4. **These rows break the batch's own convention.**
   - Commit 489483e says each new trigger is marked "(trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)"; 83 rows carry that label.
   - The same integration commit labelled B1's other new trigger in the first-live-run row "(Claude's trigger)", but not the Gemini one.
   - ADR-0107 item 5 (new in this batch) says records "mark what Claude adds".
   - In a backlog where Claude's triggers are labelled, an unlabelled trigger reads as one set by the owner or a record.

**Where the finding is weaker:**
- The Structured trigger was not added by this batch. It was already on main unlabelled; the batch re-armed it after it fired, without a label.
- The Gemini part is only note-level:
  - Main's first-live-run row already said the run "settles or feeds: ... Gemini image thinking tokens (their rows)".
  - The row opens "Charged since B1 (ADR-0109)", and ADR-0109's Decided by is "Delegated: Claude ... not explicitly approved".
  - The text is a way to settle the question, not a deferral after a fired trigger.
- The same unlabelled pattern exists in the billing-assumptions row (backlog.md:142, "Settle it from the first live runs' ledger against the providers' usage records"). It was reworded from main's similar unlabelled sentence, so it is mostly older.

**Severity:** this is not a false record of the owner's words. But it is a deferral after a fired trigger, with no label and not put to the owner, in the batch whose purpose was labelling Claude's triggers. That breaks ADR-0107 item 5, so should-fix stands; it does not block the push on its own.

**Evidence:**

**Current text:**
- docs/backlog.md:147: "Its trigger fired on 2026-10-18: a trace of the external review found ADR-0087 without its "changed by" link to ADR-0090 (added that day), and named ADR-0082 as changed by ADR-0100 item 10, which ADR-0100 does not say (item 10 corrects ADR-0088). Revisit when another missing link is found, or when a reader such as a paper reviewer needs each record to carry its own pointers". There is no label.
- docs/backlog.md:65: "... Check both against the first live run's ledger and Google's usage records (that run's Balanced images come from this model)". There is no label.

**Main and the owner's D4 decision:**
- On main (`git show 8401820:docs/backlog.md`):
  - line 140 read "Revisit when a missing link is found, or when a reader such as a paper reviewer needs ...";
  - line 61 read "Verify how they are billed before changing the image price (ADR-0091)".
- The D4 brief (docs/adr/evidence/decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md:1173) gives only the reader trigger. The owner approved D4 with "proceed. but remember my earlier point: no macOS for now." (conversation-continued.md, 2026-10-16).
- `git show 643616e^1:docs/backlog.md` and `643616e^2` contain no Structured row. 643616e itself contains "Revisit when a missing link is found", so the trigger was written in the D4 merge itself.

**Which batch commits touched the two rows:**
- `git show dca74a7 -- docs/backlog.md` contains the Structured change.
- `git show 481b2e5 -- docs/backlog.md` contains the Gemini change.
- The fix/fx-b1, fix/fx-evidence and fix/fx-records branches all still hold main's Gemini row.

**The labelling convention:**
- Commit 489483e: "A trigger sentence on each open row that had none, marked "(trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)" ... Rows owned by the B1 and evidence builders are untouched."
- `grep -c "trigger proposed by Claude" docs/backlog.md` returns 83, and "(Claude's trigger)" appears once (first-live-run row, line 155).
- ADR-0107 item 5: "Briefs and records quote the option the owner chose word for word, and mark what Claude adds."

**Not put to the owner:** the "Also open for the owner in the backlog" list (docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:45) does not include the Structured row.

**Fix:**

**docs/backlog.md:147.** Replace the last sentence with: "Claude kept it parked after the trigger fired (2026-10-18). Revisit when another missing link is found (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-17; re-armed by Claude, 2026-10-18), or when a reader such as a paper reviewer needs each record to carry its own pointers (the D4 brief's trigger, approved with D4)". Optionally add this row to the status page's list of items open for the owner (docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:45).

**docs/backlog.md:65.** Append "(trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)" after "...come from this model)".

**docs/backlog.md:142 (optional).** Do the same to "Settle it from the first live runs' ledger...", which has the same unlabelled pattern.

**Refuter 2: partly, note.**

**Reasoning:**

The facts in XDOC-6 hold. Both triggers are Claude's, and neither carries a label. Three parts of the finding are wrong or overstated, so it is a note, not a should-fix.

1. The batch did not add the "missing link" trigger. It has been on main, unlabelled, since the D4 merge 643616e (2026-10-17). That merge commit says "ADR-0103's deferrals are filed with triggers (structured relation rows, ...)". main 8401820 backlog.md:140 already reads "Revisit when a missing link is found, or when a reader such as a paper reviewer needs each record to carry its own pointers". The batch did two things to the row (dca74a7): it recorded that the trigger fired, and it changed "a" to "another". Its labelling pass (489483e) was scoped to "each open row that had none", meaning rows without a trigger. That is the scope the owner chose: "give the ~35 trigger-less residuals triggers". The Structured row already had a trigger, so the labelling pass left it alone on purpose.

2. The failure scenario overstates the risk. The owner approved D4 with "proceed". The recommendation the owner approved kept the structured rows in the backlog with one trigger only: "if a reader such as a paper reviewer needs the pointers inside each record". That trigger has not fired. So leaving the row open is the owner's own decision, not Claude's alone. The only thing that is Claude's alone is the extra "missing link" trigger. That trigger adds a point at which to look again; it does not add a gate against the owner. Nothing in the backlog says an unlabelled trigger is the owner's, so the row does not falsely credit the owner. It is only silent about who chose. One mild point: the row places this trigger right after "ADR-0103 kept them 'in the backlog with triggers'", which can suggest that ADR-0103 or the owner chose it. That was equally true on main.

3. The Gemini sentence (line 65) is a new trigger, and B1 (Claude) wrote it. Its origin can still be traced, and the tie to the first live run is not new:
   - The row opens with "Charged since B1 (ADR-0109)". ADR-0109's header reads "Delegated: Claude built backlog batch B1 ... not explicitly approved".
   - ADR-0109 line 66 already says "the first live run's ledger shows it (backlog 'Gemini image thinking tokens')".
   - The row itself calls the 2,000 tokens "Claude's planning number".
   - Main's first-live-run row already linked the two: "It settles or feeds: fal's live media host set and Gemini image thinking tokens (their rows)".
   - The sentence asks for a check at a run the owner chose. It is not a condition the owner did not choose.

What remains is a real inconsistency. This batch labelled 89 triggers "(trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)". B1's own edits in the same hand-off commit 481b2e5 labelled the first-live-run row's new date trigger "(Claude's trigger)" (backlog.md:155). The Gemini sentence was left without a label. The Structured row records a firing of Claude's trigger and Claude's choice to keep the row open, without saying either was Claude's. ADR-0107 item 5, recorded in this batch as Claude's delegated practice, says records "mark what Claude adds". The fix is two parentheticals. It is worth folding in if the batch is touched again. On its own it does not justify another fix round.

One further point for the critic, outside this finding's labelling scope. The D4 brief's rationale said "the index already holds all 30 links". The fired trigger shows that premise was already false at D4: ADR-0087's link to ADR-0090 was missing, and ADR-0082 was wrongly named as changed by ADR-0100. The row still repeats this reasoning as ADR-0103's: "since the index already holds the links". Whether to tell the owner that a premise of the D4 decision did not hold is Claude's suggestion only. It is not a defect in the record.

**Evidence:**

- Head, docs/backlog.md:147: "...Its trigger fired on 2026-10-18: a trace of the external review found ADR-0087 without its "changed by" link to ADR-0090 (added that day)... Revisit when another missing link is found, or when a reader such as a paper reviewer needs each record to carry its own pointers". There is no label and nothing says who chose.
- main 8401820, docs/backlog.md:140 (`git show 8401820:docs/backlog.md`): "...Revisit when a missing link is found, or when a reader such as a paper reviewer needs each record to carry its own pointers". Also unlabelled.
- Origin of the trigger, `git log --all --diff-merges=separate -G "Revisit when a missing" -- docs/backlog.md`: first appears in merge 643616e (2026-10-17). Its message says "ADR-0103's deferrals are filed with triggers (structured relation rows, ...)".
- docs/adr/evidence/decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md:1170-1173, the D4 recommendation: "Keep the rest of the full design as backlog items with triggers: ... Structured Changes and Later-records rows, if a reader such as a paper reviewer needs the pointers inside each record." The owner, conversation-continued.md: "D4 ... proceed. but remember my earlier point: no macOS for now." ADR-0103's header says "Decided by: User (the owner approved D4 on 2026-10-16: "proceed"...)". ADR-0103:118 says only "Kept in the backlog with triggers."
- Commit 489483e: "A trigger sentence on each open row that had none, marked "(trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)"... Rows owned by the B1 and evidence builders are untouched." At head the backlog has 89 such labels, 1 "(Claude's trigger)" and 2 coordinator's-trigger labels (`grep -o`).
- The owner's batch choice, conversation-continued.md:409-413: "file the 10 unfiled items and give the ~35 trigger-less residuals triggers".
- Head, docs/backlog.md:65 (Gemini, written in 481b2e5): "Check both against the first live run's ledger and Google's usage records (that run's Balanced images come from this model)". No label. Main's line 61 read: "Verify how they are billed before changing the image price (ADR-0091)".
- Main's first-live-run row already said: "It settles or feeds: fal's live media host set and Gemini image thinking tokens (their rows)".
- ADR-0109's header: "Delegated: Claude built backlog batch B1 ... not explicitly approved; reversible." ADR-0109:66: "the first live run's ledger shows it (backlog "Gemini image thinking tokens")".
- In the same commit 481b2e5, B1's edit to backlog.md:155 is labelled: "if the run starts after 2026-11-01, read them again first (Claude's trigger)".
- No legend in backlog.md, playbook.md or the ADR index says that unlabelled triggers are the owner's (grep).
- ADR-0107 item 5 (Claude's delegated practice): "Briefs and records quote the option the owner chose word for word, and mark what Claude adds."
- Checked: who first filed the Structured row's trigger and when; the D4 brief and the owner's answer; the scope of the labelling pass; the Gemini row's main and head text; ADR-0109's provenance; how consistently labels are used in this batch.
- Not checked: whether Claude told the owner in chat that the Structured trigger had fired. conversation-continued.md keeps only the owner's messages and Claude's option questions, not Claude's prose replies.

**Fix:**

Optional, cheap; fold it into the batch only if the batch is edited for another reason.

In backlog.md:147, change the last sentence to:

"Claude kept the row open after its trigger fired. Revisit when another missing link is found (Claude's trigger, first filed with ADR-0103 on 2026-10-17), or when a reader such as a paper reviewer needs each record to carry its own pointers (the trigger in the D4 recommendation the owner approved)."
