In backlog.md:65, append "(trigger proposed by Claude, B1, 2026-10-18)" after "...come from this model)".

Date the missing-link trigger 2026-10-17, as above, not 2026-10-18 as the finding suggests: it was first filed on 2026-10-17.

#### SEC-1: The evidence secret check cannot catch a real fal key, a bearer token or a GitHub fine-grained token

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed; lens security; where `scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts:62`.*

**What is wrong:**

The test says the evidence holds "no key-shaped string", and it has a branch meant for fal (`fal-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,}`). fal does not issue keys in that shape. Its keys are `<key id>:<secret>` (a UUID, a colon, then hex), as the repo's own redactor and test fixtures show. The check also has no pattern for `Bearer …` tokens, JWTs or `github_pat_…`. So a FAL_KEY or a bearer token copied into docs/adr/evidence from a transcript or log would pass the check. Nothing of that shape is in the evidence today (I scanned it), so this push does not leak a secret. Only the guard for future evidence is weaker than it claims. Under the brief's "a test that cannot catch what it claims to guard" this could be read as blocking; I rate it should-fix because no secret is present now.

**Evidence:**

adr-evidence.test.ts:62: `const key = /\b(?:sk-(?:ant-)?[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,}|AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{20,}|gh[pousr]_[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}|xox[abp]-[A-Za-z0-9-]{10,}|fal-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,})/g`. The repo's own shapes are in packages/gateway/src/redact.ts:8-17: "fal.ai `<key id>:<secret>`, and bearer tokens", with the regex `/[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}:[0-9a-f]{32,}/gi` and `/Bearer\s+[A-Za-z0-9._~+/-]{16,}=*/g`. held-key.ts:24 also says "a FAL_KEY (`<key id>:<secret>`)", and billed.test.ts:19 has a fixture in that shape. Proof: in my clone (scratchpad/check-clones/security) I added docs/adr/evidence/logs/planted-fake.log containing a fake fal-shaped key (uuid:hex32), a fake `github_pat_…`, a fake `Authorization: Bearer eyJ…` JWT and a short `[a key-shaped string]`. `pnpm exec vitest run scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts` then reported "Tests 3 passed (3)".

**When it goes wrong:**

A later evidence import (a session transcript, a probe output or a CI log) carries the owner's FAL_KEY, for example a line `FAL_KEY=1a2b…-…:9f8e…` or a provider error echoing `Authorization: Bearer …`. adr-evidence.test.ts stays green, CI stays green, and the key lands on main in a repository the README says never holds keys.

**Proposed fix:**

Replace the invented `fal-` branch with redact.ts's shapes: the UUID:hex FAL_KEY pattern (case-insensitive) and the Bearer pattern. Add `github_pat_[A-Za-z0-9_]{20,}` and a JWT pattern (`eyJ[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}`). Better still, export KEY_SHAPES from @byway/gateway and reuse it so the two lists cannot drift. Then plant one fake of each shape in a temporary file to show the test fails on it.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding is right, and I reproduced it. The evidence test's docstring and test name say the evidence holds "no key-shaped string". Its one fal branch, `fal-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,}`, does not match the shape the repo itself gives FAL_KEY. That shape is `<key id>:<secret>`: a UUID, a colon, then 32 or more hex characters (redact.ts line 15, held-key.ts line 23, billed.test.ts line 19). fal is one of byway's five provider keys (env.ts lines 5-11, FAL_KEY), and the only one that none of the test's other branches covers. So a real FAL_KEY in docs/adr/evidence passes the test. fal's request header is `Authorization: Key <id>:<secret>`, and that passes too. So do `Bearer` JWTs and `github_pat_` tokens. The other provider keys are covered: OpenAI and OpenRouter keys (sk-, sk-proj-, sk-or-v1-), Anthropic sk-ant- keys of real length, Gemini AIza… keys and gho_ tokens all fail the test as they should.

Severity is should-fix, not blocking. I scanned the evidence with every suggested shape and found nothing of that shape at this head, so this push carries no secret. CI on main only reports (verify.yml header: "It reports and blocks nothing"). What is broken is a guard for future evidence imports, and the fix is one line. A strict reading of the brief's "a test that cannot catch what it claims to guard" could make the dead fal branch blocking. I keep should-fix because no secret is present and the test does catch every other provider key byway holds.

Small corrections to the finding:
- The regex is at scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts line 52, not 62. The file has 61 lines.
- The planted `[a key-shaped string]` is not a real gap. Real Anthropic keys are about 100 characters, and a long sk-ant- key is caught.
- fal sends `Authorization: Key …`, not Bearer. Adding the Bearer pattern alone would not close the fal gap; the UUID:hex pattern is the one needed.
- A shape check cannot catch the 32-hex fal secret on its own without false positives on hashes and SHAs. Only the full id:secret pair is shape-checkable; held-key.ts catches the half by value at runtime, which a static evidence test cannot do.

**Evidence:**

1. Code. scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts:52:
`const key = /\b(?:sk-(?:ant-)?[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,}|AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{20,}|gh[pousr]_[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}|xox[abp]-[A-Za-z0-9-]{10,}|fal-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,})/g;`
The file was added in this batch (eedc340, 61 insertions).

2. The repo's own fal and Bearer shapes:
- packages/gateway/src/redact.ts:15 `/[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}:[0-9a-f]{32,}/gi`
- packages/gateway/src/redact.ts:16 `/Bearer\s+[A-Za-z0-9._~+/-]{16,}=*/g`
- packages/gateway/src/held-key.ts:23 "a FAL_KEY (`<key id>:<secret>`)"
- packages/gateway/test/billed.test.ts:19, a FAL_KEY fixture in uuid:hex32 form
- packages/gateway/src/env.ts:10 `fal: 'FAL_KEY'`

3. The regex alone, run with node on fake values (C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/sec1-verify/re.mjs):
- missed: `FAL_KEY=<uuid>:<hex32>`, `Authorization: Key <uuid>:<hex32>`, the hex secret alone, `Authorization: Bearer eyJ…` JWT, `github_pat_…`
- caught: `Bearer sk-proj-…`, `sk-or-v1-<64hex>`, `sk-ant-api03-` followed by 95 characters, `AIzaSy…`, `gho_` followed by 36 characters

4. The real test, run in my own clone (C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/check-clones/sec1-refute at 481b2e5, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --offline`):
- Baseline: `pnpm exec vitest run scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts` gave "Tests 3 passed (3)".
- With docs/adr/evidence/logs/planted-sec1.log holding the fake fal env line, the fal `Authorization: Key` header, a Bearer JWT and a github_pat_ token: "Tests 3 passed (3)".
- Control: one `OPENAI_API_KEY=[a key-shaped string]` line in a .log file under the same folder gave "Tests 1 failed | 2 passed (3)" and named the planted file. So the test does read .log files, and only the shapes are missing.
- I removed the planted files afterwards; `git status` in the clone is clean.

5. Current evidence, scanned at the fx-batch worktree with match counts only (no values printed). Zero matches for each of: uuid:hex32 (either case), `Bearer\s+…{16,}`, `github_pat_…{20,}`, `eyJ….…` JWT, `Key <uuid>`, `fal-…{16,}`. No file under docs/adr/evidence names FAL_KEY. No secret is present now, and the suggested patterns would raise no false positive on today's evidence.

6. No other guard. The repo has no other secret scan: verify.yml has no gitleaks or trufflehog step, and no other test under scripts/test scans files for key shapes.

**Fix:**

In scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts line 52, replace the invented `fal-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,}` branch with:
- redact.ts's fal shape `[0-9a-fA-F]{8}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{12}:[0-9a-fA-F]{32,}`
- `Bearer\s+[A-Za-z0-9._~+/-]{16,}`
- `github_pat_[A-Za-z0-9_]{20,}`
- `eyJ[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}`

The leading `\b` sits before the alternation and still works for all four. None of these patterns matches anything in today's evidence, so the test stays green on this head. Sharing one list with redact.ts would stop the two from drifting, but KEY_SHAPES is not exported, so that needs a small export from @byway/gateway. It is optional. To show the fix works, plant one fake of each shape in a temporary file under docs/adr/evidence and see the test fail.

**Refuter 2: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I could not refute it. The test says the evidence holds "no key-shaped string" (adr-evidence.test.ts:9 and :50) and has a branch meant for fal. That branch, `fal-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,}`, matches a shape fal does not issue. The repo itself says fal keys look like `<key id>:<secret>` in three places: packages/gateway/src/redact.ts:8 and :15, packages/gateway/src/held-key.ts:23-24, and the FAL_KEY fixture in packages/gateway/test/billed.test.ts. FAL_KEY is one of the five provider keys byway holds (packages/gateway/src/env.ts:10). So the check misses one of the owner's real keys. It also misses the key's 32-hex secret half on its own, and fal's `Authorization: Key <id>:<secret>` header.

Nothing in the records makes this a chosen behaviour or a recorded limit:
- The owner's choice for this batch was only "Records + evidence + B1 together (Recommended)" (conversation-continued.md:413). It says nothing about which key shapes the check covers.
- No ADR, Later note or README line mentions adr-evidence.test.ts.
- The backlog's only secret-related row is the renderer environment allowlist (backlog.md:58), which is a different topic.
- The README says keys never enter the repository (README.md:238, ADR-0009) and the CI comment says tests "fail if one leaks", so a guard this weak is a real gap, not an intended one.
- No other secret scanner runs: there is no gitleaks or trufflehog, and CI only runs `pnpm verify`.

The file walk is recursive and checks every file (scripts/test/docs.ts:15-26, called with pattern /./), so a fal-shaped key planted anywhere under docs/adr/evidence would pass.

Why should-fix and not blocking: a scan of all 161 tracked evidence files with redact.ts's shapes (plus JWT, github_pat_, sk-or- and sk-) found nothing. So this push leaks no secret; only the guard for future evidence imports is weaker than the test claims.

Corrections to the finding:
- The regex is at line 52, not 62.
- The planted `[a key-shaped string]` proves nothing. Real Anthropic keys are about 100 characters and do match `sk-(?:ant-)?…{16,}`.
- Bearer tokens from byway's own providers are already caught: OpenAI and OpenRouter send `Bearer sk-…`, which the sk- branch matches. The missing Bearer, JWT and `github_pat_` patterns are hardening for tokens that are not byway's keys. Adding them is consistent with redact.ts but is not the core defect. The core defect is the fal branch, which can never match a real fal key.

**Evidence:**

- scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts:52: `const key = /\b(?:sk-(?:ant-)?[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,}|AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{20,}|gh[pousr]_[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}|xox[abp]-[A-Za-z0-9-]{10,}|fal-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,})/g`. It was added in eedc340 (the fx-evidence branch) and is unchanged in 481b2e5.
- packages/gateway/src/redact.ts:8 says "fal.ai `<key id>:<secret>`, and bearer tokens", and :15 has `/[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}:[0-9a-f]{32,}/gi`.
- held-key.ts:23-24 says "a FAL_KEY (`<key id>:<secret>`)".
- billed.test.ts fixture: `FAL_KEY: '[a key-shaped string]'`.
- env.ts:10: `fal: 'FAL_KEY'`.
- I ran the test's exact regex in node on fakes (no file changed):
  - Missed: falKey (`FAL_KEY=<uuid>:<hex32>`), falHeader (`Authorization: Key <uuid>:<hex32>`), an opaque Bearer, a JWT, github_pat_.
  - Caught: Bearer sk-proj, sk-ant-api03 (long), sk-or-v1, AIza, gho_, and the invented `fal-` shape.
- A masked scan of docs/adr/evidence (161 files, the same count as `git ls-files`) with the fal, Bearer, JWT, github_pat_, sk-or- and sk- shapes returned "hits 0".
- No ADR, backlog or README text refers to adr-evidence.test.ts or records a limit on its key shapes. .github/workflows/verify.yml runs no secret scanner.

**Fix:**

1. In scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts:52, replace the invented `fal-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,}` with fal's real shape: `[0-9a-fA-F]{8}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{12}:[0-9a-fA-F]{32,}`. Write the hex classes in both cases because the regex has no /i flag.
2. Add redact.ts's Bearer shape, `Bearer\s+[A-Za-z0-9._~+/-]{16,}=*`. Optionally add `github_pat_[A-Za-z0-9_]{20,}` and a JWT shape.
3. Add a small self-check in the same test: the regex must match one fake of each provider's key shape, including billed.test.ts's FAL_KEY fixture form. Then the pattern list cannot silently miss a held key again, with no need to plant files under docs/adr/evidence.
4. Optional: export KEY_SHAPES from @byway/gateway and reuse it here, so the redactor and the evidence check share one list.

#### MERGE-1: Playbook still says 37 Delegated records; after the merge the index counts 38

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed; lens merge; where `docs/playbook.md:49`.*

**What is wrong:**

Each side changed the Delegated count, and neither the merge nor 481b2e5 recomputed the playbook's figure. fix/fx-records moved ADR-0100 from Delegated to User-approved and changed the playbook from 38 to 37. fix/fx-b1 added ADR-0109 as Delegated but never touched the playbook. The merge c6bdc8b recomputed the index count line to 38 delegated, but the playbook kept the records branch's 37. The playbook now disagrees with the index by one, and the record it leaves out is the new money-path record.

**Evidence:**

481b2e5:docs/playbook.md:49 "| **Review the 37 Delegated records** ..."; 481b2e5:docs/adr/README.md:13 "**109 records:** 102 accepted (15 of them by continuation, awaiting ratification; 38 delegated) and 7 proposed.". Per branch: `git show af3bf5d:docs/playbook.md` gives "Review the 37 Delegated" (that branch's index: 37 delegated). `git show 88853f5:docs/playbook.md` gives "Review the 38 Delegated", while 88853f5's index reads "107 records: 100 accepted (...; 39 delegated)". My script parsed the index at 481b2e5: 38 Accepted rows have Decided by exactly "Delegated", one of them ADR-0109 (header: "Delegated: Claude built backlog batch B1 ..."). No test compares the playbook with the index.

**When it goes wrong:**

The owner works through the playbook row "Review the 37 Delegated records" and stops at 37. ADR-0109 (Delegated, "not explicitly approved; reversible") is the money-path record before the first live run, and it is the one the count leaves out.

**Proposed fix:**

Change the playbook row to "Review the 38 Delegated records". Consider naming ADR-0109 next to the records the row already lists first, since it governs the first live run's money path.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding reproduces exactly. I parsed the ADR index table at every relevant commit. I counted Accepted rows whose Decided-by cell is exactly "Delegated" and set that against the playbook's "Review the N Delegated" figure:
- main 8401820: index 38, playbook 38. They agree.
- fix/fx-records af3bf5d: index 37 (ADR-0100 is now User-approved), playbook 37. They agree.
- fix/fx-b1 88853f5: index 39 (ADR-0109 added as Delegated), playbook 38. Already one short on the branch itself, because B1 never touched docs/playbook.md (`git diff --stat 8401820..88853f5 -- docs/playbook.md` is empty).
- merge c6bdc8b: index 38, playbook 37. The merge recomputed the README counts line but took the records branch's playbook.
- head 481b2e5: index 38, playbook 37. 481b2e5 changed docs/adr/README.md but not docs/playbook.md.

At head the Accepted Decided-by tally is 15 By continuation, 38 Delegated, 20 User and 29 User-approved. That matches the counts line "102 accepted (15 ...; 38 delegated)". ADR-0109's row reads "| Accepted | Delegated | 2026-10-18 21:30 |". ADR-0100's row reads "User-approved". So the record the playbook's 37 leaves out is the new money-path record ADR-0109.

No test guards this. adr-records.test.ts:162 counts delegated rows only to check the README counts line. ci.test.ts reads the playbook only for the "Public record for the charter" row (lines 330-342).

One correction to the finding's framing: the drift is not purely a merge artifact. fix/fx-b1 was already inconsistent on its own branch (index 39 vs playbook 38), and the merge carried the error over.

On severity: the failure scenario ("stops at 37") is softer than stated. The playbook row does not list the records; it points the owner to the "ADR index", which shows all 38 Delegated rows including 0109. Even so, the owner's to-do list states the wrong scope by one, and the record it misses governs the first live run's money path. Every earlier commit kept the two numbers equal. So this is a real defect to fix in this batch, but it does not block the push.

**Evidence:**

Command run (bash, in .worktrees/fx-batch): for each of 8401820, af3bf5d, 88853f5, c6bdc8b and 481b2e5, it parsed the index rows of docs/adr/README.md (Status column starts with Accepted, Decided-by is exactly "Delegated") and grepped "Review the N Delegated" in docs/playbook.md. Output:
8401820: "106 records ... 38 delegated", parsed 38, playbook "Review the 38 Delegated"
af3bf5d: "108 records ... 37 delegated", parsed 37, playbook "Review the 37 Delegated"
88853f5: "107 records ... 39 delegated", parsed 39, playbook "Review the 38 Delegated"
c6bdc8b: "109 records ... 38 delegated", parsed 38, playbook "Review the 37 Delegated"
481b2e5: "109 records ... 38 delegated", parsed 38, playbook "Review the 37 Delegated"

Tally of Accepted Decided-by at 481b2e5: 15 "By continuation ⚑", 38 Delegated, 20 User, 29 User-approved.

Index rows at 481b2e5:
- "| [ADR-0109](0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md) | ... | Accepted | Delegated | 2026-10-18 21:30 |"
- "| [ADR-0100](...) | ... | Accepted; changed by [ADR-0109](...) | User-approved | 2026-10-16 05:45 |"

Playbook at 481b2e5, docs/playbook.md:49: "| **Review the 37 Delegated records** ... | ADR index, ADR-0105 (\"Who decided what\"), ADR-0107 |"

History: `git log 8401820..481b2e5 -- docs/playbook.md` shows only dca74a7 (records branch). `git diff --stat 8401820..88853f5 -- docs/playbook.md` is empty. `git diff c6bdc8b 481b2e5 --stat -- docs/playbook.md docs/adr/README.md` lists only README.md.

Tests: scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts:162 counts delegated rows against the README counts line only. scripts/test/ci.test.ts:330-342 reads only the playbook's "Public record for the charter" row. No test compares the playbook figure with the index.

**Fix:**

In docs/playbook.md:49, change "**Review the 37 Delegated records**" to "**Review the 38 Delegated records**". Optionally, name ADR-0109 (the money path before the first live run) among the records the row says to review first; that addition is Claude's suggestion, not an owner decision. Optionally, add an assertion in scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts that the playbook's "Review the N Delegated" equals the parsed delegated count, so the two cannot drift again.

**Refuter 2: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I tried to refute it three ways and none worked.

1. Is the playbook figure meant to match the index? Yes. The row's Source column reads "ADR index". On main, and on each branch that changed it, the playbook figure had matched that branch's own index: main 38/38, fix/fx-records 37/37.

2. Is ADR-0109 really Delegated, which would make 38 right? Yes. Its header says "Delegated: Claude built backlog batch B1 ... not explicitly approved; reversible." Its index row says Delegated. The owner's recorded choice (conversation-continued.md:409/413, "Records + evidence + B1 together (Recommended)") chose which batch to build and how to bundle it. It did not approve B1's design choices, so labelling the record Delegated is honest.

3. Is there an owner choice or recorded limit that makes 37 intentional? No. ADR-0100 moved to User-approved on the records branch (index line 142), which explains that branch's 38 to 37. B1 then added a Delegated record and never touched the playbook. On its own branch, B1's playbook already disagreed with its index (38 vs 39). Git merged the playbook without a conflict, because only fix/fx-records changed it. So the merge kept 37 while the hand-recomputed index line says 38. 481b2e5 did not touch it either.

No test compares the two. The index count-line check added in 37b0f56 checks only the README against its own rows, and scripts/test/ci.test.ts reads only the playbook's "Public record for the charter" row.

On severity, should-fix and not blocking. The failure scenario is a little overstated. The playbook does not list which 37 records, and its Source column sends the owner to the index, where all 38 Delegated rows (ADR-0109 among them) are listed. So the owner is unlikely to skip ADR-0109 specifically. Still, it is a wrong number in the owner's own to-do list, it came from this batch's merge, and it costs one character to fix. It does not touch money, behaviour or the owner's words, so it does not block the push.

**Evidence:**

Commits traced with `git show <c>:docs/playbook.md | grep 'Review the N Delegated'` and the index count line:

| Commit | Playbook says | Index says |
|---|---|---|
| main 8401820 | 38 | 106 records, 38 delegated |
| fix/fx-records af3bf5d | 37 | 108 records, 37 delegated |
| fix/fx-evidence f77cc6f | 38 | 106 records, 38 delegated |
| fix/fx-b1 88853f5 | 38 | 107 records, 39 delegated (already off by one on that branch) |
| merge 8b16f18 | 37 | 37 |
| merge 0b9dbcc | 37 | 37 |
| merge c6bdc8b | 37 | 109 records, 102 accepted, 38 delegated |
| head 481b2e5 | 37 | 38 |

An awk count of the index rows at 481b2e5 gives 102 Accepted rows, 38 of them "Delegated". The last of these is ADR-0109.

- ADR-0109 header: "**Decided by** | Delegated: Claude built backlog batch B1 ... not explicitly approved; reversible."
- Index line 142: ADR-0100 reads "User-approved".
- `git diff 8401820..481b2e5 -- docs/playbook.md` shows only the records branch's 38 to 37 edit on line 49.
- `git grep` finds no other place that states the delegated count, apart from the index and an old review note.
- In scripts/test, only ci.test.ts:330 reads the playbook, and only its charter row.

**Fix:**

In docs/playbook.md line 49, change "**Review the 37 Delegated records**" to "**Review the 38 Delegated records**". Optionally, name ADR-0109 in that row (for example "and ADR-0109, the money path before the first live run"), because it governs the first live run. That addition is Claude's suggestion, not something the owner decided. Optionally, add a check to scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts that the playbook's "Review the N Delegated records" equals the index's delegated count. That would stop a later merge from repeating this drift.

#### MERGE-2: Five backlog triggers written on fix/fx-records point at rows the batch closed

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed-partly; lens merge; where `docs/backlog.md:76`.*

**What is wrong:**

The records branch (489483e/af3bf5d) gave open rows triggers that depend on other rows. The other two branches then did that work, and 481b2e5 removed those rows. Nobody updated the rows whose triggers named them, so five open rows now point at rows that are gone. One trigger, "Pick up with the describe-first row", has already fired: B1 built describe-first and the row was neither picked up nor given a new trigger. ADR-0109 says that double fault "is unchanged".

**Evidence:**

At 481b2e5: line 76, "Run start: reopen a run whose workflow never started (P3 double fault)": "Pick up with the describe-first row, or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)". The describe-first row "Run start: describe the workflow before marking a run failed (P3 double fault)" was removed in 481b2e5 (B1 hand-off b1[1], "Done on fix/fx-b1 (22f521b ...)"). Line 99, "Route branches in the run-order claims": "once the claims oracle is in the suite (its row)". Line 105, "directionsReactedTo and critiqueSources ...": "once the claims oracle is in the suite". Line 116, "promptShows means \"shown on some run\"": "Revisit if the claims oracle (its row) finds ...". The row "Port the claims oracle into the runner suite" was removed in 481b2e5, because 24e69f5 ported it. Line 113, "C1-H's seed build consumes the voice deck ...": "Decide before Study 1's prompt registration, with the C1-H parent pin (its row)". The row "Pin C1-H's parent hash before Study 1's prompt registration" was removed in 481b2e5 (d98d5fa/ac83bac). Each of these trigger texts first appears at af3bf5d; none is at 8401820. My row-level diff of c6bdc8b..481b2e5 shows 8 rows removed and 11 changed, and none of these five is among the changed.

**When it goes wrong:**

Someone reading row 76 waits for "the describe-first row", which no longer exists and was done on 2026-10-18, so the P3 double fault has no live trigger, against the records branch's "every open row says when it comes back". Rows 99, 113 and 116 send the reader to rows that are gone. Row 105's condition is already met, so it says less than it seems to.

**Proposed fix:**

Restate the five triggers. For row 76, say that describe-first landed in B1 (ADR-0109) and give the remaining trigger. For rows 99, 105 and 116, say that the claims oracle runs in scripts/test/claims-oracle and give the remaining condition. For row 113, say that C1-H's parent has been pinned since 2026-10-18 (C1H_PARENT_HASH), so the deadline is Study 1's prompt registration alone.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I checked every factual claim with read-only git and each one holds. One phrase in the failure scenario goes too far, and I correct it below.

1. All five rows keep triggers that name rows which are now gone. The triggers were added on the records branch (none is at 8401820, all are at af3bf5d). At c6bdc8b, the commit before the hand-off, all three target rows still exist: "Run start: describe the workflow before marking a run failed (P3 double fault)" (line 77), "Port the claims oracle into the runner suite" (line 109) and "Pin C1-H's parent hash before Study 1's prompt registration" (line 166). 481b2e5 deletes all three and does not touch the five rows that name them.

2. The work those rows asked for did land in the batch:
   - describe-first: 22f521b, ADR-0109 item 3.
   - the claims oracle: 24e69f5, now scripts/test/claims-oracle.test.ts. vitest.config.ts includes 'scripts/test/**/*.test.ts', so the condition "once the claims oracle is in the suite" is already met.
   - the C1-H parent pin: d98d5fa and ac83bac, C1H_PARENT_HASH at packages/catalog/src/c1h.ts:22.

3. ADR-0109:77 says: "The first (a refused Start pressed again gets that failed run back) is unchanged." That is row 76's double fault. ADR-0109 gives no reason for leaving it and does not point to row 76. So the moment row 76 planned to be picked up ("with the describe-first row") came and went without a record, and the backlog row is the only place the problem is tracked.

4. These five are the only broken references. I listed every "(its row)" reference in the backlog at head. All the others resolve: C2 mood boards line 86, macOS line 146, planning numbers line 156, content layer line 118, hosted byway line 185, Gemini thinking-token row line 65.

5. The integrator was watching for this kind of reference but missed these. 481b2e5's message says "The records builder's conditional edits fall away with the rows they were for" and that it fixed "three test comments that named closed rows".

The correction: the failure scenario says the P3 double fault "has no live trigger". It does have one. Row 76's second condition still applies: "or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down". It only fires if someone reports the problem, but it is a trigger. So row 76 is out of date, not orphaned. Rows 99, 105 and 116 are dangling references whose conditions are already met, and row 113's "with the C1-H parent pin (its row)" names finished work. On their own, those four would be notes. Row 76 is what makes this should-fix: the batch passed the row's planned pickup point, and neither the row nor ADR-0109 says so. The batch caused the inconsistency and it takes five text edits to fix, but nothing here affects money, behaviour, the owner's words or a test, so it does not block the push.

**Evidence:**

- `git show 481b2e5:docs/backlog.md`, the five triggers as they stand:
  - line 76: "Pick up with the describe-first row, or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)"
  - line 99: "once the claims oracle is in the suite (its row)"
  - line 105: "once the claims oracle is in the suite"
  - line 113: "with the C1-H parent pin (its row)"
  - line 116: "Revisit if the claims oracle (its row) finds..."
- The same trigger grep at 8401820 finds nothing for these five rows (the rows exist there, but without these triggers). At af3bf5d it finds all five.
- `git diff c6bdc8b 481b2e5 -U0 -- docs/backlog.md` deletes the target rows at old lines 77 (describe-first), 109 (Port the claims oracle) and 166 (Pin C1-H's parent hash). It has no hunk at old lines 76, 100, 106, 116 or 119.
- `git grep C1H_PARENT_HASH 481b2e5` finds packages/catalog/src/c1h.ts:22 and :38.
- vitest.config.ts includes 'scripts/test/**/*.test.ts'. 24e69f5's message says the oracle "runs with the unit tests".
- ADR-0109 line 77 at 481b2e5 is quoted in the reasoning above. ADR-0109 lines 35-70 (Decision item 3, Alternatives, Consequences) give no reason for leaving the first double fault and do not cite row 76.
- 481b2e5's commit message: "The records builder's conditional edits fall away with the rows they were for" and "three test comments that named closed rows".

**Fix:**

Edit docs/backlog.md in the batch, before the --no-ff merge (the backlog is not under ADR-0103's log rule). Keep each "(trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)" label, and mark any new wording as Claude's.

- Row 76: say that describe-first landed in B1 (ADR-0109 item 3, 22f521b) without reopening a never-started run, and that ADR-0109 records this double fault as unchanged. Keep the second condition ("if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down"). Optionally add a proactive trigger such as "at the next change to Runner.start", labelled as proposed by Claude.
- Rows 99 and 105: replace "once the claims oracle is in the suite (its row)" with something like "the claims oracle has run with the unit tests since 2026-10-18 (scripts/test/claims-oracle.test.ts)". Row 99's trigger is then the next change to canPrecede or canRunAfter, and row 105's the next change to those claims.
- Row 116: replace "the claims oracle (its row)" with "the claims oracle (scripts/test/claims-oracle)".
- Row 113: replace "with the C1-H parent pin (its row)" with a note that C1-H's parent has been pinned since 2026-10-18 (C1H_PARENT_HASH in packages/catalog/src/c1h.ts). The deadline is then Study 1's prompt registration alone.

**Refuter 2: partly, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

The headline holds. Five open rows in docs/backlog.md at 481b2e5 have triggers that name rows the batch closed. The records builder wrote those triggers in 489483e. Its own commit message says "Rows owned by the B1 and evidence builders are untouched", so it pointed at rows that other branches were about to close. The integration commit 481b2e5 then removed the target rows and missed the references to them.

The miss is a slip, not a choice. The same commit rewrote three test comments that named closed rows (profile.test.ts, start-describe.test.ts, adr-evidence.test.ts). It also updated the status page's F03 cell to say ADR-0109 fixed the other double fault. So the integrator meant to keep references to closed rows current and skipped these backlog rows.

Nothing in the records makes the stale text acceptable:
- All five triggers are marked "(trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)". They are not the owner's words, so restating them breaks neither the verbatim-quote rule nor the no-added-gates rule.
- docs/backlog.md is not covered by ADR-0103's rule that Context and Decision are final.
- The backlog keeps no Done section: closed rows are deleted. So "(its row)" now points at nothing.

The part I refute is the failure scenario's strongest claim, that "the P3 double fault has no live trigger". Row 76's trigger is a disjunction, and its second branch still stands: "or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down". Each of the other four also keeps a live condition once the stale clause is removed:
- Row 99: the next change to canPrecede or canRunAfter.
- Row 105: the next change to these claims in semantics.ts.
- Row 113: before Study 1's prompt registration.
- Row 116: the oracle finding such a claim. This can still happen: the ported oracle has a RENDERED check, oracle.ts:52 and :454.

So the harm is stale or dangling pointers, not rows left without a trigger. Rows 99 and 105 also misstate the current state, because "once the claims oracle is in the suite" implies it is not. It is: vitest.config.ts:6 includes scripts/test/**/*.test.ts.

Row 76 also lost something real. Its first branch ("Pick up with the describe-first row") came due in this batch and was missed. B1 built describe-first without seeing the trigger, which was on another branch. ADR-0109:77 records that the first double fault "is unchanged". B1 added no "never started" marker to the run record: client.ts only varies the error message through startFailureMessage(e, workflow). So the row is rightly still open, but its trigger text is wrong.

I found one more stale pointer of the same kind that the finding missed. The status page's F18 cell (docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:30) was added on the records branch (it appears at af3bf5d, not at 8401820). It still lists "secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6)" among the open rows. B1 closed that row (d4bd96b; 481b2e5 removed it), and 481b2e5 changed only the F01 and F03 cells of that page.

Severity: should-fix stands. These are wrong statements of state in the batch's own working records, created by the merge, and cheap to fix in this batch. They are not blocking: no money, no owner words, no ADR rule, and no row is left without a working trigger.

Checked:
- Row text at 8401820, af3bf5d, 22f521b, 88853f5, f77cc6f, c6bdc8b and 481b2e5.
- The 481b2e5 diff and commit message.
- The builders' hand-off edits (scratchpad batch-build-result.json). The records builder's own edits to closed rows were conditional ("Only if B1 leaves the row open") and correctly fell away. No builder handed off an edit for rows 76, 99, 105, 113 or 116.
- ADR-0109 lines 16, 48 and 77.
- client.ts and start-failure.ts.
- The claims oracle's location and its RENDERED check.
- vitest.config.ts includes scripts/test.
- No test checks backlog cross-references.

Not checked: other docs outside docs/backlog.md, the status page, the ADR index and the playbook, for references to the eight closed rows.

**Evidence:**

Rows that are gone at 481b2e5 but present at c6bdc8b:
- "Run start: describe the workflow before marking a run failed": c6bdc8b line 77.
- "Port the claims oracle into the runner suite": c6bdc8b line 109.
- "Pin C1-H's parent hash before Study 1's prompt registration": c6bdc8b line 166.

These come from `git show <rev>:docs/backlog.md | grep "^| ..."` run on each revision. The rows that still point at them, at 481b2e5:
- docs/backlog.md:76: "Pick up with the describe-first row, or if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)".
- docs/backlog.md:99: "once the claims oracle is in the suite (its row)".
- docs/backlog.md:105: "once the claims oracle is in the suite".
- docs/backlog.md:113: "Decide before Study 1's prompt registration, with the C1-H parent pin (its row)".
- docs/backlog.md:116: "Revisit if the claims oracle (its row) finds a claim that rests on promptShows false on a run".

At 8401820 none of these five rows has a trigger. The triggers came from 489483e, whose message says "Rows owned by the B1 and evidence builders are untouched".

In 481b2e5's own words, its "conditional edits fall away with the rows they were for". It fixed "three test comments that named closed rows", for example start-describe.test.ts, now "B1 (ADR-0109, which closed the backlog rows ...)". It also changed only the F01 and F03 cells of the status page.

The integrator knew the double fault's state. Status page line 15 reads: "the run-start double fault that reopens a run whose workflow never started (ADR-0109 fixed the other ...". ADR-0109:77 reads: "The first (a refused Start pressed again gets that failed run back) is unchanged."

client.ts still adds no "never started" marker. On a failed start it calls `await store.updateRun(id, { status: 'failed', error: startFailureMessage(e, workflow) })`.

The claims oracle is in the suite:
- vitest.config.ts:6 includes 'scripts/test/**/*.test.ts'.
- scripts/test/claims-oracle/oracle.ts:52 reads "RENDERED: the template slots the real library declares, read independently of @byway/recipe's promptShows".

The extra stale pointer is docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:30 (F18), whose "Rows:" list still includes "secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6)". That text is absent at 8401820 and present at af3bf5d and 481b2e5. B1's hand-off removed that row: "Done on fix/fx-b1 (d4bd96b ...)".

**Fix:**

Restate the five triggers and keep each one's "(trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)" label.

- **Row 76:** remove "Pick up with the describe-first row, or". Say that describe-first landed in B1 (ADR-0109) without the never-started marker, and keep "if a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down" as the trigger.
- **Rows 99 and 105:** remove "once the claims oracle is in the suite (its row)" and "once the claims oracle is in the suite". Where useful, say the oracle runs in scripts/test/claims-oracle since 2026-10-18.
- **Row 116:** replace "the claims oracle (its row)" with "the claims oracle (scripts/test/claims-oracle)".
- **Row 113:** remove "with the C1-H parent pin (its row)". The parent has been pinned since 2026-10-18 (C1H_PARENT_HASH), so the deadline is Study 1's prompt registration alone.
- **Status page, docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:30 (F18):** take "secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6)" out of the open-rows list, or mark it closed by B1 (ADR-0109).

#### MERGE-3: Status page F18 still lists the lowercase-drive secrets-file row that B1 fixed, and does not cite ADR-0109

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed; lens merge; where `docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:30`.*

**What is wrong:**

481b2e5 updated the status page's F01 and F03 rows for the batch, but not F18. F18's "What remains" still names the backlog row for secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive start. B1 fixed that (ADR-0109 item 4), and 481b2e5 removed the row from the backlog. F18's Records column also leaves out 0109, although the index now lists ADR-0084 as changed by ADR-0109. The page's header says "updated 19 Oct 2026".

**Evidence:**

481b2e5:docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:30: "| F18 | Renderer Chromium runs unsandboxed | ... | [0084](...), 0105, [0106](...), 0100, 0090, [0103](...) | ... Rows: what D2 left; what the late-close fix left; secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6); ...". The row "Secrets-file names independent of module identity" was removed in 481b2e5 (B1 hand-off b1[2], "Done on fix/fx-b1 (d4bd96b ...)"). README.md:142 now says Chromium gets no name from `.env.agents` on a lowercase-drive start either (ADR-0109). The ADR-0084 index row reads "changed by [ADR-0100], [ADR-0105], [ADR-0109]". The 481b2e5 message: "The status page's F01 and F03 rows".

**When it goes wrong:**

The owner reads the status page and believes that a lowercase-drive Windows start still leaks the secrets file's other names into Chromium, and that a backlog row tracks it. Neither is true after the batch.

**Proposed fix:**

In F18, drop "secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6)" from the remaining rows, or replace it with "fixed by ADR-0109 item 4", and add [0109](../adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md) to the Records column.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding is correct. At head 481b2e5, the F18 row on the status page still lists, under "Rows:", "secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6)". Its Records column does not include 0109.

- **The defect is fixed.** B1 moved the names map in packages/gateway/src/env.ts onto globalThis under Symbol.for('byway.gateway.env-file-names'). ADR-0109 item 4 records the fix, and ADR-0100 has a later note of 2026-10-18 saying item 6's defect is fixed. README.md:142 and the product spec (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-product-v1-design.md:42) now say Chromium gets no name from .env.agents on a lowercase-drive start either.
- **The fix holds on this machine.** I ran scripts/test/renderer-env.test.ts in the worktree on win32 and it passed: 1 test ran, none skipped. It starts a child from the lowercase spelling of the repository's directory and asserts two copies of the gateway were loaded while chromiumEnv is only {PATH}.
- **The backlog row is gone.** The row "Secrets-file names independent of module identity" was removed in 481b2e5 (git log -S finds only that commit in the range). A grep of the backlog at head for "lowercase", "module identity" and "Secrets-file names" finds nothing.
- **Only F01 and F03 were updated.** 481b2e5's change to the status page edits the header ("updated 19 Oct 2026"), F01 and F03, and adds 0109 to F03's Records only. F18's text is unchanged from 31bdb62.
- **The page's own claim is now false for this item.** Its intro says "The residuals it names are [backlog] rows, each with what brings it back." No backlog row exists for this one, and the defect it describes no longer happens.
- **No test catches it.** No test in scripts, packages or apps reads review-fix-status.md.

On severity, should-fix is right. The stale text makes the page say less than main holds, which is the safe direction, not more. It is still a false statement on a page this batch touched and dated, and it disagrees with the ADR-0084 index row ("changed by ADR-0100, ADR-0105, ADR-0109"), the README and the spec. It is not blocking: no money, behaviour, owner quote or ADR rule is affected.

**Evidence:**

- docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:30 at 481b2e5, F18 row. Records: "[0084](...), 0105, [0106](...), 0100, 0090, [0103](...)". What remains: "... Rows: what D2 left; what the late-close fix left; secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6); the network service at Medium integrity; ..."
- `git show 481b2e5 -- docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md` changes only the header line, F01 and F03 (F03 gains "[0109](../adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md)").
- `git diff 8401820..481b2e5 -- docs/backlog.md` removes the row "| Secrets-file names independent of module identity | loadEnvFile keeps the .env.agents names in a module-level map. ... (ADR-0100 item 6) ...". `git log 8401820..481b2e5 -S"Secrets-file names independent of module identity" -- docs/backlog.md` gives only 481b2e5. A grep of docs/backlog.md at head for lowercase, module identity or "Secrets-file names" finds nothing.
- packages/gateway/src/env.ts at head: `const LISTED_BY_FILE = Symbol.for('byway.gateway.env-file-names'); const listedByFile ... = (globalThis ...)[LISTED_BY_FILE] ??= new WeakMap()`.
- docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:41, item 4: "The secrets file's names are kept once per process ... ADR-0084's sentences on the renderer's environment hold on every start again, whatever the drive letter's case."
- docs/adr/0100-corrections-found-by-the-final-re-check.md:96, later note: "Item 6's defect is fixed".
- docs/adr/README.md:131, ADR-0084: "Accepted; changed by [ADR-0100], [ADR-0105], [ADR-0109]".
- `pnpm exec vitest run scripts/test/renderer-env.test.ts` in C:/Users/arvik/code/byway/.worktrees/fx-batch: "Test Files 1 passed (1) Tests 1 passed (1)", on win32, so it.runIf ran the test.
- No file under scripts, packages or apps references review-fix-status.

**Fix:**

Edit line 30 of docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md (the F18 row):
1. In "What remains", remove "secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6); " from the Rows list. Or, matching F03's wording, put after the list: "(ADR-0109 fixed the secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start, ADR-0100 item 6)".
2. Add "[0109](../adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md)" to the Records column after 0084.

**Refuter 2: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I tried to refute it on three grounds, and none holds.

(1) Was the defect really fixed, or is something left that the F18 row could still be naming? It was fixed. packages/gateway/src/env.ts:101-102 keeps the names on globalThis under Symbol.for('byway.gateway.env-file-names'). scripts/test/renderer-env.test.ts and its fixture renderer-env-twice.ts exist. ADR-0109 item 4 (line 41) says the ADR-0084 sentences "hold on every start again, whatever the drive letter's case". ADR-0100's Later note (line 96) says "Item 6's defect is fixed". The backlog row also asked to "drop the README and spec exception". README.md:142 now states the guarantee for lowercase-drive starts with no exception. The only lowercase-drive hit left in the spec is unrelated. Within docs and README at 481b2e5, the F18 cell is the only stale mention.

(2) Is the backlog row the page points to still there? No. `git log -S'Secrets-file names independent of module identity' 8401820..481b2e5 -- docs/backlog.md` shows that 481b2e5 removed it. The head backlog has no lowercase-drive row. The page's own intro says "The residuals it names are backlog rows, each with what brings it back", so the F18 cell now points to a row that does not exist.

(3) Is this something the owner chose, or a limit already filed? No. Nothing in the backlog or the ADRs keeps it as a residual. ADR-0109's section "What this changes in earlier records" lists ADR-0084 and ADR-0100 for item 4. The index row for ADR-0084 reads "changed by ADR-0100, ADR-0105, ADR-0109". F18's Records column cites 0084 and 0100 but not 0109. The 2026-10-18 trace corrected exactly this kind of omission ("The Records column left out records").

The integration commit also shows the miss was not deliberate. 481b2e5 changed the header to "updated 19 Oct 2026" and updated F03 for ADR-0109 ("ADR-0109 fixed the other"). Its message names only "The status page's F01 and F03 rows", and F18 was left as it was after e6fc542, from before B1 was merged.

Why should-fix and not blocking: the stale cell understates what main holds, which is the safe direction. It misstates no owner word or decision, breaks no ADR log rule (this is a review page, not an Accepted record), and touches no money or behaviour. It is still a false pointer on a page the owner reads, and the batch already edited this page to bring it up to date with B1, so it belongs in this batch.

A side observation, not part of this finding: ADR-0109 also changes ADR-0091 (F03/F04, the Gemini thinking-token consequence) and ADR-0092 (COMPOSED-MEDIUM, which F01 cites). F03 cites 0109; F01 and F04 do not. The same Records-column check may be worth running on those rows.

**Evidence:**

- 481b2e5:docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:30, the F18 row. Its Records cell is "[0084](...), 0105, [0106](...), 0100, 0090, [0103](...)", with no 0109. Its "What remains" cell includes "secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6)".
- `git show 481b2e5 -- docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md` changes only the header line ("updated 19 Oct 2026"), the F01 row and the F03 row (which adds [0109] and "ADR-0109 fixed the other"). F18 is not touched.
- `git log --oneline 8401820..481b2e5 -S'Secrets-file names independent of module identity' -- docs/backlog.md` gives "481b2e5 The batch's hand-offs...". That is the removal of the row "Secrets-file names independent of module identity | ... A Windows start from a lowercase-drive directory loads the gateway's env module twice ... then drop the README and spec exception".
- packages/gateway/src/env.ts:101 reads "const LISTED_BY_FILE = Symbol.for('byway.gateway.env-file-names');"; line 102 keeps the WeakMap on globalThis.
- docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:41 is item 4. Line 74 lists ADR-0084 under "What this changes in earlier records"; line 78 lists ADR-0100 ("item 6's 'Take as current' is replaced by item 4"). Line 94 names the renderer-env.test.ts test.
- docs/adr/0100-corrections-found-by-the-final-re-check.md:96 is the Later note: "Item 6's defect is fixed".
- docs/adr/README.md:131 is the ADR-0084 row: "Accepted; changed by [ADR-0100], [ADR-0105], [ADR-0109]".
- README.md:142 says Chromium's environment holds no name from `.env.agents` "on Windows also when byway is started from a current directory whose drive letter is lowercase ... ([ADR-0109])".
- `git grep -i lowercase 481b2e5 -- docs README.md apps/web/README.md ':!docs/adr/evidence'` finds no other stale claim. The spec's only hit is unrelated.

**Fix:**

Change one cell in docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:30 (F18):
- Records: add [0109](../adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md) after 0100 (for example "..., 0100, [0109](../adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md), 0090, ...").
- What remains: remove "secrets-file names reaching Chromium on a lowercase-drive Windows start (ADR-0100 item 6); " from the list of rows. Optionally add a clause in the style of F03: "(ADR-0109 fixed ADR-0100 item 6: Chromium gets no name from .env.agents on a lowercase-drive Windows start either)".
This must land in the same batch merge. The page is not an ADR, so the Later-notes rule does not apply.

#### EVID-PRIV-1: The D4 brief copied into the repository names the owner's other repositories and the org's GitHub billing, the details its header says were withheld

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed-partly; lens critic; where `docs/adr/evidence/decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md:1219`.*

**What is wrong:**

The file's own header (line 13) says two Temp files on "the GitHub account's state and Actions billing (which name the owner's other repositories), are not copied". The D4 brief and its skeptic, copied word for word below that header, carry the same data: three other repositories by name with their monthly Actions minutes, the org's September overage, and the org's plan and seat count. So the reason for withholding those two files does not hold, because their content entered the repository through the brief anyway. None of these names is on main (8401820). This batch is what brings them in.

**Evidence:**

git grep -n -w -E "[another repository]|[another repository]|[another repository]" at 481b2e5 finds only this file. Line 1105: "In September another repo ([another repository]) used the whole pool and the org paid [redacted] extra". Lines 1218-1221: "**Org Actions usage** (gh billing API; saved in github-actions-usage.txt)", "August: [another repository] used [redacted] Linux and [redacted] Windows minutes", "September: [another repository] used [redacted] Linux and [redacted] Windows minutes, and the org paid [redacted] extra", "October to date: [another repository] used [redacted] Linux minutes". Line 966: "The org is on [redacted] ([redacted], [redacted])". Line 1224: "The org plan is [redacted], with [redacted]." Line 1330 repeats the [redacted] overage. The same grep at 8401820 returns nothing. Security's lens checked owner emails, names and keys but not organisation or other-project data.

**When it goes wrong:**

The batch lands as one --no-ff merge. ADR-0103's "Protect main" ruleset blocks force pushes, so after the push these names and figures cannot be removed from main's history. Two readers are misled. One trusts the evidence index's statement that files naming other repositories were left out. The other is the owner, if a repository snapshot is ever published (ADR-0061 D12 plans DOI snapshots at milestones and a public mirror) or the repository is opened. In either case the owner's other projects and the org's billing go out with it, which the builder's own exclusion was meant to prevent.

**Proposed fix:**

Fix this before the merge, while the text is still off main. Option one: redact the three repository names (for example "another repository"), and optionally the org's plan, seat count and overage figures. Mark each redaction as Claude's in the italic note under the D4 heading, and change "The words are unchanged" in the header to say what was redacted and why. Option two: ask the owner whether these details may stay, and reword line 13 so it no longer implies they were kept out. The briefs are otherwise faithful (see checked).

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding is confirmed. I re-ran every check it cites, and the quotes, line numbers and grep results match exactly.

1. **The names come in only with this batch.** `git grep -n -i -w -E "[another repository]|[another repository]|[another repository]"` at 481b2e5 finds four lines, all in `docs/adr/evidence/decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md` (1105, 1219, 1220, 1221). The same grep at 8401820 finds nothing. `git cat-file -e 8401820:docs/adr/evidence/decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md` fails: the file does not exist on main. It was added by eedc340 on fix/fx-evidence.
2. **The org's plan and billing figures are also new.** A grep for `[redacted]|[redacted]|[redacted]|github-actions-usage|github-repo-state` finds lines 966, 1105, 1215, 1218, 1220, 1223, 1224 and 1330 of that file at head, and nothing at 8401820.
3. **The header's reason for leaving files out does not hold.** Line 13 says the two GitHub state and billing files "(which name the owner's other repositories), are not copied". The D4 tree confirms they are absent: `probes/decisions/D4-repo-admin` holds only `changed-by-count.mjs`, the `cr14-probe` files, `ff-check.mjs`, `time-gate.sh` and `timings.txt`. But the D4 brief's Evidence section (lines 1218-1224) is a summary of exactly those two files, by name ("saved in github-actions-usage.txt", "github-repo-state.txt"). It carries the three repository names with their minutes, the [redacted] overage, and "The org plan is [redacted], with [redacted]." Line 966 adds "[redacted]".
4. **Main's own record avoided these names.** ADR-0103:129 on 8401820 says only "[redacted]". It does not name [another repository]. So the records already on main kept the other repository anonymous, and this batch undoes that through the evidence file.
5. **The publication scenario is plausible but not certain.** ADR-0061 D12 plans "a public mirror of the research ADRs, Program Registry and deviation log, and DOI snapshots at milestones". The owner also chose "OSI licence, end of data collection" for open-sourcing the study engine (conversation.md:851). Whether `docs/adr/evidence` would ever be published is not decided. Even so, once the batch is on main's history it cannot be removed without lifting the "Protect main" ruleset (playbook.md:42).

One small nuance does not change the verdict. Read literally, line 13 is true: the files themselves were not copied. The defect is that its stated reason is contradicted by text copied in below it, which is what the finding's claim says.

Severity stays should-fix, not blocking:
- The data is not secret.
- The repository is private.
- The owner never ruled on it either way. I searched conversation.md and conversation-continued.md for other-repo, billing and privacy remarks and found none on this point.

It should still be fixed in this batch. The text is still off main, the fix is cheap, and the builder's own rule for leaving files out is broken.

The fix must also cover something the finding missed. Three Later notes added in this batch say the briefs are copied with "their text unchanged":
- ADR-0103:168, for the D4 brief and its challenge (checked: its `+` line is in the batch diff);
- ADR-0102:129, for D1;
- ADR-0105:166, for D2.

If D4 is redacted, the ADR-0103 note has to change too, or it will state something false. I found no test that pins the brief's text: `adr-evidence.test.ts`, `adr-log.ts` and `adr-records.test.ts` contain no hash of the file and no reference to it.

What I did not check:
- I did not open the Temp files `github-actions-usage.txt`, `github-repo-state.txt` or `owner-decisions.json`, so I did not confirm that the brief matches its source word for word.
- I did not check whether any other evidence file names the org's repositories under other spellings. My grep covered only the three names, case-insensitive, plus the `wavemanco/<repo>` pattern, which finds only `wavemanco/byway`.
- I could not check the claim about the security lens.

**Evidence:**

The names, at head and on main:
- `git grep -n -i -w -E "[another repository]|[another repository]|[another repository]" 481b2e5` returns only `decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md`, lines 1105, 1219, 1220 and 1221:
  - 1105: "In September another repo ([another repository]) used the whole pool and the org paid [redacted] extra"
  - 1219: "August: [another repository] used [redacted] Linux and [redacted] Windows minutes, all free."
  - 1220: "September: [another repository] used [redacted] Linux and [redacted] Windows minutes, and the org paid [redacted] extra."
  - 1221: "October to date: [another repository] used [redacted] Linux minutes, free."
- The same grep at 8401820 returns nothing (exit 1).

The plan and billing figures:
- Line 966: "The org is on [redacted] ([redacted], [redacted])".
- Line 1218: "**Org Actions usage** (gh billing API; saved in github-actions-usage.txt)".
- Line 1224: "The org plan is [redacted], with [redacted]."
- Line 1330 repeats the [redacted] overage.
- The pattern `[redacted]|[redacted]|[redacted]|github-actions-usage|github-repo-state` returns nothing at 8401820.

The header and the files it left out:
- Line 13: "Its logs, and two files on the GitHub account's state and Actions billing (which name the owner's other repositories), are not copied."
- Line 5: "The words are unchanged".
- `git ls-tree` of `D4-repo-admin` lists no `github-*.txt` file.

The file is new in this batch:
- `git cat-file -e 8401820:docs/adr/evidence/decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md` gives "fatal: path ... exists on disk, but not in '8401820'".
- `git log 8401820..481b2e5` on the file shows eedc340 (fix/fx-evidence).

Main already kept the repository anonymous:
- `8401820:docs/adr/0103-adr-log-rule-and-ci.md:129`: "[redacted]".

The "unchanged" claims in this batch:
- `ADR-0103:168` (a `+` line in the batch diff): "The D4 brief and its challenge ... are in evidence/decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md ..., their text unchanged".
- ADR-0102:129 and ADR-0105:166 say the same for D1 and D2.

Publication plans:
- ADR-0061:25 (D12): "a public mirror of the research ADRs, Program Registry and deviation log, and DOI snapshots at milestones".
- conversation.md:851, the owner's choice: "OSI licence, end of data collection".

**Fix:**

Fix this in this batch, before the merge, while the file is still off main.

1. In `docs/adr/evidence/decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md`, replace "[another repository]", "[another repository]" and "[another repository]" with "another repository" (or "one repository", "a second repository" and so on). This is needed at:
   - line 1105, "another repo ([another repository])", which becomes "another repo";
   - lines 1219-1221.
   Optionally also generalise the plan, seat count and price on lines 966 and 1224.
2. Add an italic Claude's note under the D4 heading saying what was redacted and why, labelled as Claude's.
3. Change "The words are unchanged" in line 5 and the line 13 sentence. They should say that the D4 brief's names of the org's other repositories are redacted, for the same reason the two GitHub files are left out.
4. Reword ADR-0103's new Later note (line 168, "their text unchanged"). It should say "their text unchanged except the other repositories' names, redacted". Otherwise the note will be false once it is on main.

The alternative is to ask the owner whether the names and figures may stay. In that case, only line 13 needs rewording, so that it no longer implies the names were kept out.

**Refuter 2: partly, note.**

**Reasoning:**

The facts in EVID-PRIV-1 hold. The batch brings the names of three other org repositories ([another repository], [another repository], [another repository]), their Actions minutes, the org's September overage of [redacted], and its plan and seat count onto main for the first time. Line 13 of the header gives "which name the owner's other repositories" as the reason two Temp files were not copied, but the D4 brief and skeptic copied below it carry almost all of those files' content. So the stated reason does not hold. I checked the excluded originals: the only things they hold that the brief does not are a fourth repo name ([another repository], [redacted]), the gross dollar figures, and two Actions settings fields (allowed_actions, sha_pinning_required). The builder did not add anything: the text is verbatim from owner-decisions.json entries 7 (analyse:D4) and 10 (challenge:D4).

What does not hold is the harm the finding claims and its should-fix severity:
(1) There is no reader today other than the owner. wavemanco/byway is PRIVATE. The org is a [redacted] org, and its Actions bill is the owner's own. All three named repos are PRIVATE too, so the names are not public anywhere.
(2) The data is the owner's own and is not a secret. It is not a key and not personal data. The owner already saw it: the D4 brief was put to the owner in chat on 2026-10-16.
(3) No owner decision asks to keep other projects or billing out of the repo. A grep of conversation.md and conversation-continued.md for privacy or other-project language finds nothing. The owner's chosen batch option was "move the Temp evidence into the repo (B2)" with no exclusion condition. The exclusion in line 13 was the builder's own choice, not the owner's.
(4) The finding's publication path is overstated. ADR-0061 D12's public mirror is scoped to "the research ADRs, Program Registry and deviation log", not docs/adr/evidence. DOI snapshots are tied to research-track milestones, and ADR-0108 (in this batch) parks the research track until the owner says otherwise.
(5) Line 13 is Claude's note in an evidence file, not the Context or Decision of an Accepted ADR, so it can be reworded at any time after the merge.

The one real asymmetry is that ADR-0103 line 59 says main's history "is not rewritten". If the owner would ever want these names out, now is the cheap moment. That is a question for the owner, not a defect that blocks the push.

What remains is a misleading parenthetical: the reason it gives for leaving the two files out is undone by the brief printed below it. That is a minor wording issue, so the severity is note. The finding also misses one thing. If the redaction it proposes (option one) were taken, ADR-0103's Later note at line 168 would also be wrong, because it says the D4 brief and challenge are in the evidence file with "their text unchanged". That Later note is new in this batch.

**Evidence:**

- `git grep -n -i -w -E "[another repository]|[another repository]|[another repository]"` matches only decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md at 481b2e5: lines 1105, 1219, 1220 and 1221. The same grep at 8401820 matches nothing. "[redacted]", "[redacted]" and "[redacted]" are also new to the repo, while the org name wavemanco is already on main (ADR-0060, ADR-0063, ADR-0103:139).
- Header line 13: "Its logs, and two files on the GitHub account's state and Actions billing (which name the owner's other repositories), are not copied."
- The excluded files (C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/byway-fixes/scratch/decisions/D4-repo-admin/):
  - github-actions-usage.txt (6 lines) names [another repository], [another repository], [another repository] and [another repository].
  - github-repo-state.txt (4 lines) holds "protected":false, rulesets 0, workflows 0, Actions enabled, allowed_actions all, sha_pinning_required false, and "org plan: [redacted], filled seats: [redacted]". Brief line 1224 restates almost all of it.
- A node walk of C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/byway-fixes/designs/owner-decisions.json finds the same strings at /7/r/today, /7/r/options/2/cons/4, /7/r/evidence and /10/r/reasoning, so the copy is verbatim.
- `gh repo view wavemanco/byway` returns {"isPrivate":true,"visibility":"PRIVATE"}. `gh repo list wavemanco` returns [another repository], [another repository] and [another repository], all PRIVATE.
- docs/adr/0061-study-1-technical-choices-accepted.md:25 (D12) reads "a public mirror of the research ADRs, Program Registry and deviation log, and DOI snapshots at milestones".
- ADR-0108 (new in this batch): "When the research track resumes is the owner's to say; the owner has not said".
- conversation-continued.md:409-413 shows the owner's choice "Records + evidence + B1 together (Recommended)", whose option text says "move the Temp evidence into the repo (B2)" with no exclusions. A grep of both conversation files for privacy or other-project wording finds nothing.
- docs/adr/0103-adr-log-rule-and-ci.md:59 reads "Main's history is not rewritten: the 'Protect main' ruleset (§8) blocks force pushes". scripts/test/ci.test.ts:327-328 records the ruleset as having no bypass actor.
- docs/adr/0103-adr-log-rule-and-ci.md:168 is a Later note added in this batch (the diff 8401820..481b2e5 shows it as a + line). It says the D4 brief and challenge are in the evidence file with "their text unchanged".

**Fix:**

Reword the parenthetical in docs/adr/evidence/decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md line 13 so it no longer suggests the other repositories were kept out. For example: "Its logs, and the two raw files the gh reads saved (github-actions-usage.txt and github-repo-state.txt), are not copied; the D4 brief below quotes what they showed, including three of the org's other repositories by name and the org's plan and September overage." Mention this to the owner in one line of the push report, because ADR-0103 says main's history is not rewritten. If the owner wants the names out before the merge, redact them in the D4 section, mark each redaction as Claude's in the italic note, and change both "The words are unchanged" in the file header and "their text unchanged" in ADR-0103's new Later note (line 168) to say what was redacted. Do not redact without the owner's say: nothing the owner decided calls for it.

### Refuted findings (0)

None.

### Notes (19)

Minor findings and observations, not put to refuters.

#### MONEY-4: ADR-0109's list of rates byway does not record omits Google's cache rates and OpenRouter's cache-write rates

*Note. Severity note; lens money; where `docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:33`.*

**What is wrong:**

Decision 1 names the unrecorded rates as gpt-6-luna's cached input, the gpt-image models' cached input and OpenRouter's cache rates. Google's pricing page lists context-cache rates for both Gemini text models the Google profile uses. gemini-3.8-flash is $0.075 through 2026-12-31 and $0.15 from 2027-01-01; gemini-3.1-pro-preview is $0.20 for prompts up to 200k. google.ts reports cachedContentTokenCount as cacheReadTokens, so those are priced at the input rate. That leans high, so the money is not at risk, but the list is incomplete and prices.ts's Google comments do not mention it. OpenRouter also lists input_cache_write at 1.25x. That is moot while OpenRouter reports usage.cost, which byway prefers (gateway.ts:860).

**Evidence:**

ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/pricing (last updated 2026-10-16): gemini-3.8-flash cache '$0.075 through December 31, 2026; $0.15 starting January 1, 2027'; gemini-3.1-pro-preview cache '$0.20 (<=200k)'. openrouter.ai/api/v1/models via curl: anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5 input_cache_write '0.0000025', anthropic/claude-opus-5.5 '0.000005', anthropic/claude-fable-5.1 '0.0000125'.

**When it goes wrong:**

No wrong money: Google cache reads are charged above cost. A reader of ADR-0109 or prices.ts would think the Google rows record everything their page lists.

**Proposed fix:**

Add the Google cache rates to Decision 1's list and to the Google comments in prices.ts, either as 'not recorded, leans high' or as cacheReadPerMTok on the rows, including a dated row for gemini-3.8-flash's 2027 cache rate.

#### START-4: The describe roughly doubles the engine-down wait (about 19 s more) and has no deadline of byway's own

*Note. Severity note; lens runstart; where `packages/runner/src/client.ts:109`.*

**What is wrong:**

ADR-0109 and the closed backlog row describe the cost only as 'a second call to Temporal' / 'a second RPC'. With the engine down, the describe goes through the SDK's 10-attempt UNAVAILABLE retry like the start does, so the person now waits about 39 s instead of about 20 s for the 503. byway sets no client deadline on the start or the describe (no withDeadline anywhere). When Temporal is reachable, the describe is bounded only by the server's own timeouts, and a server that keeps the connection alive but never answers would hang it, as it already hangs the start.

**Evidence:**

A scratch script with Connection.lazy to the closed port 127.0.0.1:59998: `start 19826 ms ServiceError Failed to start Workflow 14` then `describe 19285 ms ServiceError Failed to describe workflow 14`. `grep -rn withDeadline packages/runner/src apps/web/src` returns nothing. ADR-0109 line 40: 'The describe is a second call to Temporal, made only after a start that failed.'

**When it goes wrong:**

The engine stopped after the app connected (OPS-2). The person presses Start, the button stays busy for about 40 s, then 'not running ... Restart byway' appears.

**Proposed fix:**

Name the added wait in ADR-0109's Negative consequences. Or bound the describe (for example connection.withDeadline, or fewer retries for this one call), keeping in mind that the double-fault test needs the describe after a 14.

#### START-5: A workflow left under a run already marked failed waits forever when its recipe asks first (C4, C6, C7)

*Note. Severity note; lens runstart; where `docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:67`.*

**What is wrong:**

The 'unknown' branch (start accepted, describe failed) and the late-start residual leave a workflow running under a run the store shows as failed. Its calls are refused, so nothing is spent. But a recipe whose first action is a question opens a checkpoint (openCheckpoint has no ended-run check) and then waits forever. C4, C6 and C7 all begin D1 with optional(...), and so does a route with no pick. Cancel does nothing for an ended run, an answer is refused, and engine-start reconciliation does not touch it. 'its activities change nothing' is therefore not exact. Non-terminal events are suppressed for ended runs (events.ts emit), and the web hides questions of ended runs, so this is invisible and costs nothing.

**Evidence:**

Probe test in my clone against the harness's real Temporal: start accepted, then 4; describe made to throw 4, so 'unknown'; recipe `routed`. Output: open checkpoint '.../cp1-who choose' on the failed run; `resolve: This run is no longer running.`; after h.runner.cancel and reconcileLostRuns, `workflow status ... RUNNING run status: failed`; `checkpoint status: open`. client.ts:68 (`if (ENDED.has(run.status)) return;` in signal); human.ts:46-91 (no ended check before store.openCheckpoint); c4.ts:34 optional first.

**When it goes wrong:**

A C4 Start times out after Temporal accepted it and the describe also times out. The run is failed (correct, nothing is spent), but its Temporal workflow stays RUNNING with an open question nobody can answer or cancel, across engine restarts.

**Proposed fix:**

Either name it in the residual (and ADR-0099's 'ends' no longer holds for question-first recipes), or let beginRun/openCheckpoint end the workflow when the run has already ended (for example throw a RunStopped-style failure).

#### START-6: The 'only the writer marks failed' guard is untested

*Note. Severity note; lens runstart; where `packages/runner/src/client.ts:173`.*

**What is wrong:**

B1 restructured the repeat-delivery guard into `if (!created) throw e;`. Removing it leaves every related test green. Without it, a repeat delivery whose start failed would describe, could get 'absent' while the writer's start is still landing, and would mark the run failed under the writer's workflow: the double fault from the other side.

**Evidence:**

Mutation M5 in my clone, replacing the line with `void created;`: start-describe.test.ts, start-failure.test.ts and run-identity.test.ts gave 17 passed (17). For comparison, M1 (no describe) failed 3 start-describe tests and both new engine-state tests. M2 ('unknown' read as 'absent') failed the 'could not ask' test. M3 (describe only after a timeout) failed the P3 double-fault test.

**When it goes wrong:**

A later edit drops or moves the guard; no test notices, and a repeat delivery marks a run failed under its running workflow.

**Proposed fix:**

Add a start-describe case: the repeat's start fails with 14 while the writer's start is still pending, then lands. Expect the run not to be failed.

#### START-7: ADR-0109 lists the e2e spec as evidence for the 503's words, but the spec writes them itself

*Note. Severity note; lens runstart; where `docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:95`.*

**What is wrong:**

e2e/start-identity.spec.ts fulfils POST /api/runs with its own 503 body through page.route and asserts only the substring 'Restart byway'. It cannot detect what the server says. The edit in this batch only keeps its mock's text in step. services.test.ts is the test that pins the words.

**Evidence:**

start-identity.spec.ts:9 'Every start is stopped in the browser (page.route), so no run is started here'; line 42 defines engineDown's error text; line 59 `toContainText('Restart byway')`, which holds for both the old and the new words.

**When it goes wrong:**

A reader trusts the browser tests to guard the user-facing 503 text; a regression in EngineUnavailableError's message would pass e2e.

**Proposed fix:**

Drop '(the 503's words)' from the e2e entry, or say that the spec's mock follows the words that services.test.ts pins.

#### F07-2: run-realistic.sh: a relative out-folder runs nothing, and done.txt always records exit 0

*Note. Severity note; lens evidence-tests; where `packages/runner/test/f07-corpus/run-realistic.sh:54`.*

**What is wrong:**

(a) The script runs `mkdir -p "$OUT"` relative to the caller's directory and then `cd "$HERE/../.."` (line 18). Every later "$OUT" use (the logs, REAL_OUT, compare.mjs) is then relative to packages/runner. A relative out-folder given from the repository root, as the README's usage invites, makes every group's `>> "$OUT/$name.log"` redirection fail, so no group runs. (b) In `echo "$(date +%H:%M:%S) $name exit $?"`, `$?` is the date substitution's status, so done.txt always says exit 0.

**Evidence:**

I ran a copy with real14r replaced by echo from a scratch root with out-folder 'out-rel'. The output was 'environment: line 11: out-rel/G01.log: No such file or directory' for every group, and both out-rel folders stayed empty. `bash -c 'false; echo "$(date +%H) x exit $?"'` prints 'exit 0'.

**When it goes wrong:**

A rerun before a Playwright upgrade with a relative folder does nothing until compare fails. A crashed group shows as 'exit 0' in done.txt; compare.mjs still reports its missing answers.

**Proposed fix:**

Resolve OUT to an absolute path before the cd (`OUT=$(cd "$OUT" && pwd)` after the mkdir). Capture the status first: `REAL_OUT=... npx tsx ...; rc=$?; echo "$(date +%H:%M:%S) $name exit $rc"`.

#### C1H-1: The lineage test still names the pinned parent C1 'at Milestone 0', which ac83bac itself found is not so

*Note. Severity note; lens evidence-tests; where `packages/catalog/test/conformance.test.ts:66`.*

**What is wrong:**

ac83bac says C1 got 686bd97… at product phase 0 (ba0e5b9), and that the Milestone 0 review fix (54eed9e) left C1 at another hash. The test is still titled 'C1-H derives from C1 as it stood at Milestone 0' and its constant is still `C1_AT_MILESTONE_0`. The pin's value is correct.

**Evidence:**

`git show 54eed9e:recipes/c1.json` has hash 87ae49e3…, and C1-H's was 2a40fa68…. `git show ba0e5b9:recipes/c1.json` has 686bd972…, which equals C1H_PARENT_HASH; C1-H's hash moved then to d2cf85ec…. recipes/c1.json has not changed on main's first-parent history since ba0e5b9.

**When it goes wrong:**

A reader auditing Study 1's registration takes the test title at its word and looks for C1's Milestone 0 hash (87ae49e…) as C1-H's parent.

**Proposed fix:**

Rename the constant and the test title to 'C1 as of product phase 0 (ba0e5b9)', matching the c1h.ts comment.

#### EVID-2: The Temp-citation check matches only the AppData spelling and only looks for the note's marker

*Note. Severity note; lens evidence-tests; where `scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts:20`.*

**What is wrong:**

TEMP is /AppData[\\/]Local[\\/]Temp/i. A record citing `%TEMP%\byway-fixes\…` passes, and that is the spelling the builders use themselves in claims-oracle.test.ts and variants.ts. The check also only requires the marker line '**2026-10-18, the evidence kept only under Temp.**' to be present, not that the note accounts for each cited path. Any record citing Temp therefore passes by copying the marker.

**Evidence:**

Lines 19-20 and 33-36. For today's records I checked each cited AppData/Local/Temp path in the nine citing records (0081, 0092, 0093, 0097, 0098, 0102, 0103, 0105, 0106) against its Later notes: every path or its range ('semantics-2-r6/ to semantics-2-r11/') is accounted for, and no record cites Temp in another spelling.

**When it goes wrong:**

A future Accepted record cites `%TEMP%\…` evidence, or cites a new Temp path with a copied marker note, and the check stays green while the evidence stays only under Temp.

**Proposed fix:**

Add `%TEMP%`, `$env:TEMP` and `\bTemp\b[\\/]byway-` forms to TEMP. Require each cited Temp path's last segment to appear in the Later notes.

#### REC-5: ADR-0107 item 2 generalizes two instance-specific instructions into a standing rule without the 'Claude's reading' label item 4 uses

*Note. Severity note; lens records; where `docs/adr/0107-code-is-checked-before-it-is-pushed.md:20`.*

**What is wrong:**

The owner's two quotes in item 2 are about F07's round 14 and D1/D4's round 2. The record turns them into "After each check, Claude reports what it found, and the next round, or none, is decided with the owner. Claude neither starts another round nor rules one out alone.", and the index row presents that as the owner's (User). This is unmarked, while item 4's similar extension is marked as Claude's reading.
