The title's "each further fix round is the owner's call" is stronger than the owner's "I would like to take that call together". Context's "The owner set these rules" also covers item 5, which is Claude's. The generalization does fit the owner's stated reason "(because we can't keep running loops indefinitely)" and every later exchange, so this is wording only.

**Evidence:**

conversation-continued.md 2026-10-15 00:54: "once round 13 is done, don't automatically jump to round 14...". 2026-10-17 00:32: "run the first round but then report the result instead of unilaterally denying round 2. I would like to take that call together." README index line 43: "no further fix round starts, or is ruled out, without the owner ... | Accepted | User". ADR-0107 line 13: "The owner set these rules in conversation between 2026-10-15 and 2026-10-18."

**When it goes wrong:**

A later agent cites ADR-0107 item 2 as the owner's own words for a rule the owner gave only for two specific rounds. That is the kind of over-attribution the owner asked records to avoid.

**Proposed fix:**

Add a sentence such as "That this holds for every round is Claude's reading of these two answers and their reason". Use "decided together with the owner" in the title, and write "The owner set rules 1 to 4" in Context.

#### XDOC-7: A ninth row closed: "R3 remainder"

*Note. Severity note; lens backlog; where `docs/backlog.md`.*

**What is wrong:**

Nine rows were removed from main's backlog, not eight. "R3 remainder: outcome pointers for decisions already taken" was closed by the records branch (dca74a7). The orchestrator's list and the message of 481b2e5 ("8 rows closed") do not include it. I checked that the work is done, so nothing is lost.

**Evidence:**

dca74a7's message: "the open-decisions history says where each was decided or is tracked (the R3 remainder row closes)". docs/adr/README.md:151–170 has a new "Where it stands" column (ADR-0061 D5, D12, D14, D16; ADR-0064 and ADR-0108 for the form; backlog Pricing) and a link to Deferred decisions. Spec line 165 already says "ADR-0040, proposed, adopted here for the product" on main.

**When it goes wrong:**

A checker counting removed rows against the list of eight flags this one as lost, or a later reader cannot find where R3's remainder went.

**Proposed fix:**

None needed in the docs. Count nine closures in the push summary.

#### XDOC-8: "(its row)" pointers to closed rows

*Note. Severity note; lens backlog; where `docs/backlog.md:99`.*

**What is wrong:**

Several open rows still point to rows closed in this batch, so their triggers read as still waiting on work that is done. Rows 99 and 105 say "once the claims oracle is in the suite (its row)", and row 116 says "the claims oracle (its row)". The claims oracle now runs in scripts/test. Row 113 says "with the C1-H parent pin (its row)", which is pinned now.

**Evidence:**

The rows "Port the claims oracle into the runner suite" and "Pin C1-H's parent hash before Study 1's prompt registration" were removed. scripts/test/claims-oracle.test.ts exists. c1h.ts defines C1H_PARENT_HASH.

**When it goes wrong:**

A reader looks for a row that does not exist, or thinks those triggers are still blocked.

**Proposed fix:**

Change the pointers to scripts/test/claims-oracle.test.ts and c1h.ts (pinned 2026-10-18), and drop the "(its row)" pointers.

#### XDOC-9: The merge rule is stated without saying it is Claude's reading after D2; the Windows CI write-up still gives the old rule

*Note. Severity note; lens backlog; where `README.md:208`.*

**What is wrong:**

The README states "It merges when the Linux job passes and the Windows job passes or fails only on a timing flake already named in the backlog; any other failure is fixed first" as a general rule. ADR-0107 records that the owner chose this for D2, and that applying it to later changes is Claude's delegated reading. ADR-0107 also says any other failure "stops the merge and is reported", not "is fixed first". The write-up docs/reviews/2026-10-18-windows-ci.md:22 still says a risky change "merges only when both jobs pass". Its header sends readers to three backlog rows, not to ADR-0107.

**Evidence:**

ADR-0107 Decided by: "Delegated to Claude, reversible: ... applying item 4 to risky changes after D2". Item 4: "Any other failure stops the merge and is reported. The owner chose this for D2. That it holds for later risky changes too is Claude's reading."

**When it goes wrong:**

A reader of the README takes Claude's reading as the owner's rule and fixes a failure without reporting it first. A reader of the write-up applies the superseded rule.

**Proposed fix:**

In the README, add that applying the rule after D2 is Claude's reading (ADR-0107), and use "stops the merge and is reported". In the write-up's header, note that ADR-0107 changed §1's merge rule.

#### XDOC-10: "Such a fix needs the owner's go-ahead (ADR-0107)" cites a rule ADR-0107 does not contain

*Note. Severity note; lens backlog; where `docs/backlog.md:171`.*

**What is wrong:**

Rows 170, 171 and 172 add "such a fix needs the owner's go-ahead (ADR-0107)". ADR-0107 states no such rule: item 2 is about further fix rounds after a check, and item 4 quotes "Not now". The gate is Claude's inference from the owner's "timing flakes get named and set aside", but the rows present it as written in the record, with no label.

**Evidence:**

ADR-0107's Decision items 1–5 contain no sentence saying a flake fix needs the owner's go-ahead.

**When it goes wrong:**

A reader believes the owner set a gate on flake fixes that the owner never chose.

**Proposed fix:**

Attribute it as Claude's reading of the owner's "Not now" (ADR-0107 item 4), or drop the ADR-0107 citation.

#### XDOC-11: The gate's known renderer-check flake is not named in the backlog

*Note. Severity note; lens backlog; where `docs/backlog.md:174`.*

**What is wrong:**

New on this point only: the README's CI rule says a test that fails only sometimes is named in the backlog and set aside. ADR-0107 admits only "a timing flake already named in the backlog". This batch's one gate failure, renderer-check.test.ts (its probe without the sandbox runs past PROBE_MS 15 s under load), which also failed in B1's own run, is named in no row. "What D2 left" item (5) covers only the product's 15 s probe on a slow machine or on CI, not this test under parallel load.

**Evidence:**

`grep -n renderer-check docs/backlog.md` finds nothing. README.md:204: "A test that fails only sometimes is named in the backlog and set aside".

**When it goes wrong:**

If Windows CI goes red on this test, the ADR-0107 merge condition does not cover it, because it is not named.

**Proposed fix:**

Name it in a timing-flake row, for example next to "Runner tests: Temporal's local test server...", or in "What D2 left" item (5), with a labelled trigger.

#### XDOC-12: Small status-page and trigger gaps

*Note. Severity note; lens backlog; where `docs/reviews/2026-10-16-review-fix-status.md:22`.*

**What is wrong:**

F10 says its rows are "(their rows, before the first live C5 run)". One of them, the shot-check estimate row, is triggered by the planning-numbers check, not by the C5 run. Separately, the backlog row "C6 angles and react to the author's write as reads" (backlog.md:89) still has no trigger anyone can observe ("deferred until person-step read semantics are designed"), although the batch set out to give every open row one.

backlog.md:87: "Revisit when the advisor's planning numbers are checked against the ledger (its row)". backlog.md:89: "Another reads instance, deferred until person-step read semantics are designed (ADR-0089)".

**When it goes wrong:**

A reader expects the shot-check estimate row to be settled before the first C5 run. The C6 row has nothing that brings it back.

**Proposed fix:**

In F10, say which rows are due before the first C5 run. Give the C6 row a labelled trigger, for example at the next recipe-IR change.

#### SEC-2: Copied content outside docs/adr/evidence gets no secret or email check, and the F07 pages carry real-looking contact details

*Note. Severity note; lens security; where `packages/runner/test/f07-corpus/pages/n4-portfolio-tw.html`.*

**What is wrong:**

The batch commits content that came from Temp and from sessions in three places: docs/adr/evidence, packages/runner/test/f07-corpus and docs/reviews/2026-10-18-windows-ci.md. adr-evidence.test.ts checks only the first. I scanned the other two by hand with wider patterns: no key, token, bearer, JWT or env dump. The model-written corpus pages do carry contact details that look real. The email addresses are on domains that are not reserved (for example [an address in F07's corpus], [an address in F07's corpus], [an address in F07's corpus], [an address in F07's corpus], [an address in F07's corpus]). Some phone numbers fall outside the fictional ranges (+44 117 900 0000, +44 20 7000 0000). There are social links to real-looking handles (linkedin.com/in/noor, linkedin.com/in/inesduarte, instagram.com/loaf). The pages are pinned byte for byte by f07-corpus.test.ts digests and are never served (the oracles abort every host outside the font and CDN allowlist), and the repository is private (gh: isPrivate true). So this is minor, but the email check could not be extended to the corpus without exemptions.

**Evidence:**

My grep of the changed files for email addresses: 8x [an address in F07's corpus], 8x [an address in F07's corpus], 6x [an address in F07's corpus] and 4x [an address in F07's corpus] in f07-corpus/expected/round14.json, and the same addresses in pages/*.html. Phone numbers: pages/n6-bistro-menu.html:91 `+441179000000`; pages/n3-restaurant-flex.html:83 `+442070000000`. Fictional-range numbers (555-0142, +44 20 7946 0000) and example.com addresses also appear. No email or key in docs/reviews/2026-10-18-windows-ci.md.

**When it goes wrong:**

Nothing breaks today. If the corpus were ever published or served, it would show addresses, numbers and profile links that may belong to real people or businesses. A future copy into f07-corpus or docs/reviews that carried a real key would get no automatic check.

**Proposed fix:**

Either extend the key-shape check (fixed as in SEC-1) to every path this batch copied in (packages/runner/test/f07-corpus/** and docs/reviews/**), keeping the email check to docs/adr/evidence, or state in the corpus README that its contact details are model-written and not checked. The pages cannot be scrubbed without changing round 14's digests and outcomes.

#### SEC-3: Opening a corpus page runs third-party CDN scripts with no integrity check, and two of them are unpinned

*Note. Severity note; lens security; where `packages/runner/test/f07-corpus/pages`.*

**What is wrong:**

The 50 committed pages vendor no library code (each page is under 10 KB, with no license headers and no data: images). They load GSAP, ScrollTrigger, Tailwind's browser build, AOS, Bootstrap and Alpine from cdnjs, jsDelivr, unpkg and cdn.tailwindcss.com, with no `integrity` attribute. Two references are not pinned to a version (`@tailwindcss/browser@4` and `cdn.tailwindcss.com`), so a developer who opens such a page from disk runs whatever those CDNs serve that day. The inline scripts are harmless: no fetch, XHR, sendBeacon, cookies, localStorage, eval, analytics or trackers; the forms post nowhere external. The by-hand harness (oracle12.mts:160-169, vo13.mts:184-188) lets only those font and CDN hosts through and aborts everything else. It uses the scripted gateway (helpers/voice-activity.ts), so it never reaches a model provider, and it does not run in CI.

**Evidence:**

`grep -ohE '<script[^>]+src="https?://…'` over pages/*.html and pages-verifier/*.html found 8x gsap 3.12.5, 8x ScrollTrigger 3.12.5, 7x @tailwindcss/browser@4.1.11, 4x aos@2.3.4, 3x bootstrap@5.3.3, 2x aos@2.3.1, 2x `https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@tailwindcss/browser@4`, 1x `https://cdn.tailwindcss.com` and 1x alpinejs@3.14.1, none with integrity=. A grep for fetch|XMLHttpRequest|sendBeacon|gtag|analytics|document.cookie|localStorage|eval(|new Function|window.open found nothing.

**When it goes wrong:**

A developer double-clicks a corpus page to look at it. The browser fetches and runs the current `@tailwindcss/browser@4` and `cdn.tailwindcss.com` builds, which are third-party code that may differ from what round 14 ran. The risk is the ordinary one for any CDN script tag.

**Proposed fix:**

No change is needed for the push: the pages must stay byte for byte. Optionally the corpus README could say that opening a page runs CDN scripts, and that an exact rerun needs the same CDN versions (two are floating).

#### SEC-4: Two build logs lost three progress-line CR bytes, though the logs README says they are unchanged

*Note. Severity note; lens security; where `docs/adr/evidence/logs/ci-timing-2026-10-17/gate-build.log:14`.*

**What is wrong:**

The root `.gitattributes` (`* text=auto eol=lf`) turned the only CRLFs in gate-build.log and 32-gate-build.log into LF when they were committed. The logs README says both sets were brought in "unchanged". The only difference is three `\r` bytes at the ends of `next build` progress lines. Stripping them makes the copy in the working tree byte-identical to the committed blob. No figure, hash or claim depends on those bytes, so this does not matter for the evidence. All other evidence files are LF in the index and in the working tree. The F07 pages keep their CRLF through `pages/** -text`.

**Evidence:**

`git ls-files --eol docs/adr/evidence` shows `i/lf w/mixed` for exactly these two files. A node byte comparison found: worktree 1367 bytes, blob 1364 bytes, 3 CR bytes, on lines 14-16 ("  Generating static pages using 15 workers (3/14) \r", "(6/14) \r", "(10/14) \r"), and "equal after strip: true" for both files.

**When it goes wrong:**

None in practice. A reader who hashes the committed log against GitHub's or Temp's original would see a mismatch.

**Proposed fix:**

Optional: add `docs/adr/evidence/logs/** -text` (as f07-corpus does for its pages) and re-add the two logs, or say in logs/README.md that line ends were normalized to LF.

#### MERGE-4: ADR-0105's new audit note cites a 2026-10-16 owner message only through the transcript; the merged evidence note does not point to its kept copy

*Note. Severity note; lens merge; where `docs/adr/0105-renderer-os-sandbox-on-with-an-explicit-off-switch.md:162`.*

**What is wrong:**

The two branches added notes to ADR-0105 without seeing each other. The records branch's note quotes the owner at 2026-10-16 09:17 and says "The message is in this session's transcript (2026-10-16T16:17:34Z; Evidence names the file)". The evidence branch's note maps only "The owner's answers of 2026-10-18 ... (09:22 and 14:02 local)" to evidence/conversation-continued.md. The 2026-10-16 message is in that file (line 170), but nothing in ADR-0105 says so. 481b2e5 added this kind of pointer to ADR-0107 and ADR-0108 ("Each is also in evidence/conversation-continued.md, under its time"), but not to this note.

**Evidence:**

0105 Later notes at 481b2e5: records' first note ends "(2026-10-16T16:17:34Z; Evidence names the file)". The evidence note says "The owner's answers of 2026-10-18, cited from this session's transcript, are in [evidence/conversation-continued.md] (09:22 and 14:02 local)." `grep -n "switches forgotten argument" docs/adr/evidence/conversation-continued.md` returns line 170, with the full quoted text verbatim. `grep -c 2026-10-16T16:17 conversation-continued.md` returns 1.

**When it goes wrong:**

A reader checking the owner's quoted reasons in ADR-0105 is sent to a transcript outside the repository, although a kept copy is in the repository. Nothing is false.

**Proposed fix:**

When the batch's next records edit is made, extend the evidence note in ADR-0105 (still a draft on this branch) to say the 2026-10-16 09:17 message is also in evidence/conversation-continued.md.

### What each lens checked

#### money

- Anthropic pricing page (platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing, read 2026-10-19). The rows for claude-fable-5-1 (10/50, cache hit 0.25, 5m write 12.50), claude-opus-5-5 (4/20, 0.20, 5), claude-sonnet-5-5 (2/10, 0.10, 2.50), claude-opus-5 and claude-opus-4-8 (5/25, 0.50, 6.25) all match prices.ts. Footnote 2 says 0.05x for Opus 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5, and the prose says $0.10 on Sonnet 5.5, so ADR-0109's statement that the table, footnote and prose agree is true. 1M context at standard pricing, so there is no long-context surcharge. Caching is opt-in (top-level cache_control), which byway does not send.
- Anthropic models overview: claude-fable-5-1, claude-opus-5-5 and claude-sonnet-5-5 are current, retiring not sooner than Sep 1, Sep 22 and Sep 28, 2027. ADR-0109's statement is true.
- OpenAI pricing page: input, cached input and output for gpt-6-astra, gpt-6.1-sol and gpt-6-luna match. gpt-image-2.5-flare and gpt-image-2.5-sunburst: text input $5, image output $30, no per-image price ('use the calculator'), cached input only for Responses API images, which byway does not use. The models page lists all five ids, none deprecated, and Sunburst is described as 'Our most capable model for image generation and editing'. Cache writes were missed (MONEY-1).
- OpenAI image guide, 'Handling blocked requests and other errors': moderation_blocked, type image_generation_user_error, optional error.moderation_details with moderation_stage input, output or unknown. No HTTP status, no content_policy_violation, nothing on billing. ADR-0109 and the openai.ts comment are accurate. The OpenAI error-codes page says nothing on billing failed requests.
- Google pricing page (last updated 2026-10-16): gemini-nano-banana-2.1 input $1.50, output '$7.50 (text and thinking)' and '$30.00 (images)', $0.0336 per 1K image, 1,120 tokens. gemini-3.8-flash $0.75/$3.75 through 2026-12-31, then $1.50/$7.50. gemini-3.1-pro-preview $2/$12 (>200k $4/$18). All match prices.ts.
- Google image guide: thinking 'enabled by default and cannot be disabled in the API'; nano-banana-2.1's default level is medium; interim thought images are 'not charged'; 'thinking tokens are billed by default for thinking models' (in the second half of the page). ADR-0109's statements are true.
- Google models page (2026-10-13) and deprecations page (2026-10-12): all three ids are listed, the stable ones are stable, and none has a shutdown date.
- OpenRouter /api/v1/models (curl, no key): anthropic/claude-fable-5.1 10/50, claude-opus-5.5 4/20, claude-sonnet-5.5 2/10 per 1M, matching. The provider-selection page's base-slug and service-tier wording matches the profiles.ts comment. The errors page says only that an upstream provider may charge for prompt processing when no content is generated.
- fal: kling v2.5-turbo pro $0.35 for 5 s plus $0.07 per extra second; kling v3 pro $0.112/s with audio off; ffmpeg extract-frame $0.0002/s; the FAQ says 'Server errors (HTTP 500+) are never charged'. All match. The Anthropic errors page says nothing on billing failed requests.
- google.ts image usage, read against Google's UsageMetadata docs and the @google/genai 2.27.0 typings: thinking is reported apart from candidates, so it is not double counted; IMAGE details are excluded, so the image is not charged both per image and as tokens; with details absent, image tokens are charged at the text rate as well (documented as leaning high). Non-numeric and negative counts go through finiteUsage's fallback of 2,000 tokens, flagged 'assumed'. The IMAGE-only-details undercount is MONEY-2.
- Estimate: maxImageCost and the advisor's images() add 2,000 x $7.50/M = $0.015 an image; recomputed per configuration and profile at main and head (MONEY-3). The 12-board C2 figure ($0.59 against $0.41) is correct.
- VOICE removal: the runner's writeVoice and writePageVoice return costUsd 0 with no model call (human.ts:314-331, 351ff), so dropping voice.apply from the estimate is correct.
- COMPOSED-MEDIUM: walk() visits every node type, including relay chains, gate bodies and pick judges. It refuses op 'relay' and model role 'relay' steps when the medium is not media, and allows tool RELAY. The interpreter calls storyboardShots for both the chain and a lone model relay step, so the refusal matches what would fail at run time. forceComposed keeps the nearest recipe's medium unless the model sets one, the mock composer keeps the nearest's medium, only C5 (media) has relay in the catalog, and advisor.admit re-checks at start (api.ts admitComposed). Nothing the catalog or demo composer produces is refused.
- env.ts: the globalThis WeakMap under Symbol.for holds a Set of names only and never values. Both module copies default to the same process.env object and share the map. Chromium is a separate process launched with withoutSecrets(env, namesFromEnvFile(env)). pages.ts runs in activity code, not in Temporal's workflow vm. Nothing logs the names.
- errors.ts and openai.ts changes are comments only, apart from the test fixture; their statements about the pages hold.
- Mutation proofs in my own clone (scratchpad/check-clones/money), each reverted after: google.ts outputTokens back to candidatesTokenCount fails 3 tests (providers, pricing x2); counting IMAGE tokens fails 3 tests; nano-banana outputPerMTok 0 fails 7 tests (pricing, billed, gateway); dropping estimatedOutputTokensPerImage 2000 fails pricing.test's plan test; sonnet cacheRead back to 0.2 fails pricing.test's row; env.ts back to a module-local WeakMap fails env.test's two-copy test; removing COMPOSED-MEDIUM fails 2 profile tests; stripping moderation_details from the stored body fails the providers test; main's estimate.ts (voice.apply restored) fails the VOICE test. Each new test catches the revert of its fix.

*Not checked by this lens:*

- Whether OpenAI's pricing page showed the cache-write column on 2026-10-18 itself. It does on 2026-10-19, and a July 2026 OpenAI community thread shows GPT-5.6 cache_write_tokens and billing, so it very likely did.
- The real usageMetadata shape of gemini-nano-banana-2.1 image answers: whether TEXT appears in candidatesTokensDetails and whether thought images count in thoughtsTokenCount. Checking needs a keyed, paid call, which was not allowed. MONEY-2 rests on Google's field definitions plus a third-party measurement of the same model family.
- Whether a Gemini image answer can carry two final images (units is always 1). This predates B1.
- Google's troubleshooting page and Anthropic's streaming page on billing of failed requests. The Anthropic errors, OpenAI error-codes and OpenRouter errors pages were checked.
- The runner and web parts of ADR-0109 (start-failure.ts, client.ts describe-first, engine-state and start-describe tests, the 503 wording) and scripts/test/renderer-env.test.ts's lowercase-drive run. These are outside this lens and were not run.

Left to the records lens; I only confirmed that the index rows for 0084, 0091, 0092, 0099 and 0100 carry 'changed by ADR-0109'.

*Its findings:*

- MONEY-1 [blocking] OpenAI cache writes are billed at 1.25x input, but byway records them at the plain input rate, and ADR-0109 says unrecorded rates lean high (packages/gateway/src/providers/openai.ts)
- MONEY-2 [should-fix] A Gemini image's text and 'internal' answer tokens go uncharged when candidatesTokensDetails lists only IMAGE (packages/gateway/src/providers/google.ts)
- MONEY-3 [should-fix] ADR-0109's stated budget effects are wrong: C3's Balanced default budget is $2.50 before and after B1, and VOICE removal changes every configuration, not just C3 and C4 (docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md)
- MONEY-4 [note] ADR-0109's list of rates byway does not record omits Google's cache rates and OpenRouter's cache-write rates (docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md)

#### runstart

- client.ts start(): the describe runs only for the delivery that wrote the run (created), after any start failure other than a named run's WorkflowExecutionAlreadyStartedError. 'exists' returns the record; 'absent' and 'unknown' mark the run failed and rethrow. No path starts a second workflow, and no path leaves the writer's run 'queued' without a workflow except store-write failure or a repeat delivery (pre-existing; engine-start reconcile settles those).
- workflowAfterFailedStart: WorkflowNotFoundError means 'absent'; any other error means 'unknown'. The SDK maps NOT_FOUND, including an unknown namespace, to WorkflowNotFoundError (workflow-client.js rethrowGrpcError), so 'absent' after a namespace error is still correct.
- startFailureMessage matrix: 14/ECONNREFUSED/ECONNRESET give ENGINE_DOWN_AT_START, whatever the describe found. A timeout gives ENGINE_SLOW_AT_START ('absent') or ENGINE_SLOW_UNCONFIRMED ('unknown'). Any other error gives the old prefix ('absent') or the 'cannot tell' text ('unknown'). 'Nothing was spent' appears only in the 14 cases and the timeout+absent case. The UNCONFIRMED wording matches liveness.ts (guardGateway refuses calls for ended runs; settleLateCost raises the cost afterwards).
- Code-14 'Nothing was spent' judgement (ADR-0109 line 68): sound, and stronger than stated. The SDK resends the same StartWorkflowExecution bytes (one requestId, workflow-client.js:923) on UNAVAILABLE, up to 10 attempts, and Temporal dedups by request id. So a reset after acceptance usually ends in success. A 14 reaches byway only after about 20 s of unreachability, while no worker can run the workflow, and the run is failed before it can call.
- gRPC 4 reaches byway only from the server: byway sets no client deadline, and the SDK does not retry DEADLINE_EXCEEDED. isTimeout matches code 4 anywhere in the cause chain (the SDK wraps it in ServiceError with the gRPC error as cause), and its regex does not match '14 ...'.
- Ended-run guards that make a late or unknown workflow harmless for money: beginRun only promotes 'queued'; setRunStatus, finishRun, failRun and cancelRun leave ended runs alone; emit drops non-terminal events for ended runs; guardGateway refuses complete/image/video with ENDED_CALL (non-retryable).
- apps/web: engine-runner still reads 4 as a transport failure, so start, resolve and controls answer 503 with the new words. The 'exists' path returns normally (200). There is no client-side fetch timeout on /api/runs, so the longer engine-down wait does not cause a resend. The screen mints a new id after any answer (503).
- Mutations in my clone (check-clones/run-start): M1 (describe removed) failed start-describe (3 tests) and engine-state's two new tests against real Temporal. M2 ('unknown' read as 'absent') failed the 'could not ask' test. M3 (describe only after a timeout) failed the P3 double-fault test. All tests pass unmutated (start-describe, start-failure, run-identity, services, and engine-state -t 'timed out').
- engine-state new tests: no fixed sleeps and no asserted durations. They use the harness waitFor (30 s, 20 ms polls) and a 60 s per-test limit, the same as the file's existing OPS-1 tests. The one ordering race is START-1.
- ADR-0109 item 3's Decision text matches the code (messages verbatim, 'already started' handling, the web 503 wording). The code-14 point is labelled as Claude's judgement. ADR-0099 has the Later note, and the README index lists it as 'changed by ADR-0109'.
- client-entry still exports the runner's isTransportFailure, which no longer includes 4, but nothing imports it; apps/web uses its own copy.

*Not checked by this lens:*

- Whether a real Temporal 1.32 server under persistence stalls actually returns DEADLINE_EXCEEDED (rather than UNAVAILABLE or INTERNAL) for StartWorkflowExecution, and how long after that the history service may still commit the start. This affects how often the 'absent then late start' case happens; not reproduced.
- Real-engine behaviour of the double-fault variant in START-2 with an actual restart of byway between retries. Shown only with the stand-in client.
- A full-suite or CI-load run of engine-state.test.ts to observe START-1 without an injected delay (not run, to avoid loading the shared machine).
- Whether any catalog recipe other than C4, C6 and C7 (or routes without a pick in the web's starts) opens a question before its first provider call. Only D1 openings were checked.
- The web route handler's full error mapping for non-transport start errors in the 'unknown' case (500 vs other) was not traced end to end.
- SQLite busy-timeout contention between the web process's store.updateRun and the engine's writes, which could lengthen the window ADR-0109 calls 'at once'.
- Other lenses: prices, Gemini charging, the env-file symbol, the advisor estimate, COMPOSED-MEDIUM, the evidence/corpus, and the ADR-0107/0108 records.

*Its findings:*

- START-1 [should-fix] New real-Temporal test asserts 'waiting' at once, racing two concurrent activities (packages/runner/test/engine-state.test.ts)
- START-2 [should-fix] ADR-0109 and ADR-0099's Later note say the second double fault is 'closed'; a variant remains (docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md)
- START-3 [should-fix] Backlog trigger points at the describe-first row, which this batch removed (docs/backlog.md)
- START-4 [note] The describe roughly doubles the engine-down wait (about 19 s more) and has no deadline of byway's own (packages/runner/src/client.ts)
- START-5 [note] A workflow left under a run already marked failed waits forever when its recipe asks first (C4, C6, C7) (docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md)
- START-6 [note] The 'only the writer marks failed' guard is untested (packages/runner/src/client.ts)
- START-7 [note] ADR-0109 lists the e2e spec as evidence for the 503's words, but the spec writes them itself (docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md)

#### evidence-tests

- Claims oracle port: diffed scripts/test/claims-oracle/oracle.ts and variants.ts against the Temp originals (scratch/ports-rendered/claims-pr.oracle.test.mts, gen-pr.mts). The checks and mutations are unchanged. The only changes are the env switches made into options, the loosening switches LOOSE_D/LOOSE_G/REFUSED removed, an audio ASSET skipped, the admitted (forced) recipe judged, and checkAnswer throws now failing the test rather than being counted (stricter).
- Math.imul fix: the new LCG (a=1103515245, c=12345, m=2^31) has full period by Hull-Dobell (c odd, a-1 divisible by 4). Its draws are read through the high bits (x/0x7fffffff), so the distribution is fine. Per-variant seeding is deterministic. xoshiro128** and splitmix32 in variants.ts match the reference algorithms. Reproduced the old generator's 10,466-draw cycle (see CLAIMS-1 for what that claim gets wrong).
- Claims oracle default corpus: 118 admitted variants, 6 runs, outcomes only done/RunFailure/RunStopped. The 14 EXERCISED kinds are all claimed. 'seedOne', 'refusedPersonCritique' and 'refusedAnswered' are never claimed at the default size.
- Claims oracle mutation in a scratch clone: the relay taking shots[0] instead of shots.at(-1) (interpreter.ts:337) fails 'finds every claim true' with G-RELAY-ONE-NOT-LAST-SHOT.
- Claims oracle at full size: seed 1 of 40,000 tries admits 1,210 variants on both f77cc6f (evidence branch) and 481b2e5 (batch head), so B1's COMPOSED-MEDIUM does not change that seed's corpus.
- Timings, each file run alone on this machine: claims-oracle.test.ts 8.1 s (6 tests); f07-corpus.test.ts 0.16 s; adr-evidence.test.ts 0.22 s; adr-records.test.ts 12.2 s (13.3 s on main, so no new cost); conformance.test.ts 0.65 s; renderer-env.test.ts 0.85-1.05 s (1.05 s with tsx's cache off); start-describe + start-failure 0.8 s. Even at 4x slower on CI, none adds minutes.
- renderer-env.test.ts: runs only on win32. The child's real limit is spawnSync's 60 s, under the 120 s test limit; the child takes about 1 s here (B1 measured 5.5 s under load), leaving wide headroom. Mutation in a clone: replacing the globalThis map with a per-module WeakMap in gateway/src/env.ts makes it fail (Chromium env receives ODD_CREDENTIAL and PLAIN_SETTING). The fixture is typechecked by scripts/tsconfig and is not collected as a test.
- C1-H pin: C1H_PARENT_HASH equals recipes/c1.json's hash at ba0e5b9 and at 8401820. recipes/ is unchanged in the batch, as are all pinned hashes in conformance.test.ts. Mutation: restoring main's c1h.ts (parent from c1.hash) fails the new doMock lineage test. A changed C1 or C1-H fails the pinned-hash test.
- f07-corpus.test.ts: pure file reads and hashing, with no network, browser or child process; deterministic. All 46 pages and the verifier's 4 are byte-identical to the Temp originals (r14/ev/pages-all, verify-F07-r13/pages). The nested .gitattributes (-text) was added in the same commit as the pages; git ls-files --eol shows 6 i/crlf pages. expected/round14.json, regenerated from Temp's out/real14b with compare.mjs's outcome function, matches all 10,055 keys, with no key collisions and no conflicting outcomes.
- f07 corpus scripts: each .mts differs from its Temp source only in import and output paths (vo13.mts only after stripping CRLF). vcases/ and cases/ are identical to their sources. oracle12's `playwright-core/lib/utilsBundle` resolves through createRequire. npx finds tsx by walking up to the root's node_modules/.bin.
- .mts/.mjs under packages/runner/test/f07-corpus and docs/adr/evidence: tsc --listFilesOnly on tsconfig.json and scripts/tsconfig.json lists none of them. vitest include is *.test.ts under packages/apps/scripts. Playwright testDir is apps/web/e2e. The repo has no eslint config. vitest, tsconfig, package.json, lockfile and CI workflow are unchanged in the batch.
- adr-records.test.ts count-line test: an independent count of the index gives 109 rows = 101 Accepted + 1 Accepted (direction) + 7 Proposed, with 15 By continuation and 38 Delegated, matching the count line. A simulated --no-ff merge of fix/fx-batch into main (8401820) in a scratch clone passes adr-records and adr-evidence (30/30) with HEAD on main.
- adr-evidence.test.ts: the '@type.googleapis.com' exception is narrow. It drops only matches whose domain is exactly type.googleapis.com; the occurrences are '\\n@type.googleapis.com/temporal...' in gate-vitest.log. All relative links in evidence .md (apart from the three quoted files), in every Later note, and in the f07 README, the new reviews, ADR-0107/0108/0109, the backlog and the README resolve and match tracked paths case-sensitively, so they will also resolve on Linux CI. A manual scan for emails, owner name forms and key shapes found nothing in the evidence.
- New wall-clock limits in the diff: renderer-env (60 s child, 120 s test), claims-oracle (3,600 s per test and beforeAll; it cannot be hit by slow machines, but an async hang would run to CI's 75-min job limit), and engine-state's two new Temporal tests at 60 s, following that file's existing pattern. No Date.now, performance.now or duration assertions were added in tests outside the hand-run corpus.

It is reachable only from JS in the same Node realm: the serve process's main thread, where the Temporal worker's activities and pages.ts run. Temporal workflow sandboxes have their own globalThis. Chromium page scripts run in another OS process, and pages.ts has no exposeBinding or exposeFunction bridge. The web (Next) process does not launch Chromium. An in-process dependency could replace the map, but it could already read process.env, so the global adds no new exposure.

In my clone I reverted listedByFile to a module-local WeakMap and both failed: renderer-env reported chromiumEnv containing "ODD_CREDENTIAL": "hunter2", and env.test.ts got [] instead of the names. I then restored the file. The fixture asserts twoCopies:true, so it cannot pass without actually loading two copies. serve.ts calls loadEnvFile (line 163) before checkRenderer and startDevEngine. chromiumLaunchOptions defaults to process.env, the same object the file was loaded into.

## The re-check of the fix round (2026-10-21)

*From `recheck-result.json`. The focused re-check after the owner chose "Fix all, focused re-check, push (Recommended)" at 13:41: four lenses (money path, run start, records, evidence) on the fix round's commits, with refuters, and each of the check's findings marked as the re-check found it.*

### Findings that survived the refuters (2)

#### REC2-1: ADR-0103 still states the organisation plan's included monthly minutes, while the batch's notes say that detail is redacted

*Finding. Severity blocking; outcome confirmed-partly; lens records; where `docs/adr/0103-adr-log-rule-and-ci.md:129`.*

**What is wrong:**

ADR-0103's Consequences 'Money' bullet still states how many minutes the organisation's plan includes each month. [redacted] The owner asked on 2026-10-21 that 'The quantities of minutes and overages should also be redacted', and the coordinator's list of organisation details includes 'included minutes'. The fix round's own text tells readers that the included minutes were redacted from the D4 brief, but the record that cites the brief prints the figure about 40 lines above that note.

**Evidence:**

git grep at c001ced: docs/adr/0103-adr-log-rule-and-ci.md:129 ('... of the [figure] minutes the organization's plan includes each month ... [redacted]'). It is the only place in the tree that states it. Fix-round lines that say the figure is gone: ADR-0103:168 (batch-added Later note: 'their text unchanged but for a redaction ... its plan's name, seats, price and included minutes'); decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md:5 and :944 (headers); evidence/README.md:9. The brief also keeps byway's own minutes beside their share of the included pool (decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md:1096 and :1147), and the header says that share is kept 'as written'. From those two lines the redacted pool size can be worked out. Line 129 has been on main since D4 and is already pushed, so the history cannot be cleaned. The records pass left it open on purpose ('ADR-0103's Consequences on main (line 129) still state one of the organisation's plan figures'). Batch patches, commit messages and every local branch: 0 hits for the redacted values (counted by pattern, not printed). No ref reaches the pre-rewrite commits 481b2e5 or eedc340.

**When it goes wrong:**

The owner, or anyone reading the private repository after this push, opens ADR-0103 from the evidence note that says the included minutes were redacted, and reads the figure in Consequences. The redaction the owner asked for does not hold in the current tree.

**Proposed fix:**

Put it to the owner, since the coordinator's rule for this batch lets records on main gain only Later notes. ADR-0103 §1.1 itself allows it ('The header table (its Status row included) and the other sections may change'). Replace the figure in line 129 with '[redacted]' and add a Later note saying what was removed, when and at whose request. Optionally also redact the share-of-pool percentages at decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md:1096 and :1147, or say in the header that they are kept. The figure stays in main's pushed history whatever is done, and the report to the owner should say so.

**Refuter 1: partly, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

Every factual part of REC2-1 holds up when re-run. I could not refute any of it.

- ADR-0103:129 at c001ced, in Consequences ('Money'), states the plan's monthly included minutes. The same line holds [redacted].
- The figure is the same value the batch redacted from the D4 brief. The original owner-decisions.json under Temp holds it twice, as "&lt;figure> Linux-rate minutes" and "&lt;figure> minutes". In the committed brief both places now read "[redacted]" (decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md:966 and :1215).
- In this sense it is the only place in the tree. git grep for every spelling of the figure finds only line 129. The other hits are unrelated: [redacted].
- Line 129 is the same on main and on origin/main (both 8401820). It came in with 51fedcf (2026-10-16 21:49 -0700), so it is already pushed and cannot be removed from history.
- ADR-0103:168, the batch's Later note 39 lines below, says the brief's included minutes were redacted. The brief's headers (:5, :944) and evidence/README.md:9 say the same.
- records-result.json shows the records pass left line 129 open on purpose and handed it to the coordinator and the owner.
- The finding understates one point. The figure is GitHub's published allowance for one plan, so line 129 also gives away the plan name the brief redacts. Line 129 also lets the figure be worked out: divide its '$9 to $37' by $0.006 a minute, then by its '[redacted]'. Replacing the number alone is therefore not enough. The brief's kept share-of-pool lines (:1096, and more roughly :1147) let a reader estimate it too, as the finding says.

I put the severity at should-fix rather than blocking, for four reasons.

1. **The push changes no exposure.** The fix round neither adds the figure nor repeats it. I took the 20 redaction replacements from the JSON, aligned word by word against the brief's D4 section. I then counted each value in four places: the c001ced tree, `git log -p 8401820..c001ced`, the batch's commit messages, and `git log -p --branches --not origin/main`. The only hits were inside unrelated text: [redacted]. No ref contains the pre-rewrite commits (481b2e5, eedc340, f77cc6f, 0b9dbcc, c6bdc8b, 10fbf88, cab3c77); for-each-ref --contains gives 0 for each.
2. **The owner's request was about the brief, and the brief is done.** The owner's 13:41 answer redacted the minutes and overages in reply to a question that named only "The D4 brief copied into the repo". That redaction is complete in the brief.
3. **No batch record is false.** ADR-0103:168 and the headers describe the brief accurately. evidence/README.md:19's claim about the batch's commits is true by my counts. The batch's text is misleading only when read next to line 129.
4. **The fix can land at any time.** Consequences is not a fixed section under ADR-0103 §1.1. adr-log.ts fixes only `PARTS = ['Context','Decision']`, so the edit can land before or after the push without breaking adr-records.test.ts. It needs the owner's decision only because the coordinator's batch rule allows Later notes alone. The owner's process ('if it finds anything, I report first') puts it to the owner anyway.

If the coordinator reads the rubric's 'any of the organisation's details in the repository' literally, it is blocking. But nothing the push does makes this worse or harder to undo.

**Fix:**

Put this to the owner in the report that comes before the push.

**What to tell the owner:**
- ADR-0103:129 has stated the plan's included minutes on pushed main since 51fedcf.
- That figure also identifies the plan whose name the brief redacts.
- It stays in main's history whatever is done.

**If the owner agrees,** make the change in this batch before the push. That saves a separate CI run.
- In ADR-0103:129, replace the figure and the derivable '[redacted]' with '[redacted]'. The '$9 to $37' estimate can stay. Do not leave the percentages, or the figure can be computed back from them.
- Add a Later note dated 2026-10-21 saying what was removed, by Claude, at the owner's request. Quote the owner's words verbatim.
- adr-records.test.ts will not fail: Consequences is not fixed under ADR-0103 §1.1.
- Optionally, add to the brief's header that the kept share-of-pool percentages (:1096, :1147) let the pool be estimated. Or ask the owner whether to redact them as well.

**If the owner declines,** add one clause to the Later note at ADR-0103:168 saying that the figure is still stated in Consequences above. Then the note does not imply the repository no longer holds it.

**Refuter 2: confirmed, blocking.**

**Reasoning:**

I could not refute it. The core claim holds, and nothing the owner recorded, no record and no backlog row turns the remaining figure into a choice or a recorded limit.

1. The figure is in the tree. At c001ced, docs/adr/0103-adr-log-rule-and-ci.md:129 (Consequences, "Money") states the organisation plan's included monthly minutes. The task's own list of details to keep out of the repository names "included minutes". Because that number is a plan tier's published allowance, it also points to the plan, whose name the brief redacts. Line 129 is the only place in the tree that states it. I grepped every hit of the number's spellings at c001ced and the rest are unrelated ([redacted]).

2. The owner did not choose to keep it. On 2026-10-21 13:41 the owner said "The quantities of minutes and overages should also be redacted." The question was about the D4 brief, but the coordinator's scope for this batch ("out of the repository") and the memory rule written afterwards both reach records too. The memory rule exempts ADR-0103 "(log rule)", and the evidence builder's hand-off for the owner's report says line 129 "under ADR-0103 ... can only gain a Later note, not be edited". That reading is wrong under the repository's own rule. ADR-0103 §1.1 says "The header table (its Status row included) and the other sections may change". scripts/test/adr-log.ts:14 says "Only the Context and Decision sections are fixed", and PARTS = ['Context','Decision'] at lines 29-31. So Consequences can be edited without breaking the log test. The stricter rule given to this check ("only Later notes and changed-by links") is the coordinator's rule, not the owner's, and no owner answer keeps the figure. There is no backlog row with a trigger and no note in the brief's header about it. It exists only as an open item in records-result.json and in the hand-off text, which gives the wrong reason.

3. The fix round's own text points readers at it. The new Later note at ADR-0103:168 says the brief's "included minutes" were redacted. The brief's header (line 5), its D4 note (line 944, which links ADR-0103 as "where it was decided") and evidence/README.md:9 say the same. Each statement is true of the brief alone, so I found no false record. But the redaction the owner asked for does not hold in the tree.

Two small corrections, which do not change the verdict:
- "From those two lines the redacted pool size can be worked out": only partly. Line 1096 gives Windows minutes beside "% of the included pool". Taken at face value they give about half the figure. With GitHub's double count for Windows minutes they give it within rounding (ratio 1.00 at the low end, 1.17 at the high end, because the percentages are rounded). The brief never states that double count. Line 1147 has no minute count. The point is moot anyway, because line 129 states the figure outright, and line 129's own "[redacted]" beside byway's minutes would let it be worked back too.
- The brief's header already says byway's share of the included minutes is kept "as written", which the finding itself notes, so its optional "or say so in the header" is already done.

Why blocking and not lower: the severity rule names "any of the organisation's details in the repository". The owner's push condition was "If that is clean, push ... if it finds anything, I report first". The line was not introduced by the fix round, though. It has been on origin/main since D4 (origin/main = 8401820 holds it), and pushing the batch adds no exposure. What it blocks is pushing on the strength of the hand-off's account. Told that the line can only gain a Later note, the owner could accept leaving a figure they asked to have redacted.

**Evidence:**

- git grep at c001ced for the figure's spellings: the only hit about the plan is docs/adr/0103-adr-log-rule-and-ci.md:1 line (line 129). The other hits are [redacted].
- `git show 8401820:docs/adr/0103-adr-log-rule-and-ci.md` has the same line 129. `git branch -r --contains 8401820` lists origin/main.
- `git diff 8401820 c001ced -- docs/adr/0103-adr-log-rule-and-ci.md` adds only the two Later notes at 167-168. Line 168 says "a redaction the owner asked for on 2026-10-21 (... its plan's name, seats, price and included minutes; the file's header says what)".
- ADR-0103:39 (§1.1): "The header table (its Status row included) and the other sections may change". scripts/test/adr-log.ts:14: "Only the Context and Decision sections are fixed". adr-log.ts:29-31 sets PARTS = ['Context','Decision'].
- build-result.json, the hand-off for the owner's report: "under ADR-0103 it can only gain a Later note, not be edited". records-result.json: "ADR-0103's Consequences on main (line 129) still state one of the organisation's plan figures. I did not touch it, as instructed (it is on main). The decision is for the coordinator and the owner".
- conversation-continued.md:440 (2026-10-21 13:41), the owner: "Go with your recommendation but explain to me exactly what is D4 brief and why do Action Minutes and overages feature at all? The quantities of minutes and overages should also be redacted."
- decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md:5: the header lists the included minutes as redacted and says "byway's share of the included minutes ... are as written". Line 944 links ADR-0103 as "where it was decided".
- I worked the pool size out from line 1096 in a local script that printed only ratios: about 0.5 at face value, and 1.00 to 1.17 with Windows minutes counted double. The brief never states that double count (grep for multiplier, 2x, twice and double finds only model-price text).
- `git for-each-ref --contains` for 481b2e5 and for eedc340: no ref reaches either. Origin has main, fix/fx-ci-timing, fix/fx-d2 and fix/fx-d4-ci.
- Not checked: whether the coordinator has already corrected the hand-off before reporting. The full gate was not re-run, because it is out of scope for a records finding.

**Fix:**

Report this to the owner before pushing, and give the rule correctly. ADR-0103 §1.1 and scripts/test/adr-log.ts:14 fix only Context and Decision, and Consequences "may change". Line 129 can therefore be edited in this batch without breaking the log test. Only the coordinator's stricter rule for this batch stands in the way, and relaxing it is the owner's call. Drop the hand-off sentence "can only gain a Later note, not be edited" and the memory note's "(log rule)" reason from anything sent to the owner. If the owner agrees, replace the figure in ADR-0103:129 with "[redacted]". Also remove or reword the "[redacted] of …" share next to it, since the figure can be worked back from that share and byway's minutes on the same line. Then add a Later note (or extend the batch's draft note at :168) saying what was removed, on 2026-10-21, at the owner's request (conversation-continued.md 13:41), and that Claude did it. Tell the owner the figure stays in origin/main's pushed history (8401820 and earlier) whatever is done. If the owner keeps it, record that as the owner's choice in the brief's header or in the :168 note, so that the redaction notes no longer suggest the figure is gone from the repository. The brief's share-of-pool lines (1096, 1147) need nothing more: the header already says they are kept as written.

#### EVPR-R1: ADR-0103's Consequences on main still state the plan's included minutes, and two of the batch's statements say the redaction is complete

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed-partly; lens evidence; where `docs/adr/evidence/README.md:19`.*

**What is wrong:**

The owner asked for 'the quantities of minutes and overages' to be redacted. The D4 brief, the conversation record and the rewritten history are now clean. One listed detail is still in the repository: ADR-0103 line 129 (Accepted, on main at 8401820 and on GitHub since before the batch) gives the plan's included monthly minutes. [redacted] Two batch statements read as if the redaction were complete. docs/adr/evidence/README.md:19 says the batch's commits were rewritten 'so none of them holds the details', but every commit in 8401820..c001ced holds this figure through ADR-0103:129. ADR-0103's own Later note (line 168, added by this batch and still a draft) says the D4 brief's 'included minutes' were redacted, while line 129 of the same record states them. The brief also keeps byway's estimate in minutes and dollars a month (line 155) and byway's percentage share of the pool, and from those two the pool's size and dollar value can be worked back. Under the brief's definition ('any of the organisation's details in the repository') this could be read as blocking. I rate it should-fix because the push adds nothing GitHub does not already hold, and only the owner can decide on a rewrite of main.

**Evidence:**

I scanned the trees and messages of all 42 commits in 8401820..c001ced, and the working tree, with a script (scratchpad/recheck-evid/org-scan.cjs). Its patterns came from the excluded usage file, the repo-state file and the brief's fixed shapes; the masked output is in scratchpad/recheck-evid/org-scan.out. The only hit for the included-minutes figure in organisation context was docs/adr/0103-adr-log-rule-and-ci.md:129, present in every commit and in the working tree. The other hits for that number are unrelated: [redacted]. The coordinator told the owner in chat at 2026-10-21T20:45:30Z (13:45 local) that ADR-0103's Consequences cannot be redacted under the log rule. No owner answer on that point follows. The owner's only later message is 'workflow was interrupted. recover what you can and proceed' (21:17Z). Nothing in the repository records the remainder.

**When it goes wrong:**

A reader trusts the evidence README's 'none of them holds the details', or ADR-0103's note that the included minutes were redacted, and takes the plan's size to be out of the repository. If a snapshot or mirror is ever published (ADR-0061 D12), the figure goes with it, together with the September event, and the brief's kept percentages confirm it.

**Proposed fix:**

Report this to the owner before the push, as the owner's option 1 requires ('if it finds anything, I report first'). The owner chooses between keeping the remainder as recorded and a rewrite of main, which needs the 'Protect main' ruleset lifted. Whichever the owner chooses, correct the two batch statements. The evidence README sentence and ADR-0103's batch-added Later note should say that ADR-0103's Consequences on main still give the plan's included minutes and the September event, that the log rule forbids editing them, and that the owner was told on 2026-10-21. Optionally, ask the owner whether byway's share-of-pool percentages in the brief should go too.

**Refuter 1: partly, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

Most of the finding reproduces. One part of its suggested fix is wrong, and that part matters for what the owner gets told.

Confirmed:
(1) ADR-0103 line 129 (Consequences, Money) gives the organisation plan's included monthly minutes and [redacted]. This text is on origin/main at 8401820. It was added by 51fedcf, and every remote head contains that commit.
(2) My own scan of all 42 commits in 8401820..c001ced (trees and commit messages) finds the figure in an organisation context in exactly one place: ADR-0103:129, in all 42 commits. The other hits are unrelated: [redacted]. The D4 brief and conversation-continued.md at c001ced do not hold it. The pre-rewrite commit eedc340 is on no local or remote branch.
(3) docs/adr/evidence/README.md:19 says "so none of them holds the details". "The details" are those redacted in the D4 brief, and the brief's own header lists the plan's included minutes among them. Every commit is a full snapshot, and every one holds that figure through ADR-0103, so read literally the sentence is false.
(4) The session transcript shows the coordinator told the owner at 20:45:30Z that ADR-0103's Consequences still hold the figure. The owner never answered that point; the only later message was "workflow was interrupted. recover what you can and proceed". conversation-continued.md ends at 13:41 local, and nothing in the repository records the remainder. The evidence builder and the records pass both raised it in their reports and left the decision to the coordinator and the owner.
(5) The pool size really can be worked back from the brief's kept numbers. The brief keeps a range of Windows minutes per month and that range as a share of the included pool. With Windows minutes counted twice, which the brief's own "Linux-rate minutes" wording implies, the result is 1.00 to 1.17 times the real figure. But the header says these estimates were kept on purpose, and the owner chose the recommendation that keeps D4's reasoning readable. So this is a note.

Wrong or overstated:
(a) The finding says ADR-0103's Later note (line 168) reads as if the redaction were complete. It does not. The note describes accurately what was redacted in the brief and makes no claim about the rest of the repository. At most it is an omission in the same record, not a false statement.
(b) The finding cites "line 155" of the brief for byway's estimates. That line is about anthropic.ts; the share lines are 1096, 1147 and 1180.
(c) The main correction: the suggested fix would have the records say "the log rule forbids editing them", and it offers the owner only two choices, keeping the text or rewriting main. The coordinator said the same to the owner ("your log rule forbids editing it"), and the memory file repeats it. The owner's own rule says otherwise. ADR-0103 Decision 1.1, owner-decided, says only Context and Decision are final, and "The header table (its Status row included) and the other sections may change". scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts enforces only Context and Decision. So the owner was given a false premise about their own rule. There is a third option: redact the figure from ADR-0103's Consequences in this batch, with a Later note saying so. That clears the tree from the push onward, and only main's history would keep the figure unless it is rewritten.

Severity: should-fix. The push adds nothing GitHub does not already hold, so no new disclosure. But README:19 overstates the redaction, and the owner declined to pursue the remainder (by not answering) on a false premise about the log rule. Under the owner's option 1 ("if it finds anything, I report first"), this has to go to the owner before the push. Read literally, the brief's blocking definition ("any of the organisation's details in the repository") also covers it; I rate it should-fix only because the detail is already public on main.

Checked and sound: the brief and the conversation record hold no included-minutes figure; the rewritten history keeps the old evidence commit off every branch; the brief's header discloses the estimates and share it kept.

Not checked: GitHub's server-side copies of main (forks, caches), and whether any mirror or snapshot under ADR-0061 D12 exists.

**Evidence:**

All read-only, except one mutation in my own clone.

**Fix:**

1. Report to the owner before the push, as option 1 requires. Quote the owner's answer word for word, and add no gate the owner did not choose.
2. Whatever the owner chooses, correct docs/adr/evidence/README.md:19. Scope it to the two evidence files' history, and say that ADR-0103's Consequences on main give the plan's included minutes and the September event, and that the owner was told on 2026-10-21. Do not write that the log rule forbids editing them.
3. Optionally, in the ADR-0103 Later note the batch added, add a sentence that this record's own Consequences still give the figure (or that they were redacted, if option b is chosen).

The next owner message, at 21:17:00Z, is "workflow was interrupted. recover what you can and proceed". conversation-continued.md ends at 13:41 local and records only the owner's messages and Claude's questions. `git grep` over c001ced finds no other record of the remainder.
- The memory note C:/Users/arvik/.claude/projects/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/memory/org-account-details-stay-private.md says "that Accepted text stays (log rule)". It states the figure itself; it is outside the repository.
- The brief's choice is disclosed. decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md line 5 says "byway's own CI estimates (... and byway's share of the included minutes) ... are as written", and line 944 says "byway's own estimates are as written". The percentages are at lines 1096 and 1147. In the transcript at 20:45:30Z: "byway's own CI cost estimates stay, since they're what D4 was decided on."
- The rewritten history. 21 commits in 8401820..c001ced carry the brief, and every one has 0 unredacted lines of the fixed shapes ('That plan includes', 'the org paid', '[redacted] includes', 'The org plan is').
- The builders knew. build-result.json says ADR-0103:129 "was not edited, as the brief said". records-result.json says "I did not touch it, as instructed (it is on main)".

**Fix:**

Report this to the owner before the push, as option 1 requires, and correct what the owner was told at 20:45:30Z. The owner's own rule, ADR-0103 Decision §1 (and adr-log.ts:14), does allow ADR-0103's Consequences to be edited. Three choices for the owner:
(a) Redact ADR-0103:129 forward in this batch: the included-minutes figure, and the September sentence if the owner wants. Add a Later note saying what was redacted, when, and at the owner's request. No rewrite of main and no change to the ruleset. GitHub's history keeps the old text.
(b) Keep the line as it is, recorded as the owner's choice.
(c) Also rewrite main's history, which needs the "Protect main" ruleset lifted.
Whatever the owner chooses, rewrite README:19 so it no longer says the commits hold none of the details. It should name ADR-0103's Consequences (on main since 9521fc5, 2026-10-16) and say what was done with them. Do not write "the log rule forbids editing them": that contradicts ADR-0103 §1. Give ADR-0103:168 at most a clause pointing to the Consequences. Correct the reason in the memory note org-account-details-stay-private.md ("(log rule)"). Optionally ask the owner whether byway's share-of-pool percentages in the brief (lines 1096 and 1147, kept by the header at line 5) should be redacted as well.

#### MONEY-R1: The backlog's planning-numbers row leaves out one budget that would rise if OpenAI writes were planned (OpenAI profile with an Anthropic key: C6a $5 to $5.50)

*Note. Severity note; lens money; where `docs/backlog.md:158`.*

**What is wrong:**

The row "Check the advisor's planning numbers against the ledger" says "Planned as all written, these default run budgets would rise:" and then gives a list that looks complete. For the OpenAI profile it lists only C1 and C5, plus C3, C6, C6a and C7 without an Anthropic key. It leaves out the one change the code gives with an Anthropic key: C6a goes from $5 to $5.50. ADR-0109's matching Negative bullet does include it ("(with one, C6a $5 to $5.50)").

**Evidence:**

I recomputed independently from two full clones (c001ced, and main at 8401820), using each clone's own profileStatus over all 32 key sets and apps/web's suggestBudget rule. Script: C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/recheck-clones/money-lens-est.mts. Output: C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/recheck-clones/money-lens-est.txt. Its line under 'WRITES-PLANNED CHANGES': "openai img=1 vid=0 oth=1 c6a: $5 -> $5.5 (high 3.67 -> 4.04)". backlog.md:158 has no entry for it. Every other figure in that row matches the output.

**When it goes wrong:**

Someone uses the backlog row to decide whether OpenAI writes should be planned. With an OpenAI and an Anthropic key, they do not see that C6a's default budget on the OpenAI profile would also move. No money is wrong; the record is incomplete.

**Proposed fix:**

In backlog.md:158, after "C6a $6.50 to $7 and C7 $2.50 to $3", add "(with one, C6a $5 to $5.50)", as ADR-0109's Negative bullet has.

#### MONEY-R2: Claude's choice to keep paying OpenAI's 1.25x writes does not record the option of turning implicit caching off

*Note. Severity note; lens money; where `docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:72`.*

**What is wrong:**

ADR-0109 now says byway sends no prompt_cache_options, so OpenAI writes every uncached prompt of 1,024 tokens or more at 1.25x input. The estimates and the pre-call reservation still plan it at 1.0x, and a backlog row checks this at the first live run. The installed SDK documents a request option that avoids the premium altogether. With mode 'explicit' and no breakpoints, "the request does not use prompt caching". The ADR's Alternatives considered does not name that option. Note that the option also gives up the 0.1x reads wherever prefixes repeat. This is Claude's reading, offered as an alternative to record, not a defect.

**Evidence:**

node_modules/.pnpm/openai@7.28.0_ws@8.22.0_zod@4.6.5/node_modules/openai/resources/chat/completions/completions.d.ts:2097-2106, PromptCacheOptions.mode: "With `explicit`, OpenAI does not create an implicit cache breakpoint ... If there are no explicit breakpoints, the request does not use prompt caching." OpenAI's prompt-caching guide, read today, says the same for explicit mode: "When no explicit breakpoints are placed, the request does not use prompt caching or create cache writes." ADR-0109's Alternatives considered (lines 71-74) lists only the gRPC-4, env-names and Gemini-price alternatives.

**When it goes wrong:**

No wrong money today: the ledger now charges writes at their rate. The estimate-low residual and the live-run check exist only because implicit writes are left on. A reader cannot tell whether removing them was considered.

**Proposed fix:**

Either add an Alternatives bullet, labelled as Claude's, saying why implicit caching stays on (reads at 0.1x where prefixes repeat), or file the option in the backlog row "OpenAI cache writes" as something to decide once the first live run's ledger shows the read/write split.

#### RS2-1: "Waits for good" is too strong: the next engine start after a change to byway's workflow code ends such a workflow

*Note. Severity note; lens runstart; where `docs/adr/0109-money-path-before-the-first-live-run.md:80`.*

**What is wrong:**

ADR-0109's new bullet "A workflow left under a failed run" says a question-first workflow under a failed run "waits for good" and "the workflow stays open in the engine". Its reasons include "the engine's start-up reconcile leaves ended runs alone". ADR-0099's Later note ("waits for good rather than ending") and the backlog row "Paid-call residuals named only in ADRs" ("waits for good") say the same. But the same start-up step first runs retireStaleRuns, which does not look at the store's status. It terminates every running run workflow whose memo hash differs from the worker's bundle, and settleLostRun withdraws its open checkpoints even when the run has already ended. So such a workflow waits only until a byway update changes the workflow bundle, then ends at the next engine start.

**Evidence:**

packages/runner/src/dev-engine.ts:104-106: settleBeforePolling runs worker.retireStaleRuns(client) and only then reconcileLostRuns. packages/runner/src/versioning.ts:35-52 lists `WorkflowType = 'runWorkflow' AND ExecutionStatus = 'Running'`, skips only a matching hash or historyLength <= 2, then terminates and calls settleLostRun. A workflow that opened a checkpoint has run tasks, and workflow/run.ts:36 upserts its hash memo at its start. packages/runner/src/lost-runs.ts:57: `await store.withdrawCheckpoints(runId);` runs before the ENDED early return on line 58. reconcileLostRuns (lost-runs.ts:120) is the part that skips ENDED runs.

**When it goes wrong:**

Nothing is spent and nothing is shown, so no money or behaviour is wrong. A reader of the residual takes the workflow and its question to be permanent, and then sees them gone after an update (terminated as "started by an older version of byway"). The backlog trigger "the engine is seen holding a running workflow for a run History shows as failed" then cannot fire for that workflow.

**Proposed fix:**

In ADR-0109 line 80, ADR-0099's 2026-10-18 Later note and backlog row 132, replace "waits for good" with wording such as "waits until byway's workflow code changes: the next engine start after such an update terminates it and withdraws its question (retireStaleRuns)", and keep "the start-up reconcile leaves ended runs alone" as the reason it outlives an ordinary restart. The ADR-0099 note is this batch's draft, and ADR-0109 is not on main, so both can still be edited in place.

#### RS2-2: ADR-0099's Later note says a late start's workflow "cannot spend"; ADR-0109 and the backlog say a call could begin

*Note. Severity note; lens runstart; where `docs/adr/0099-paid-operations-keyed-to-their-caller.md:90`.*

**What is wrong:**

The rewritten Later note says: "A start that lands after the describe still finds the run failed; its workflow cannot spend". ADR-0109's "A late start" (line 79), which the note cites, and the backlog row "Paid-call residuals named only in ADRs" both name an exception: "between the describe and that store write, which follows it at once, a call could begin". The two records now disagree on the same residual.

**Evidence:**

client.ts:176-178: `const workflow = await workflowAfterFailedStart(id); if (workflow === 'exists') return record; await store.updateRun(id, { status: 'failed', ... })`. Nothing stops a start that lands between the describe and the updateRun, and the gateway's liveness refusal applies only to calls that begin after the failed mark. ADR-0109:79 and backlog.md:132 say so. ADR-0099:90 does not.

**When it goes wrong:**

A reader of ADR-0099 alone takes the late start to be free of any spend. The window is very narrow (a workflow task, beginRun and a model-step activity must all run before one SQLite write), so the practical effect is small.

**Proposed fix:**

In the ADR-0099 Later note, write "its workflow's calls are refused (but for the moment between the describe and the store write, ADR-0109 'A late start')" instead of "its workflow cannot spend".

#### REC2-2: Records disagree on whether Google's price page was read again on 2026-10-21

*Note. Severity note; lens records; where `README.md:41`.*

**What is wrong:**

The README says the price table was 'last read on 2026-10-18, OpenAI's again on 2026-10-21'. The backlog's Gemini row says Google's pricing page was read 2026-10-18 'and again 2026-10-21'. ADR-0109's Evidence lists the Google, Anthropic and OpenRouter pages as read on 2026-10-21 too. prices.ts's GOOGLE source string still names only 2026-10-18.

**Evidence:**

README.md:41 (fix-round edit). docs/backlog.md:65: 'Google's pricing page (read 2026-10-18, and again 2026-10-21)'. ADR-0109:103-105: 'Pages read on 2026-10-21 ... Google: ... ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/pricing (last updated 2026-10-19; byway's figures unchanged) ... Anthropic: platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing'. prices.ts:43: "const GOOGLE = '... read 2026-10-18'".

**When it goes wrong:**

Someone deciding whether to re-read prices before the first live run (the row's 2026-11-01 trigger) gets different dates for Google's page from the README, the backlog and prices.ts. Nothing is priced wrong.

**Proposed fix:**

Pick one account. Either the README says the Google and Anthropic pages were also read again on 2026-10-21 with figures unchanged, or the Gemini row drops 'and again 2026-10-21' for the pricing page and names the API reference instead.

#### REC2-3: First-live-run row: an 'and then' that reads as a step, and a gate restated without its label

*Note. Severity note; lens records; where `docs/backlog.md:157`.*

**What is wrong:**

The proposed run order's parenthesis reads '(with every key, estimated at $0.07 to $0.46, default budget $1, and then the only Balanced configuration whose high estimate is under $1)'. 'and then' reads as another run, but it means C4 is the only such configuration. The sentence 'Before the C3 run, settle the row "asset.integrate's ..."' puts a gate in the owner's procedure with no label of its own. It restates Claude's trigger from the asset.integrate row, where it is labelled.

#### REC2-5: One record on main had more than a Later note changed: ADR-0100's Decided by row (before the fix round; the repository's own rule allows it)

The batch made this change before the fix round; the fix round did not touch ADR-0100. ADR-0103 §1.1 allows it ('The header table (its Status row included) and the other sections may change'), and the first check accepted it (REC-4). No change needed if the repository's rule is the one meant. Otherwise state the rule as ADR-0103 §1.1 has it in later briefs.

#### REC2-6: Off-path: page-copy.test.ts still uses the lossy LCG product that ADR-0097's note calls a defect

*Note. Severity note; lens records; where `packages/runner/test/page-copy.test.ts:1230`.*

**What is wrong:**

The ADR-0097 Later note describes `seed * 1103515245` as losing its low bits, so it is not the intended generator. The same expression drives a fuzz test's rnd() in packages/runner/test/page-copy.test.ts. This is outside the fix round and outside this lens. It is parked here only so it is not lost.

**Evidence:**

git grep 1103515245 at c001ced: packages/runner/test/page-copy.test.ts:1230 'const rnd = () => ((seed = (seed * 1103515245 + 12345) & 0x7fffffff) / 0x7fffffff);'. scripts/test/claims-oracle/oracle.ts:85 uses Math.imul.

**When it goes wrong:**

The fuzz test's stream may enter a short cycle sooner than intended and so sample fewer distinct cases. Impact is likely small for a fixed seed.

**Proposed fix:**

File a backlog row, or switch it to Math.imul (a test-only change) in a later batch.

#### KEY-R1: Key check: the stand-in is exempted as a substring, and lowercase 'bearer', URL-encoded fal keys and the claims-oracle port are outside it

*Note. Severity note; lens evidence; where `scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts:47`.*

**What is wrong:**

Every shape the test claims is caught. Four small gaps remain. (1) withoutStandIns replaces the stand-in anywhere in a line, so any key-shaped string that begins with the 24-letter stand-in passes whole. (2) The bearer shape it shares with the gateway's redactor is case-sensitive, so 'authorization: bearer &lt;token>' passes, and HTTP auth schemes are case-insensitive. (3) A fal key written with %3A for the colon passes. (4) The header says the folders 'copied in from Temp' are scanned, but scripts/test/claims-oracle, ported from Temp in 24e69f5, is not among them.

**Evidence:**

I planted 25 random fakes, one per line, in docs/adr/evidence/logs, packages/runner/test/f07-corpus and docs/reviews in my clone (recheck-clones/evid-lens; scratchpad/recheck-evid/plant.cjs). All 20 claimed shapes failed the test in all three folders, with every line printed masked: Anthropic (api03, admin), OpenAI (proj, legacy, svcacct), OpenRouter, Google in a URL, fal lower-case, upper-case and in JSON, Bearer, Bearer JWT, ghp_, gho_, ghs_, github_pat_, xoxb-, xoxp-, JWT, and a CRLF line. These passed: 'sk-proj-ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWX' followed by 40 more key characters, a lowercase 'bearer' line, and a %3A fal key. With redact.ts's fal pattern made case-sensitive, the self-test failed on falUpperCase, so the shared shapes cannot drift silently. A hand scan of scripts/test/claims-oracle, the ADRs, the backlog, the playbook and README.md with the same shapes found 0 lines.

**When it goes wrong:**

Unlikely in practice. A log that prints a lowercase 'bearer' header, or a URL-encoded FAL_KEY, lands in docs/adr/evidence and the check stays green.

**Proposed fix:**

Anchor the exemption with a regex: the stand-in followed by (?![A-Za-z0-9_-]). Optionally make redact.ts's Bearer shape case-insensitive, which would also fix the product redactor. Add scripts/test/claims-oracle to the scanned folders, or narrow the header's wording.

#### HIST-R1: After the rewrite, 14 commits hold the redacted D4 text while their header and 11fc3b0's message say the text is unchanged

*Note. Severity note; lens evidence; where `docs/adr/evidence/decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md:5`.*

**What is wrong:**

The rewrite changed only the 8 value lines in each commit that held the file. So from 11fc3b0 to b15a878 the file has '[another repository]' 4 times and '[redacted]' 17 times, while its own line 5 still says 'The words are unchanged;'. 11fc3b0's message also says 'text unchanged'. The explanation first appears in 15acbd2's header and in the evidence README at the tip. Once merged, main's history keeps those 14 versions.

**Evidence:**

Each old commit and its rewrite (eedc340 to 11fc3b0 through 06506f1 to b15a878) have the same message, author and dates, and differ only in that file, '8 8' lines. c1ea439 to 15acbd2 changes only the message's last line, and cab3c77 to b12ff64 changes nothing. Marker counts: 11fc3b0 and 95f1eaf have 4 and 17, with the 'unchanged;' claim present. c001ced has 6 and 19, and the claim is gone. No ref holds any of the 16 old commits (git for-each-ref --contains is empty for each), and the remote has no mirror setting.

**When it goes wrong:**

Someone reading the file at an older commit, for example through git blame, sees redaction markers under a header that says the words are unchanged, with no reason given.

**Proposed fix:**

No history change is needed. The tip says what happened. Optionally, the push summary or the evidence README could say that the commits before 15acbd2 hold the redacted text under their original header.

#### TEMP-R1: EVID-2: the marker-copy half is neither fixed nor filed, and a9c4b58's subject claims 'every spelling of Temp'

*Note. Severity note; lens evidence; where `scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts:24`.*

But a9c4b58's subject says 'the Temp check reads every spelling of Temp', and $TEMP/, ${TEMP}/, %TMP%, $env:TMP, %LOCALAPPDATA%\Temp\claude\… and Git Bash's /tmp/ all still pass.
