#### F07-R1: The corpus README states as fact that the pages' contact details are not real people's, though none was checked

*Note. Severity note; lens evidence; where `packages/runner/test/f07-corpus/README.md:68`.*

**What is wrong:**

'Their names, email addresses, phone numbers and social links were made up with them; they are not real people's or businesses' details, though a made-up address or number can match a real one by chance, and none was checked.' A model can reproduce a real address or profile path, and SEC-2 named some on unreserved domains. That they are not real is Claude's belief, not something anyone checked, so the sentence should be labelled as Claude's.

**Evidence:**

README.md line 68 at c001ced. SEC-2's evidence lists addresses on unreserved domains and profile paths in pages/*.html and expected/round14.json.

**When it goes wrong:**

A reader takes 'they are not real people's details' as verified and copies a page into something public.

**Proposed fix:**

Reword: 'model-written; Claude did not take them from real people and did not check whether any belongs to one'.

### The earlier findings, as the re-check found them (49)

- **MONEY-1** (money), fixed: prices.ts gives the three OpenAI text rows cacheWritePerMTok 12.5, 2.5 and 0.125. I checked these today against developers.openai.com/api/docs/pricing ('Short context 30m write'). parseChatCompletion reads prompt_tokens_details.cache_write_tokens (?? 0, so null and absent count as 0). tokenCost charges plain = prompt − cached − written at input, cached at the read rate and written at the write rate. That is exactly the guide's 'not an additive fee' split (its example: 12000 − 4000 − 7000). Worked by hand for 16,500 prompt tokens (4,000 cached, 12,000 written) and 800 output on gpt-6-astra: 500×10 + 4000×1 + 12000×12.5 + 800×50 = 199,000 → $0.199, with no token counted twice or dropped. The old code gave $0.169. OpenRouter rows have no write rate, so a write falls back to the input rate, as before, and reportedCostUsd still wins. I proved the tests in a clone. Reverting openai.ts fails 3 tests (both providers tests, and the gateway test: 0.169 vs 0.199). Reverting prices.ts fails 5 tests. Removing only gpt-6.1-sol's write rate fails its row-table test. ADR-0109's Context and Decision 1 are corrected, and ADR-0109 is not on main (git show 8401820 has no such file), so editing it is allowed.
- **MONEY-2** (money), fixed: textAndThinkingTokens now returns thoughts plus max(non-IMAGE listed, candidatesTokenCount − IMAGE sum). It falls back to thoughts + candidatesTokenCount when the breakdown is not an array, and to the listed tokens when candidatesTokenCount is null or undefined. This is consistent with Google's UsageMetadata reference, read today: totalTokenCount is 'prompt + tool-use prompt + thoughts + response candidates', and candidatesTokensDetails is only 'List of modalities that were returned in the response'. I probed edge cases at runtime. A null breakdown gives 1600, the same as absent. An empty array gives every token at the text rate. A null entry or null tokenCount counts as 0. A count below the IMAGE sum floors at the listed tokens, never negative. NaN in candidatesTokenCount, thoughts or an IMAGE count gives NaN, which finiteUsage replaces and flags. The third-party sample (615/2478/208/IMAGE 2240) gives 446 output tokens. Mutations in a clone: reverting google.ts fails 2 tests (200 vs 830; +0 vs NaN). Math.min fails. Treating an empty array as listed-only fails (300 vs 1600). Number(x)||0 fails the NaN test. An absent breakdown counting thoughts only fails 3 tests.
- **MONEY-3** (money), fixed: I recomputed from two full clones (not the builder's copied files), with each clone's own profileStatus over all key sets. No Balanced default budget changes. Balanced C3 is $2.50 with all keys (high 1.64 → 1.62), $2 with an OpenAI key and no Gemini key, and $1.50 without OpenAI. Google C1 goes $3.50 → $4 (2.75 → 2.88). The Best changes and the OpenAI-profile-without-Anthropic changes are exactly as ADR-0109's Positive bullet states. One image costs $0.04905 against $0.03405, and 12 boards $0.59 against $0.41. The writes-planned list in ADR-0109's Negative bullet matches the output entry for entry.
- **REC-2** (money), fixed: ADR-0109 Consequences now names the VOICE drop in every configuration. High-end calls (C1 61→60, C2 24→23, C3 24→23, C4 12→10, C5 83→81, C6 32→31, C6a 44→43, C7 18→17) and low ends (C4 −1, C5 −2) match my recomputation. The false '$2 to $2.50' sentence is gone.
- **MONEY-4** (money), fixed: The prices.ts comments and ADR-0109 Decision 1 now list each unrecorded rate with its lean. I checked them against pages fetched today. High: Google cache $0.20 / $0.075 → $0.15, with implicit caching on by default for Gemini 2.5 and newer; gpt-6-luna cached $0.01. None today: OpenRouter reads 0.05x (0.025x Fable) and writes 1.25x / 2x 1h (curl of api/v1/models matches), since byway sends no cache_control (grep) and the usage.cost path is preferred; the gpt-image cached input is Responses-API only (page note quoted). Low, not requested: OpenAI 12h writes 2.5x ($25 vs $10), long context >272K, Google >200K, the 10% regional uplift (no baseURL or regional endpoint in the gateway), and Anthropic's 1h writes, inference_geo 'us' 1.1x and fast mode (Anthropic page). The leans are right in each case.
- **START-1** (runstart), fixed: engine-state.test.ts: both one-shot reads, the 'Temporal accepted it' test and the reconcile test's live run, now wait with statusIs. The helper's new comment is correct: openCheckpoint and setRunStatus are separate activities with no fixed order. The added comment 'A run marked failed never reads waiting' is also correct: setRunStatus (lifecycle.ts:34-38) returns early for an ENDED run. In my clone (recheck-clones/runstart) I delayed setRunStatus('waiting') by 250 ms: engine-state.test.ts passed 10 of 10. I then made client.ts mark an 'exists' run failed: 'Temporal accepted it' failed ('timed out waiting for an open checkpoint'), so the test still catches the regression. The remaining one-shot read at line 160 follows a statusIs wait and a no-op retire, so it does not race.
- **START-6** (runstart), fixed: The new start-describe case is deterministic. The writer's stand-in start awaits the repeat, and the repeat's start throws 14 before accept() runs. Proved in my clone: with client.ts:173 `if (!created) throw e;` replaced by `void created;`, the new test fails at line 158 ('status: queued' expected, 'failed' received), 1 failed and 17 passed across start-describe, start-failure and run-identity. With the guard restored, all 7 tests in start-describe pass.
- **START-2** (runstart), fixed: 'Narrowed, not closed' now appears in ADR-0109's Alternatives, its 'What this changes' entry for ADR-0099, the ADR-0099 Later note and the status page's F03 row. The DEADLINE_EXCEEDED claim holds: grpc-retry.js retryableCodes excludes code 4. The restart claim holds: serve.ts stops the web before the engine, and engine-watch.ts uses a 5 s interval and 3 failures. Positive bullet 63 now names its exceptions. No 'closes the double fault' wording remains in docs or code. One leftover inconsistency is RS2-2.

- **START-4** (runstart), recorded-as-chosen: Recorded only, as the brief chose: a new ADR-0109 Negative bullet, Decision 3's added sentence, and the new backlog row 'Run start: the describe doubles the wait when the engine is down', its trigger labelled as Claude's. - **START-5** (runstart), partly: Recorded as chosen: a new ADR-0109 Negative bullet, the ADR-0099 note and the backlog sentence. The facts hold. human.ts openCheckpoint has no ended-run check. C4 (c4.ts:34, inside seq), C6 (c6.ts:56) and C7 (c7.ts:48) open D1 with optional(...). signal() returns early for an ENDED run, and resolve() refuses one. reconcileLostRuns skips ENDED runs. Non-terminal events are suppressed for ended runs (events.ts:22-25), and the web's liveQuestions filters questions to live runs (apps/web/src/view/run.ts:35-37), so 'unseen' holds. But 'waits for good' and 'stays open in the engine' miss retireStaleRuns, which ends such a workflow at the next engine start after a workflow-code change (RS2-1).

- **renderer-check flake (gate)** (runstart), fixed: c001ced replaces the builder's 90 s probe and 180 s test limit with HUNG_PROBE_MS = 50_000 and keeps each test's 60_000, which is the owner's option text quoted verbatim in the commit message. Three tests get the bound, and those are exactly the ones whose answer needs a real probe draw: 'on, and the page renderer does not start...' (a real probe without the sandbox), the first check of 'off: ready...' (a real probe with the sandbox), and the refusal test 'the start-up check found the sandbox cannot start...' (a real probe without the sandbox). The others were rightly left on the product's 15 s, because their probes reject at once with no Chromium: 'Chromium does not start at all' (MISSING), 'dies at launch' (DIED both ways), 'never throws' (StepError), and the second check in 'off: ready' (SANDBOXED). The held-probe tests keep 50 ms and 600 s on purpose. PROBE_MS in packages/runner/src/pages.ts is unchanged: there is no diff in packages/runner/src from 8401820 or from 95f1eaf to c001ced. In my clone, a 20 s delay on the probe launch in the gate's test passed with probeMs 50 s, and failed with 'sandbox: unknown' after the bound was removed (the 15 s default). That shows the fix covers the flake's mechanism. renderer-check.test.ts passed 21 of 21 at c001ced in my clone (10.8 s). The backlog's item (5) records this with the owner's option label verbatim and says these tests are not a named flake, which follows from the owner choosing 'Fix' over 'Name it in the backlog'.

- Fixed wall-clock limits that CI could hit: main's last Windows CI run (39257745247, read-only via gh) ran all of renderer-check.test.ts in 6.0 s and engine-state.test.ts in 6.1 s. ## The small re-check of the second fix round (2026-10-21)

*From `recheck2-result.json`. The re-check the owner chose at 16:14, "Gate + small records/evidence re-check, then push (Recommended)": a records lens and an evidence lens on the second fix round's commits, with refuters.*

### Findings that survived the refuters (2)

#### REC3-1: Evidence README says byway's share was taken out of the batch's commits, but 42 of the 47 still carry ADR-0103's share line

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed; lens records; where `docs/adr/evidence/README.md:19`.*

**What is wrong:**

The paragraph says byway's share of the plan's included minutes "was then taken out of those commits too, at the third" (16:42). It names only "the number of included minutes itself" as still in the history, through ADR-0103's Consequences. But every batch commit before e5289cf (42 of 8401820..d8f077c) still holds ADR-0103's Money line in its old form: byway's monthly minutes as a percentage of the included minutes, and the figure itself. That includes b015077, which brought the brief in, and a032125. The second rewrite changed only the brief. The sentence is true of the brief's copies, but it reads as if no share remains in those commits, and the README does not say the share also stays in history.

**Evidence:**

spawnSync git show &lt;c>:docs/adr/0103-adr-log-rule-and-ci.md, matched by pattern with no values printed: the share-and-figure clause matches once in b015077, a032125 and 0cf577a, and not in e5289cf. Over all 47 commits, the share string is in 42 trees and the figure in the same 42 (only ADR-0103 among byway's org statements). The line is identical in 51fedcf, 0283d09, 15d7ede, df2d536 and 8401820 (equal md5 of the matched clause). 51fedcf is an ancestor of 057352e, pushed to origin/fix/fx-d4-ci at 2026-10-16 21:57 -0700 (remote-tracking reflog). The same scan finds the brief's two share values in 0 of the 26 commits that hold the brief. This is not new exposure: the same text has been on GitHub since 2026-10-16, answer 1 keeps "the figure" in that history, and the 16:41 question said the figure is in GitHub's history through ADR-0103. So it is not blocking, but the record understates what the pushed batch holds.

**When it goes wrong:**

The owner reads the README after the push to learn what GitHub's history still holds. They conclude that byway's share appears nowhere in the batch's commits and only the bare number is left. In fact 42 pushed batch commits, and main since 51fedcf, state the share percentage next to the number.

**Proposed fix:**

Reword the last two sentences, for example: "The repository's history still holds the number of included minutes, and byway's share of it as a percentage: ADR-0103's Consequences gave both until Claude redacted them there on 2026-10-21 at that second answer. That text came in with 51fedcf, pushed to GitHub on 2026-10-16, has been on main since 643616e, and is unchanged in the batch's commits before e5289cf." Docs only; the adr-evidence and README tests are unaffected.

**Refuter 1: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I could not refute it. Every factual claim in REC3-1 reproduces, and the README's wording does understate what the batch's commits hold.

README line 19 says byway's share "was then taken out of those commits too". "Those commits" means the commits that brought conversation-continued.md and the D4 brief in, and git log --diff-filter=A shows that is b015077 alone. It then says only "the number of included minutes itself" stays in history. But b015077 and 41 other batch commits still carry ADR-0103's old Money line, which gives byway's monthly minutes as a percentage of the included minutes together with the figure. The brief's two redacted percentages are different numbers from ADR-0103's (compared as booleans, no values printed). So ADR-0103's share is a separate statement that the second rewrite never touched. The sentence is true only of the brief's copies. The README never says a share percentage stays in history. ADR-0103's Later note, which the README points to, says only "The figure stays". That matches the owner's chosen option ("the figure stays in the history pushed on 2026-10-16"), so it is not a false record of the owner's words.

Why should-fix and not blocking. The task's blocking rule names "any organisation detail ... in any commit of 8401820..d8f077c", and read literally that would catch these 42 commits. But answer 1 keeps the figure in history. The same clause (same md5) is on origin/main and has been on GitHub since 51fedcf was pushed on 2026-10-16. Rewriting the batch would not remove it from GitHub. So no new information goes public. What remains is a record that understates. It matters because at 16:42 the owner chose to rewrite so the brief's percentages "never reach GitHub". The 16:41 question did not mention that ADR-0103's old line is also in the unpushed commits.

Small things the finding gets slightly wrong or leaves out:
(a) The failure scenario says "main since 51fedcf". 51fedcf was pushed to fix/fx-d4-ci on 2026-10-16 and reached main through the merge 643616e on 2026-10-17. The suggested fix text already has this right.
(b) "Not new exposure" is true of the information, but not of the git objects. 29 of the 42 commits carry five versions of ADR-0103 that differ from main's (the batch edited other parts of ADR-0103), so the push uploads new versions that still contain the same clause.
(c) The README limits the rewrite to "the commits that brought ... in". In fact the second rewrite changed the brief in 21 commits, every commit that held the old brief.

What I checked and found sound:
- The second rewrite changed only the brief. Pairing the old chain 8401820..5abafbe with the new chain 8401820..ca24d4e: 25 pairs have identical trees, the other 21 differ only in decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md, and the final trees are equal.
- The brief's share values appear in 0 of the 26 commits that hold the brief, against 21 of 25 before the rewrite.
- None of the 47 commit messages contains ADR-0103's figure or share.
- In d8f077c's ADR-0103, evidence README and brief, the figure and share are absent.
- The figure's other matches in the trees are unrelated numbers ([redacted]).
- adr-evidence.test.ts makes no assertion about the README paragraph's text, so a docs-only rewording breaks no test (found by grep; I did not run the tests).

What I could not check: GitHub's server-side state directly. For that I relied on the remote-tracking reflog and the origin/main ref.

**Evidence:**

Scripts in C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/rec3-1/ (scan.mjs, ctx.mjs, hist.mjs, rewrite.mjs, msgs.mjs). They use spawnSync('git', ...) without a shell and print only counts, ids and md5s. Results:
- Of 47 commits in 8401820..d8f077c, 42 have ADR-0103's clause "&lt;p>% to &lt;q>% of the &lt;N> minutes the organization's plan includes each month". Every copy has md5 47a3f805..., the same as in 8401820 and origin/main.
- The 5 commits without it are e5289cf, fc7ac3a, 0442ef8, ca24d4e and d8f077c. b015077, a032125 and 0cf577a have it.
- The figure appears in ADR-0103 in exactly those 42 commits.
- The pickaxe (git log -S with the clause) on main gives only 51fedcf (2026-10-16 21:49 -0700); its parent 04e6824 has no clause. 51fedcf, 057352e, 0283d09, 15d7ede, df2d536, 643616e and 8401820 all have the same md5.
- Reflog: refs/remotes/origin/fix/fx-d4-ci@{2026-10-16 21:57:46 -0700} = 057352e "update by push". 51fedcf is an ancestor of 057352e and of 643616e (merge, 2026-10-17 10:28), and 643616e is an ancestor of 8401820.
- Old chain against new chain (fix/fx-batch reflog: 5abafbe before "filter-branch: rewrite", ca24d4e after): 25 pairs have identical trees, 21 differ only in docs/adr/evidence/decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md, and the final trees are equal.
- The brief's redacted share tokens are in 0 of the 26 commits that hold the brief now, against 21 of 25 before. Neither token equals ADR-0103's percentages.
- ADR-0103 versions across the 42 commits: 13 commits carry main's version, 29 carry 5 versions not on main.
- 0 of 47 commit messages contain the figure or the share.
- conversation-continued.md (CLAUDE-ASKS 16:41:21): the "No" option says "that exact figure is already in GitHub's history through ADR-0103 (since 2026-10-16)". The question names only the D4 brief's percentages as being in the unpushed commits.
- README line 19 reads "...was then taken out of those commits too, at the third ... The repository's history still holds the number of included minutes itself: ADR-0103's Consequences gave it until Claude redacted it there...". It makes no mention of the percentage.

**Fix:**

This is a docs-only fix, made before the push.
1. In docs/adr/evidence/README.md, change "was then taken out of those commits too" to say the brief's percentages were taken out of every batch commit's copy of the brief.
2. Replace "The repository's history still holds the number of included minutes itself: ADR-0103's Consequences gave it until..." with something like: "The repository's history still holds the number of included minutes, and byway's monthly minutes as a percentage of it: ADR-0103's Consequences gave both until Claude redacted them there on 2026-10-21, at that second answer. That text came in with 51fedcf, pushed to GitHub on 2026-10-16, has been on main since the merge 643616e of 2026-10-17, and is unchanged in the batch's commits before e5289cf."
3. Optionally, add "with the percentage of it" after "The figure" in ADR-0103's Later note. Keep the owner's quoted words as they are.
4. In the push report, tell the owner that ADR-0103's old Money line (figure and share) is still in 42 of the batch's commits. It is the same text as on main since 643616e, so the push makes nothing new public.

**Refuter 2: confirmed, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

I tried to refute it on three grounds. None of them holds.

(1) Read on its own scope, the sentence might be true. The paragraph opens by limiting itself to "those commits' copies of the two files". The share sentence says the share was "redacted in the brief". On a strict reading, it only claims the brief's copies lost the share, and that part is true: the brief's share lines are redacted in all 26 commits that hold the brief. But the sentence then says the share "was then taken out of those commits too". The only commit that brought both files in is b015077, and b015077 still states byway's share in ADR-0103's Money line. The next sentence says the history "still holds the number of included minutes itself". The word "itself" sets the number against the share, so a reader takes it that only the bare number is left. ADR-0103's Consequences gave byway's monthly minutes as a percentage of that number. That percentage is in 42 of the 47 batch commits and on main. The README never says so.

(2) It might follow the owner's chosen wording. It does not. Answer 1 told the ADR-0103 Later note to say "that the figure stays in the history pushed on 2026-10-16", and that note does so. The README paragraph is round two's own new text (d8f077c), not words the owner chose. The owner never chose to leave the share out of the account of what history holds, so this is not an owner's choice.

(3) It might be harmless because nothing new is exposed. That is true for exposure: the same line has been on GitHub since 51fedcf on 2026-10-16, main's history cannot be rewritten, and so it is not blocking. But the README is the record of what history holds. d8f077c's subject says "the evidence says what the history now holds", and the 16:41 question's other option said "The evidence README already says what the history still holds". The owner's answer at 16:42, "Yes, rewrite again before the push", also shows they care that share percentages not reach GitHub, even with the figure already there. So an account that leaves out a share still in the pushed history is a real error in a privacy record. The fix is small and touches docs only.

One small overstatement in the finding: its title says "the batch's commits". The README actually says "those commits", meaning the ones that brought the two files in. The substance holds anyway, since b015077 is that commit.

What I checked and found sound:
- The 42/47 count, and that the line is unchanged since 51fedcf.
- The ancestry and the push.
- The brief's share values are a different pair of percentages from ADR-0103's. So ADR-0103's share is a separate org detail that the second rewrite did not touch, and the README's "taken out of those commits too" does not cover it.
- The HEAD tree holds neither the share clause nor the figure in any minutes context.

What I could not check: whether GitHub's server-side copy of 057352e is still present. I relied on the local remote-tracking reflog for that.

**Evidence:**

- **Scans.** Run read-only with spawnSync('git', ['-C', worktree, 'show', `${c}:docs/adr/0103-adr-log-rule-and-ci.md`]). The scripts are in C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/rec3-1/ (scan.mjs, cmp.mjs, ctx.mjs, fig.mjs). Values were matched and compared but never printed.
- **ADR-0103's share and figure.** The Money clause "at list price, X of the Y minutes the organization's plan includes each month" holds a share with percentage tokens and a figure with digits in 8401820, 51fedcf, 643616e and the first 42 commits of 8401820..d8f077c (rev-list count 47). That includes b015077, a032125 and 0cf577a. The matched clause is the same in all of them. In e5289cf, fc7ac3a, 0442ef8, ca24d4e and d8f077c both are "[redacted]".
- **The brief's values.** The pre-second-rewrite commit c001ced (from the branch reflog) has the brief's two share lines unredacted. Their percentage tokens overlap none of ADR-0103's two share tokens. The second rewrite therefore removed different values, and ADR-0103's share is untouched in those commits.
- **Which commit brought the files in.** git log --diff-filter=A shows that b015077 alone added both conversation-continued.md and decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md.
- **Ancestry and push.** 51fedcf (2026-10-16 21:49 -0700) is an ancestor of 643616e (the merge of fix/fx-d4-ci, 2026-10-17), which is an ancestor of main 8401820. 51fedcf is also an ancestor of 057352e. The reflog of origin/fix/fx-d4-ci shows "057352e update by push" at 2026-10-16 21:57:46 -0700.
- **The README's wording.** README.md:19 (added in d8f077c) says the share "was then taken out of those commits too, at the third" and "The repository's history still holds the number of included minutes itself". It never says the percentage stays.
- **ADR-0103's Later note.** At ADR-0103:169 it lists the two replaced figures, the number and "byway's monthly minutes as a percentage of that number". It then says "The figure stays in the repository's history", which is the owner's chosen wording.
- **HEAD.** No file in d8f077c holds ADR-0103's old share clause. Every match for the figure's digits is an unrelated number ([redacted]).

**Fix:**

This is a docs-only change in docs/adr/evidence/README.md:19.
- **Share sentence.** Narrow it to "byway's share of the plan's included minutes as the D4 brief gave it, redacted there at the owner's second answer, was then taken out of those commits' copies of the brief too, at the third".
- **History sentence.** Replace it with: "The repository's history still holds the number of included minutes, and byway's monthly minutes as a percentage of it: ADR-0103's Consequences gave both until Claude redacted them there on 2026-10-21, at that second answer. That text came in with 51fedcf, pushed to GitHub on 2026-10-16, has been on main since the merge 643616e of 2026-10-17, and is unchanged in the batch's commits before e5289cf; removing it from history would need a force-push to main, ..." Keep the rest of the sentence as it is.
- **ADR-0103's Later note.** Leave its quoted owner wording ("the figure stays in the history") unchanged. Optionally add after it, in Claude's voice: "with the percentage beside it".

No test changes are needed.

#### HIST2-1: The merge message's planned slip list uses ids the second rewrite replaced, and the rewrite added two new slips

*Finding. Severity should-fix; outcome confirmed-partly; lens evidence; where `docs/adr/evidence/decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md:5`.*

**What is wrong:**

The owner chose to name the commit-message slips in the merge message. The items queued for that message (round2-result.json 'open', and the re-check's HIST-R1, MSG-R1 and REC2-4) cite 15acbd2, a9c4b58, 11fc3b0 and b15a878. The second rewrite replaced all four, and the old ids will not exist on GitHub. The rewrite also created two slips of its own: (a) e5289cf's message says 'The D4 brief: its two share-of-pool percentages read [redacted]', but its diff no longer makes that change; (b) seven commits hold the brief with byway's share reading [redacted] while the file's own header says that share is 'as written'.

**Evidence:**

hist2-map.json maps 15acbd2 to a032125, a9c4b58 to 7db9230, 11fc3b0 to b015077 and b15a878 to 2e5fbfa. In 0cf577a (e5289cf's parent), lines 1096 and 1147 of the brief already read [redacted]. git diff 0cf577a..e5289cf on the brief changes only line 5 (the header) and the D4 note. I checked every commit that holds the brief. In a032125, e5eb672, b098380, 428b194, e8905f9, d187fab and 0cf577a, line 5 reads '...byway's own CI estimates (minutes and dollars per run and per month, and byway's share of the included minutes), on which D4 was decided, are as written', and line 1096 reads '[redacted] of the included pool'. In the 14 commits before them (b015077 to 2e5fbfa), line 5 says 'The words are unchanged;'. That is HIST-R1's case, which now also covers the share markers. The evidence README at the tip says the share was 'taken out of those commits too', but not that their headers still call it as written.

**When it goes wrong:**

The merge message cites 15acbd2, for example, and the commit cannot be found on GitHub. Or a reader checks out a032125..0cf577a and finds [redacted] where the header promises byway's share is as written, with no note in the merge message explaining why.

**Proposed fix:**

In the merge message, use the current ids: a032125 (was 15acbd2), 7db9230 (was a9c4b58), b015077 to 2e5fbfa (was 11fc3b0 to b15a878). Also name the two new slips: e5289cf's message describes a brief redaction that its parent already carries, and a032125..0cf577a carry the share as [redacted] under a header that says it is as written. No further history change is needed.

**Refuter 1: partly, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding makes two claims. The first is refuted; the second, the two new slips, reproduces exactly.

Claim 1 is refuted: the merge message does not use the replaced ids. The queued items do still cite the old ids: round2-result.json 'open' names 15acbd2, and HIST-R1, MSG-R1 and REC2-4 in recheck-result.json name 11fc3b0, b15a878, a9c4b58 and 15acbd2. But the merge message the coordinator actually drafted is scratchpad/merge-msg.txt. It was written at 16:54, after the rewrite map (16:50) and after d8f077c (16:52). It uses only the current ids 7db9230, a032125 and b015077, all of which are ancestors of d8f077c, and it contains none of the four old ids. The failure scenario 'the merge message cites 15acbd2' therefore does not happen.

Claim 2 is confirmed: the rewrite created two slips, and the draft names neither.
(a) e5289cf's message is word for word the same as the old f8c0932's, and the two trees are identical. f8c0932 changed 4 lines of the brief: the header, the D4 note and the two share lines. e5289cf changes only 2: the header and the D4 note. In its parent 0cf577a, lines 1096 and 1147 already read [redacted]. Yet e5289cf's subject says the brief 'no longer state[s] ... byway's share of them', and its body says 'The D4 brief: its two share-of-pool percentages read [redacted]'. The message describes a change its diff no longer makes.
(b) Seven commits hold the same brief (blob 1c28408e): a032125 and its descendants e5eb672, b098380, 428b194, e8905f9, d187fab and 0cf577a. In each, line 5 says byway's share of the included minutes is 'as written', while lines 1096 and 1147 read [redacted]. Those two lines are byway's share; I checked their context with all digits masked.

The draft makes (b) worse than an omission. Its last slip says '(from a032125 on, the header says what was redacted)', which is wrong about byway's share for exactly those seven commits. The evidence README at the tip says the share 'was then taken out of those commits too'. That is not false, but it says nothing about the headers.

Minor imprecision in the finding: the 14 commits with the old header are b015077..2e5fbfa plus three side-branch commits, 38eafa8's sibling 194d6d2 and the merges aadfba0 and cf03a8d (all blob 4779ffde). That count of 14 is right.

Severity is should-fix, not blocking. No organisation value is exposed, the owner's words are quoted correctly, and the owner chose to name slips in the merge message rather than rewrite history. The fix is a merge-message text change, but without it the merge message would say something false about seven commits.

What I checked and found sound: every commit in 8401820..d8f077c that holds the brief has [redacted] on lines 1096 and 1147. The final brief (blob 0163ac80) carries the second-redaction note from e5289cf on. d8f077c changes only docs.

What I could not check: whether merge-msg.txt is the final text the coordinator will use. It is the only draft in the scratchpad containing the slip list.

**Evidence:**

Commands run in C:/Users/arvik/code/byway/.worktrees/fx-batch (read-only):

- C:/Users/arvik/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-arvik-code-byway/67b5b9ac-8595-4cc6-898a-d7d911b9d422/scratchpad/merge-msg.txt (mtime 16:54; d8f077c committed 16:52:24; hist2-map.json 16:50):
  - `grep -c -E '15acbd2|a9c4b58|11fc3b0|b15a878'` gives 0.
  - The only short ids in it are 7db9230, a032125 and b015077, each an ancestor of d8f077c.
  - Its last slip reads: "from b015077 up to a032125 the D4 brief carries its redacted text under a header that still says its words are unchanged (from a032125 on, the header says what was redacted)."
- Header and share lines across the history: I looped over `git rev-list 8401820..d8f077c` and, for each commit, read line 5 and lines 1096/1147 of the brief with digits masked.
  - Blob 4779ffde: b015077, f5f8723, 3ac3af5, 98d016d, 00401a0, 248e6fe, f7d7d91, 38eafa8, 7db9230, fd5655d, 2e5fbfa, 194d6d2, aadfba0, cf03a8d. Header "The words are unchanged"; both lines read "[redacted] of the (included) pool".
  - Blob 1c28408e: a032125, e5eb672, b098380, 428b194, e8905f9, d187fab, 0cf577a. Header "unchanged but for one redaction"; it contains "byway's share of the included minutes), on which D4 was decided, are as written", while both lines read [redacted].
  - Blob 0163ac80: e5289cf through d8f077c. Header "two redactions"; it contains "Claude replaced byway...".
- e5289cf against its pre-rewrite version f8c0932:
  - `git diff --quiet f8c0932 e5289cf` shows the trees are identical, and the messages are identical.
  - `git diff --numstat c001ced f8c0932 -- brief` gives "4 4"; `git diff --numstat 0cf577a e5289cf -- brief` gives "2 2" (only line 5 and the D4 note).
  - e5289cf's body says "The D4 brief: its two share-of-pool percentages read [redacted]; its header and its D4 note say so".
- Evidence README at d8f077c: "byway's share of the plan's included minutes, redacted in the brief at the owner's second answer, was then taken out of those commits too". It says nothing about the seven headers.
- round2-result.json 'open' still says "For the merge message: HIST-R1, MSG-R1, and REC2-4's slip in 15acbd2's message", but the draft has already translated those ids.

**Fix:**

No history change. Edit only the planned merge message, scratchpad/merge-msg.txt:

1. Keep its ids (7db9230, a032125, b015077); they are already current.
2. Replace the parenthetical "(from a032125 on, the header says what was redacted)" with something like: "from a032125 to 0cf577a the header names the first redaction but still says byway's share of the included minutes is as written, though the second rewrite redacted it there too; from e5289cf on, the header names both redactions."
3. Add: "e5289cf's message says the D4 brief's two share-of-pool percentages read [redacted], but after the second rewrite its parent already carries them; the commit changes only the brief's header and D4 note."

Optionally, add one line to the evidence README (a docs-only change) saying the headers of a032125..0cf577a still call the share 'as written'.

**Refuter 2: partly, should-fix.**

**Reasoning:**

The finding has three parts, and they come out differently.

(1) Stale ids: refuted for the merge message itself. The scratch items that feed the message still cite the old ids: round2-result.json 'open' and recheck-result.json's HIST-R1, MSG-R1 and REC2-4 name 15acbd2, a9c4b58, 11fc3b0 and b15a878. But the drafted merge message, scratchpad/merge-msg.txt, was written at 16:54, after the rewrite (hist2-map.json, 16:50) and after d8f077c (16:52). It already uses the new ids 7db9230, a032125 and b015077. None of the 25 pre-rewrite ids in hist2-map.json appears in the draft, in the tree at d8f077c, or in any commit message of 8401820..d8f077c. So the failure scenario, a merge message citing a commit GitHub does not have, does not happen with the draft as it stands.

(2b) Header says the share is "as written": confirmed, and the draft gets it wrong. The draft says the brief carries redacted text under an "unchanged" header from b015077 up to a032125, and that "(from a032125 on, the header says what was redacted)". In fact a032125, e5eb672, b098380, 428b194, e8905f9, d187fab and 0cf577a have a line 5 that names the first redaction and then says byway's own estimates "and byway's share of the included minutes ... are as written". In all seven, both share lines read [redacted]. So the parenthetical is false for the share in exactly those seven commits. The ranges are also loose in graph terms. 248e6fe, f7d7d91, 38eafa8, 194d6d2, aadfba0 and cf03a8d are not ancestors of a032125, yet all six hold the old "The words are unchanged;" header. The last three come after a032125 in date order, so "from a032125 on" is also wrong for them. This slip is a side effect of the second rewrite, which changed only the two share lines in old copies. The owner chose the rewrite, not this side effect. Under answer 2's rule ("Name the two commit-message slips in the merge message rather than rewriting history again"), it belongs in the merge message, stated correctly.

(2a) e5289cf's message: confirmed, but minor. The message says "The D4 brief: its two share-of-pool percentages read [redacted]; its header and its D4 note say so", and its subject says the brief "no longer state[s] ... byway's share". After the rewrite, its parent 0cf577a already has both share lines redacted. Its brief diff (4 lines) changes only line 5 and the D4 note. d8f077c's message ("Only the two share lines changed in those copies") and the evidence README at the tip ("was then taken out of those commits too, at the third") let a careful reader piece this together. After the first rewrite, the coordinator added a closing sentence to a032125's message saying the values were also taken out of earlier commits; e5289cf's message got nothing like it. The draft merge message does not name this slip.

Severity: should-fix fits. It does not reach blocking: no organisation detail is exposed (every share line in every commit is [redacted]), no owner words are misquoted, and the error sits in Claude's own header text and in a message not yet committed. But the owner's chosen remedy is to name the slips accurately in the merge message, and the draft currently says something false about seven commits.

Not checked: whether a later version of merge-msg.txt exists elsewhere, and the gate itself, which was out of scope for this refutation.

**Evidence:**

- Timing: merge-msg.txt mtime 16:54, hist2-map.json 16:50, d8f077c committed 2026-10-21 16:52:24. The draft's slip paragraph names 7db9230, a032125 and b015077.
- Old ids: grep for all 25 old 7-character ids from hist2-map.json found 0 hits in `git grep d8f077c`, 0 in `git log --format=%B 8401820..d8f077c`, and 0 in merge-msg.txt.
- Per-commit line 5 of docs/adr/evidence/decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md, by script over `git rev-list 8401820..d8f077c`:
  - "The words are unchanged;" header, both share lines [redacted] (14 commits): b015077, f5f8723, 3ac3af5, 98d016d, 00401a0, 248e6fe, f7d7d91, 38eafa8, 7db9230, fd5655d, 2e5fbfa, 194d6d2, aadfba0, cf03a8d.
  - "...and byway's share of the included minutes), on which D4 was decided, are as written" header, both share lines [redacted] (7 commits): a032125, e5eb672, b098380, 428b194, e8905f9, d187fab, 0cf577a.
  - Two-redactions header: e5289cf through d8f077c.
- `git merge-base --is-ancestor`: 194d6d2, aadfba0, cf03a8d, 248e6fe and 38eafa8 are not ancestors of a032125. `git rev-list a032125^..0cf577a` includes 194d6d2, aadfba0, cf03a8d, 248e6fe, f7d7d91 and 38eafa8, all with the old header.
- `git diff --stat 0cf577a e5289cf`: the brief changes 4 lines (header and D4 note only). Its message reads "The D4 brief: its two share-of-pool percentages read [redacted]; its header and its D4 note say so". a032125's message carries the first rewrite's added sentence ("The redacted values were also taken out of this branch's earlier commits ..."); e5289cf has no equivalent.
- All values were masked when read; none are reproduced here.

**Fix:**

Edit only the merge message draft; no further change to history. The ids in the draft are already correct, so keep them.
1. Replace the HIST-R1 sentence and its parenthetical with an exact list. In b015077, f5f8723, 3ac3af5, 98d016d, 00401a0, 248e6fe, f7d7d91, 38eafa8, 7db9230, fd5655d, 2e5fbfa, 194d6d2, aadfba0 and cf03a8d, the brief's header still says its words are unchanged. In a032125, e5eb672, b098380, 428b194, e8905f9, d187fab and 0cf577a, the header names the first redaction but still says byway's share of the included minutes is as written, though it reads [redacted] there. From e5289cf on, the header names both redactions.
2. Add one slip: e5289cf's message describes redacting the brief's two share lines, but after the second rewrite its parent already carries them redacted, and its diff changes only the header and the D4 note.

#### REC3-2: The second rewrite left 7 commits whose brief header says byway's share is "as written" while their share lines read [redacted]

*Note. Severity note; lens records; where `docs/adr/evidence/decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md:5`.*

**What is wrong:**

The 16:42 rewrite changed only the two share lines (1096, 1147) in old copies of the brief. In the 7 commits from a032125 (the first redaction's commit) to 0cf577a, the header still says the words are "unchanged but for one redaction". It also says byway's own estimates, "and byway's share of the included minutes", "are as written", and the D4 note says "byway's own estimates are as written". Their share lines now read [redacted]. The 13 older copies (b015077 to 7db9230 and others) have the HIST-R1 header that says the words are unchanged. No record or message names the new mismatch: d8f077c's message says only "Only the two share lines changed in those copies".

**Evidence:**

I mapped the 46 commits of 8401820..5abafbe (the reflog's pre-rewrite head) to 8401820..ca24d4e by author, date and subject. 21 kept their ids and none of them holds the brief. 21 brief holders changed exactly 2 lines of the brief (git diff -U0 shows 4 +/- lines) and nothing else. 4 round-two commits changed id only because their parents changed. The final trees are equal. Header check on the new ids: a032125, e5eb672, b098380, 428b194, e8905f9, d187fab and 0cf577a match "unchanged but for one redaction" and "and byway's share of the included minutes), on which D4 was decided, are as written". b015077's header reads "The words are unchanged; ...".

**When it goes wrong:**

Someone reads the brief at one of those 7 commits on GitHub after the push. The header says byway's share is printed as written, but the text shows [redacted], and nothing explains the gap. The owner chose a value-only rewrite and earlier declined a header pass, so this is expected, but it is not yet recorded.

**Proposed fix:**

Name it in the merge message together with HIST-R1. For example: the brief's header in a032125 to 0cf577a says byway's share is as written, but the 2026-10-21 16:42 rewrite redacted it; in b015077 to 7db9230 the header says the text is unchanged though the first answer's details are redacted. No file change is needed.

#### REC3-3: The slips planned for the merge message are named by pre-rewrite ids that are no longer on fix/fx-batch

*Note. Severity note; lens records; where `docs/adr/evidence/README.md:19`.*

**What is wrong:**

Round two's hand-off (round2-result.json "open") and the re-check's HIST-R1, MSG-R1 and REC2-4 name the commit-message slips by the ids 15acbd2, a9c4b58 and 11fc3b0. After the second rewrite those commits are a032125, 7db9230 and b015077. The old ids now resolve only to unreachable objects kept by the reflog. A merge message that copies them would cite commits nobody can reach on GitHub.

**Evidence:**

spawnSync git merge-base --is-ancestor &lt;id> d8f077c: 15acbd2, a9c4b58 and 11fc3b0 are NOT on the branch; a032125, 7db9230 and b015077 are. The rewrite map (author, date and subject match) gives 15acbd2->a032125, a9c4b58->7db9230 and 11fc3b0->b015077. Every id in the tree's text files and in the messages of 8401820..d8f077c resolves to a commit on fix/fx-batch, so only the not-yet-written merge message is at risk.

**When it goes wrong:**

The merge message says "the slip in 15acbd2's message". A reader on GitHub cannot find that commit, because the push carries a032125 instead.

**Proposed fix:**

Use the new ids in the merge message: a032125 (REC2-4's 'five entries' slip; MSG-R1's message that describes SEC-4 but holds none of it), 7db9230 (holds SEC-4's change and does not name it; its subject's 'every spelling of Temp') and b015077 (HIST-R1's 'unchanged' header and message).

#### REC3-4: "Owner's answer 1/2" in the commit messages and "the owner's second/third answer" in the evidence docs count differently

*Note. Severity note; lens records; where `docs/adr/evidence/README.md:19`.*

**What is wrong:**

The round's commit messages number the three 16:14 answers as "Owner's answer 1 (2026-10-21 16:14)" and "Owner's answer 2 (2026-10-21 16:14)". The evidence README, the brief's header and the D4 note number the owner's answers of the day instead: first = 13:41, second = 16:14, third = 16:42. The brief gives the time with each number. The README gives none for "the owner's first answer that day" or "the owner's second answer", and gives 16:42 only through its link.

**Evidence:**

e5289cf, fc7ac3a, 0442ef8 and ca24d4e subjects: "Owner's answer 1 (2026-10-21 16:14)" and "Owner's answer 2 (2026-10-21 16:14)". README line 19: "redacted at the owner's first answer that day", "redacted in the brief at the owner's second answer", "at the third". README row line 9: "at the owner's second answer that day". Brief line 5: "At the owner's first answer (13:41) ... At the owner's second answer (16:14)". Each statement is true under its own scheme.

**When it goes wrong:**

A reader matches the commit 'Owner's answer 2' (the 14 notes) to the README's 'second answer' (the redaction, all of 16:14) and looks for the wrong decision.

**Proposed fix:**

Add the times in the README, as the brief does: "the owner's first answer that day (13:41)", "second answer (16:14)", "the third (16:42)".

#### KEY2-1: The stand-in exemption's anchor misses '.', '/', '+' and '~', so some longer key-shaped strings built on the stand-in are not checked whole

*Note. Severity note; lens evidence; where `scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts:51`.*

**What is wrong:**

STAND_IN_ALONE checks for [A-Za-z0-9_-] on either side of the stand-in. The bearer shape's alphabet also includes . ~ + /, and the JWT shape joins its segments with '.'. So when the stand-in sits next to one of those characters it is still replaced with [stand-in], and the surrounding token is never seen whole. That contradicts the comment ('only on its own: a longer key-shaped string that starts or ends with it is checked whole') and 0442ef8's message.

**Evidence:**

I rebuilt the test's keyShaped in scratchpad/recheck2-lens/probe-redact.mts against the clone's redact.ts. Four strings give keyShaped=false although each is key-shaped without the exemption: `eyJ${'h'.repeat(20)}.${STAND_IN}`, `Authorization: Bearer ab.${STAND_IN}`, `Authorization: Bearer ${STAND_IN}.abcdefghij` and `Authorization: Bearer ${STAND_IN}/abcdefghij`. The only exempted occurrence today is at docs/reviews/2026-10-08-external-review-verification.md:3707, and it is in single quotes, so a wider anchor would still exempt it.

**When it goes wrong:**

Unlikely in practice. A bearer token or JWT that embeds the 24-letter stand-in after a '.' or '/' lands in the evidence or the reviews, and the scan stays green.

**Proposed fix:**

Anchor with the widest key alphabet on both sides, (?&lt;![A-Za-z0-9._~+/-]) and (?![A-Za-z0-9._~+/-]). Add one such fake to the self-test, for example `eyJ${'h'.repeat(20)}.${STAND_IN}`. Or narrow the comment to 'a longer string of key characters [A-Za-z0-9_-]'.

#### KEY2-2: The case-insensitive bearer shape now also redacts lowercase prose where 'bearer' is followed by a long unbroken word or path

*Note. Severity note; lens evidence; where `packages/gateway/src/redact.ts:17`.*

**What is wrong:**

/Bearer\s+[A-Za-z0-9._~+/-]{16,}=*/gi redacts 'bearer' followed by any run of 16 or more token characters. With the i flag that now includes ordinary lowercase prose. Only the gateway's diagnostic copies are affected: stored request and response bodies, ledger labels and error messages. The redact.ts contract calls these lossy on purpose and never rewrites product text. Before the change the same already happened after a capital 'Bearer'. No test pins where the boundary lies.

**Evidence:**

Probe against the clone's makeRedactor({}): 'flag [a key-shaped string] include the opening parade' becomes 'flag [redacted] include the opening parade'. 'the [a key-shaped string] note' becomes 'the [redacted] note'. '[a key-shaped string]' becomes '[redacted]'. These pass unchanged: 'The bearer of this letter is a friend.', 'A bearer token is required for this endpoint.', 'Missing bearer authentication header', 'She was the standard-bearer for the movement.' and a WWW-Authenticate realm line. requestSha256 and responseSha256 are computed over the redacted copies (gateway.ts:913-916) and are read nowhere else in src. errors.test.ts:33 'leaves ordinary text alone' has no 'bearer' prose case. The %3A fal shape left 'cursor=&lt;uuid>%3Asha256' and '&lt;uuid>%3A done' alone, and redacted '&lt;uuid>%3a<32 hex>'.

**When it goes wrong:**

A stored diagnostic copy, or an error message shown to the person, loses a phrase such as '[a key-shaped string]'. That is cosmetic, and it is within the redactor's stated contract.

**Proposed fix:**

No change needed. Optionally, add a prose case to 'leaves ordinary text alone' (for example 'A bearer token is required') so that a later widening of the shape cannot start eating short prose unnoticed.

*Note. Severity note; lens evidence; where `scripts/test/adr-evidence.test.ts:117`.*

**What is wrong:**

24e69f5 ported three files from Temp: scripts/test/claims-oracle/oracle.ts, scripts/test/claims-oracle/variants.ts and scripts/test/claims-oracle.test.ts. KEY-R1 added only the folder, so the test file is not scanned. A fixture pasted into claims-oracle.test.ts from a Temp run carries a key, and the scan does not read that file.

Optional: add 'scripts/test/claims-oracle.test.ts' to the scanned list (filesUnder takes a folder, so a one-file entry would need a small change), or say in the header that the scan covers only the oracle's folder.

#### HIST2-2: Pre-rewrite commits are still reachable locally through ORIG_HEAD and the reflogs; no ref and no push setting exposes them

*Note. Severity note; lens evidence; where `docs/adr/evidence/README.md:19`.*

**What is wrong:**

git for-each-ref is clean. Pre-rewrite objects remain in the local store, though. The fix/fx-batch reflog names 5abafbe and the commits before it. Scratch clones made before the rewrites have HEAD at c001ced or 481b2e5. A branch push sends none of this.

**Evidence:**

cat .git/worktrees/fx-batch/ORIG_HEAD gives f57389c1f639, which is not an ancestor of d8f077c. A word-bounded, case-insensitive name scan of f57389c finds names in docs/ (counts only). The same scan of every tree in 8401820..d8f077c, and of their messages, finds none. git config shows only remote.origin.url and fetch: no mirror, no push refspec, no upstream for fix/fx-batch. Every scratch clone under recheck-clones/ and check-clones/ has origin set to C:/Users/arvik/code/byway, not GitHub.

**When it goes wrong:**

Only an unusual command (pushing ORIG_HEAD or a reflog entry by id) would send these objects to GitHub. A normal push of fix/fx-batch cannot.

**Proposed fix:**

Nothing is needed before the push. Push fix/fx-batch by name. The old objects go away locally with reflog expiry and gc, whenever the owner wants.

#### evidence

- History, paired (scratchpad/recheck2-lens/ids.cjs): both 8401820..5abafbe (before the rewrite) and 8401820..ca24d4e (after) hold 46 commits. 21 kept their ids: the fix/fx-records lineage 37b0f56..af3bf5d plus 8b16f18, fix/fx-b1 6e4eb65..88853f5, and d98d5fa, ac83bac, 24e69f5 and d754402, from before the brief came in. The 25 rewritten commits follow hist2-map.json exactly. Their author and committer lines are byte-identical and their parents are remapped. Each tree differs from its old one only in decision-briefs-2026-10-16.md, at exactly lines 1096 and 1147 (masked values replaced by '[redacted]'), or not at all for the four round-two commits. Only two messages changed, and only by id swaps that follow the map: e8905f9 (one) and b098380 (four).
- Final tree: ca24d4e's tree equals 5abafbe's (7120d5e). round2-verify.log, which records 5,061/5,061 unit tests and 55/55 browser tests from about 16:41 to 16:48, ran after ca24d4e's commit time of 16:36. 5abafbe..d8f077c differs in exactly three docs: ADR-0109, the evidence README and conversation-continued.md.
- Organisation details in the history (scratchpad/recheck2-lens/scan.cjs and names-ci.cjs; output masked). I scanned for 81 token spellings derived from hist-redact.cjs's own pairs and from ADR-0103's pre-redaction Money line, with comma, space, k and thousand variants and percentage-range variants. The scan covered all 1,598 distinct blobs of the 48 trees (47 commits plus main), the refs/codex tree, and all 47 messages. Hits: ADR-0103's Money line only in commits before e5289cf (that line is byte-identical to main's and came from 51fedcf), plus coincidental numbers ([redacted]). There are no repository names (case-insensitive, word-bounded), plan, seats, price, overage or share-of-pool percentages anywhere in trees or messages. Positive controls: the same scans find the names in f57389c and the share percentages in 8401820..c001ced.
- Share phrasing: greps of the head tree and the messages for pool, included-minutes, share, fraction and overage wording find only redacted lines, byway's own estimates, and unrelated uses (rater pools, the corpus's 'Every plan includes free delivery'). There are no spelled-out minute figures.
- Refs: every local branch is an ancestor of d8f077c. fix/fx-evidence points at the rewritten f5f8723. fix/fx-b1 (88853f5) and fix/fx-records (af3bf5d) kept their ids. The origin refs are ancestors of main. There are no refs/original, stash, tags or notes. refs/codex points at a tree with no hits.
- Citations (cites.cjs): every 7-40-character hex token in the 47 messages and the 227 changed text files that names a commit names one reachable from d8f077c or origin, and none is ambiguous. ADR-0109's four new ids (e5eb672, 248e6fe, f7d7d91, 38eafa8) match the map. The hex tokens that resolve to nothing are bundle, recipe and session hashes.
- ADR-0103's Later note and the evidence README's history claims: origin/fix/fx-d4-ci's reflog shows 51fedcf pushed on 2026-10-16 at 21:49. It reached main through 643616e on 2026-10-17, as its second parent. 8401820, 0cf577a and main have the same Money line; e5289cf and d8f077c have the redacted one.
- Conversation record: in the transcript, the only owner input after 16:14 is the 16:42 answer, apart from task notifications. The record's 16:41 question and 16:42 answer match the verbatim text in the task.
- Mutation proofs in the clone recheck2-clones/evid-hist (baseline: 36/36 across adr-evidence, errors and keys; files restored and checked by sha256; git status clean). Bearer back to /g: errors' case test and the self-test fail (bearerLowerCase). [Bb]earer only: the errors test fails on BEARER. Fal colon only: errors' fal test and the self-test fail (falUrlEncoded). Stand-in by substring: the self-test fails (longerThanTheStandIn, endingInTheStandIn). No lookbehind: fails on endingInTheStandIn. No lookahead: fails on longerThanTheStandIn. Old Temp pattern: the spelling test fails (%TMP%, $env:TMP, $TEMP/, ${TEMP}/). No slash after TEMP: the negative $TEMPLATE/x fails. No \b after $env:TMP: $env:TEMPLATE fails. Key planted in claims-oracle/oracle.ts: the scan fails. The same key with the folder taken off the list: all pass, so the new folder entry is what catches it.
- Temp pattern (temp.cjs): the old and new patterns flag the same nine Accepted records (0081, 0092, 0093, 0097, 0098, 0102, 0103, 0105, 0106), all with the note. No record of any status holds a newly read spelling. The spellings still not read (/tmp/, $TMP/, %LOCALAPPDATA%\Temp) appear in no record's cited text. ADR-0066's %LOCALAPPDATA% is the data folder, not Temp.
- Redactor probes against the clone's redact.ts: short 'bearer' prose and a realm header pass unchanged. Underscore-bearing bearer tokens are redacted, since the class includes '_'. A URL-encoded uuid followed by short text passes; a uuid, %3a and 32 hex characters is redacted.
